The capability is already implemented and already reachable. `Verify_Document_Checksum` parses and verifies the whole group for all ten algorithms for exactly these documents (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:5033-5039`, ending at `:5163`):

```ada
      function Verify_Document_Checksum
        (Document : String) return Document_Checksum_Status
      is
         SDK_Count : constant Natural := Apps.Request_Header_Count
           (X, "x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm");
         Trailer_Declaration_Count : constant Natural :=
           Apps.Request_Header_Count (X, "x-amz-trailer");
```

and the crate already applies the accept-either rule on other configuration routes, in `Read_Bucket_Scalar_Control` (`:5194-5201`):

```ada
         elsif Require_Checksum
           and then MD5_Count = 0
           and then SDK_Count = 0
           and then Checksum_Value_Header_Count = 0
         then
            Send_Error
              (X, 400, "InvalidRequest",
               Operation_Name & " requires a request checksum", Target_Text);
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) makes the checksum group the way the requirement is met, and does not make `Content-MD5` mandatory anywhere. Printed from the model with python3:

```
DeleteObjects httpChecksum= {"requestAlgorithmMember": "ChecksumAlgorithm", "requestChecksumRequired": true}
  input shape DeleteObjectsRequest ContentMD5 ABSENT | required: ['Bucket', 'Delete']
  members: Bucket, Delete, MFA, RequestPayer, BypassGovernanceRetention, ExpectedBucketOwner, ChecksumAlgorithm
PutBucketVersioning httpChecksum= {"requestAlgorithmMember": "ChecksumAlgorithm", "requestChecksumRequired": true}
  input shape PutBucketVersioningRequest ContentMD5 PRESENT | required: ['Bucket', 'VersioningConfiguration']
  members: Bucket, ContentMD5, ChecksumAlgorithm, MFA, VersioningConfiguration, ExpectedBucketOwner
PutObjectTagging httpChecksum= {"requestAlgorithmMember": "ChecksumAlgorithm", "requestChecksumRequired": true}
  input shape PutObjectTaggingRequest ContentMD5 PRESENT | required: ['Bucket', 'Key', 'Tagging']
  members: Bucket, Key, VersionId, ContentMD5, ChecksumAlgorithm, Tagging, ExpectedBucketOwner, RequestPayer
```

`ContentMD5` is in no such operation's `required` list, and for `DeleteObjects` the member does not exist in the input shape, so a client generated from this model has no modelled way to send it there. The model's `DeleteObjectsRequest.ChecksumAlgorithm` documentation, with HTML stripped, states what does satisfy the requirement:

```
Indicates the algorithm used to create the checksum for the object when you
use the SDK. ... When you send this header, there must be a corresponding
x-amz-checksum-algorithm or x-amz-trailer header sent. Otherwise, Amazon S3
fails the request with the HTTP status code 400 Bad Request .
```

The same shape carries `requestChecksumRequired: true` on 27 operations, so the other configuration and object-control PUTs listed above are affected by inspection.

The crate documents the rule as intended behaviour and never discloses that a model-driven client cannot comply (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:506-509`):

```
DeleteObjects accepts the exact `delete` subresource forms, validates a
bounded 1,000-key REST/XML document and payload hash, and requires canonical
Content-MD5 over the exact admitted body. It also verifies each of the ten
modeled SDK checksum algorithm/header pairs.
```

That statement contradicts the model it is written against: the model marks the operation `requestChecksumRequired` and gives its request shape no `ContentMD5` member. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:638-639` states the same rule for the configuration PUTs, and `docs/qualification/delete-objects.md:10-12` repeats it ("It requires one canonical Content-MD5 computed over the exact body bytes and can verify one matching SDK checksum algorithm/value pair").

The existing tests pass because they never send the model-conformant shape. `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:16039-16045` asserts the rejection of a request with no integrity header at all, which AWS also rejects, and so locks in the current gate:

```ada
         Require
           (Has
              (Run
                 (Signed_Delete_Objects_Request
                    (Payload, Include_MD5 => False)),
               "<Code>InvalidRequest</Code>"),
            "DeleteObjects accepted a missing Content-MD5");
```

The ten-algorithm loop at `:16080-16097` leaves `Include_MD5` at its default `True` (`:1097-1113`), so every accepted flexible-checksum case also carries `Content-MD5`, and the cross-server wire slice drives DeleteObjects only through the pinned s5cmd image (`tests/scripts/run-s3-server-slice.sh:9` and `:104-107`), whose requests this server accepts today, so no lane sends a checksum-only batch.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates two buckets and four objects in the in-process memory-backed application, then sends DeleteObjects, PutBucketVersioning and PutObjectTagging twice each, once with a correct `x-amz-checksum-crc32` plus `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32` and no `Content-MD5` (asserting 200, which is what the model describes), and once with `Content-MD5` as the control. It also re-reads the key the checksum-only batch should have deleted.

```ada
   function Flexible_Checksum_Headers (Payload : String) return String is
     ("x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32" & CRLF &
      "x-amz-checksum-crc32: " & CRC32_Base64 (Payload) & CRLF);
...
      Response : constant String :=
        Run (Signed_Request
               ("POST", "/md5-bucket", Payload,
                Extra_Headers => Flexible_Checksum_Headers (Payload),
                Query         => Delete_Query));
   begin
      Check (Status_Code (Response) = 200,
             "DeleteObjects with a valid x-amz-checksum-crc32 and " &
             "x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32 and no Content-MD5 is 200");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: fixture: CreateBucket /md5-bucket
  ok: fixture: PutObject /md5-bucket/modelled
  -- DeleteObjects flexible checksum headers: x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32\r\nx-amz-checksum-crc32: YZjakw==\r\n
  -- DeleteObjects CRC32 only, no Content-MD5: status 400  body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidRequest</Code><Message>DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header</Message><Resource>/md5-bucket?delete</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: DeleteObjects with a valid x-amz-checksum-crc32 and x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32 and no Content-MD5 is 200
  FAILED: DeleteObjects CRC32-only response reports the deleted key
  -- GET /md5-bucket/modelled after the CRC32-only batch: status 200  body payload
  FAILED: the CRC32-only DeleteObjects actually removed the key
  -- CONTROL DeleteObjects with Content-MD5: status 200  body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><DeleteResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Deleted><Key>control</Key></Deleted></DeleteResult>
  ok: CONTROL: DeleteObjects with Content-MD5 is 200
  -- PutBucketVersioning CRC32 only, no Content-MD5: status 400  body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidRequest</Code><Message>PutBucketVersioning requires one valid Content-MD5 header</Message><Resource>/md5-bucket?versioning</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: PutBucketVersioning with a valid flexible checksum and no Content-MD5 is 200
  -- CONTROL PutBucketVersioning with Content-MD5: status 200  body 
  ok: CONTROL: PutBucketVersioning with Content-MD5 is 200
  -- PutObjectTagging CRC32 only, no Content-MD5: status 501  body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>NotImplemented</Code><Message>Object tagging SDK checksums are not implemented</Message><Resource>/md5-control/tagged?tagging</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: PutObjectTagging with a valid flexible checksum and no Content-MD5 is 200
  -- CONTROL PutObjectTagging with Content-MD5: status 200  body 
  ok: CONTROL: PutObjectTagging with Content-MD5 is 200
BUG REPRODUCED: 5 failed expectation(s)
```

The control requests, which differ only in carrying `Content-MD5`, all succeed, so the bodies, the signatures and the routes are right and only the integrity-header choice is refused.

Two stock clients were then driven against the real application to confirm this is the current default and not a hypothetical.

aws-cli/2.36.34 with awscrt 0.36.2, driven against the crate's own loopback listener. A second program created the same fixtures, opened the listener on 127.0.0.1 and held it open for 45 s; the CLI was pointed at that loopback endpoint with only `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` and `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` set and no checksum configuration of any kind:

```
### aws s3api list-objects-v2 (sanity)
{
    "Contents": [ { "Key": "modelled", ... "Size": 7 } ], ...
}

### aws s3api delete-objects (stock defaults)
aws: [ERROR]: An error occurred (InvalidRequest) when calling the DeleteObjects operation: DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header

### aws s3api put-bucket-versioning (stock defaults)
aws: [ERROR]: An error occurred (InvalidRequest) when calling the PutBucketVersioning operation: PutBucketVersioning requires one valid Content-MD5 header

### aws s3api put-object-tagging (stock defaults)
aws: [ERROR]: An error occurred (NotImplemented) when calling the PutObjectTagging operation: Object tagging SDK checksums are not implemented

### aws s3api put-bucket-tagging (stock defaults)
aws: [ERROR]: An error occurred (InvalidRequest) when calling the PutBucketTagging operation: PutBucketTagging requires a valid Content-MD5
```

`list-objects-v2`, `s3 cp`, `s3api put-object` and single-key `s3 rm` all succeeded against the same listener in the same session, so authentication, signing and routing are fine and only these routes fail. The bytes the CLI put on the wire, captured with a plain socket listener so nothing about the Ada server could influence them:

```
POST /md5-bucket?delete HTTP/1.1
Host: 127.0.0.1:64829
User-Agent: aws-cli/2.36.34 md/awscrt#0.36.2 ... md/command#s3api.delete-objects
x-amz-checksum-crc64nvme: hBxFfoI/0Yk=
x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC64NVME
X-Amz-Date: 20260907T013904Z
X-Amz-Content-SHA256: 063b9d00...8ad7
Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=AKIDEXAMPLE/...,
  SignedHeaders=host;x-amz-checksum-crc64nvme;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date;x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm, Signature=...
Content-Length: 101

<Delete xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Object><Key>modelled</Key></Object></Delete>
```

There is no `Content-MD5` field, and the default algorithm is CRC64NVME, which the server's own `Verify_Checksum` supports.

boto3 and botocore 1.43.89 on Python 3.13.7, resolved from PyPI by `uv run --python 3.13 --with boto3` with nothing installed system-wide, driven against the repository's prebuilt `tests/bin/s3_memory_server` (started as `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=AKIDEXAMPLE AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=... ./tests/bin/s3_memory_server 0`, no container). The client reported its own defaults as `request_checksum_calculation = when_supported` and `response_checksum_validation = when_supported`. Its `delete_objects` call, verbatim:

```
=== e. delete_objects (two keys), boto3 defaults ===
--- request POST http://127.0.0.1:58179/boto3-checksum-probe?delete
    Authorization: b'AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=AKIDEXAMPLE/20260907/us-east-1/s3/aws4_request, SignedHeaders=host;x-amz-checksum-crc32;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date;x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm, Signature=eaa6e7167dbba6eab89843e988b58361b1e60cc65249b0b4e7e4a78592b2e6cd'
    Content-Length: 137
    X-Amz-Content-SHA256: b'4795d650e61e4eca68f8f20f376576c402445deb89b9b58ec953d1a43eb9f038'
    x-amz-checksum-crc32: b'ApXd/g=='
    x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: b'CRC32'
--- request body: bytes(137): b'<Delete xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Object><Key>absent-1</Key></Object><Object><Key>absent-2</Key></Object></Delete>'
--- response status 400
    Content-Type: application/xml
--- response body: '<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidRequest</Code><Message>DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header</Message><Resource>/boto3-checksum-probe?delete</Resource></Error>'
--- CLIENT ERROR: An error occurred (InvalidRequest) when calling the DeleteObjects operation: DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header
```

`put_bucket_versioning` failed identically. In the same session `create_bucket`, `put_object` with defaults and with an explicit algorithm, `create_multipart_upload`, two 5 MiB `upload_part` calls, `complete_multipart_upload` and six `head_object`/`get_object` calls with `ChecksumMode=ENABLED` were all accepted with 200, so the object path is unaffected and only the `requestChecksumRequired` routes fail. The probe exits 1 on those two cases alone.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

An operation the model marks `requestChecksumRequired` must be satisfiable by the checksum group the model names, `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm` together with `x-amz-checksum-<alg>` or `x-amz-trailer`, because that is the integrity mechanism the trait selects and the only one a client generated from this model can use on DeleteObjects. The server already computes and compares that group over the exact admitted body, so accepting it costs no integrity guarantee.

## Suggested treatment

Move the flexible-checksum verdict ahead of the `Content-MD5` gate on these routes and accept it as an alternative, which is the shape `Read_Bucket_Scalar_Control` already uses at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:5194-5201`: in the DeleteObjects arm accept when `Checksum_Status` is the verified value or a valid `Content-MD5` is present, and in the PutBucketVersioning, PutBucketTagging and object-tagging arms call `Verify_Document_Checksum` before rejecting for a missing `Content-MD5`. For object tagging the 501 at `:15888-15893` has to become an accept path as well, otherwise the operation stays uncallable. No new public constant, type or parameter is needed for any of that.

What remains open is the policy at the other end of the rule, and it needs a maintainer decision:

- Accept either header and require at least one, so a request carrying neither is still 400 `InvalidRequest`. This matches what the crate does on the routes that go through `Read_Bucket_Scalar_Control` and keeps the current corpus assertion for a request with no integrity header true.
- Accept either header and require neither, so an unauthenticated-integrity request is admitted like an ordinary PUT. This is closer to what a request with no `httpChecksum` trait gets, and it weakens the guarantee the qualification documents claim.
- Keep `Content-MD5` mandatory where the model still defines the member and relax only DeleteObjects and PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration, whose request shapes have no `ContentMD5` member. This is the smallest change but leaves stock clients broken on the other 25 operations.

Whichever is chosen, `docs/compatibility/s3.md:506-509` and `:638-639`, `docs/qualification/delete-objects.md:10-12`, `docs/qualification/bucket-versioning.md:47` and `docs/qualification/bucket-tagging.md:51` state the current rule as a qualification claim and must be restated with it.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the DeleteObjects checksum block: the helper `Signed_Delete_Objects_Request` (`:1097-1113`) already takes `Include_MD5 => False` and an `Extra_Headers` string, so the new case is one call asserting 200 for a checksum-only batch, and the ten-algorithm loop at `:16080-16097` should be repeated with `Include_MD5 => False`. The assertion at `:16039-16045` pins the current rejection of a request with no integrity header and must be reworded or kept deliberately, depending on which option above is taken. The spike is the test. Because the failure is a stock-client interop break rather than a header-shape detail, a case driving a current aws-cli v2 or boto3 through `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh` (which already starts `tests/bin/s3_memory_server` and hands it to a wire slice) would catch a regression the in-process corpus cannot, since the corpus builds its own requests.

## Severity

Four ordinary operations, and by inspection every one of the 27 the model marks `requestChecksumRequired`, cannot be called from a current AWS SDK with default settings. The user does nothing unusual: an unconfigured `aws s3api delete-objects` or a boto3 `delete_objects` on a default client is refused with 400 `InvalidRequest`, and nothing is deleted or configured. `DeleteObjects` has no workaround at all, because the request shape has no `ContentMD5` member to set by hand; the others can be worked around only by setting `request_checksum_calculation=when_required` and passing `ContentMD5=` explicitly. The object path is unaffected, so a deployment that only puts and gets objects will not notice. Deterministic.

## Summary

In a versioned bucket the memory backend folds "the selected generation is a delete marker" onto the same `Natural` 0 it uses for "no such generation", so every read of a marker returns `Not_Found` and the server answers a bare 404 `NoSuchKey`. Two behaviours the pinned model states verbatim are lost: a read of a key whose current version is a delete marker does not carry `x-amz-delete-marker: true`, and a read of the marker's own version returns 404 instead of 405 with `Last-Modified`. A versioning-aware client therefore cannot tell "this key was deleted" from "this key never existed", and every key in a versioned bucket enters that state on the next read after any DELETE.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The selector discards the distinction before any read can see it (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:318-329`):

```ada
      function Selected_Object_Index
        (Bucket : String; Key : String; Selector : Version_Selector)
         return Natural
      is
         Selected : constant Natural :=
           Selected_Generation_Index (Bucket, Key, Selector);
      begin
         return
           (if Selected /= 0
              and then not Objects (Selected).Is_Delete_Marker
            then Selected else 0);
      end Selected_Object_Index;
```

The GetObject arm forwards the resulting status unchanged (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:17259-17265`):

```ada
                  elsif not Apps.Wire_Response_Started (X) then
                     if Result = Invalid_Range and then Info.Size >= 0 then
                        Apps.Set_Header
                          (X, "Content-Range",
                           "bytes */" & Decimal (Info.Size));
                     end if;
                     Send_Backend_Error (X, Result, False, Target_Text);
```

and so does the HeadObject arm (`:16954-16955`). `Send_Backend_Error` has no marker-aware branch (`:1927-1934`):

```ada
         when Not_Found =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 404,
               (if Is_Bucket then "NoSuchBucket" else "NoSuchKey"),
               (if Is_Bucket
                then "The specified bucket does not exist"
                else "The specified key does not exist"),
               Resource);
```

Nothing in the server can recover the fact, because nothing in the backend contract carries it. The public `Status` type has no marker outcome (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:39-62`):

```ada
   type Status is
     (Success,
      Not_Found,
      Bucket_Not_Found,
      Tag_Set_Not_Found,
...
      Not_Implemented,
      Backend_Unavailable);
```

`Object_Information` has no marker field either (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:554-562`):

```ada
   type Object_Information is record
      Size          : Byte_Count := 0;
      Modified      : Unix_Time := 0;
      Entity_Tag    : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Content_Type  : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Version       : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Checksum      : Checksum_Information;
      Metadata      : Object_Metadata;
   end record;
```

so the read contracts have no channel for it (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2049-2069`):

```ada
   --  Return one selected immutable metadata snapshot. Conditions are
   --  evaluated against the same snapshot and retained on conditional failure.
...
   procedure Head_Object
     (Item   : in out Backend;
      Bucket : String;
      Key    : String;
      Token    : access Flyology.Cancellation.Token;
      Deadline : Ada.Real_Time.Time;
      Info   : out Object_Information;
      Result : out Status;
      Conditions : Read_Conditions := Default_Read_Conditions;
      Selector : Version_Selector := Current_Version_Selector) is abstract;
```

`Get_Object_Attributes` (`:2106-2116`) reports through the same `Result : out Status` with no marker channel either, and `Get_Object` reports through the same type. The information does exist in the store: `Listed_Version.Is_Delete_Marker` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:98-104`) is populated at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:2671` and comes back correctly from `List_Object_Versions`, so this is a gap in the read interface rather than a memory-backend slip, and the files and SQLite backends read through the same contract.

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) states both behaviours in `shapes.GetObjectOutput.members.DeleteMarker`, which binds `"location": "header"`, `"locationName": "x-amz-delete-marker"`. Its documentation, with HTML stripped:

```
Indicates whether the object retrieved was (true) or was not (false) a Delete
Marker. If false, this response header does not appear in the response. If the
current version of the object is a delete marker, Amazon S3 behaves as if the
object was deleted and includes x-amz-delete-marker: true in the response. If
the specified version in the request is a delete marker, the response returns
a 405 Method Not Allowed error and the Last-Modified: timestamp response
header.
```

`shapes.HeadObjectOutput.members.DeleteMarker` carries the same header binding.

MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned by `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` (`quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`), started by hand as a single container on `127.0.0.1:19000` and driven with the `aws` CLI, answers exactly that way: HEAD of a key whose current generation is a delete marker returns 404 with `x-amz-delete-marker: true` and `x-amz-version-id: <marker>`, and HEAD and GET of the exact marker version return 405.

This is not a documented limitation. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:538-541` documents marker publication and removal with no read-side caveat:

```
Omitted, `null`, and opaque VersionId members map to current, null, and exact
backend selectors. Memory, files, and SQLite publish current delete markers, remove
selected data or markers, echo requested version identities, and return
created/removed marker identities.
```

and the exclusion lists for HeadObject (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:59`) and GetObject (`:858-859`, "GetObject part selection, non-null versions, and encrypted SSE-C objects remain explicit cross-cutting exclusions") do not mention delete-marker reads.

No existing test asserts the right behaviour and none asserts the wrong one over the wire: `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:18202-18207` covers only the DeleteObject response headers, and `:17750-17755` only that an exact-version delete carries no marker header. At the backend interface the current behaviour is pinned, `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:859-861`:

```ada
      Store.Head_Object
        (Bucket, "alpha", null, Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Info, Result);
      Require (Result = Not_Found, "suspended marker did not hide current");
```

with the same shape at `:447-450`, so a fix must update those expectations.

## What happens now

Spike: the first program below creates a bucket, enables versioning, writes `k` and a second live key, deletes `k` and captures the marker version id from `x-amz-delete-marker`/`x-amz-version-id`, then asserts the two documented behaviours on GET and HEAD of the current version and of the marker's own version. Controls confirm the DELETE itself reports the marker and a version id, a live key still reads 200, and a key that never existed reads 404 with no marker header.

```ada
      Response : constant String :=
        Run (Signed_Request ("GET", "/marker-bucket/k"));
   begin
      Check (Status_Code (Response) = 404,
             "GET of a current delete marker is 404");
      Check (Header_Value (Response, "x-amz-delete-marker") = "true",
             "GET of a current delete marker carries " &
             "x-amz-delete-marker: true");
...
      Response : constant String :=
        Run (Signed_Request
               ("GET", "/marker-bucket/k",
                Query =>
                  (1 => SigV4.Pair
                     ("versionId", Marker_Version (1 .. Marker_Last)))));
   begin
      Check (Status_Code (Response) = 405,
             "GET of an exact delete-marker version is 405 " &
             "MethodNotAllowed");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: fixture: PutBucketVersioning Enabled
  ok: fixture: PutObject /marker-bucket/k
  -- CONTROL DELETE /marker-bucket/k: status 204  x-amz-delete-marker=[true]  x-amz-version-id=[f37f4dffd982f843741984a21d253e64aa73d8a80500283bd999f2928f6f4eed]  Last-Modified=[]
  ok: CONTROL: DELETE reports x-amz-delete-marker: true
  -- GET a key whose current version is a delete marker: status 404  x-amz-delete-marker=[]  x-amz-version-id=[]  Last-Modified=[]
  --    body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>NoSuchKey</Code><Message>The specified key does not exist</Message><Resource>/marker-bucket/k</Resource></Error>
  ok: GET of a current delete marker is 404
  FAILED: GET of a current delete marker carries x-amz-delete-marker: true
  -- HEAD a key whose current version is a delete marker: status 404  x-amz-delete-marker=[]  x-amz-version-id=[]  Last-Modified=[]
  ok: HEAD of a current delete marker is 404
  FAILED: HEAD of a current delete marker carries x-amz-delete-marker: true
  -- GET ?versionId=<the marker itself>: status 404  x-amz-delete-marker=[]  x-amz-version-id=[]  Last-Modified=[]
  FAILED: GET of an exact delete-marker version is 405 MethodNotAllowed
  FAILED: GET of an exact delete-marker version carries x-amz-delete-marker: true
  FAILED: GET of an exact delete-marker version carries Last-Modified
  -- HEAD ?versionId=<the marker itself>: status 404  x-amz-delete-marker=[]  x-amz-version-id=[]  Last-Modified=[]
  FAILED: HEAD of an exact delete-marker version is 405 MethodNotAllowed
  FAILED: HEAD of an exact delete-marker version carries x-amz-delete-marker: true
  -- CONTROL GET of a live key: status 200  x-amz-delete-marker=[]  x-amz-version-id=[77d55163c888e8ba05bf5972434e4870281279b1f5546f8145f9158af0f53a58]  Last-Modified=[Mon, 07 Sep 2026 06:11:21 GMT]
  ok: CONTROL: GET of a live key still reads 200
  -- CONTROL GET of a key that never existed: status 404  x-amz-delete-marker=[]  x-amz-version-id=[]  Last-Modified=[]
  ok: CONTROL: GET of an absent key is 404
  ok: CONTROL: GET of an absent key carries no delete-marker header
BUG REPRODUCED: 7 failed expectation(s)
```

The 404 for the marker-hidden key and the 404 for the key that never existed are byte-identical.

A second program shows why the fix cannot be made in the server alone. It drives a private `Backends.Memory.Store` directly, publishes a marker, and asks only that the read surface distinguish the two cases in any observable way at all, on the status, the version, or the timestamp:

```ada
   Store.Head_Object
     (Bucket, "never-existed", null, Deadline, Absent_Info, Absent_Status);
   Store.Head_Object (Bucket, Key, null, Deadline, Marker_Info, Marker_Status);
   Check (Marker_Status /= Absent_Status
            or else US.To_String (Marker_Info.Version) /=
              US.To_String (Absent_Info.Version)
            or else Marker_Info.Modified /= Absent_Info.Modified,
          "current HEAD distinguishes a delete marker from an absent key");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: setup: object published
  -- setup: live version 41b6ea08994cc7f6cbd3be48b86d242d2028e178ac6b576a668acdc4fe92a602
  ok: setup: simple delete published a delete marker
  -- setup: marker version 9386a25901aec99598857ac6f2616db093bd4fc09678d8ca98ce1c22bef3b6f3
  -- control: listed version '9386a25901aec99598857ac6f2616db093bd4fc09678d8ca98ce1c22bef3b6f3' is_latest TRUE is_delete_marker TRUE
  ok: control: the listing surface reports the marker generation
  -- head absent key          : NOT_FOUND size 0 modified 0 version ''
  -- head marker-hidden key   : NOT_FOUND size 0 modified 0 version ''
  FAILED: current HEAD distinguishes a delete marker from an absent key
  -- head unknown version id  : NOT_FOUND size 0 modified 0 version ''
  -- head exact marker version: NOT_FOUND size 0 modified 0 version ''
  FAILED: exact HEAD distinguishes a delete marker from an unknown version
  FAILED: exact HEAD of a marker carries the marker's Last-Modified time
  -- head exact live version  : SUCCESS size 7 modified 1788761516 version '41b6ea08994cc7f6cbd3be48b86d242d2028e178ac6b576a668acdc4fe92a602'
  ok: control: exact HEAD of the retained live generation succeeds
  -- attributes exact marker  : NOT_FOUND
  FAILED: GetObjectAttributes on a marker generation is not plain Not_Found
BUG REPRODUCED: 4 failed expectation(s)
```

The listing control passes, so the store holds the fact and only the read contracts cannot carry it. `Get_Object_Attributes` has the same hole, and every other read in that backend resolves through the same selector function, which has sixteen call sites in `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb`.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A read of a key whose current version is a delete marker should still be a 404, but carry `x-amz-delete-marker: true`, and a read that names the marker's own version should answer 405 `MethodNotAllowed` with `Last-Modified`, as the pinned model's `GetObjectOutput.DeleteMarker` documentation states and as the pinned MinIO release does.

## Suggested treatment

The backend must be able to say that the generation it selected is a marker, so this needs a maintainer decision. The choice is a visible addition to `src/flyology-object_storage.ads`, and under the "Ada interface values" and "Decision authority" sections of `AGENTS.md` it is not the implementer's to make:

- Add a `Status` literal, for example `Delete_Marker_Selected`, to the visible enumeration at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:39-62`, returned with an `Object_Information` that still carries `Modified` and `Version`. Every `case` on `Status` in the crate and in any out-of-tree backend has to gain an arm, which is the wider break, but the outcome stays a single value that `Send_Backend_Error` can map.
- Add an `Is_Delete_Marker` field to `Object_Information` at `:554-562` and keep returning `Not_Found` with a populated snapshot. Existing `case` statements keep compiling, but a caller that ignores the new field sees no change, and the read contracts (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2049-2069` for `Head_Object`, `:2106-2116` for `Get_Object_Attributes`) have to state that `Info` is meaningful on that particular `Not_Found`.

Either way the change spans the interface: `Selected_Object_Index` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:318-329`) must stop collapsing the two cases, the reads that turn its 0 into `Not_Found` must propagate the new outcome (`Head` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:1736-1746`, `Fetch` at `:1562-1565`, and `Attributes` beside them), the files and SQLite backends must implement it identically, the `Head_Object`, `Get_Object` and `Get_Object_Attributes` contracts must document it, and `Send_Backend_Error` (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1927-1934`) plus the two read arms must emit `x-amz-delete-marker: true` on the current-version 404 and 405 with `Last-Modified` on the exact-version read.

The wire regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the DeleteObject marker assertions at `:18202-18207`, and the backend-level one in `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb`, whose assertions at `:859-861` and `:447-450` require a marker read to be plain `Not_Found` today and must change with the outcome. Both programs above are the tests.

## Severity

Every key in a versioned bucket enters this state on the next read after any DELETE, so it is not an unusual input: the client that just deleted a key, or any other client, gets a response indistinguishable from "this key never existed". Replication, backup and sync tooling keys on `x-amz-delete-marker` to reproduce deletions, and a tool that walks ListObjectVersions and reads each generation cannot tell that the version it asked for is a marker. No data is lost and no wrong body is returned; the object's earlier generations remain readable by exact version. Deterministic.

## Summary

HeadObject writes `x-amz-version-id` unconditionally and substitutes the literal `null` when the object has no version, while GetObject writes the header only when a version exists or the request asked for one, and PutObject omits it for unversioned publication. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450` defines three distinct cases and reserves the `null` spelling for a suspended bucket, so HEAD reports an object in a never-versioned bucket with the spelling that means something else. A client doing HEAD then GET on the same key gets two different answers for the same object.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

HeadObject's `Set_Common_Headers` always writes the header (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16619-16622`):

```ada
                     Apps.Set_Header
                       (X, "x-amz-version-id",
                        (if US.Length (Info.Version) > 0
                         then US.To_String (Info.Version) else "null"));
```

The GetObject responder writes it conditionally (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:4189-4194`):

```ada
            if US.Length (Info.Version) > 0 then
               Apps.Set_Header
                 (X, "x-amz-version-id", US.To_String (Info.Version));
            elsif Object_Read_Request.Has_Version_ID then
               Apps.Set_Header (X, "x-amz-version-id", "null");
            end if;
```

The crate's own rule has three cases, not two (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450`):

```
Successful PutObject returns its version identity from the same backend
publication snapshot as the body and metadata. The server omits the header for
unversioned publication, emits the exact opaque identity for enabled
memory/files/SQLite publication, and emits `x-amz-version-id: null` for
suspended memory/files/SQLite publication.
```

HeadObject conflates the first case with the third. The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) gives the two reads no licence to differ: `shapes.HeadObjectOutput.members.VersionId` and `shapes.GetObjectOutput.members.VersionId` are the same binding, `{"shape": "ObjectVersionId", "location": "header", "locationName": "x-amz-version-id"}`, with the same documentation "Version ID of the object." What AWS itself emits on a never-versioned bucket was not checked against a live server, and the finding does not rest on it: the disagreement is between the two arms of this server and its own compatibility document.

No existing test asserts either spelling directly, but one exchange depends on the current one.

That consequence is worth stating before anyone changes the header, because it is load-bearing for the in-repository test lanes. The typed client rejects a 200 whose returned version identity does not equal the one the request selected, and it applies that to the `null` selection like any other. `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5641-5658`:

```ada
   function Head_Object_Response_Bound
     (Value    : Head_Object_Result;
      Prepared : Prepared_Request) return Boolean
   is
      Requested_Version : constant String := US.To_String
        (Prepared.Requested_Head_Object_Version_ID);
...
   begin
      return
        (Requested_Version'Length = 0
         or else US.To_String (Value.Version_ID) = Requested_Version)
```

with the same predicate for GetObject at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:121-137` and for HeadObject at `:139-155`. The implementation corpus makes exactly that request against a never-versioned bucket (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1144-1157`):

```ada
            Parameters : Low_Level.Head_Object_Parameters;
         begin
            Parameters.Version_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String ("null");
```

Because this server always supplies a version identity on HEAD, and supplies `null` on GET whenever the request named one, that case passes against this server and is red against the pinned third-party reference servers. Those client predicates are reported separately as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/39 and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/46.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below writes one object into a never-versioned bucket and one into a versioning-enabled bucket, then compares the `x-amz-version-id` header of GET and HEAD for the same key. Controls confirm PutObject omits the header for unversioned publication, the two reads agree on a real version identity in the versioned bucket, and an explicit `?versionId=null` selection reports `null` on both reads.

```ada
      Got  : constant String := Run (Signed_Request ("GET", "/plain-bucket/k"));
      Head : constant String := Run (Signed_Request ("HEAD", "/plain-bucket/k"));
      G    : constant String := Header_Value (Got, "x-amz-version-id");
      H    : constant String := Header_Value (Head, "x-amz-version-id");
   begin
      Check (G = H,
             "GET and HEAD report the same x-amz-version-id for the same " &
             "unversioned object");
      Check (Header_Count (Head, "x-amz-version-id") = 0,
             "HEAD of an object in a never-versioned bucket omits " &
             "x-amz-version-id, as GetObject already does");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: fixture: CreateBucket /plain-bucket (never versioned)
  ok: fixture: PutObject /plain-bucket/k
  -- PutObject on the never-versioned bucket: x-amz-version-id header count 0 value []
  ok: CONTROL: PutObject omits x-amz-version-id for unversioned publication, as docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450 states
  ok: fixture: PutBucketVersioning Enabled
  ok: fixture: PutObject /versioned-bucket/k
  -- never-versioned bucket: GET status 200 x-amz-version-id=[] (header count 0)
  -- never-versioned bucket: HEAD status 200 x-amz-version-id=[null] (header count 1)
  ok: CONTROL: both reads of the unversioned object are 200
  FAILED: GET and HEAD report the same x-amz-version-id for the same unversioned object
  FAILED: HEAD of an object in a never-versioned bucket omits x-amz-version-id, as GetObject already does
  -- CONTROL versioned bucket: GET x-amz-version-id=[45dda79046d651db6ab22b5ae137da89aebed4224cb052ff10a9d76ab6530b12]  HEAD x-amz-version-id=[45dda79046d651db6ab22b5ae137da89aebed4224cb052ff10a9d76ab6530b12]
  ok: CONTROL: in a versioned bucket GET and HEAD agree on a real version id
  -- CONTROL explicit ?versionId=null: GET x-amz-version-id=[null]  HEAD x-amz-version-id=[null]
  ok: CONTROL: an explicit versionId=null selection reports null on both reads
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The `null` value does round-trip: a client that feeds HEAD's answer back as `?versionId=null` still gets 200 from both reads, which is why no client behaviour was observed to change.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model. Not compared against a third-party server.

## What should happen

HeadObject and GetObject should report the same `x-amz-version-id` for the same object, and the header should be omitted for an object published without versioning, which is the case `docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450` distinguishes from suspended publication and which PutObject and GetObject already implement.

## Suggested treatment

Make the HEAD arm match the GET arm: replace the unconditional `Set_Header` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16619-16622` with the conditional form at `:4189-4194`, so the header is written when the object has a version and written as `null` only when the request asked for a version. No public value, type or parameter changes.

Sequencing matters more than the edit. The typed client predicates quoted above require a returned version identity whenever a version was selected, and this server's unconditional header is what keeps the in-repository corpus green against it; the corpus fails against third-party servers at the same exchange. The narrow fix above preserves the `null` answer for an explicit `?versionId=null` request, so it leaves the lanes green, but any change that also dropped the header in that case would turn them red until https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/39 and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/46 are fixed. Those two should land first, so that a later decision about the explicit-`null` case is not blocked by the client.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the other HeadObject header assertions: one case asserting that HEAD of an object in a never-versioned bucket carries no `x-amz-version-id`, and one asserting that HEAD and GET of the same key agree. The spike is the test; no existing assertion has to change.

## Severity

The two reads of one object disagree, and HEAD uses the spelling this crate documents for a suspended bucket to describe a bucket that was never versioned, so a client that keys on the header sees a bucket state that does not exist. No client behaviour was observed to change: the value round-trips as `?versionId=null` and both reads still answer 200. It affects every HEAD of every object in a never-versioned bucket, which is the default bucket state. Deterministic.

## Summary

Every successful CompleteMultipartUpload returns `<Location>/bucket/key</Location>`. The pinned model documents that member as the URI that identifies the newly created object, and a value with no scheme is a relative reference, not a URI. A caller that treats the member as an address has to know the endpoint out of band to use it, and cannot compare it with what AWS or another S3 server returned for the same upload.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The completion result is built with a path (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:13192-13197`):

```ada
                        Response : Multipart.Complete_Multipart_Upload_Result
                          := (Location => US.To_Unbounded_String
                                ("/" & Bucket & "/" &
                                 Encoding.URI_Encode
                                   (Key, Encode_Slash => False)),
                              Bucket => US.To_Unbounded_String (Bucket),
                              Key => US.To_Unbounded_String (Key),
```

and the serializer emits it verbatim (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart.adb:1125`):

```ada
      Append_Optional (Content, "Location", US.To_String (Value.Location));
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) documents `shapes.CompleteMultipartUploadOutput.members.Location` as:

```
{'shape': 'Location'}
The URI that identifies the newly created object.
```

The `Location` shape is a plain string, so the model constrains only the documented meaning, not a syntax. RFC 3986 section 3 requires a scheme in a URI, so `/mpu-bucket/dir/object.bin` is a relative reference and does not identify the object on its own.

The crate documents nothing about this member. The CompleteMultipartUpload row of the compatibility matrix (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:70`) claims the operation without excluding the result address:

```
| CompleteMultipartUpload | memory, files, SQLite | complete typed composable and synchronous clients; authenticated general-purpose path-style server | atomic condition/size/checksum completion, strict signed control and body-boundary corpus, 46-case admission/certainty oracle including embedded HTTP-200 errors, ...
```

No existing test asserts either form.

The reason this is not a mechanical fix is that the server has no scheme to build a URI from. `Apps.Request_Scheme` is `Plain_HTTP` for every request this repository can produce, because neither the runtime nor the test harness passes a `Scheme` to `Apps.Create` (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:168-170`):

```ada
            X : Apps.Exchange := Apps.Create
              (Request, Client, Peer, Cancellation,
               HTTP.Request_Deadline (Client));
```

and `tests/src/s3_server_harness.adb:89-91`:

```ada
         X : Apps.Exchange := Apps.Create
           (Request, HTTP, Peer, Cancellation,
            HTTP_Server.Request_Deadline (HTTP));
```

## What happens now

Spike: the program below runs a one-part multipart upload on `/mpu-bucket/dir/object.bin` and asserts that `<Location>` is an absolute URI naming the object. Controls confirm the completion is 200 and that `<Bucket>`, `<Key>` and the composite `<ETag>` are present and correct, and that `<Location>` at least names the object.

```ada
      Where : constant String := Element_Text (Doc, "Location");
...
      Check (Contains (Where, "://"),
             "CompleteMultipartUpload <Location> is an absolute URI " &
             "(the model documents 'The URI that identifies the newly " &
             "created object')");
      Check (Contains (Where, "dir/object.bin"),
             "CONTROL: <Location> at least names the object");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: fixture: CreateBucket /mpu-bucket
  ok: fixture: CreateMultipartUpload returns an UploadId
  ok: fixture: UploadPart is 200
  -- CompleteMultipartUpload status 200
  --    body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><CompleteMultipartUploadResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Location>/mpu-bucket/dir/object.bin</Location><Bucket>mpu-bucket</Bucket><Key>dir/object.bin</Key><ETag>"dba2eb2ed95ff6ac73da0388b617482b-1"</ETag><ChecksumCRC64NVME>6d18xdZ10Zg=</ChecksumCRC64NVME><ChecksumType>FULL_OBJECT</ChecksumType></CompleteMultipartUploadResult>
  --    <Location> = [/mpu-bucket/dir/object.bin]
  ok: CONTROL: CompleteMultipartUpload is 200
  ok: CONTROL: the result names the bucket
  ok: CONTROL: the result names the key
  ok: CONTROL: the result carries a composite ETag
  FAILED: CompleteMultipartUpload <Location> is an absolute URI (the model documents 'The URI that identifies the newly created object')
  ok: CONTROL: <Location> at least names the object
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

Every other member of the result document is correct; only `<Location>` is a path.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

`<Location>` should identify the newly created object, which is what the pinned model documents for that output member. Either it carries an absolute URI, or the crate states in `docs/compatibility/s3.md` that it deliberately returns a path and why.

## Suggested treatment

Building an absolute URI requires choosing where the scheme and authority come from, which is externally visible policy, so this needs a maintainer decision:

- Derive the address from the request: `http` or `https` from `Apps.Request_Scheme` plus the signed `Host` header, giving `scheme://host/bucket/key`. This needs no new configuration, but it makes a response member depend on a client-supplied header, and it always yields `http://` for this repository's own server, since `Apps.Create` is never given a `Scheme` at either call site above.
- Add a configured public base URI to the S3 application's construction, used as the prefix. This produces a correct address behind a TLS terminator or a proxy, and it is a new public configuration value with a default to choose, which the "Ada interface values" section of `AGENTS.md` reserves for the maintainer.
- Keep the path deliberately and document it as a boundary in `docs/compatibility/s3.md`, next to the CompleteMultipartUpload row at `:70`. Nothing changes on the wire and the divergence from the model's documented meaning stays, disclosed.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, in the CompleteMultipartUpload block, asserting whichever form is chosen for `<Location>` on an ordinary one-part completion. The spike is the test; no existing assertion has to change, because none covers the member.

## Severity

Every multipart completion returns a value of the wrong form in a modelled output member. Nothing fails today: aws-cli and boto3 surface the member verbatim without dereferencing it, and the rest of the completion document is correct, so the effect is confined to a caller that stores or resolves the address, or a conformance comparison against another S3 server. It affects every user of multipart upload, and the response is otherwise a success. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Verify_Request` treats "the payload hash is neither 64 hex digits nor `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`" as the same failure as a broken Authorization header, so a request carrying `x-amz-content-sha256: STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER` is answered 400 `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed` with "The authorization header is malformed". That sentinel is what aws-cli v2 and boto3 send for PutObject and UploadPart over HTTPS, and the real cause is an unimplemented payload encoding, which the crate documents and reports correctly one branch away as 501 `NotImplemented` with "aws-chunked payloads are not implemented". The operator is told their credentials are wrong when the signature was never examined.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The payload-hash shape check is one disjunct of the chain that returns `Malformed_Credentials` (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:138-148`):

```ada
        or else
          (Payload_Hash /= SigV4.Unsigned_Payload
           and then not Encoding.Valid_SHA256_Hex (Payload_Hash))
        or else (Applications.Request_Header_Count
                   (X, "x-amz-security-token") > 0
                 and then (Applications.Request_Header_Count
                              (X, "x-amz-security-token") /= 1
                           or else not Verification.Header_Is_Signed
                             (Parsed.Data, "x-amz-security-token")))
      then
         return (Status => Malformed_Credentials, others => <>);
      end if;
```

That chain starts at `:130` and returns before the provider is asked to verify anything; the signature work begins in the block at `:177`. The S3 layer renders the single status as a signing complaint (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1889-1892`):

```ada
         when Authentication.Malformed_Credentials =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 400, "AuthorizationHeaderMalformed",
               "The authorization header is malformed", Resource);
```

The arm that describes the real situation is one branch away and is reached only when the payload hash has an ordinary shape (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14892-14895`):

```ada
                     Send_Error
                       (X, 501, "NotImplemented",
                        "aws-chunked payloads are not implemented",
                        Target_Text);
```

with the UploadPart twin at `:12618-12621`.

Rejecting `aws-chunked` framing is a documented limitation, and this issue does not ask for it to be implemented. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:342-345` states the boundary:

```
general-purpose profile, including fixed-length and HTTP transfer-chunked
streaming and physical checksum trailers. Inner SigV4 `aws-chunked` framing,
advanced policy controls, directory buckets, and HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 server
qualification remain explicit separate capabilities.
```

and `:457-459` repeats it:

```
server-compatibility boundary. SigV4 streaming `aws-chunked` payloads are also
rejected before reading the source until a signature-chain decoder is
available; ordinary application content encodings remain supported verbatim.
```

Two things keep this worth reporting. The documented exclusion names *SigV4 streaming* `aws-chunked`, while `STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER` is the unsigned variant, and `:342-343` positively claims physical checksum trailers, so the document does not name the sentinel a modern client actually sends. And neither sentence says anything about the status code, so a documented capability boundary is being reported with an error code that points at the wrong subsystem.

The sentinel is the current default over HTTPS. Captured from aws-cli/2.36.34 through a local TLS terminator holding a self-signed certificate, for `s3api put-object`:

```
PUT /live-bucket/tls.txt HTTP/1.1
Expect: 100-continue
Transfer-Encoding: chunked
Content-Encoding: aws-chunked
X-Amz-Trailer: x-amz-checksum-crc64nvme
X-Amz-Decoded-Content-Length: 15
x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC64NVME
X-Amz-Content-SHA256: STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER
Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=..., SignedHeaders=content-encoding;host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date;x-amz-decoded-content-length;x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm;x-amz-trailer, Signature=...
```

Over plain `http://` the same client sends a fixed-length body with a header checksum instead, and the server accepts it: an ordinary `aws s3 cp` and `aws s3api put-object` both succeeded against the crate's own loopback listener. This crate ships no TLS listener, so the failing shape appears only when a TLS terminator sits in front of the server, which is the ordinary way to serve S3 over HTTPS.

No existing test covers a payload-hash sentinel that is neither hex nor `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below sends PutObject and UploadPart with `Content-Encoding: aws-chunked`, `x-amz-decoded-content-length`, `x-amz-trailer` and `x-amz-content-sha256: STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER`, and asserts that the server does not blame the authorization header and answers the 501 it already uses for the same situation. Controls confirm that the same request with an ordinary hex payload hash gets exactly that 501, that an ordinary PutObject still succeeds, and that a well-shaped but wrong payload hash is still `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`. The sentinel is substituted into an otherwise ordinary signed request, which is faithful because the server decides before checking the signature, and necessary because the crate's own signer rejects that payload hash before producing a signature (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4.adb:255-258`):

```ada
        or else (Payload_Hash /= Unsigned_Payload and then
                 not Encoding.Valid_SHA256_Hex (Payload_Hash))
      then
         raise Invalid_Signing_Input with "invalid SigV4 input";
```

The essential lines of the program:

```ada
   Chunked_Headers : constant String :=
     "Content-Encoding: aws-chunked" & CRLF &
     "x-amz-decoded-content-length: 15" & CRLF &
     "x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC64NVME" & CRLF &
     "x-amz-trailer: x-amz-checksum-crc64nvme" & CRLF;
...
      Response : constant String :=
        Run (With_Sentinel (Base, "STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER"));
   begin
      Check (not Contains (Body_Of (Response),
                           "<Code>AuthorizationHeaderMalformed</Code>"),
             "an unimplemented streaming payload encoding is not reported " &
             "as a malformed authorization header");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: fixture: CreateBucket /stream-bucket
  -- PutObject with STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER + aws-chunked: status 400
  --    body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>AuthorizationHeaderMalformed</Code><Message>The authorization header is malformed</Message><Resource>/stream-bucket/o</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: an unimplemented streaming payload encoding is not reported as a malformed authorization header
  FAILED: PutObject with STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER is 501 NotImplemented, like the aws-chunked path it belongs to
  -- UploadPart with STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER + aws-chunked: status 400
  --    body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>AuthorizationHeaderMalformed</Code><Message>The authorization header is malformed</Message><Resource>/stream-bucket/o?partNumber=1&amp;uploadId=0123456789abcdef</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: UploadPart does not report the streaming sentinel as a malformed authorization header
  -- CONTROL aws-chunked with an ordinary signed payload hash: status 501 body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>NotImplemented</Code><Message>aws-chunked payloads are not implemented</Message><Resource>/stream-bucket/o</Resource></Error>
  ok: CONTROL: aws-chunked with an ordinary hash is 501 NotImplemented
  ok: CONTROL: an ordinary PutObject still succeeds
  -- CONTROL a genuinely wrong (but well-shaped) payload hash: status 400 body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch</Code><Message>The provided payload hash does not match the request body</Message><Resource>/stream-bucket/bad</Resource></Error>
  ok: CONTROL: a wrong payload hash is still rejected
```

The two controls are the point: the identical request with an ordinary payload hash is answered 501 with the correct explanation, and a genuinely wrong payload hash is answered with the code that names the payload hash. Only the recognised streaming sentinel is reported as a credentials problem.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A request whose payload hash is a recognised streaming sentinel the server does not implement should be reported as unimplemented, with the 501 `NotImplemented` and the "aws-chunked payloads are not implemented" message the crate already emits for the same situation, and not as a malformed authorization header. A payload hash that is genuinely unrecognisable should keep its current treatment.

## Suggested treatment

This changes where the boundary between authentication and the S3 application sits, so it needs a maintainer decision:

- Give `Verify_Request` a distinct outcome for a recognised-but-unimplemented streaming payload sentinel, and map it in `Send_Authentication_Error` beside `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1889-1892`. This is a new literal in the visible `Authentication` outcome type, which the "Ada interface values" section of `AGENTS.md` reserves for the maintainer, and every consumer of that type gains an arm.
- Have the S3 application inspect `x-amz-content-sha256` for the streaming sentinels before calling `Verify_Request`, and answer 501 there. No public type changes, but it moves a decision ahead of authentication, so an unauthenticated caller could distinguish this build's streaming support from a generic rejection.

Either way the recognised set has to be written down: the unsigned trailer sentinel captured above alone, or the signed streaming payload sentinels as well. No `STREAMING-` value appears anywhere in `src/` or `docs/` today, so the set is new text either way. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:342-345` and `:457-459` should name the sentinel and the status code a client receives, since they currently describe the exclusion in terms of SigV4 streaming only.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the existing aws-chunked admission cases, asserting the chosen status and code for a PutObject and an UploadPart carrying the sentinel. The spike is the test; no existing assertion has to change.

## Severity

Over plain HTTP, which is the only transport this crate serves, a stock PutObject does not use this shape and succeeds, so an ordinary deployment is unaffected. It matters once TLS fronts the server, which is the normal way to expose S3: aws-cli v2 and boto3 then send the sentinel on every PutObject and UploadPart, every upload fails, and the message accuses the operator's credentials while the signature was never checked. No data is at risk and the request is refused either way; what is wrong is the diagnosis, and it sends the operator to the signing code instead of to the documented streaming boundary. Deterministic.

## Summary

When a PutObject request carries `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: <A>` together with a value header `x-amz-checksum-<B>` for a different algorithm, the preflight answers 400 `InvalidRequest` with "The checksum algorithm and value header differ". The pinned model's documentation for `PutObjectRequest.ChecksumAlgorithm` says Amazon S3 fails that request with a `BadDigest` error. The status is 400 either way and the object is not stored either way, so what differs is the `<Code>` a caller or a conformance harness keyed on the error code observes.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The selector is taken from the SDK header (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14786-14802`), and the branch inside `Checksum_Value_Header_Count = 1` rejects a disagreement (`:14813-14821`):

```ada
                     if Count ("x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm") = 1
                       and then Selected /= Checksum_Value_Algorithm
                     then
                        Send_Error
                          (X, 400, "InvalidRequest",
                           "The checksum algorithm and value header differ",
                           Target_Text);
                        return;
                     end if;
```

`Put_Object_Annotation` uses the same mapping with the message "The checksum algorithm and value differ" (`:15555-15562`), but that is a Flyology extension operation with no model text and is unaffected either way.

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) documents the opposite code. `shapes.PutObjectRequest.members.ChecksumAlgorithm`, with HTML stripped:

```
Indicates the algorithm used to create the checksum for the object when you
use the SDK. ... If the individual checksum value you provide through
x-amz-checksum-algorithm doesn't match the checksum algorithm you set through
x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm , Amazon S3 fails the request with a BadDigest
error.
```

The server is not missing a `BadDigest` mapping: it emits that code at 18 sites in the same unit, including `:17689` and `:18017`, and does so on this very operation for a genuine value mismatch, as the second control below shows.

Two facts weigh against the model's prose, and they are why this is a question about which code is right rather than a plain defect.

First, `BadDigest` is not a modelled error shape anywhere in the pinned model (a search for a shape whose name contains `BadDigest` returns nothing), while PutObject's modelled `errors` list is exactly:

```
--- PutObject errors --- ['InvalidRequest', 'InvalidWriteOffset', 'TooManyParts', 'EncryptionTypeMismatch']
```

so the code the server emits is PutObject's own modelled error, and `BadDigest` appears only inside documentation strings.

Second, the model's error-code table defines `BadDigest` as a content mismatch, which is exactly the meaning this server reserves it for:

```
Code: BadDigest
Description: The Content-MD5 you specified did not match what we received.
HTTP Status Code: 400 Bad Request
SOAP Fault Code Prefix: Client
```

Reserving `BadDigest` for a real digest mismatch and `InvalidRequest` for an inconsistent header combination is a defensible reading of the same document.

The model gives UploadPart a different rule for the same header pair, "If you provide an individual checksum, Amazon S3 ignores any provided ChecksumAlgorithm parameter", and the server implements that separately, so the PutObject branch is not a consequence of one shared policy.

Nothing in the crate records a deliberate deviation. `docs/qualification/put-object.md:33` is the only statement about the member and says nothing about error codes:

```
| 11 | `ChecksumAlgorithm` | Select one ordinary full-object algorithm. |
```

An existing test pins the current code. `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6334-6341` builds the mismatched request:

```ada
      SDK_Mismatch : constant String := Run
        (Signed_Request
           ("PUT", "/test-bucket/put-budget", "bad",
            Extra_Headers =>
              "x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: SHA256" & CRLF &
              "x-amz-checksum-crc32: " & Checksum_Value (Core.CRC32, "bad") &
              CRLF,
            Hash_Override => Wrong_Hash));
```

and `:6428` requires the answer this issue questions, under the message at `:6431`:

```ada
      Require
        (Has (SDK_Mismatch, "<Code>InvalidRequest</Code>")
         and then Has (SDK_Missing_Value, "<Code>InvalidRequest</Code>")
         and then Has (Invalid_MD5, "<Code>InvalidDigest</Code>"),
         "PutObject checksum-group preflight failed");
```

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates a bucket in the in-process memory-backed application, computes the canonical CRC32 of the body `"bad"` with the crate's own checksum API, and sends three signed PutObjects. Control 1 is the same request with a matching selector, which must be accepted. Control 2 is a well-formed but wrong CRC32 value, which must be `BadDigest`. The third is the finding: `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: SHA256` with an `x-amz-checksum-crc32` carrying the body's true CRC32, asserting the `BadDigest` the model documents.

```ada
      Mismatch : constant String := Run
        (Signed_Request
           ("PUT", "/" & Bucket & "/" & Key, Body_Text,
            Extra_Headers =>
              "x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: SHA256" & CRLF &
              "x-amz-checksum-crc32: " & CRC32_Value & CRLF));
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- canonical CRC32 of "bad" = gis5+w==
  -- CreateBucket status 200
  ok: control: CreateBucket is 200
  -- matching selector status 200
  ok: control: matching sdk-algorithm/value header is accepted
  -- value/content mismatch status 400
  -- value/content mismatch body: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>BadDigest</Code><Message>The checksum does not match the request body</Message><Resource>/checksum1-bucket/mismatch-key-wrong</Resource></Error>
  ok: control: a value/content mismatch is answered BadDigest
  -- algorithm-name mismatch status 400
  -- algorithm-name mismatch body: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidRequest</Code><Message>The checksum algorithm and value header differ</Message><Resource>/checksum1-bucket/mismatch-key</Resource></Error>
  ok: algorithm-name mismatch is a 400
  FAILED: model documents BadDigest for an sdk-algorithm/value mismatch
  FAILED: algorithm-name mismatch is not answered InvalidRequest
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

Control 1 shows the request shape, the payload hash and the CRC32 value are all valid, so the rejection comes from the selector disagreement alone; control 2 shows the server does emit `BadDigest` on this operation when the digest itself is wrong.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

One of the two codes must be the crate's answer for this input, chosen against the pinned model and written down. Today the model's `ChecksumAlgorithm` prose names `BadDigest`, its modelled `errors` list for PutObject names `InvalidRequest`, its error table defines `BadDigest` as a content mismatch, and the crate documents nothing.

## Suggested treatment

The error code is externally visible, so this needs a maintainer decision:

- Emit `BadDigest` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14813-14821`, matching the model's `ChecksumAlgorithm` documentation literally. This is a one-line change, and it overloads a code the same model's error table defines as a content mismatch, which the server currently uses for exactly that. `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6428` asserts `InvalidRequest` for this case and would have to change.
- Keep `InvalidRequest` and record the reading in `docs/qualification/put-object.md`, beside the `ChecksumAlgorithm` row at `:33`: `BadDigest` is reserved for a digest that does not match the body, and an inconsistent header combination is a malformed request, which is the general one of PutObject's four modelled errors. Nothing changes on the wire and the existing assertion stands, with the divergence from the model's prose disclosed rather than silent.

Whichever is chosen, `Put_Object_Annotation` at `:15555-15562` should be brought into line with it so the two paths do not drift apart.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, where the case already exists: the fixture at `:6334-6341` stays and the assertion at `:6428` states the chosen code. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A client that sets both checksum headers inconsistently gets 400 with a different `<Code>` than the model's prose describes. Nothing else changes: the status is 400 either way, the object is not stored either way, and a stock SDK raises the same exception class for both, so no caller is known to branch on the difference. It is reached only by a client that names one algorithm in the SDK header and another in the value header, which a stock SDK does not do on its own. A conformance harness keyed on the error code is the case that sees it. Deterministic.

## Summary

The shared multipart listing builder orders same-key uploads by initiation time, filters them against the `upload-id-marker` by lexical upload ID, and builds the continuation cursor from the last upload it returned. Those three rules are mutually inconsistent whenever the two orders disagree, which is the normal case here because upload IDs are SHA-256 digests with no relation to creation order. A client that pages ListMultipartUploads with the markers the service itself returned loses every same-key upload whose ID sorts at or before the marker, and the truncated flag goes false, so nothing tells the client that uploads are missing.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The page order for a shared key is initiation time, with the upload ID only as an equal-time tie break (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-multipart_listing.adb:17-30`):

```ada
   function Before (Left, Right : Candidate) return Boolean is
      Left_Key  : constant String := US.To_String (Left.Key);
      Right_Key : constant String := US.To_String (Right.Key);
   begin
      return Left_Key < Right_Key
        or else
          (Left_Key = Right_Key
           and then
             (Left.Initiated < Right.Initiated
              or else
                (Left.Initiated = Right.Initiated
                 and then US.To_String (Left.Upload_ID) <
                   US.To_String (Right.Upload_ID))));
   end Before;
```

The exclusive marker test is purely lexical on the upload ID and never mentions `Initiated` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-multipart_listing.adb:84-91`):

```ada
         if Candidate_Key < After_Key
           or else
             (Candidate_Key = After_Key
              and then
                (After_ID'Length = 0 or else Candidate_ID <= After_ID))
         then
            return;
         end if;
```

The continuation cursor is the key and upload ID of the last returned candidate, so the service produces the marker pair that its own filter then misreads (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-multipart_listing.adb:141-149`):

```ada
      if Result.Is_Truncated then
         if Returned = 0 then
            Result.Next_After := Item.Options.After;
         else
            Result.Next_After.Key := Item.Candidates (Returned).Key;
            Result.Next_After.Upload_ID :=
              Item.Candidates (Returned).Upload_ID;
         end if;
      end if;
```

The two orders disagree by construction in the memory backend. The upload ID is a SHA-256 digest (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:2831-2834`):

```ada
         Upload_ID := Ada.Strings.Unbounded.To_Unbounded_String
           (GNAT.SHA256.Digest
              (Bucket & Character'Val (0) & Key & Character'Val (0) &
               Long_Long_Integer'Image (Next_Upload)));
```

and the initiation stamp has whole-second resolution (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:207-208`):

```ada
   function Current_Unix_Time return Unix_Time is
     (Unix_Time (Long_Long_Integer (Ada.Calendar.Clock - Epoch)));
```

so two uploads more than a second apart get distinct `Initiated` values and the page order is decided by time, while the marker filter still decides by digest.

Nothing above the backend repairs it. The server copies `key-marker` and `upload-id-marker` straight into the backend options (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11191-11197`):

```ada
                            (Prefix    => Request.Prefix,
                             Delimiter => Request.Delimiter,
                             After     =>
                               (Key       => Request.Key_Marker,
                                Upload_ID => Request.Upload_ID_Marker),
                             Maximum   =>
                               Backends.List_Limit (Request.Max_Uploads));
```

and copies the backend's cursor straight back out (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11240-11247`):

```ada
                           if Page.Is_Truncated then
                              Response.Next_Key_Marker :=
                                US.To_Unbounded_String
                                  (Encoded
                                     (US.To_String (Page.Next_After.Key)));
                              Response.Next_Upload_ID_Marker :=
                                Page.Next_After.Upload_ID;
                           end if;
```

The two rules are the two AWS rules, and the repository has already written down that they must not be assumed to coincide (`docs/qualification/list-multipart-uploads-preparation.md:39-45`):

```
AWS separately describes response order as ascending key and, for a shared
key, ascending initiation time. Its marker rule permits same-key uploads only
when their upload IDs compare after the supplied upload-ID marker. The later
implementation and oracle must exercise inverse upload-ID/initiation-time data
rather than assume those orders coincide. A service-produced marker pair must
always resume without skipping, repeating, or looping, with a deterministic
upload-ID tie break for equal initiation times.
```

The pinned botocore S3 model at botocore commit `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768` states the same two halves. `ListMultipartUploadsRequest.UploadIdMarker`: "Together with key-marker, specifies the multipart upload after which listing should begin. If key-marker is not specified, the upload-id-marker parameter is ignored. Otherwise, any multipart uploads for a key equal to the key-marker might be included in the list only if they have an upload ID lexicographically greater than the specified `upload-id-marker`." `ListMultipartUploadsOutput.NextUploadIdMarker`: "When a list is truncated, this element specifies the value that should be used for the `upload-id-marker` request parameter in a subsequent request." A page bound is not unusual input: `ListMultipartUploadsRequest.MaxUploads` is documented there as "from 1 to 1,000".

The backend contract states the two rules side by side without reconciling them (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2711-2715`):

```ada
   --  Return one atomic snapshot of active uploads after the exclusive marker.
   --  Keys use unsigned bytewise lexical order. Uploads with the same key use
   --  initiation-time order and upload ID breaks equal-time ties; marker
   --  filtering independently follows S3's lexical upload-ID rule.
   --  Delimiter-collapsed prefixes count toward Maximum like returned uploads.
```

The existing coverage passes while asserting a weaker property. All five uploads are created in one tight loop (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:3318-3328`), so the two same-key uploads share an `Initiated` value and the page order falls through to the upload-ID tie break, which coincides with the marker rule. The marker assertion uses a deliberately chosen smaller and larger ID rather than creation order (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:3379-3389`):

```ada
      Options := (others => <>);
      Options.After.Key := US.To_Unbounded_String ("same");
      Options.After.Upload_ID := Smaller_ID;
      Store.List_Multipart_Uploads
        (Bucket, Options, null, Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Page, Result);
      Assert
        (Result = Success and then Page.Uploads.Length = 2
         and then US.To_String (Page.Uploads (1).Upload_ID) =
           US.To_String (Larger_ID)
         and then US.To_String (Page.Uploads (2).Key) = "zeta",
         "multipart upload-ID marker filtering");
```

No test creates same-key uploads across a second boundary, so no test asserts the wrong behaviour and none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below initiates six multipart uploads for one key on a private memory store, one second apart, lists them unbounded as a control, then walks the listing with `Maximum => 1` following the cursor the service returns, asserting each upload comes back exactly once. It then repeats the walk over the in-process S3 application with `?uploads&max-uploads=1&key-marker=...&upload-id-marker=...`.

```ada
      for Index in 1 .. Uploads_Wanted loop
         if Index > 1 then
            Support.Advance_One_Second;
         end if;
         Store.Create_Multipart_Upload
           (Bucket, Key, Backends.Default_Multipart_Options, null, Deadline,
            Created (Index), Result);
...
      --  The walk under test: Maximum = 1, following the returned cursor.
      Options := (others => <>);
      Options.Maximum := 1;
      for Step in 1 .. 3 * Uploads_Wanted loop
         Store.List_Multipart_Uploads
           (Bucket, Options, null, Deadline, Page, Result);
...
         exit when not Page.Is_Truncated;
         Options.After := Page.Next_After;
      end loop;
```

Output, exit status 1, with the six per-upload initiation lines trimmed to the first and last:

```
  ok: backend: bucket created
  ok: backend: upload1 initiated
  -- backend: initiation order1 id dc3e6a657ac580f3a4df5f53a1aa570d861b135eabcd560f2e5936367c9cddd5
  ok: backend: upload6 initiated
  -- backend: initiation order6 id c4dd2ccd99b9625d41552478b4bd692ba625a4db414a4994e09cd6ae36d60da3
  ok: control: unbounded listing succeeds and is complete
  ok: control: unbounded listing returns every active upload
```
