```
  -- backend: page order1 initiated 1788761467 id dc3e6a657ac580f3a4df5f53a1aa570d861b135eabcd560f2e5936367c9cddd5
  -- backend: page order6 initiated 1788761472 id c4dd2ccd99b9625d41552478b4bd692ba625a4db414a4994e09cd6ae36d60da3
  -- backend: page1 uploads1 truncated TRUE next-key 'k' next-id 'dc3e6a657ac580f3a4df5f53a1aa570d861b135eabcd560f2e5936367c9cddd5'
  -- backend: page2 uploads0 truncated FALSE next-key '' next-id ''
  ok: backend: every page succeeded
  -- backend: walk returned1 of6 uploads
  FAILED: backend: paired-cursor walk returns every active upload
  ok: backend: initiation order1 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: backend: initiation order2 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: backend: initiation order3 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: backend: initiation order4 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: backend: initiation order5 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: backend: initiation order6 returned exactly once by the walk
  ok: control: wire ListMultipartUploads is 200
  -- control: unbounded wire listing reported6 uploads, IsTruncated false
  ok: control: unbounded wire listing returns every upload
  ok: wire: page1 is 200
  -- wire: page1 uploads1 IsTruncated TRUE NextKeyMarker 'k' NextUploadIdMarker '942bb21a5ebfcde5ebd2b01a2554177a62e43461baaccb9ce3061d20b450690e'
  ok: wire: page2 is 200
  -- wire: page2 uploads1 IsTruncated TRUE NextKeyMarker 'k' NextUploadIdMarker 'eea53f36e17e5f3ad8155674dc19707d4c5ceaa7311c335b78da5ea3a80d4579'
  ok: wire: page3 is 200
  -- wire: page3 uploads0 IsTruncated FALSE NextKeyMarker '' NextUploadIdMarker ''
  -- wire: walk returned2 of6 uploads
  FAILED: wire: paired-cursor walk returns every active upload
  ok: wire: initiation order1 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: wire: initiation order2 returned exactly once by the walk
  ok: wire: initiation order3 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: wire: initiation order4 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: wire: initiation order5 returned exactly once by the walk
  FAILED: wire: initiation order6 returned exactly once by the walk
BUG REPRODUCED: 11 failed expectation(s)
```

The unbounded listing returns all six uploads. The paged walk returns 1 of 6 at the backend interface and 2 of 6 over the wire, and in both cases the last page reports the listing as not truncated, so a client is told the walk is complete. Every digest in the run is deterministic, because the upload ID is a digest of the bucket, the key and the store counter.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A marker pair the service itself returned must resume the listing without skipping any active upload, which is what `docs/qualification/list-multipart-uploads-preparation.md:43-45` requires and what `NextUploadIdMarker` promises in the pinned botocore S3 model. The page order and the marker filter must therefore use one and the same ordering key.

## Suggested treatment

The fix belongs in `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-multipart_listing.adb`, which the files and SQLite backends share, not in one store. Which of the two S3 rules wins is externally visible ordering policy, so this needs a maintainer decision:

- Make the page order for a shared key lexical upload ID, by dropping `Initiated` from `Before` at `:17-30`. The marker filter at `:84-91` then matches the page order exactly and no cursor can skip. This diverges from the initiation-time response order that `docs/qualification/list-multipart-uploads-preparation.md:39-40` records as AWS's, and the sentence at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2712-2714` has to change with it.
- Keep the initiation-time page order and make the marker filter follow it, by resolving `After_ID` to its `Initiated` value and comparing candidates on the same `(Initiated, Upload_ID)` key `Before` uses. This matches AWS's stated response order, but it makes a marker meaningful only while the upload it names still exists, which raises the same unknown-cursor policy question already settled the other way for ListObjectVersions at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:2687-2690`, where an unknown paired cursor is `Invalid_Request`. That policy answer has to be chosen and documented in the contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2711-2715`.

The regression test belongs with the existing multipart listing case in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:3273-3400`. It must create same-key uploads whose initiation seconds differ, then walk the listing with `Maximum => 1` following the returned cursor and assert every upload is returned exactly once, which is the property the spike asserts. The spike is the test. The assertions at `:3318-3328` and `:3379-3389` do not have to change under either option, because their same-key uploads share an initiation second and the page order there already falls through to the upload-ID tie break.

## Severity

A client that pages ListMultipartUploads with the markers the service returned is silently given an incomplete list and is told the list is complete. Callers who page are exactly the ones that care: multi-writer retry loops, orphan-upload sweepers and lifecycle cleanup, which then leave staged parts charged and uncollected. It needs several uploads on one key whose initiation seconds differ and a `max-uploads` bound reached inside that key, both of which ordinary concurrent writers produce. The same builder serves the memory, files and SQLite backends. Deterministic.

## Summary

The self-copy guard in `Copy_Object` compares the source and destination bucket and key and the four directive fields, and never looks at `Options.Source_Selector`. The operation S3 clients use to restore a previous version, CopyObject with `x-amz-copy-source: /bucket/key?versionId=OLD` and the same destination bucket and key, is therefore answered `Invalid_Request` and HTTP 400 before the source snapshot is even fetched, and no generation is published. The same predicate is duplicated verbatim in the memory, files and SQLite backends, so all three refuse the restore. The pinned MinIO release answers 200 to that request and rejects only the same-key copy that names no source version.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The guard in the memory backend (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:4977-4986`):

```ada
      elsif Source_Bucket = Destination_Bucket
        and then Source_Key = Destination_Key
        and then Options.Metadata_Directive = Copy_Metadata
        and then Options.Tagging_Directive = Copy_Tags
        and then Options.Annotation_Directive = Copy_Annotations
        and then Options.Selected_Checksum = No_Checksum
        and then not Options.Metadata.Website_Redirect_Location.Is_Set
      then
         Result := Invalid_Request;
         return;
```

The `return` happens before `Fetch_For_Copy` at `:4989`, so the selected source generation is never read.

The same predicate, verbatim, in the files backend (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7602-7611`):

```ada
      elsif Source_Bucket = Destination_Bucket
        and then Source_Key = Destination_Key
        and then Options.Metadata_Directive = Copy_Metadata
        and then Options.Tagging_Directive = Copy_Tags
        and then Options.Annotation_Directive = Copy_Annotations
        and then Options.Selected_Checksum = No_Checksum
        and then not Options.Metadata.Website_Redirect_Location.Is_Set
      then
         Result := Invalid_Request;
         return;
      end if;
```

and in the SQLite backend (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2436-2445`):

```ada
      elsif Source_Bucket = Destination_Bucket
        and then Source_Key = Destination_Key
        and then Options.Metadata_Directive = Copy_Metadata
        and then Options.Tagging_Directive = Copy_Tags
        and then Options.Annotation_Directive = Copy_Annotations
        and then Options.Selected_Checksum = No_Checksum
        and then not Options.Metadata.Website_Redirect_Location.Is_Set
      then
         Result := Invalid_Request;
         return;
      end if;
```

The server hands the selector through unaltered, so the versioned request reaches the guard intact (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14090-14101`):

```ada
                        Source_Read_Request := Object_Reads.Parse_Query
                          (Source_Query, Object_Reads.Get_Object);
                        if not Source_Read_Request.Has_Version_ID
                          or else Source_Read_Request.Has_Part_Number
                          or else
                            Source_Read_Request.Has_Response_Overrides
                        then
                           raise Object_Reads.Malformed_Object_Read_Request;
                        end if;
                        Copy_Options_Value.Source_Selector :=
                          To_Version_Selector
                            (True, Source_Read_Request.Version_ID);
```

Nothing in the contract licenses the rejection. The `Copy_Object` GNATdoc documents source selection, directives, checksums, conditions and identities and states no self-copy rule at all (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:1969-1974`):

```ada
   --  Copy one immutable source snapshot to the destination. The snapshot
   --  contains the exact body, Object_Information, metadata, and tags from one
   --  source generation; source predicates are evaluated against that same
   --  generation. Source_Bucket_Not_Found and Source_Not_Found remain
   --  distinct, while destination-bucket absence is Not_Found.
```

The behaviour also contradicts two published documents. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:61` claims the exact-source lane on all three backends:

```
| CopyObject | memory/files/SQLite current, null, and retained exact source generations | complete typed composable, synchronous, and convenience clients; authenticated general-purpose path-style server | atomic source/destination version identities, exact/null selection, one-shot no-replay execution with typed publication certainty, exhaustive 44-input/24-output projection, signed raw-wire corpus, repeated six-server byte oracle, and files/SQLite exact-source process-restart oracle |
```

and `docs/qualification/copy-object.md:39` requires the selection without qualification:

```
| 9 | `CopySource` / `x-amz-copy-source` | Parse strictly, including one optional bounded `versionId`, and select that immutable current, null, or exact source snapshot on memory, files, and SQLite. |
```

A real server was measured. The image pinned by `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` (`quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`, reporting `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb`) was run as a single detached container on `127.0.0.1:19000` and driven with the `aws` CLI:

| request | MinIO answer |
|---|---|
| versioning on, `copy-source b/k?versionId=<v1>` to `b/k` | 200, `CopySourceVersionId=<v1>`, a new `VersionId`, current body becomes `v1` |
| versioning on, `copy-source b/k?versionId=null` to `b/k` | 200, `CopySourceVersionId=null`, current body becomes the null generation's |
| versioning on, `copy-source b/k?versionId=<the current version>` to `b/k` | 200 |
| versioning on, `copy-source b/k` with no version named, to `b/k` | 400 `InvalidRequest`, "This copy request is illegal because it is trying to copy an object to itself without changing the object's metadata, storage class, website redirect location or encryption attributes." |
| unversioned bucket, `copy-source b/k` to `b/k` | 400 `InvalidRequest`, same message |

The reference server's rule is therefore to reject only when no source version is named, which is exactly the discriminator this predicate omits.

No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour; the case is simply uncovered. `tests/src/copy_object_conformance.adb` exercises the same-key rejection with no selector only, and the versioned conformance copies an exact source and a null source to a different bucket and key (`tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:335-339` and `:816-820`):

```ada
         Options.Source_Selector := Exact (V1);
         Store.Copy_Object
           (Bucket, "alpha", Copy_Bucket, "exact", Options, null,
            Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Copied, Source_Identity,
            Destination_Identity, Result);
```

```ada
         Options.Source_Selector := Null_Version_Selector;
         Store.Copy_Object
           (Bucket, "alpha", Copy_Bucket, "null", Options, null,
            Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Copied, Source_Identity,
            Destination_Identity, Result);
```

## What happens now

Spike: the program below enables versioning on a private memory store, PUTs `v1` then `v2`, confirms as a control that a same-key copy with the default current-version selector is still `Invalid_Request`, then performs the same-key copy with an exact selector naming `v1` and asserts success, a source identity naming `v1`, and a current body of `v1`. A second store repeats the case with the null-version selector. It finally drives the same restore over the in-process S3 application with `x-amz-copy-source: /copy-restore/k?versionId=<v1>` to destination `/copy-restore/k`.

```ada
      --  Control: a same-key copy with NO source selector must stay
      --  Invalid_Request (this is what AWS and MinIO reject).
      Options := Backends.Default_Copy_Options;
      Store.Copy_Object
        (Bucket, Key, Bucket, Key, Options, null, Deadline,
         Info, Source_Identity, Destination_Identity, Result);
...
      --  Under test: restore v1 onto the same key.
      Options := Backends.Default_Copy_Options;
      Options.Source_Selector :=
        (Kind => Backends.Exact_Version, ID => V1);
      Store.Copy_Object
        (Bucket, Key, Bucket, Key, Options, null, Deadline,
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- wire: response body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidRequest</Code><Message>Invalid request</Message><Resource>/copy-restore/k</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: wire: versioned same-key CopyObject is 200
```

The exact-version restore, the null-version restore and the same request over HTTP are all refused, and the current body stays at `v2` in every case. The control shows the unselected same-key copy is still rejected, which is what AWS and MinIO also do.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image.

## What should happen

A CopyObject that names a source version and targets the same bucket and key must select that generation and publish it as a new current generation, which is what `docs/qualification/copy-object.md:39` requires, what `docs/compatibility/s3.md:61` claims, and what the pinned MinIO release does. Only a same-key copy that names no source version and changes nothing should be `Invalid_Request`.

## Suggested treatment

Add the source-selector discriminator to the guard, so it fires only when the request names no source generation. The narrow reading, which is the one measured on MinIO, is to add `and then Options.Source_Selector.Kind = Current_Version` to the predicate at each of the three sites. This is externally visible policy that the `Copy_Object` contract does not currently state, so it needs a maintainer decision:

- Reject only when the selector is `Current_Version`. This matches the pinned MinIO release exactly, and it also permits an explicit copy of the current version onto itself.
- Additionally reject an exact selector that resolves to the current generation. This is stricter than the measured MinIO behaviour and requires resolving the selector before the guard can answer, which moves the check after `Fetch_For_Copy`.

Whichever is chosen, the rule has to be written into the `Copy_Object` contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:1969-2010`, which states no self-copy rule today, and the same change has to be applied at all three sites (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:4977-4986`, `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7602-7611`, `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2436-2445`), because the predicate is duplicated verbatim.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb`, the backend-neutral version state machine, next to the existing exact and null copy cases at `:335-345` and `:810-825`. That suite is exercised against the memory and files backends from `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:7964` and `:7998` and against SQLite from `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:5191`, so one case covers all three backends. It should assert the exact-version and null-version same-key restores succeed and republish the selected bytes, and keep the unselected same-key rejection as a control. The spike is the test. No existing assertion has to change.

## Severity

A version restore, the standard way an S3 client rolls an object back to a previous generation, fails with HTTP 400 on every Flyology backend. `aws s3api copy-object` with a versioned copy source, the console's restore-previous-version action, and any rollback or lifecycle tooling built on the same idiom all hit it, on a default configuration and with no way to work around it other than reading the old version and PUTting it back. It is not silent: the caller gets an error rather than a wrong result. Deterministic, and identical in the memory, files and SQLite backends.

## Summary

`Complete_Multipart_Upload` publishes its record without an annotation generation identity, so the persisted field is empty and the sidecar identity falls back to the SHA-256 of the object path. In an unversioned bucket every generation of a key lives at the same fixed path, so two completions in a row on one key share one annotation sidecar: annotations acknowledged against the first completed object are served, byte for byte and with their original ETag, as annotations of the second completed object's bytes. Completion also never removes the replaced generation's sidecar, which ordinary `PutObject` does, so a completion that replaces a PUT-created generation leaves a `generation-*` directory that no later request can read or delete. The reference memory backend clears the annotation map on completion, so the two backends disagree on what a newly completed object contains.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

In an unversioned bucket the completion targets the one fixed object path, and it writes the record with no annotation generation identity (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:11810-11811` and `:11852-11855`):

```ada
      if Configuration.Status = Versioning_Unconfigured then
         Target := US.To_Unbounded_String (Object_Path (Item, Bucket, Key));
...
      Validate_New_Temp_Target (Item, US.To_String (Temp));
      SIO.Create (File, SIO.Out_File, US.To_String (Temp));
      Opened := True;
      Write_Header (File, Key, Info, Parts => Completed_Parts);
```

That parameter defaults to the empty string (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:2340-2349`):

```ada
   procedure Write_Header
     (File : in out SIO.File_Type;
      Key  : String;
      Info : Object_Information;
      Tags : Object_Tag_Set := Empty_Object_Tags;
      Parts : Completed_Object_Part_List :=
        Completed_Object_Part_Vectors.Empty_Vector;
      Object_Lock : Persisted_Object_Lock_State := (others => <>);
      Annotation_Generation_ID : String := "";
      Checksum_Value_At : access SIO.Positive_Count := null)
```

and it is written as the record's trailing field (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:2493-2494`):

```ada
      Write_U32 (File, Annotation_Generation_ID'Length);
      Write_String (File, Annotation_Generation_ID);
```

read back into `Info.Version` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:2811-2827`). An empty field makes the sidecar identity a function of the path (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:2892-2897`):

```ada
      if US.Length (Info.Version) = Generated_Version_ID_Length then
         return US.To_String (Info.Version);
      elsif US.Length (Info.Version) = 0 then
         --  FOSOBJ01 through FOSOBJ06 predate annotation generations.  Their
         --  path-derived identity preserves the established sidecar layout.
         return GNAT.SHA256.Digest (US.To_String (Selected.Path));
```

Every annotation read and write resolves its directory through that value (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:892-899`):

```ada
   function Annotation_Generation_Directory
     (Item     : Store;
      Bucket   : String;
      Key      : String;
      Selected : Selected_Generation) return String
   is
     (Annotation_Generation_Directory_For_ID
        (Item, Bucket, Key, Stored_Annotation_Generation_ID (Selected)));
```

Ordinary `PutObject` does neither of these things. It mints a fresh identity per publication (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7093-7099`):

```ada
      Item.Temp_Sequence.Next (Number);
      Annotation_Generation_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String
        (GNAT.SHA256.Digest
           ("flyology-files-annotation-generation" & Character'Val (0) &
            Bucket & Character'Val (0) & Key & Character'Val (0) &
            Long_Long_Integer'Image (Number) & Character'Val (0) &
            Ada.Calendar.Time'Image (Ada.Calendar.Clock)));
```

writes it into the header (`:7128-7132`), captures the replaced generation's identity before the rename (`:7247-7249`), and removes that sidecar after it (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7388-7392`):

```ada
      Published := True;
      if US.Length (Previous_Annotation_ID) > 0 then
         Remove_Annotation_Generation_For_ID
           (Item, Bucket, Key, US.To_String (Previous_Annotation_ID));
      end if;
```

`Complete_Multipart_Upload` (`:11554-11954`) contains no call to either removal helper.

The reference backend publishes the completed object with an empty annotation set (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:3343-3349`):

```ada
            --  Finish every allocating metadata operation before consuming
            --  the staged upload or publishing its assembled payload.
            Next_Version := New_Order;
            Objects (Object_At).Annotations.Clear;
            Objects (Object_At).Bucket := Stored_Bucket;
            Objects (Object_At).Key := Stored_Key;
            Objects (Object_At).Info := Completed_Info;
```

The backends are required to agree. `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:26` states:

```
Memory, pure-files, and SQLite implement the same interface. Pure-files keeps
```

and the annotation contract binds each annotation to one generation (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2288-2291`):

```ada
   --  Atomically replace one named annotation on a selected generation. The
   --  payload is consumed before publication; checksum and parent-object
   --  predicates are evaluated over the same selected generation. The
   --  returned ETag is an unquoted lowercase MD5 digest.
```

Versioned buckets are not affected: an enabled or suspended completion publishes under a generation file name that carries the publication number and, for an enabled bucket, sets `Info.Version` to the minted wire identifier (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:11812-11843`), so the identity differs per publication. The defect is confined to `Versioning_Unconfigured`.

The orphan cannot be reclaimed through the API while the bucket exists. `Delete_Bucket` tests emptiness for `objects/`, `multipart/` and `versions/` only and then removes the whole bucket tree (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3846-3855`):

```ada
      elsif Has_Objects (Join (Path, "objects"))
        or else Has_Objects (Join (Path, "multipart"))
        or else
          (Ada.Directories.Exists (Join (Path, "versions"))
           and then Has_Objects (Join (Path, "versions")))
      then
         Result := Bucket_Not_Empty;
      else
         Validate_Tree_Symlink_Free (Path);
         Ada.Directories.Delete_Tree (Path);
```

so the only way to reclaim an orphan `generation-*` directory is to empty and delete the bucket.

The annotation operations are reachable over HTTP against a files store: the S3 application dispatches all four through the `*_If_Supported` wrappers (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15622`, `:15653`, `:15683`, `:15736`) and `Files.Store` is declared with `Object_Annotation_Backend` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.ads:14-16`).

No test covers the combination. The shared annotation exercise runs on both backends (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19734` and `:19743` call `Exercise_Object_Annotations`, declared at `:19555`) and contains no multipart step. `tests/src/multipart_part_conformance.adb` contains no annotation step. No existing assertion is wrong, so none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below runs two scenarios against the memory backend as a control and then against a files store on a fresh root, both on an unversioned bucket. Scenario A is `PutObject`, `PutObjectAnnotation`, then a one-part `CompleteMultipartUpload` on the same key. Scenario B is `CompleteMultipartUpload`, `PutObjectAnnotation`, then `CompleteMultipartUpload` again. After each it asserts an empty annotation page and `Annotation_Absent`, and it finally counts the `generation-*` directories left on disk under each key's annotation subtree.

```ada
      Annotate (Store, "put-then-complete", "alpha", Label);
      Complete_One_Part_Upload
        (Store, "put-then-complete", "mp-body-1", Label);
      Check (Annotation_Count (Store, "put-then-complete", Label) = 0,
             Label & " A: the completed object starts with no annotations");
...
      Complete_One_Part_Upload (Store, "complete-twice", "mp-body-2", Label);
      Annotate (Store, "complete-twice", "beta", Label);
      Complete_One_Part_Upload (Store, "complete-twice", "mp-body-3", Label);
      Check (Annotation_Count (Store, "complete-twice", Label) = 0,
             Label & " B: the second completed object starts with no "
             & "annotations");
```

Output, exit status 1, with repeated `ok:` lines from the memory control and from the harness steps trimmed:

```
  ok: memory (control) A: the completed object starts with no annotations
  ok: memory (control) A: GetObjectAnnotation reports the annotation absent
  -- memory (control) B: annotations after the second COMPLETE = 0
  ok: memory (control) B: the second completed object starts with no annotations
  ok: memory (control) B: GetObjectAnnotation reports the annotation absent
  ok: files  (subject) CreateBucket succeeds
  ok: files  (subject) PutObject succeeds
  ok: files  (subject) PutObjectAnnotation put-then-complete/alpha
  ok: files  (subject) the annotation is visible before replacement
  ok: files  (subject) CompleteMultipartUpload put-then-complete
  -- files  (subject) A: annotations after COMPLETE replaced a PUT = 0
  ok: files  (subject) A: the completed object starts with no annotations
  ok: files  (subject) A: GetObjectAnnotation reports the annotation absent
  ok: files  (subject) CompleteMultipartUpload complete-twice
  ok: files  (subject) PutObjectAnnotation complete-twice/beta
  ok: files  (subject) the annotation is visible before the second completion
  ok: files  (subject) CompleteMultipartUpload complete-twice
  -- files  (subject) B: annotations after the second COMPLETE = 1
  FAILED: files  (subject) B: the second completed object starts with no annotations
  FAILED: files  (subject) B: GetObjectAnnotation reports the annotation absent
  -- files A: sidecar generation- directories left under /private/tmp/.../buckets/anno-bucket/annotations/7075742D7468656E2D636F6D706C657465.annotations = 1
  FAILED: files  (subject) A: the PUT generation's sidecar directory is removed by the completion that replaced it (no orphan)
  -- files B: sidecar generation- directories left under /private/tmp/.../buckets/anno-bucket/annotations/636F6D706C6574652D7477696365.annotations = 1
  FAILED: files  (subject) B: the replaced completion's sidecar directory is removed by the second completion
BUG REPRODUCED: 4 failed expectation(s)
```

Read the two scenarios separately. In A the annotation correctly disappears from the API after the completion, because the PUT had minted its own identity and the completed record resolves a different one; what A leaves behind is the orphan directory, one per key, which the count line reports and no request can reach. In B, where the replaced generation was itself produced by a completion, both records resolve the same path-derived identity and the annotation written against the first completed object is returned for the second: the listing has one entry and `GetObjectAnnotation` reports it present. The memory backend answers 0 in both scenarios.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A newly published object generation starts with no annotations, and publishing it releases the replaced generation's annotation storage. That is what the reference backend does (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:3346`), what the files PUT path does (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7093-7099` and `:7388-7392`), and what `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:26` requires of the three backends together.

## Suggested treatment

Give `Complete_Multipart_Upload` the same two steps that `Put_Object_Internal` already has: mint an annotation generation identity before `Write_Header` and pass it at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:11855`, and capture the replaced generation's identity under the gate before the rename so that `Remove_Annotation_Generation_For_ID` (`:1048`) can run after it, as at `:7388-7392`. This is confined to the unversioned branch, since the versioned branch already sets `Info.Version` at `:11842`. A crash between the rename and the removal then leaves an orphan directory instead of a shared identity, which is the same residue that the reordering asked for in https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/31 produces, so one orphan sweep serves both.

The second half of the fix is a maintainer decision, because it changes the meaning of records already on disk:

- Keep the path-derived fallback at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:2894-2897` for every record with an empty field. Records written by current code stop taking it, but any FOSOBJ01 through FOSOBJ06 record and any record written by an earlier build of the current format keeps the shared identity, so an unversioned key that already has two completed generations stays affected until it is republished.
- Retire the fallback for the current record version and treat an empty field as no annotation storage. This makes the behaviour uniform immediately and changes the on-disk contract for the older record versions the comment at `:2895-2896` names.

The regression test belongs with the shared backend conformance helpers in `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-testing.adb`, as a procedure over `Backends.Backend'Class` called from both the memory and the files fixture in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb`, so that both backends are gated by one assertion. The nearest existing coverage is `Exercise_Object_Annotations` (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19555`), which is already run against both backends at `:19734` and `:19743` and has no multipart step. The spike is the test: the second scenario asserts the inheritance and the count of leftover `generation-*` directories asserts the orphan.

## Severity

A client that uses object annotations together with multipart uploads on an unversioned bucket reads one object's annotations as another object's, with the original ETag and payload, after an ordinary sequence of two completions on the same key. Nothing in the response marks the annotations as stale, and the same sequence on the memory backend returns none, so an application that trusts either backend gets different answers from the two. It needs no concurrency, no unusual configuration and no error path. The orphan half wastes space that no request can reclaim until the bucket is deleted, which is a smaller effect than the inheritance. Deterministic. Files backend only.

## Summary

The files backend expands every key byte to two hexadecimal characters and starts a new path component every 100 characters, so a key of N bytes costs about 2.02N path bytes below `buckets/<name>/objects/`, while the crate's storage boundary admits keys of up to 1,024 bytes and the memory backend stores them. On macOS, where `PATH_MAX` is 1024, `mkdir` on an intermediate component fails with `ENAMETOOLONG`, `Ada.Directories.Create_Directory` raises `Use_Error`, and the blanket handler in `Put_Object_Internal` reports `Backend_Unavailable`, which the S3 layer renders as 503 SlowDown. Stock clients retry 503 with backoff, so a `PutObject` for such a key never completes and never reports a permanent error, and `HeadObject`, `GetObject` and `DeleteObject` for the same key fail identically. The ceiling is not documented anywhere and moves with the length of the configured root: on a 49-byte root it is exactly 464 bytes.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The storage boundary publishes 1,024 bytes as the key limit (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:667-670`):

```ada
   --  Validate an object key at the storage boundary.
   --  @param Value Candidate object key
   --  @return True for a nonempty key of at most 1,024 bytes without NUL
   function Valid_Object_Key (Value : String) return Boolean;
```

and the implementation enforces exactly that (`src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-483`):

```ada
   function Valid_Object_Key (Value : String) return Boolean is
   begin
      if Value'Length not in 1 .. 1_024 then
         return False;
      end if;
```

The pinned botocore S3 model expresses no maximum of its own for the shape, so the crate's own bound is the contract here. `ObjectKey` in the model at botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768` (pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) is:

```json
{
  "type": "string",
  "min": 1
}
```

The files backend turns that key into a path that is roughly twice as long (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:835-860`):

```ada
   function Encoded_Object_Path
     (Root : String; Key : String; Suffix : String) return String
   is
      Result  : US.Unbounded_String := US.To_Unbounded_String (Root);
      Segment : String (1 .. 100);
      Used    : Natural := 0;
   begin
      for Character_Value of Key loop
         Segment (Used + 1) :=
           Hex (Character'Pos (Character_Value) / 16);
         Segment (Used + 2) :=
           Hex (Character'Pos (Character_Value) mod 16);
         Used := Used + 2;
         if Used = Segment'Length then
            US.Set_Unbounded_String
              (Result, Join (US.To_String (Result), Segment));
            Used := 0;
         end if;
      end loop;
      if Used = 0 then
         return US.To_String (Result) & Suffix;
      else
         return Join
           (US.To_String (Result), Segment (1 .. Used) & Suffix);
      end if;
   end Encoded_Object_Path;
```

The arithmetic matches the measured threshold exactly. With a 49-byte root, `/buckets/long-key-bucket/objects` adds 32 bytes. A 464-byte key is 928 hexadecimal characters: nine full components at 101 bytes each including their separator, then a separator plus a 28-character remainder plus `.fos`. That is 49 + 32 + 909 + 33 = 1023 bytes, one below `PATH_MAX`. Each further key byte adds two characters, so 465 bytes gives 1025 and crosses the limit. A deployment with a longer root has a proportionally lower ceiling.

`Ensure_Directory` composes absolute paths and creates each ancestor (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:227-238`):

```ada
      declare
         Parent : constant String := Ada.Directories.Containing_Directory
           (Path);
      begin
         if Parent = Path or else Parent'Length = 0 then
            raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Name_Error;
         end if;
         Ensure_Directory (Item, Parent);
         Ada.Directories.Create_Directory (Path);
         Sync_Directory (Item, Path);
         Sync_Directory (Item, Parent);
      end;
```

and the resulting `Use_Error` reaches the blanket handler (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7449-7455`):

```ada
         Info := Empty_Info;
         Identity := (others => <>);
         if In_Callback then
            raise;
         else
            Result := Backend_Unavailable;
         end if;
```

which the S3 layer renders as a status that clients are supposed to retry (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`):

```ada
         when Capacity_Exceeded | Backend_Unavailable =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 503, "SlowDown",
               "Please reduce your request rate", Resource);
```

Nothing narrower catches the key first. The only length-related guard on the encoded path bounds recursion, not bytes (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:113`):

```ada
   Maximum_Object_Path_Depth : constant Natural := 32;
```

and a 1,024-byte key needs 21 components, so it never fires. The layout document bounds each component and says nothing about the total (`docs/architecture/files-layout.md:3-6`):

```
The files backend requires exclusive ownership of its configured root by one
process. Buckets are ordinary validated directory names. Object keys are
opaque bytes encoded as uppercase hexadecimal and split into bounded path
components; caller-provided slashes and dot segments are never interpreted.
```

There is no files-specific key-length limit in `docs/architecture/files-layout.md`, `docs/compatibility/s3.md` or `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.ads`.

macOS is a qualified platform, not an incidental one: the root and SQLite test job runs on both operating systems (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:66-67`):

```yaml
      matrix:
        os: [ubuntu-latest, macos-latest]
```

so a root written on Linux, where `PATH_MAX` is 4096, holds objects that the same code cannot read when the root is opened on macOS.

No existing test puts a long key into a files store. The only 1,024-byte key in the suite exercises the request target parser (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:8879-8889`):

```ada
      declare
         Exact_Key : constant String := (1 .. 1_024 => 'x');
         Parsed : constant Requests.Target_Result :=
           Requests.Parse_Target ("/example-bucket/" & Exact_Key);
      begin
         Assert
           (Parsed.Status = Requests.Target_Parsed
            and then Requests.Object_Key
              ("/example-bucket/" & Exact_Key, Parsed)'Length = 1_024,
            "maximum decoded key length");
      end;
```

No assertion states the wrong behaviour, so none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below checks the documented boundary, then puts a 1,024-byte key on the memory backend as a control and on a files store rooted at a 49-byte path, and finally binary-searches the largest key length the files store accepts on this host and prints it.

```ada
   Check (Storage.Valid_Object_Key (String'(1 .. 1_024 => 'k')),
          "a 1,024-byte key satisfies the documented storage boundary");
...
      Result := Put_Key (Control, 1_024);
      Check (Result = Storage.Success,
             "memory (control) accepts a 1,024-byte key");
...
      Result := Put_Key (Subject, 1_024);
      Check (Result = Storage.Success,
             "files  (subject) accepts a 1,024-byte key");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- files root /private/tmp/... (49 bytes)
  ok: a 1,024-byte key satisfies the documented storage boundary
  ok: 1,024 bytes is the documented maximum
  ok: memory (control) CreateBucket
  -- memory (control) PUT 1024-byte key -> SUCCESS
  ok: memory (control) accepts a 1,024-byte key
  ok: files  (subject) CreateBucket
  -- files  (subject) PUT 8-byte key -> SUCCESS
  ok: files  (subject) accepts a short key (harness control)
  -- files  (subject) PUT 1024-byte key -> BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE
  FAILED: files  (subject) accepts a 1,024-byte key
  -- files  (subject) largest accepted key length on this host = 464 bytes; 465 bytes already fails
  -- files  (subject) status at 465 bytes -> BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE
  -- the S3 adapter maps Backend_Unavailable to 503 SlowDown (server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954)
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The 8-byte key on the same store succeeds, so the store and the harness are sound; the memory backend takes the same 1,024-byte key. The measured cut is exactly where the arithmetic above puts it, at 464 accepted and 465 refused.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A key that `Valid_Object_Key` admits and that the memory backend stores must either be storable on the files backend as well, or be refused with a permanent, documented status that names the reason. `Backend_Unavailable` is neither: it is rendered as 503 SlowDown, which tells the client the request may succeed on retry, and it never will.

## Suggested treatment

This needs a maintainer decision, because either route changes an externally visible contract:

- Publish a files-backend key-length limit and enforce it at admission, so an over-long key is refused with `Invalid_Request` or `Capacity_Exceeded` rather than `Backend_Unavailable`. This makes the failure permanent and self-explaining, but it narrows the crate's documented 1,024-byte key bound for one backend, and the usable figure still depends on the configured root unless the root length is also bounded and documented.
- Change the on-disk key encoding so the total path is bounded independently of the key, for example a fixed digest-prefixed shard directory with the exact key kept in the record where it already lives (`Read_Header` compares it at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3085-3086`). This keeps the full 1,024-byte range on every platform, and it changes the layout described in `docs/architecture/files-layout.md`, so existing roots need a migration or a compatibility read path.

A smaller alphabet is not a way out on its own: even a 1.34-times expansion puts a 1,024-byte key past 1023 bytes before the root is counted.

The regression test belongs in a new files probe under `tests/src/`, launched from `tests/scripts/test.sh` beside the existing files probes with a root created by `mktemp -d`, so the root length is fixed by the script rather than by the checkout path. A case added to the files-store section of `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb` would work as well for the chosen contract, but its root is composed from `Ada.Directories.Current_Directory`, so the exact threshold it observes varies from host to host. The spike is the test: it asserts the documented boundary and reports the measured ceiling.

## Severity

On macOS an object key between roughly 465 and 1,024 bytes is silently unusable on the files backend, and the client is told to retry a request that can never succeed, so `aws s3 cp` and boto3 spin through their retry budget and then report a timeout or a server error. Keys of that length are unusual but legitimate, and they arise from generated or path-like naming schemes. The same root moved between Linux and macOS turns readable objects into unreadable ones with no warning, and both are qualified platforms. There is no way for an operator to discover the ceiling short of measuring it, since it depends on the root path length and is documented nowhere. Deterministic. Files backend only.

## Summary

`Read_String` declares `Result : String (1 .. Length)`, a local sized by the persisted document length, while the matching writer streams the same document in 64 KiB chunks and admission accepts up to 16 MiB. Seven singleton bucket-configuration readers on the files backend go through it: policy, CORS, encryption, ownership controls, lifecycle, logging and notification. A document larger than the reading task's primary stack is therefore accepted, acknowledged with 200 and durable on disk, and then cannot be read back. The failure is not an exception: from a task with the GNAT default 2 MiB stack, which is what every Flyology HTTP worker gets, the oversized frame runs past the guard page and the process is killed with SIGKILL, so one `GetBucketPolicy` for an already-accepted configuration takes the server down for every concurrent client.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

`Read_String` streams the file in 64 KiB chunks but assembles the result in one contiguous stack local (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1579-1587`):

```ada
   function Read_String
     (File : in out SIO.File_Type; Length : Natural) return String
   is
      Result : String (1 .. Length);
      Data   : Ada.Streams.Stream_Element_Array
        (1 .. Ada.Streams.Stream_Element_Offset
          (File_String_IO_Chunk_Bytes));
      Target_Position : Positive := Result'First;
      Remaining       : Natural := Length;
```

The matching writer, `Write_String` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1478-1504`, writes the same document 64 KiB at a time, and the comment above it states the intent that the working set does not follow the admitted value (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1474-1476`):

```ada
   File_String_IO_Chunk_Bytes : constant Positive := 64 * 1_024;
   --  Match the files backend's existing streaming chunk convention while
   --  bounding the stack working set independently of the admitted value.
```

`Read_Bucket_Policy` calls it with the persisted length (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1780-1796`):

```ada
   procedure Read_Bucket_Policy
     (File : in out SIO.File_Type; Policy : out US.Unbounded_String)
   is
      File_Magic : constant String :=
        Read_String (File, Bucket_Policy_Magic'Length);
      Length : constant Natural := Read_U32 (File);
   begin
      Policy := US.Null_Unbounded_String;
      if File_Magic /= Bucket_Policy_Magic
        or else Byte_Count (Length) > Maximum_Bucket_Policy_Bytes
        or else SIO.Size (File) /=
          SIO.Count (Bucket_Policy_Magic'Length + 4 + Length)
      then
         raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error;
      end if;
      Policy := US.To_Unbounded_String (Read_String (File, Length));
   end Read_Bucket_Policy;
```

Six more readers have the same shape, each ending in the same statement: `Read_Bucket_CORS` (declared at `:1809`, reads at `:1824`), `Read_Bucket_Encryption` (`:1839`, `:1857`), `Read_Bucket_Ownership_Controls` (`:1872`, `:1890`), `Read_Bucket_Lifecycle` (`:1908`, `:1930`, and again for its metadata field at `:1932`), `Read_Bucket_Logging` (`:1947`, `:1965`) and `Read_Bucket_Notification` (`:1980`, `:1998`).

The chunked reader that keeps the frame bounded already exists and is already used by the replication and website readers (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1610-1624`):

```ada
   procedure Read_Unbounded_String
     (File   : in out SIO.File_Type;
      Length : Natural;
      Value  : out US.Unbounded_String)
   is
      Remaining : Natural := Length;
   begin
      Value := US.Null_Unbounded_String;
      while Remaining > 0 loop
         declare
            Count : constant Natural :=
              Natural'Min (File_String_IO_Chunk_Bytes, Remaining);
         begin
            US.Append (Value, Read_String (File, Count));
            Remaining := Remaining - Count;
```

`Read_Bucket_Replication` ends in `Read_Unbounded_String (File, Length, Document);` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:2104`) and `Read_Bucket_Website` in the identical call (`:2137`).

The document that reaches these readers is bounded at 16 MiB, not at any stack figure (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2792`):

```ada
   Maximum_Bucket_Policy_Bytes : constant Byte_Count := 16 * 1_024 * 1_024;
```

and admission accepts anything up to it (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.adb:199-200`):

```ada
   function Valid_Bucket_Policy (Policy : String) return Boolean is
     (Byte_Count (Policy'Length) <= Maximum_Bucket_Policy_Bytes);
```

The S3 route sizes the request body from the same budget, so nothing narrower stands between a client and the backend (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:186-187`):

```ada
   Maximum_Bucket_Policy_Body : constant Byte_Count :=
     Byte_Count (XML.Default_Limits.Maximum_Document_Bytes);
```

`Maximum_Document_Bytes` defaults to `16 * 1_024 * 1_024` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.ads:14`).

The reading task's stack is the real bound, and it is 2 MiB for every server worker. A GNAT task that declares no `Storage_Size` gets the default (toolchain `adainclude/s-parame.adb:55-60`, GNAT 16.1.0):

```ada
   function Default_Stack_Size return Size_Type is
      Default_Stack_Size : constant Integer;
      pragma Import (C, Default_Stack_Size, "__gl_default_stack_size");
   begin
      if Default_Stack_Size = -1 then
         return 2 * 1024 * 1024;
```

and the handler task in the pinned flyology 0.1.1-dev release declares none (`src/flyology-io-structured_servers.adb:386-389`):

```ada
            task type Worker with CPU => Handler_CPU is
               pragma Task_Info (Handler_Model);
               entry Start;
            end Worker;
```

`Get_Bucket_Policy` has a `when others` handler that would turn a caught `Storage_Error` into a status (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:6268-6277`):

```ada
      when others =>
         if Opened then
            SIO.Close (File);
         end if;
         if Locked then
            Item.Publication.Release;
         end if;
         Policy := US.Null_Unbounded_String;
         Configured := False;
         Result := Backend_Unavailable;
```

In a secondary task with the default stack the process never reaches it.

The existing tests do not reach this. `Exercise_Bucket_Singleton_Configuration_Bounds` is the case that uses 16 MiB documents, and it round-trips only replication, which goes through the chunked reader (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:1543-1550`):

```ada
      Store.Put_Bucket_Replication
        (Bucket, At_Limit.all, null, Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Result);
      Assert
        (Result = Success,
         "replication rejected its exact configuration byte limit");
      Store.Get_Bucket_Replication
        (Bucket, null, Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last,
         Document, Configured, Result);
```

Its website and notification steps only assert that an over-limit document is refused (`:1562-1571`). The policy and CORS round-trip cases use short literals (`:583-600` and `:702-711`). The case runs against a files store (`:5843-5844`), so the gap is the document size, not the backend.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below stores a 1 MiB and a 3 MiB bucket policy on a files store, reads both back from the environment task as a control, and then re-executes itself as a child process that performs each read from a task with the GNAT default `Storage_Size`. The child runs under `/bin/sh` so its termination status is visible. It asserts that a policy the backend acknowledged can be read back from a default-stack task.

```ada
      declare
         task Worker;

         task body Worker is
            Policy     : US.Unbounded_String;
            Configured : Boolean;
         begin
            Subject.Get_Bucket_Policy
              (Bucket, null, Forever, Policy, Configured, Worker_Result);
            Worker_Length := US.Length (Policy);
...
      Check (Small = 0,
             "files  (subject) a default-stack task reads a 1 MiB policy "
             & "back (child-process control)");
...
      Check (Large = 0,
             "files  (subject) a default-stack task reads the acknowledged "
             & "3 MiB policy back");
```

Output, exit status 1 (the absolute path in the `/bin/sh` diagnostic is shortened for width; every other line is verbatim):

```
  -- documents: 1048576 bytes and 3145728 bytes; documented admission bound is 16 MiB (backends.ads:2792); GNAT default task stack is 2 MiB
  ok: memory (control) CreateBucket
  ok: memory (control) PutBucketPolicy accepts 3 MiB
  -- memory (control) GetBucketPolicy -> SUCCESS length 3145728
  ok: memory (control) round-trips the 3 MiB policy
  ok: files  (subject) CreateBucket small-policy-bucket
  -- files  (subject) PutBucketPolicy(small-policy-bucket, 1048576 bytes) -> SUCCESS
  ok: files  (subject) PutBucketPolicy acknowledges small-policy-bucket
  -- files  (subject) GetBucketPolicy(small-policy-bucket) from the environment task -> SUCCESS length 1048576
  ok: files  (subject) the environment task reads small-policy-bucket back (the bytes are on disk and intact)
  ok: files  (subject) CreateBucket large-policy-bucket
  -- files  (subject) PutBucketPolicy(large-policy-bucket, 3145728 bytes) -> SUCCESS
  ok: files  (subject) PutBucketPolicy acknowledges large-policy-bucket
  -- files  (subject) GetBucketPolicy(large-policy-bucket) from the environment task -> SUCCESS length 3145728
  ok: files  (subject) the environment task reads large-policy-bucket back (the bytes are on disk and intact)
  -- [child] small-policy-bucket GetBucketPolicy from a default-Storage_Size task -> SUCCESS length 1048576
  -- [sh] child for small-policy-bucket terminated with status 0 (128+n means signal n)
/bin/sh: line 1: 89763 Killed: 9               '.../the spike' worker '/private/tmp/...' large-policy-bucket 3145728
  -- [sh] child for large-policy-bucket terminated with status 137 (128+n means signal n)
  -- files  (subject) child status for the 1 MiB policy 0
  ok: files  (subject) a default-stack task reads a 1 MiB policy back (child-process control)
  -- files  (subject) child status for the 3 MiB policy 137
  FAILED: files  (subject) a default-stack task reads the acknowledged 3 MiB policy back
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

Status 137 is 128 plus signal 9: the child was killed, not terminated by an Ada exception, and printed no diagnostic of its own. The 1 MiB child on the same store and the same code path exits 0, so the failure follows the document size against the task stack and not the store or the request. The environment-task reads of both documents succeed, so the bytes on disk are intact and this is a read-path defect rather than a corrupted record. The memory backend round-trips the same 3 MiB policy.

The status a caller sees depends on which task issues the read. From the environment task with 8 MiB of headroom (`ulimit -s` reports 8176 KiB here) the same read succeeds; with a 3 MiB local already on that stack it returns `Backend_Unavailable` through the handler at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:6268-6277`. Both are the same defect.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A bucket policy, CORS, encryption, ownership-controls, lifecycle, logging or notification document that `Valid_Bucket_Policy` and the S3 route admitted, that was written and acknowledged, must be readable afterwards, and no read of a persisted document may consume stack in proportion to the document. `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1474-1476` already states that intent for the file string helpers, and the replication and website readers already meet it.

## Suggested treatment

Route the seven readers through the existing `Read_Unbounded_String` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1610-1631`) as `Read_Bucket_Replication` (`:2104`) and `Read_Bucket_Website` (`:2137`) already do: replace `Policy := US.To_Unbounded_String (Read_String (File, Length));` at `:1795` with `Read_Unbounded_String (File, Length, Policy);`, and the same at `:1824`, `:1857`, `:1890`, `:1930`, `:1932`, `:1965` and `:1998`. No constant, parameter or status changes value, and the persisted format is untouched. `Read_String` should keep only its short fixed-size uses, such as the magic value at `:1784`.

Whether these documents should also carry a bound smaller than `Maximum_Bucket_Policy_Bytes` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2792` is a separate, externally visible policy question and is not needed to close this defect.

The regression test belongs in a new files probe under `tests/src/`, launched from `tests/scripts/test.sh` beside the existing files probes, because a failing read terminates the process and would take a whole in-process suite run with it: the probe should store a policy larger than a default task stack, read it back from a task that declares no `Storage_Size`, and require exit status 0. The spike is the test. A second, in-process case for a policy that fits, added to `Exercise_Bucket_Singleton_Configuration_Bounds` at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:1524-1579`, would cover the remaining six readers cheaply; no existing assertion there is wrong, so none has to change.

## Severity

An operator who stores a bucket policy or CORS, encryption, ownership-controls, lifecycle, logging or notification document of a few MiB gets a 200 and a durable file, and the next read of that document from a server worker kills the server process. Every concurrent request on that process dies with it, and there is no Ada exception, no log line and no status to attribute the failure to the request that caused it, so the operator sees an unexplained restart loop that repeats on every retry of the same `GetBucketPolicy`. The documents are admitted up to 16 MiB and the worker stack is 2 MiB, so the whole range between them is reachable through ordinary requests with no unusual configuration. It is fully deterministic. Files backend only.

## Summary

`Delete_One` removes the object's annotation sidecar, including the directory fsync that makes the removal durable, before it issues the unlink that is the delete's publication point. When the unlink fails, the batch is reported as `Backend_Unavailable`, the object is still present and still readable, and the annotations that the client stored and the server acknowledged are gone. No request asked for their deletion, annotations are independent payloads with their own size, ETag and checksum, so nothing can re-derive them, and the response is indistinguishable from an ordinary transient failure.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

`Delete_One` removes the sidecar first in both branches that unlink a generation. The unversioned current delete (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8428-8437`):

```ada
            case Configuration.Status is
               when Versioning_Unconfigured =>
                  if Condition_Exists then
                     Outcome.Publication.Kind := Object_Version_Removed;
                     Remove_Annotation_Generation
                       (Item, Bucket, Key, Selected);
                     Remove_Path (US.To_String (Selected.Path));
                  else
                     Outcome.Publication.Kind := No_Version_Removed;
                  end if;
```

and the exact-version delete (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8419-8425`):

```ada
               else
                  if not Selected.Is_Delete_Marker then
                     Remove_Annotation_Generation
                       (Item, Bucket, Key, Selected);
                  end if;
                  Remove_Path (US.To_String (Selected.Path));
               end if;
```

The first step is durable before the second is attempted. `Remove_Annotation_Generation` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1034-1046`):

```ada
   procedure Remove_Annotation_Generation
     (Item     : Store;
      Bucket   : String;
      Key      : String;
      Selected : Selected_Generation)
   is
      Directory : constant String :=
        Annotation_Generation_Directory (Item, Bucket, Key, Selected);
   begin
      if Annotation_Directory_Exists (Item, Bucket, Key, Selected) then
         Remove_Tree (Item, Directory);
      end if;
   end Remove_Annotation_Generation;
```

and `Remove_Tree` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:338-347`):

```ada
   procedure Remove_Tree (Item : Store; Path : String) is
      Parent : constant String := Ada.Directories.Containing_Directory (Path);
   begin
      if GNAT.OS_Lib.Is_Symbolic_Link (Path) then
         raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Name_Error;
      end if;
      Validate_Tree_Symlink_Free (Path);
      Ada.Directories.Delete_Tree (Path);
      Sync_Directory (Item, Parent);
   end Remove_Tree;
```

The second step has several ways to fail (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8320-8332`):

```ada
         procedure Remove_Path (Path : String) is
         begin
            if GNAT.OS_Lib.Is_Symbolic_Link (Path)
              or else not Ada.Directories.Exists (Path)
              or else Ada.Directories.Kind (Path) /=
                Ada.Directories.Ordinary_File
            then
               raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error;
            end if;
            Ada.Directories.Delete_File (Path);
            Sync_Directory
              (Item, Ada.Directories.Containing_Directory (Path));
         end Remove_Path;
```

`Delete_File` fails on EACCES against a directory that lost write permission, on EPERM after a read-only remount, and on EIO; `Data_Error` is raised when a concurrent external change made the path something other than an ordinary file. Any of these reaches the batch handler, which discards the outcomes and reports failure (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8537-8542`):

```ada
      when others =>
         if Locked then
            Item.Publication.Release;
         end if;
         Outcomes.Clear;
         Result := Backend_Unavailable;
```

The S3 boundary renders that as a retryable 503 (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`):

```ada
         when Capacity_Exceeded | Backend_Unavailable =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 503, "SlowDown",
               "Please reduce your request rate", Resource);
```

Nothing can reconstruct what was removed. An annotation is a payload in its own right (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:571-577`):

```ada
   type Object_Annotation_Information is record
      Size       : Byte_Count;
      Modified   : Unix_Time;
      Entity_Tag : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Checksum   : Checksum_Information;
      Version    : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
   end record;
```

A later successful `DeleteObject` removes only the object, and no path recreates a removed sidecar.

The delete qualification record covers the opposite direction only (`docs/qualification/delete-object.md:46-51`):

```
Pure-files unlink is the publication point. A later directory-sync failure can
return `Backend_Unavailable` after the name is already gone, so a single caller
must reconcile the exact key/generation. A batch may expose a deleted prefix
after a later failure or cancellation. The deterministic barrier and
cancellation lanes gate both cases; no status or exception after publication is
documented as proof that deletion did not occur.
```

It documents that a failure status is not proof the object deletion did not occur. It says nothing about destroying a different acknowledged payload in the case where the deletion demonstrably did not occur.

The same ordering leaves the same window across a crash, because the sidecar removal is fsynced through `Sync_Directory` at `:346` before the unlink at `:8424` and `:8434` is issued: a power loss between the two steps leaves the object present without its annotations on any filesystem. That variant is established by reading the code above only. The crash corpus does not gate it: `tests/src/files_crash_probe.adb` contains no annotation step at all (its `delete` scenario at `:399-408` seeds an object and deletes it with a condition), and the matrix in `tests/scripts/test.sh` runs `run_crash_cases delete 1` (`:136`) and `run_crash_cases delete-objects 2` (`:137`) over that annotation-free scenario. There is also a narrower window on the PUT path, where the previous sidecar is removed at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7389-7392` before the destination directory barrier at `:7393-7394`; whether a crash there can lose the sidecar while keeping the old record depends on filesystem-specific ordering and could not be established on APFS, so no claim is made about it.

No fault injection targets `Delete_File` anywhere in the suite, so the reported-failure case below is not covered either.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below seeds an object plus one annotation twice on a files store. The control key is deleted with no injection, so the sequence and the harness are shown sound. For the subject key it makes `buckets/<bucket>/objects/` mode `0500`, which leaves the sidecar removal (under `annotations/`) able to succeed while the unlink (needing write permission on `objects/`) fails, calls `DeleteObject`, restores the mode, and then asserts that the surviving object still has the annotation.

```ada
   Chmod ("0500", Objects_Directory);
   Subject.Delete_Object (Bucket, "doomed-key", null, Forever, Result);
   Note ("injected: DeleteObject -> " & Result'Image);
   Chmod ("0700", Objects_Directory);

   Check (Result /= Storage.Success,
          "the injected delete is reported as failed");
   Check (Object_Present ("doomed-key"),
          "the object survives the failed delete");
   Check (Annotation_Count ("doomed-key") = 1,
          "the surviving object keeps the annotation that no request ever "
          & "asked to delete");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: CreateBucket
  ok: PutObject control-key
  ok: PutObjectAnnotation control-key/alpha
  ok: control: the annotation is listed before the delete
  -- control: DeleteObject -> SUCCESS
  ok: control: the uninjected delete succeeds
  ok: control: the object is gone after a successful delete
  ok: PutObject doomed-key
  ok: PutObjectAnnotation doomed-key/alpha
  ok: the annotation is listed before the injected delete
  ok: chmod 0500 /private/tmp/.../buckets/delete-bucket/objects succeeds
  -- injected: DeleteObject -> BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE
  ok: chmod 0700 /private/tmp/.../buckets/delete-bucket/objects succeeds
  ok: the injected delete is reported as failed
  ok: the object survives the failed delete
  -- annotations listed for the surviving object = 0
  FAILED: the surviving object keeps the annotation that no request ever asked to delete
  -- GetObjectAnnotation(doomed-key/alpha) -> SUCCESS presence ANNOTATION_ABSENT
  FAILED: the annotation payload is still readable after the failed delete
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The delete reported `Backend_Unavailable`, the object is still readable, and the annotation is gone: the listing returns an empty page and `GetObjectAnnotation` reports `Annotation_Absent` for a name that was acknowledged and never deleted. The control key deleted cleanly in the same run, so the injection is what makes the difference.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A delete that does not remove its target must leave the target's annotations intact. The publication point is the unlink (`docs/qualification/delete-object.md:46`), so nothing that a failure can still cancel may be destroyed before it, and a status of `Backend_Unavailable` with the object still in place must mean the store is unchanged.

## Suggested treatment

Reorder the two steps in both branches, so the unlink at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8424` and `:8434` runs first and `Remove_Annotation_Generation` follows it. The sidecar identity is read from the record, so it has to be captured before the unlink, exactly as the PUT path already captures `Previous_Annotation_ID` at `:7247-7249` and applies it after the rename at `:7389-7392`; `Remove_Annotation_Generation_For_ID` (`:1048`) is the form that takes the captured identity. After the reorder the failure mode inverts from silent loss to a recoverable orphan directory, which is the same residue that https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/28 leaves behind on multipart completion.

Where the orphan sweep that collects those directories belongs is a maintainer decision:

- In `Open`, beside the existing `tmp/` sweep and `Recover_Null_Tombstones` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3553-3566`, so a crash is repaired at the next start. This adds work proportional to the annotation tree to every open.
- In `Delete_Bucket`, which today removes the whole bucket tree at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3854-3855` and checks emptiness only for `objects/`, `multipart/` and `versions/` (`:3846-3850`). This reclaims the space eventually but leaves the orphan in place for the life of the bucket.
- In both.

No public constant, variant, parameter or status changes under any of these.

The crash variant belongs in the deterministic crash corpus: add a `delete-with-annotation` scenario to `tests/src/files_crash_probe.adb` beside the existing `delete` scenario at `:399-408`, seeding an annotation in `Prepare` and asserting on reopen that the object and its annotation are either both present or both gone, and register it in the matrix in `tests/scripts/test.sh` beside `run_crash_cases delete 1` at `:136`. The reported-failure variant is what the spike covers, and it fits the same probe as an injected-permission case, since it needs a `chmod` between the two steps that an in-process suite case cannot express cleanly. No existing assertion in either file is wrong, so none has to change.

## Severity

A `DeleteObject` that fails and does not delete anything destroys a different payload that the client stored and the server acknowledged, with no request having asked for it, no way to reconstruct it and no diagnostic that separates this from ordinary backpressure: the client sees 503 SlowDown and retries. It needs the unlink to fail, which happens on a permission or mount change, on an I/O error, or when something outside the store changed the path, and the same state follows from a power loss between the two steps on any filesystem. The reported-failure variant reproduces every time under the injection above. Deterministic. Files backend only.

## Summary

The files backend locates an active multipart upload by bucket and upload ID alone, and discovers a key mismatch only when `Read_Header` validates the manifest's stored key, which it signals with `Data_Error`. Every multipart entry point's blanket `when others` handler turns that into `Backend_Unavailable`, which the S3 layer renders as 503 SlowDown. `UploadPart`, `ListParts`, `CompleteMultipartUpload` and `AbortMultipartUpload` sent with the wrong key therefore return a retryable server-overload status where the backend contract and the reference memory backend say `Not_Found`, which the same S3 layer renders as 404 NoSuchUpload. A client that mangles a key on a retry spins through its retry budget instead of being told the upload does not exist, and an `AbortMultipartUpload` with the wrong key can neither retire the upload nor say why.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The contract is explicit on both points. Upload identifiers are scoped to the key (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2657-2659`):

```ada
   --  Start one upload. Upload identifiers are backend-generated, opaque,
   --  nonempty, and scoped to the exact bucket and key. Backends that do not
   --  override multipart primitives inherit a Not_Implemented result.
```

and a missing upload has one status (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2771-2773`):

```ada
   --  Retire an upload and every staged part atomically with Conditions. A
   --  missing upload is Not_Found; a mismatched initiation time is
   --  Precondition_Failed and leaves the upload and all parts unchanged.
```

`Read_Manifest` looks the upload up without the key and only then checks it (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3424-3431`):

```ada
   begin
      Inspect_Manifest_Path (Item, Bucket, Upload_ID, Exists);
      if not Exists then
         raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Name_Error;
      end if;
      Validate_Upload_Contents (Item, Bucket, Upload_ID);
      SIO.Open (File, SIO.In_File, Manifest_Path (Item, Bucket, Upload_ID));
      Read_Header (File, Key, Info, Body_At);
```

`Read_Header` reports the mismatch as a corrupt-data condition (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3085-3087`):

```ada
      if US.To_String (Key) /= Expected then
         raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error;
      end if;
```

The presence test that runs before it does not consider the key. `Put_Multipart_Part` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:10830-10837`):

```ada
      if not Manifest_Exists (Item, Bucket, Upload_ID)
      then
         Result := Not_Found;
         Item.Publication.Release;
         Locked := False;
         return;
      end if;
      Read_Manifest (Item, Bucket, Key, Upload_ID, Upload_Options);
```

The same shape appears in `List_Multipart_Parts` (`:11115-11122`), `Complete_Multipart_Upload` (`:11611-11618`) and `Abort_Multipart_Upload` (`:11980-11987`).

`Data_Error` is not special-cased in any of the four handlers. `Put_Multipart_Part` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:11061-11078`):

```ada
      when others =>
         if Opened then
            SIO.Close (File);
         end if;
         if Locked then
            Item.Publication.Release;
         end if;
         if not Published and then US.Length (Temp) > 0
           and then Ada.Directories.Exists (US.To_String (Temp))
         then
            Ada.Directories.Delete_File (US.To_String (Temp));
         end if;
         Info := Empty_Info;
         if In_Callback then
            raise;
         else
            Result := Backend_Unavailable;
         end if;
```

The upload identifier does not carry the key in a way that would prevent the case. It is a digest, and the upload directory is named by a digest of the identifier alone (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:10690-10694`):

```ada
      Upload_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String
        (GNAT.SHA256.Digest
           (Bucket & Character'Val (0) & Key & Character'Val (0) &
            Long_Long_Integer'Image (Number) &
            Ada.Calendar.Time'Image (Ada.Calendar.Clock)));
```

so a manifest created for key `alpha` is found under a request for key `beta`.

The reference backend matches all three parts of the identity together (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:331-348`):

```ada
      function Upload_Index
        (Bucket : String; Key : String; Upload_ID : String) return Natural
      is
      begin
         for Index in Uploads'Range loop
            if Uploads (Index).Used
              and then Ada.Strings.Unbounded.To_String
                (Uploads (Index).Bucket) = Bucket
              and then Ada.Strings.Unbounded.To_String
                (Uploads (Index).Key) = Key
              and then Ada.Strings.Unbounded.To_String
                (Uploads (Index).ID) = Upload_ID
            then
               return Index;
            end if;
         end loop;
         return 0;
      end Upload_Index;
```

The two statuses reach the client as very different answers. `Not_Found` becomes a definitive 404 (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:12638-12643`):

```ada
                  if Result = Not_Found then
                     Send_Error
                       (X, 404, "NoSuchUpload",
                        "The specified multipart upload does not exist",
                        Target_Text);
                     return;
```

while `Backend_Unavailable` becomes the retryable one (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`):

```ada
         when Capacity_Exceeded | Backend_Unavailable =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 503, "SlowDown",
               "Please reduce your request rate", Resource);
```

Nothing tests either direction. `tests/src/multipart_part_conformance.adb` is 180 lines and contains no occurrence of `Not_Found` and no mismatched-key call. No existing assertion states the wrong behaviour, so none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below runs one sequence against the memory backend as a control and then against a files store. It creates an upload for key `alpha`, uploads a part with the correct key as a sanity control, and then drives `UploadPart`, `ListParts`, `CompleteMultipartUpload` and `AbortMultipartUpload` for key `beta` with that same upload identifier, asserting `Not_Found` each time.

```ada
         Store.Put_Multipart_Part
           (Bucket, Wrong_Key, US.To_String (Upload_ID), 1, Payload,
            null, Forever, Info, Result);
         Check (Result = Storage.Not_Found,
                Label & " UploadPart with a mismatched key is Not_Found");

      Store.Abort_Multipart_Upload
        (Bucket, Wrong_Key, US.To_String (Upload_ID),
         Backends.No_Abort_Multipart_Conditions, null, Forever, Result);

             Label & " Abort with a mismatched key is Not_Found");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  FAILED: files  (subject) Complete with a mismatched key is Not_Found
```

All four entry points differ from the control, and the correct-key part upload in the same run succeeds, so the upload itself is sound.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A multipart operation whose (bucket, key, upload ID) triple does not name an active upload is `Not_Found`, as `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2657-2659` and `:2771-2773` require and as the memory backend answers, so the S3 boundary returns 404 NoSuchUpload rather than 503 SlowDown.

## Suggested treatment

Compare the manifest's stored key against the requested key and report the mismatch as absence rather than as a failure. The narrowest change is in `Read_Manifest`: read the header with `Read_Header_Any` instead of `Read_Header` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3431`, compare the returned key, and signal a mismatch with the same condition that `Inspect_Manifest_Path` uses for a missing manifest at `:3426-3428`, so it reaches the existing `Not_Found` paths. Alternatively, extend `Manifest_Exists` at `:10830`, `:11115`, `:11611` and `:11980` to take the key and answer False on a mismatch, which keeps the four entry points as they are. Either way `Read_Manifest`'s remaining `Data_Error` cases, the size, entity-tag and checksum checks at `:3433-3438`, must keep reporting a corrupt manifest as a failure rather than as absence. No public constant, variant, parameter or status changes.

The regression test belongs with the shared backend conformance helpers in `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-testing.adb`, as a procedure over `Backends.Backend'Class` called from both the memory and the files fixture in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb`, so the behaviour is gated for every backend by one assertion; `tests/src/multipart_part_conformance.adb` is the file that covers multipart today and has no mismatched-key case at all. The spike is the test: it asserts `Not_Found` at each of the four entry points and runs the identical sequence against both backends.

## Severity
