A client that sends a multipart operation with the wrong key gets 503 SlowDown, which aws-cli and boto3 retry with exponential backoff, so a permanent error is turned into a long retry loop that ends in an unrelated-looking timeout, and an `AbortMultipartUpload` with the wrong key never retires the upload and never explains why, leaving staged parts behind. It needs a client mistake to trigger, such as a mangled key on a retry, so it is an edge case rather than something a correct client meets. It affects all four multipart entry points and reproduces every time. Deterministic. Files backend only.

## Summary

The files backend derives an object's opaque version identifier from `max(existing publication) + 1`, scanned from the retained generation files, and an exact delete unlinks the generation file outright, leaving nothing that keeps the counter high. Deleting the newest generation and republishing therefore mints the deleted generation's exact version identifier again. `GET key?versionId=<that identifier>` then serves the new object's bytes with 200 instead of 404, so a client, replicator or audit consumer keyed on (key, version ID) silently reads different content under an identifier it believes it has already seen or already deleted. The memory backend uses a monotonic counter and never repeats, so the two backends disagree on an externally visible identity.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The publication number comes from a scan of what is still on disk (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1254-1270`):

```ada
   function Next_Generation_Publication
     (Item : Store; Bucket : String; Key : String) return Long_Long_Integer
   is
      Highest : Long_Long_Integer := 0;

      procedure Consider (Candidate : Generation_Entry) is
      begin
         Highest := Long_Long_Integer'Max
           (Highest, Candidate.Publication);
      end Consider;
   begin
      Scan_Generations (Item, Bucket, Key, Consider'Access);
      if Highest = Long_Long_Integer'Last then
         raise Storage_Error;
      end if;
      return Highest + 1;
   end Next_Generation_Publication;
```

The version identifier is a pure function of that number (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7290-7296`):

```ada
            Publication := Next_Generation_Publication (Item, Bucket, Key);
            if Kind = Opaque_Object then
               Version_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String
                 (GNAT.SHA256.Digest
                    (Files_Version_ID_Domain & Character'Val (0) &
                     Bucket & Character'Val (0) & Key & Character'Val (0) &
                     Long_Long_Integer'Image (Publication)));
```

The same derivation is repeated for delete markers (`:8244-8250`) and for multipart completion (`:11822-11828`).

An exact delete of a non-marker generation removes the file and leaves no residue (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8419-8425`):

```ada
               else
                  if not Selected.Is_Delete_Marker then
                     Remove_Annotation_Generation
                       (Item, Bucket, Key, Selected);
                  end if;
                  Remove_Path (US.To_String (Selected.Path));
               end if;
```

`Remove_Path` unlinks the generation file (`:8329`). The one branch that does leave a marker behind applies only to null-version deletes, and it removes its own tombstone immediately (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8400-8418`):

```ada
               if Request_Entry.Selector.Kind = Null_Version
                 and then not Selected.Legacy
               then
                  declare
                     Publication : Long_Long_Integer;
                     Ignored_ID  : US.Unbounded_String;
                     Tombstone : US.Unbounded_String;
                  begin
                     Publish_Generation
                       (Key, Null_Tombstone, Publication, Ignored_ID);
                     Tombstone := US.To_Unbounded_String
                       (Join
                          (Generation_Directory (Item, Bucket, Key),
                           Generation_File_Name
                             (Publication, Null_Tombstone)));
                     Remove_Superseded_Null_Generations
                       (Item, Bucket, Key, US.To_String (Tombstone));
                     Remove_Path (US.To_String (Tombstone));
                  end;
```

So after deleting every generation numbered above some N, the next publication is numbered N+1 again and its identifier is the identifier of a generation that existed before.

The identifier is a caller-visible identity that the selector treats as naming one generation (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:49-52`):

```ada
   --  Kind of retained generation selected by a read or metadata operation.
   --  @enum Current_Version Select the newest visible non-delete generation
   --  @enum Null_Version Select the distinguished S3 null generation
   --  @enum Exact_Version Select one generated opaque version identifier
```

and the files backend records that it is meant to be an opaque S3 identity (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:50-52`):

```ada
   Generated_Version_ID_Length : constant Positive := 64;
   --  Flyology files versions use one SHA-256 hexadecimal digest.  The value
   --  is an opaque S3 identity; changing its width would alter persisted paths
```

The reference backend states the intended rule in its own declaration (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.ads:1844-1846`):

```ada
      --  Every retained generation receives one strictly increasing order.
      --  Exhaustion fails closed rather than reusing a version identity.
      Next_Version : Version_Publication_Order := 0;
```

It never rescans surviving generations: it takes `New_Order := Next_Version + 1` and refuses at the type's last value (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:1429-1433`).

The existing versioning coverage only asserts that three consecutive publications differ (`tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:543-549`):

```ada
      Require
        (US.Length (V1.Version) > 0
         and then US.Length (V1.Version) <= Maximum_Version_ID_Length
         and then V1.Version /= V2.Version
         and then V2.Version /= V3.Version
         and then V1.Version /= V3.Version,
         "enabled version IDs are not bounded and unique");
```

No test deletes the newest generation and republishes. No existing assertion states the wrong behaviour, so none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below enables versioning, puts `"AAAA"` under one key and records the returned identifier V1, deletes that exact version, puts `"BBBB"` under the same key and records V2, and then asserts that the identifiers differ and that a read selecting V1 does not return the new bytes. The memory backend runs the identical sequence first as the control.

```ada
      Store.Delete_Selected_Object
        (Bucket, Key,
         (Kind => Backends.Exact_Version, ID => First_ID),
         Backends.No_Delete_Object_Conditions, False, null, Forever,
         Outcome, Result);
...
      Check (US.To_String (Second_ID) /= US.To_String (First_ID),
             Label & " the republished version ID differs from the deleted "
             & "one");
...
         Store.Get_Object
           (Bucket, Key, Storage.Whole_Object, Sink, null, Forever, Info,
            Result,
            Selector =>
              (Kind => Backends.Exact_Version, ID => First_ID));
         Check (US.To_String (Sink.Data) /= "BBBB",
                Label & " GET of the deleted version ID does not serve the "
                & "new bytes");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  ok: memory (control) CreateBucket succeeds
  ok: memory (control) versioning enabled
  ok: memory (control) first PUT succeeds
  -- memory (control) V1 = fda44a6321d0e2d57a2c2c3e611e58b1cb46bb78665b52a0c95fc6eb2d9eff69
  -- memory (control) DELETE versionId=V1 -> SUCCESS
  ok: memory (control) exact delete of V1 succeeds
  ok: memory (control) second PUT succeeds
  -- memory (control) V2 = 64315f7b3a3cb1d53b86836356810b6115bc08ec33fc24d40663d3d25f2702a7
  ok: memory (control) the republished version ID differs from the deleted one
  -- memory (control) GET versionId=V1 -> NOT_FOUND body=""
  ok: memory (control) GET of the deleted version ID does not serve the new bytes
  ok: files  (subject) CreateBucket succeeds
  ok: files  (subject) versioning enabled
  ok: files  (subject) first PUT succeeds
  -- files  (subject) V1 = 0972e6ee85f810eaa3704c8907a13fc770ba5d0db58255e6d1b97eef10ac12c4
  -- files  (subject) DELETE versionId=V1 -> SUCCESS
  ok: files  (subject) exact delete of V1 succeeds
  ok: files  (subject) second PUT succeeds
  -- files  (subject) V2 = 0972e6ee85f810eaa3704c8907a13fc770ba5d0db58255e6d1b97eef10ac12c4
  FAILED: files  (subject) the republished version ID differs from the deleted one
  -- files  (subject) GET versionId=V1 -> SUCCESS body="BBBB"
  FAILED: files  (subject) GET of the deleted version ID does not serve the new bytes
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

On the files store V2 is character for character V1, and the read that selects the deleted identifier succeeds and returns `"BBBB"`, the bytes of the object published after the delete. The memory backend mints a different identifier and answers `Not_Found` for the deleted one.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

An opaque version identifier names one generation for the life of the bucket, so an identifier that was returned for one object's bytes is never returned again for different bytes, and a read selecting a deleted identifier is `Not_Found`. That is what the memory backend guarantees at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.ads:1844-1846`, and the two backends have to agree on a caller-visible identity.

## Suggested treatment

This needs a maintainer decision, because every option changes the on-disk layout or the record format:

- Persist a per-key monotonic publication high-water mark and derive the next publication from it instead of from the scan at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1265`. This keeps the identifier reproducible from the publication number and adds one more persisted object per key that has to be written under the publication gate, synced, and recovered when it is missing from an existing root.
- Leave a tombstone for exact deletes the way the null-version path at `:8400-8418` already does, so the scan still sees the high-water mark. This reuses an existing mechanism and no new file kind is needed, but it turns every exact delete into a publication, and the tombstones have to be retained and skipped by listings for as long as the key exists.
- Mix an entropy source or the publication time into the digest at `:7292-7296`, `:8246-8250` and `:11824-11828`. This is the smallest code change and it ends the current property that a generation's identifier is reproducible from its file name, which the file-name derivation and any tooling that relies on it would have to stop assuming.

The regression test belongs with the shared backend conformance helpers in `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-testing.adb`, as a procedure over `Backends.Backend'Class` called from both the memory and the files fixture in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb`, so that both backends are gated by the same assertion. The nearest existing coverage is the uniqueness assertion in `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:543-549`, which only compares three consecutive publications and would still pass. The spike is the test: it asserts both the distinct identifier and the second-order effect that the deleted identifier does not read back the new bytes.

## Severity

A client, replicator or audit consumer that keys on (object key, version ID) reads different content under an identifier it has already recorded, and a `GET` for a version it deleted returns 200 with someone else's bytes instead of 404. Nothing in the response marks the identifier as reused. It needs an exact delete of the newest generation, or of every generation above some point, followed by a republish of the same key, which is a normal retention or correction operation rather than an error path but is not the common case. The same sequence on the memory backend behaves correctly, so an application validated against one backend misbehaves on the other. Deterministic. Files backend only.

## Summary

`Get_Object` in the SQLite backend calls `Catalogs.Find_Selected_Object` without the `Check` hook, so the catalog operation gate is released before the payload file is opened. A concurrent `Put_Object` on the same key unlinks the superseded payload as soon as it leaves the gate, the reader's `SIO.Open` raises `Name_Error`, and the `when others` handler turns that into `Backend_Unavailable`, which the S3 boundary renders as 503 `SlowDown`. A GET of a key that exists before and after the request therefore fails while another client overwrites it. `Head_Object` and `Copy_Object` already pass the hook for exactly this purpose.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

`Get_Object` looks the payload up, releases the gate inside the lookup, and only then opens the file (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2666-2683`):

```ada
      Catalogs.Find_Selected_Object
        (Item.Catalog, Bucket, Key, Selector, Payload, Info, Result);
      if Result /= Success then
         return;
      elsif not Is_Payload_Name (US.To_String (Payload)) then
         raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error;
      end if;
      Result := Evaluate_Read_Conditions
        (Conditions, US.To_String (Info.Entity_Tag), Info.Modified);
      if Result /= Success then
         return;
      end if;
      SIO.Open
        (File, SIO.In_File,
         Join (Objects_Path (Item), US.To_String (Payload)));
      if SIO.Size (File) /= SIO.Count (Info.Size) then
         raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error;
      end if;
```

With a null `Check`, that overload unlocks before it returns (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:5701-5709`):

```ada
      Item.Gate.Acquire;
      Locked := True;
      Find_Selected_Object_Internal
        (Item, Bucket, Key, Selector, Payload, Info, Result);
      if Result = Success and then Check /= null then
         Check.all (US.To_String (Payload), Info);
      end if;
      Item.Gate.Release;
      Locked := False;
```

The catalog contract states what the hook is for (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.ads:710-712`):

```ada
   --  When present, Check runs after a successful lookup while the catalog
   --  operation gate is still held. It may validate the immutable external
   --  payload before a concurrent publication retires the previous file.
```

and repeats it for this operation (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.ads:739`):

```ada
   --  @param Check Optional payload validation while the gate is held
```

The two sibling readers use it. `Head_Object` (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2581-2583`):

```ada
      Catalogs.Find_Selected_Object
        (Item.Catalog, Bucket, Key, Selector, Payload, Info, Result,
         Check_Payload'Access);
```

`Copy_Object` (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2448-2451`):

```ada
      Catalogs.Find_Selected_Object
        (Item.Catalog, Source_Bucket, Source_Key, Options.Source_Selector,
         Payload, Source_Info, Source_Tags, Source_Annotations,
         Selected_Source, Result, Open_Source'Access);
```

The replaced payload is unlinked as soon as the writer leaves the gate (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2232-2235`):

```ada
      if US.Length (Previous) > 0 then
         begin
            Delete_Payload_If_Present (Item, US.To_String (Previous));
            Sync_Path (Objects_Path (Item), Directory => True);
```

Any exception outside a sink callback becomes `Backend_Unavailable` (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2736-2745`):

```ada
      when others =>
         if SIO.Is_Open (File) then
            SIO.Close (File);
         end if;
         Info := Empty_Info;
         if In_Callback then
            raise;
         else
            Result := Backend_Unavailable;
         end if;
```

and the server renders that as 503 (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`):

```ada
         when Capacity_Exceeded | Backend_Unavailable =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 503, "SlowDown",
               "Please reduce your request rate", Resource);
```

The backend's architecture document states the invariant the code does not keep (`docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23`):

```
current-object, retained-generation, or multipart catalog, then flushes both
directories. Superseded and deleted bodies are
intentionally retained until that recovery pass so an already-open reader is
never invalidated by replacement.
```

The code retires superseded bodies at commit time, not at the next recovery pass, and the reader is not already open when replacement happens.

The two multipart readers have the same window. `Copy_Multipart_Part` resolves the source payload with `Catalogs.Find_Object` and no `Check` argument (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:4023-4025`):

```ada
      Catalogs.Find_Object
        (Item.Catalog, Source_Bucket, Source_Key,
         Payload, Source_Info, Result);
```

and opens the file afterwards at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:4067-4069`. `Complete_Multipart_Upload` reads the part records at `:4175-4176` and then opens each part payload outside the gate at `:4295-4297`. That overload of `Find_Object` is the one whose `Check` hook is documented at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.ads:710-712`.

The existing SQLite races cover writers against writers only. The conditional race declares two writer workers (`sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:1366-1368`):

```ada
               task type Worker (Delete_Worker : Boolean);

               task body Worker is
```

and the second race is a versioning-against-delete race (`sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:1431-1435`):

```ada
      for Round in 1 .. 16 loop
         declare
            Race_Bucket : constant String :=
              "sqlite-delete-version-" & Ada.Strings.Fixed.Trim
                (Positive'Image (Round), Ada.Strings.Both);
```

No test reads a key while another task overwrites it, so no existing test asserts the wrong behaviour and none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program opens a SQLite store on a fresh root, creates a bucket, seeds one key with a single byte, and then runs two 20 second phases of 32 reader tasks against 2 writer tasks. Every writer publishes the same bytes, so the entity tag never changes and every GET is required to return `Success`. Phase A reads with no conditions; phase B reads with a 14,284 byte matching `If-Match` list, which is the only work `Get_Object` performs between the gate release and `SIO.Open`. A single-task control GET runs first and must succeed.

The reader and writer loops:

```ada
   task body Reader is
      Conditions : constant OSB.Read_Conditions := Read_Template;
   begin
      while Ada.Real_Time.Clock < Stop_Time loop
            Store.Get_Object
              (Bucket, Key, OSS.Whole_Object, Sink, null, Never,
               Info, Result, Conditions);
            Tally.Record_Get (Result);
      end loop;

   task body Writer is
   begin
      while Ada.Real_Time.Clock < Stop_Time loop
            Store.Put_Object
              (Bucket, Key, Source, OSS.Default_Put_Options, null, Never,
               Info, Identity, Result);
            Tally.Record_Put (Result);
      end loop;
```

Output, exit status 1, with the scratch root path elided:

```
  ok: Create_Bucket succeeds
  ok: Put_Object seeds the key
  -- entity tag 9dd4e461268c8034f5c8564e155c67a6 (constant: every writer publishes the same bytes)
  -- control Get_Object SUCCESS bytes 1
  ok: control Get_Object succeeds
  ok: control Get_Object round-trips the body
  -- phase A (plain reads) GET total 361969: SUCCESS=355387 BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE=6582
  -- phase A (plain reads) PUT total 2082: SUCCESS=2082
  ok: phase A (plain reads): the writers made progress
  ok: phase A (plain reads): tally is consistent
  FAILED: phase A (plain reads): no concurrent Get_Object reports Backend_Unavailable for a key that always exists
  ok: phase A (plain reads): no concurrent Get_Object reports Not_Found for a key that always exists
  -- phase B If-Match list length 14284 (matching, evaluated after the gate release)
  -- phase B (long matching If-Match) GET total 334399: SUCCESS=326960 BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE=7439
  -- phase B (long matching If-Match) PUT total 1430: SUCCESS=1430
  ok: phase B (long matching If-Match): the writers made progress
  ok: phase B (long matching If-Match): tally is consistent
  FAILED: phase B (long matching If-Match): no concurrent Get_Object reports Backend_Unavailable for a key that always exists
  ok: phase B (long matching If-Match): no concurrent Get_Object reports Not_Found for a key that always exists
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

Phase A failed 6,582 of 361,969 reads, 1.8 %, against 2,082 successful overwrites, about three failed reads per overwrite. Phase B failed 7,439 of 334,399 reads, 2.2 %, against 1,430 overwrites. Widening the unprotected window with the long `If-Match` list did not change the rate materially, so the dominant factor is reader scheduling rather than the amount of work in the window. Zero `Not_Found` was recorded in either phase, so the catalog lookup always succeeded and only the file open failed. All writes returned `Success`, so the key exists for the whole run.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A GET of a key that exists before and after the request returns `Success` and serves either generation. The catalog contract provides the `Check` hook so a reader can bind the payload file before a concurrent publication retires it (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.ads:710-712`), and `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23` states that an already-open reader is never invalidated by replacement.

## Suggested treatment

Give `Get_Object` a `Check` hook that performs the `SIO.Open` while the gate is still held, in the shape `Head_Object` uses at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2581-2583` and `Copy_Object` at `:2448-2451`; an open POSIX descriptor survives unlink, so the remaining work outside the gate is unaffected. Apply the same change to `Copy_Multipart_Part`, whose `Catalogs.Find_Object` call at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:4023-4025` accepts the same hook, and to the part opens in `Complete_Multipart_Upload` at `:4295-4297`. No public interface changes.

This needs a maintainer decision: the code and `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23` disagree about payload retirement, and closing the window does not by itself resolve that.

- Keep the immediate unlink after commit, close the window with the `Check` hook, and rewrite the sentence at `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23` to say that superseded bodies are unlinked at commit and that readers bind the file under the gate. Disk use is unchanged; the document stops promising retention.
- Adopt the documented behaviour and defer unlinking of superseded and deleted bodies to the startup recovery pass. Readers are then safe without a hook, but a busy store holds every superseded body until the next restart, which changes the disk footprint of an overwrite-heavy workload.

The gate is also what a raising `Check` hook interacts with, so any hook added here should be read together with https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/36: on that path a raise inside the hook currently leaks the gate.

The regression test belongs in `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb`, next to the two existing races at `:1365` and `:1431`, because the mechanism is specific to this backend's external payload files and catalog gate. The spike is the test: a bounded reader-against-writer race on one key whose bytes never change, asserting that no GET reports `Backend_Unavailable` or `Not_Found`.

## Severity

Every S3 client that reads a key while another client overwrites it can get 503 `SlowDown` for an object that exists throughout, and multipart copy and completion have the same window on part payloads. Nothing is lost and no request is served with wrong bytes, because the payload names are immutable and the size check at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2681-2683` rejects a mismatched file, and 503 is retriable. It needs concurrency on one key, so it is not deterministic per request, but at 32 readers against 2 writers it produced about three failed reads per overwrite and is statistically certain under ordinary server load. SQLite backend only.

## Summary

The SQLite catalog keeps a source annotation payload alive across a copy with a temporary lease table declared `refs INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(refs>0)`, but it releases a lease with `SET refs=refs-1`. A lease taken at 1 therefore violates the constraint on its very first release, `Step` raises `SQLite_Error`, and because the backend clears its "leases held" flag only after that fallible call returns, the exception handler re-enters the release and raises again, so `SQLite_Error` escapes `Copy_Object` entirely. The destination has already been committed at that point, so an S3 client gets 500 `InternalError` for a CopyObject that actually succeeded. Every CopyObject of an annotated source is affected, in both annotation directives, on the first copy of that source in a process.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The lease table forbids a zero reference count (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:2842-2846`):

```ada
      DB.Execute
        (Item.Database,
         "CREATE TEMP TABLE annotation_copy_leases(" &
         "payload TEXT PRIMARY KEY,refs INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(refs>0)) " &
         "WITHOUT ROWID");
```

Leases are taken at 1 (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:847-856`):

```ada
               DB.Prepare
                 (Lease, Item.Database,
                  "INSERT INTO annotation_copy_leases(payload,refs) " &
                  "VALUES(?1,1) ON CONFLICT(payload) DO UPDATE " &
                  "SET refs=refs+1");
               DB.Bind (Lease, 1, Payload);
               if DB.Step (Lease) /= DB.Done then
                  raise Catalog_Error with
                    "annotation lease returned a row";
               end if;
```

and released by decrementing to zero (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:875-892`):

```ada
            DB.Prepare
              (Update, Item.Database,
               "UPDATE annotation_copy_leases SET refs=refs-1 " &
               "WHERE payload=?1 AND refs>0");
            DB.Bind (Update, 1, US.To_String (Annotation.Payload));
            if DB.Step (Update) /= DB.Done
              or else DB.Changes (Item.Database) /= 1
            then
               raise Catalog_Error with "annotation lease was not held";
            end if;
            DB.Prepare
              (Delete, Item.Database,
               "DELETE FROM annotation_copy_leases " &
               "WHERE payload=?1 AND refs=0");
            DB.Bind (Delete, 1, US.To_String (Annotation.Payload));
            if DB.Step (Delete) /= DB.Done then
               raise Catalog_Error with "annotation lease delete returned row";
            end if;
```

`UPDATE ... SET refs=refs-1` against `CHECK(refs>0)` cannot succeed for a lease at 1, and the `DELETE ... WHERE payload=?1 AND refs=0` that follows is unreachable for the same reason, so the intended reference-count lifecycle cannot complete even once. A failed step raises (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-databases.adb:398-400`):

```ada
      else
         raise SQLite_Error with Error_Message (Item.Resource.Database, Code);
      end if;
```

The vendored build suppresses no CHECK constraint (`sqlite/flyology_object_storage_sqlite.gpr:24-35`):

```ada
      for Default_Switches ("C") use
        ("-O2",
         "-fPIC",
         "-DSQLITE_THREADSAFE=1",
         "-DSQLITE_DQS=0",
         "-DSQLITE_DEFAULT_MEMSTATUS=0",
         "-DSQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED",
         "-DSQLITE_ENABLE_API_ARMOR",
         "-DSQLITE_ENABLE_COLUMN_METADATA",
         "-DSQLITE_ENABLE_DBSTAT_VTAB",
         "-DSQLITE_ENABLE_RTREE",
         "-DSQLITE_USE_URI=1");
```

and the run below reports the constraint firing with the SQLite message `CHECK constraint failed: refs>0`.

Leases are taken on every successful source selection, before the `Check` callback and irrespective of the copy directive (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7338-7345`):

```ada
      if Result = Success then
         Read_Version_Object_Tags_Internal
           (Item, Bucket, Key, US.To_String (Version_ID), Tags);
         Read_Version_Annotations_Internal
           (Item, Bucket, Key, US.To_String (Version_ID), Annotations);
         if Check /= null then
            Check.all (US.To_String (Payload), Info, Tags);
         end if;
```

`Copy_Object` is the only caller of that overload (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2448-2451`) and it clears its flag only after the fallible release returns (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2378-2393`):

```ada
      procedure Release_Annotations is
      begin
         if Annotation_Leases_Held then
            Catalogs.Release_Annotation_Copy_Snapshot
              (Item.Catalog, Source_Annotations);
            Annotation_Leases_Held := False;
```

so the raise on the success path (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2520-2521`):

```ada
      SIO.Close (File);
      Release_Annotations;
```

leaves `Annotation_Leases_Held` set, and the handler calls the same procedure again (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2532-2540`):

```ada
      when others =>
         if SIO.Is_Open (File) then
            SIO.Close (File);
         end if;
         Release_Annotations;
         Info := Empty_Info;
         Source_Identity := (others => <>);
         Destination_Identity := (others => <>);
         Result := Backend_Unavailable;
```

The second call raises inside the handler, so nothing assigns `Result` and `SQLite_Error` propagates out of `Copy_Object`. The S3 application dispatches the copy at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14555-14560` and its catch-all answers 500 (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:18056-18065`):

```ada
      when others =>
         if Apps.Response (X) /= Apps.Not_Started
           or else Apps.Wire_Response_Started (X)
         then
            Apps.Mark_Failed (X);
         else
            Send_Error
              (X, 500, "InternalError",
               "We encountered an internal error", Target_Text);
         end if;
```

The `Backends` contract states that a copy reports its outcome through `Result` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2010`):

```ada
   --  @param Result Copy result; Backend_Unavailable may be ambiguous
```

There is a second effect. The stranded `refs=1` row keeps the annotation payload referenced for the life of the process, because payload reclamation counts the lease table (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:9150-9156`):

```ada
      DB.Prepare
        (Query, Item.Database,
         "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM objects WHERE payload=?1), " &
         "EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM multipart_parts WHERE payload=?1), " &
         "EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM object_versions WHERE payload=?1), " &
         "EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM object_annotations WHERE payload=?1), " &
         "EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM annotation_copy_leases WHERE payload=?1)");
```

No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour, because no SQLite-backed test performs any annotation operation at all; see https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/38. The annotation copy directives are asserted only against the memory and pure-files stores, by the class-wide exerciser at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19660-19675`, driven at `:19734` and `:19743`. An unannotated copy takes no lease and succeeds, which is why the maintained copy tests pass.

## What happens now

Spike: the program opens a SQLite store on a fresh root, copies an unannotated source as a control, then puts a source, attaches one annotation to it, and copies it, asserting that `Copy_Object` neither raises nor returns a non-success status. It then reads the copied annotation and heads the destination to show that the copy was committed anyway, repeats the subject with `Exclude_Annotations` on a second freshly annotated source, and finally copies the first source a second time.

The subject call:

```ada
      Options : OSB.Copy_Options := OSB.Default_Copy_Options;
      ...
      Options.Annotation_Directive := Directive;
      begin
         Store.Copy_Object
           (Bucket, From, Bucket, To, Options, null, Never,
            Info, Source_Identity, Destination_Identity, Result);
      exception
         when E : others =>
            Raised := True;
      ...
      Check (not Raised, Label & ": Copy_Object does not raise");
      Check (Result = OSS.Success, Label & ": Copy_Object returns Success");
```

Output, exit status 1, with the scratch root path elided:

```
  ok: Create_Bucket succeeds
  ok: Put_Object plain-src succeeds
  -- control (no annotation): Copy_Object returned SUCCESS
  ok: control (no annotation): Copy_Object does not raise
  ok: control (no annotation): Copy_Object returns Success
  ok: Put_Object annotated-src succeeds
  -- Put_Object_Annotation on annotated-src SUCCESS size 24
  ok: Put_Object_Annotation on annotated-src succeeds
  -- subject (one annotation): Copy_Object raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.SQLITE.DATABASES.SQLITE_ERROR -- CHECK constraint failed: refs>0
  FAILED: subject (one annotation): Copy_Object does not raise
  FAILED: subject (one annotation): Copy_Object returns Success
  -- Get_Object_Annotation on the copy SUCCESS presence ANNOTATION_PRESENT bytes 24
  ok: Get_Object_Annotation on the copy succeeds
  ok: the copied annotation is present on the destination
  ok: the copied annotation payload round-trips
  -- Head_Object on the copy afterwards SUCCESS size 14
  ok: Put_Object excluded-src succeeds
  -- Put_Object_Annotation on excluded-src SUCCESS size 24
  ok: Put_Object_Annotation on excluded-src succeeds
  -- subject (EXCLUDE directive): Copy_Object raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.SQLITE.DATABASES.SQLITE_ERROR -- CHECK constraint failed: refs>0
  FAILED: subject (EXCLUDE directive): Copy_Object does not raise
  FAILED: subject (EXCLUDE directive): Copy_Object returns Success
  -- second copy of same source: Copy_Object returned SUCCESS
  ok: second copy of same source: Copy_Object does not raise
  ok: second copy of same source: Copy_Object returns Success
BUG REPRODUCED: 4 failed expectation(s)
```

The exception message is the SQLite text `CHECK constraint failed: refs>0`. The lines after the first failure show the destination was published: its annotation reads back with the source payload and `Head_Object` on the copy succeeds. `Exclude_Annotations` fails identically, because the lease is taken before the directive is consulted. The final copy of the same source succeeds, because the stranded row makes that lease go 1 to 2 to 1; that is what masks the defect after the first copy.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

`Copy_Object` of an annotated source returns `Success` and does not raise, reporting its outcome through `Result` as the contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2010` specifies, and the lease taken for the source annotation payload is released so that payload can be reclaimed later.

## Suggested treatment

Make the release consistent with the table's own constraint, and clear the backend flag before the fallible call so a raise cannot re-enter the handler.

This needs a maintainer decision on the intended lease lifecycle, because the two statements in `Release_Annotation_Copy_Leases_Internal` encode different ones:

- Drop `CHECK(refs>0)` from the TEMP table at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:2842-2846` and keep the existing decrement plus delete-at-zero. The table then holds transient `refs=0` rows between the two statements.
- Keep the constraint and replace the release with a `DELETE FROM annotation_copy_leases WHERE payload=?1 AND refs=1` and `UPDATE annotation_copy_leases SET refs=refs-1 WHERE payload=?1 AND refs>1` pair, so a row never holds zero. The existing `DB.Changes (Item.Database) /= 1` guard has to cover whichever statement applied.

Independently of that choice, move `Annotation_Leases_Held := False` above the call to `Catalogs.Release_Annotation_Copy_Snapshot` at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2381-2383`, so a failed release cannot be retried from `Copy_Object`'s own handler and the original outcome survives.

The exception path of the same release is the trigger of https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/36, which wedges the whole store; that one needs its own fix and is not closed by repairing the lease arithmetic.

The regression test belongs in `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb`, which today performs no annotation operation. The spike is the test: put a source, attach one annotation, copy it once with the default directive and once with `Exclude_Annotations`, and assert `Success` with no exception in both cases and on a second copy of the same source. The wider gap it exposes is https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/38.

## Severity

Every CopyObject of an annotated object fails on the first copy of that source in a process, and the S3 boundary answers 500 `InternalError` for a copy that was committed, so a client that retries against a versioning-enabled bucket publishes a second destination generation. Nothing is lost or corrupted and the retry succeeds. The annotation payload of that source is also never reclaimed while the process lives, because the stranded lease row keeps it referenced. It needs only an annotated source, which is an ordinary use of the annotation operations. The failure is deterministic. SQLite backend only.

## Summary

The exception handler of the annotation-carrying `Find_Selected_Object` overload runs `Release_Annotation_Copy_Leases_Internal` before `Item.Gate.Release`, and that release is fallible. When it raises, the unlock never runs, `Held` stays `True` for the life of the process, and every later catalog call on the store blocks in the protected entry with no deadline and no cancellation. The store stops answering every operation, on every bucket, permanently: an S3 server on this backend serves no further request and cannot even close the store, and re-opening does not recover it in the same process. The caller-reachable sequence is a CopyObject of a source generation that carries at least one annotation, where the `Open_Source` callback raises because the caller's token was cancelled, because the caller's deadline has passed, or because the source body file is gone.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The handler puts fallible work before the unlock (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7349-7360`):

```ada
   exception
      when others =>
         if Locked then
            Release_Annotation_Copy_Leases_Internal (Item, Annotations);
            Item.Gate.Release;
         end if;
         Payload := US.Null_Unbounded_String;
         Info := Empty_Info;
         Tags := Empty_Object_Tags;
         Annotations.Clear;
         Identity := (others => <>);
         raise;
```

That release raises whenever its update does not change exactly one row, and it raises unconditionally today for a lease at 1 because the table declares `CHECK(refs>0)` (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:875-884`):

```ada
            DB.Prepare
              (Update, Item.Database,
               "UPDATE annotation_copy_leases SET refs=refs-1 " &
               "WHERE payload=?1 AND refs>0");
            DB.Bind (Update, 1, US.To_String (Annotation.Payload));
            if DB.Step (Update) /= DB.Done
              or else DB.Changes (Item.Database) /= 1
            then
               raise Catalog_Error with "annotation lease was not held";
            end if;
```

That constraint defect is reported separately as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/35. This defect does not depend on it: any SQLite failure in the release has the same effect, because the handler performs fallible work before the unlock.

The gate has no owner tracking, no timeout, and no controlled unlock (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:918-928`):

```ada
   protected body Operation_Gate is
      entry Acquire when not Held is
      begin
         Held := True;
      end Acquire;

      procedure Release is
      begin
         Held := False;
      end Release;
   end Operation_Gate;
```

Every catalog operation begins with `Item.Gate.Acquire`, including `Delete_Bucket` at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:3349`, the plain object lookup at `:5701`, this overload at `:7329`, payload reclamation at `:9148`, and `Close` at `:2863`. Once `Held` stays `True` all of them block.

The gate-safe sibling shows the shape the handler should have (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7368-7377`):

```ada
      Item.Gate.Acquire;
      Locked := True;
      Release_Annotation_Copy_Leases_Internal (Item, Annotations);
      Item.Gate.Release;
      Locked := False;
   exception
      when others =>
         if Locked then
            Item.Gate.Release;
         end if;
```

The only caller of the leaking overload is `Copy_Object` (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2448-2451`):

```ada
      Catalogs.Find_Selected_Object
        (Item.Catalog, Source_Bucket, Source_Key, Options.Source_Selector,
         Payload, Source_Info, Source_Tags, Source_Annotations,
         Selected_Source, Result, Open_Source'Access);
```

and the hook it passes raises on three ordinary conditions (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2402-2411`):

```ada
      begin
         Check_Context (Token, Deadline);
         if not Is_Payload_Name (Payload_Name)
           or else not Ada.Directories.Exists (Path)
           or else GNAT.OS_Lib.Is_Symbolic_Link (Path)
           or else Ada.Directories.Kind (Path) /=
             Ada.Directories.Ordinary_File
         then
            raise Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error;
         end if;
```

`Check_Context` raises on a cancelled token or an expired deadline (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:169-176`):

```ada
      if Token /= null and then Token.Requested then
         raise Flyology.Cancellation.Operation_Cancelled;
      end if;
      if Deadline /= Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last
        and then Ada.Real_Time.Clock >= Deadline
      then
         raise Flyology.IO.Timeout_Error;
      end if;
```

Both are plain arguments of the public copy profile (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2018-2019`):

```ada
      Token              : access Flyology.Cancellation.Token;
      Deadline           : Ada.Real_Time.Time;
```

and the S3 application passes the request's own cancellation and deadline on every CopyObject (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14555-14560`):

```ada
                  Store.Copy_Object
                    (Requests.Bucket_Name (Source_Target, Source_Parsed),
                     Requests.Object_Key (Source_Target, Source_Parsed),
                     Bucket, Key, Copy_Options_Value,
                     Apps.Cancellation (X), Apps.Deadline (X), Info,
                     Source_Identity, Destination_Identity, Result);
```

so an ordinary S3 CopyObject whose client aborts, or whose request deadline expires, reaches the handler. The missing source body file is the third route, and one way to produce it is the payload race reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/34.

`Get_Object` and `Head_Object` cannot reach this handler. They use the overload at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:5701-5715`, which takes no leases and whose handler releases the gate unconditionally, so a cancelled or timed-out GET of an annotated object is not enough.

An amplifier belongs with the fix. `Gate.Acquire` is an unconditional protected entry, so a caller waiting for it does not consult its own `Token` or `Deadline`, and long operations hold the gate meanwhile. `List_Objects` acquires it at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7964` and then scans every object of the bucket under it (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7981-7989`):

```ada
      DB.Prepare
        (Query, Item.Database,
         "SELECT object_key,size,modified,entity_tag,content_type," &
         "checksum_algorithm,checksum_method,checksum_value," &
         "(SELECT count(*) FROM object_parts WHERE bucket_name=objects." &
         "bucket_name AND object_key=objects.object_key) " &
         "FROM objects WHERE bucket_name=?1 ORDER BY object_key");
      DB.Bind (Query, 1, Bucket);
      while DB.Step (Query) = DB.Row loop
```

On its own that is latency. Together with this handler it is what makes the timed-out CopyObject trigger easy to reach, because a copy request can spend its whole deadline queued behind such an operation and then find the deadline already expired when `Open_Source` runs under the gate.

No existing test covers this. The SQLite suite performs no annotation operation at all, so no test can reach the lease-taking overload, and no test asserts that the store remains usable after a failed `Copy_Object`. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour.

## What happens now

Spike: two programs, both of which put a source, annotate it, confirm `Head_Bucket` answers, provoke a `Copy_Object` failure, and then probe `Head_Bucket` from a second task with a bounded 5 second wait. The probe timing out is the observation: the gate was never released. Both programs exit through `GNAT.OS_Lib.OS_Exit (1)` on the timeout rather than hanging with a blocked task.

The first program establishes the mechanism deterministically. It removes the source body file so `Open_Source` raises `Data_Error` under the gate:

```ada
      Ada.Directories.Delete_File (Body_Payload);
      ...
      Store.Copy_Object
        (Bucket, "src", Bucket, "dst", OSB.Default_Copy_Options, null, Never,
         Info, Source_Identity, Destination_Identity, Result);
      ...
      select
         Completion.Wait (Probe);
      or
         delay 5.0;
```

Output, exit status 1, with the scratch root path elided:

```
  ok: Create_Bucket succeeds
  ok: Put_Object src succeeds
  ok: exactly one payload file before annotating
  ok: Put_Object_Annotation succeeds
  -- control Head_Bucket SUCCESS
  ok: control Head_Bucket succeeds
  -- removed the source body payload <root>/objects/0c2b44addcdd34f164dc191cac24a2acb5ff851120baf4c8910613092031af8d.blob
  -- Copy_Object with a missing source body returned BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE
  -- probing the store from a second task (bounded 5 s wait)
  -- probe Head_Bucket did not return within 5 s: the catalog operation gate was never released
  FAILED: the store still serves Head_Bucket after a failed Copy_Object
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

This shows the mechanism: a `Check` callback that raises under the gate leaves the store wedged, and the control `Head_Bucket` immediately before proves the store was answering.

The second program shows the same outcome with no filesystem interference and nothing but a caller-supplied deadline through the public backend interface. The source carries 200 annotations, which only widens the interval between the entry check and the `Open_Source` callback, and the deadline is scanned upward in 3 microsecond steps. Attempts that are too short raise `Timeout_Error` at the entry check, take no leases and are harmless, so the scan is safe:

```ada
   for Step in 1 .. 400 loop
      declare
         Budget : constant Integer := Step * 3;
         Deadline : constant Ada.Real_Time.Time :=
           Ada.Real_Time.Clock + Ada.Real_Time.Microseconds (Budget);
      begin
         Store.Copy_Object
           (Bucket, "src", Bucket, "dst", OSB.Default_Copy_Options, null,
            Deadline, Info, Source_Identity, Destination_Identity, Result);
         Engaged := True;
      exception
         when Flyology.IO.Timeout_Error =>
            Entry_Timeouts := Entry_Timeouts + 1;
```

Output, exit status 1, with the scratch root path elided:

```
  ok: Create_Bucket succeeds
  ok: Put_Object src succeeds
  -- attached 200 annotations to src
  -- control Head_Bucket SUCCESS
  ok: control Head_Bucket succeeds
  -- CopyObject with a 6 us deadline returned BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE (the deadline expired inside the gate-held window)
  -- attempts rejected at the entry check: 1
  -- probing the store from a second task (bounded 5 s wait)
  -- probe Head_Bucket did not return within 5 s: the catalog operation gate was never released
  FAILED: the store still serves Head_Bucket after a CopyObject whose deadline expired
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

This shows the trigger is caller-reachable: one CopyObject with a 6 microsecond deadline, after a single attempt rejected at the entry check, and the store never answers again. It also shows a secondary effect visible in both runs. The timed-out `Copy_Object` reports `Backend_Unavailable` rather than propagating `Timeout_Error`, because the SQLite exception from the lease release replaced the original one before `Copy_Object`'s `when Operation_Cancelled | Timeout_Error => raise` handler could see it.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A `Copy_Object` that fails for any reason leaves the store usable: the catalog operation gate is released on every path out of `Find_Selected_Object`, exactly as the sibling `Release_Annotation_Copy_Snapshot` at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7368-7377` and the non-leasing overload at `:5701-5715` already do. The caller then sees the outcome the `Backends` contract defines, `Result` or the propagated `Operation_Cancelled` or `Timeout_Error` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2010`).

## Suggested treatment

Make the unlock unconditional in the handler at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7349-7360`: release the gate first and perform the lease release afterwards, or hold the lock in a controlled object whose `Finalize` releases it, so no fallible work can precede the unlock. Guard the lease release so that its own failure cannot displace the exception that is being propagated. The separate lease repair in https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/35 is still needed; it removes today's guaranteed failure but not the structural one.

This needs a maintainer decision on two points that change documented behaviour rather than fixing an internal ordering:

- Whether `Gate.Acquire` should honour the caller's `Token` and `Deadline` while waiting. It is currently `entry Acquire when not Held` with no timeout (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:918-928`), so a queued request waits without bound and its deadline can expire inside the gate-held window, which is what makes the timed-out-copy trigger easy to reach under load. Honouring them would make a queued request fail with `Timeout_Error` or `Operation_Cancelled` where it currently waits, which is an externally visible change of outcome for every catalog operation. Leaving it as is keeps the current latency behaviour and relies on the handler fix alone.
- Whether a `Copy_Object` whose deadline expires inside the gate-held window should propagate `Timeout_Error` instead of returning `Backend_Unavailable`. Both runs above show `Backend_Unavailable`, because the replacement exception reaches `Copy_Object`'s handler instead of the original. Preserving the original exception changes the outcome a caller observes for a timed-out copy.

The regression test belongs in `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb`, because the gate and the lease table are specific to this backend. The spike is the test, in the first program's deterministic form: annotate a source, make the source body unavailable so the copy's payload check raises under the gate, call `Copy_Object`, then assert from a second task with a bounded wait that `Head_Bucket` still answers. A test written this way must keep the bounded wait, because a failing run otherwise blocks the suite instead of reporting.

## Severity

The store stops answering every operation, on every bucket, for the life of the process, and it cannot be closed or recovered in that process because `Close` also acquires the gate. An S3 server on this backend serves no further request after one CopyObject of an annotated object that is cancelled, that times out, or whose source body is unavailable, and cancellation and deadline are ordinary arguments a server passes on every request. Both routes are demonstrated end to end through the public backend interface, and both programs reproduce the wedge on every run. The mechanism is deterministic; the exact deadline value at which the second program lands is timing dependent, so it scans rather than using a fixed one. SQLite backend only.

## Summary

`Catalogs.Delete_Bucket` decides whether a bucket is empty from the current-object mirror and the multipart table, and never consults `object_versions`. A versioning-enabled bucket whose keys have all been deleted through DeleteObject has no mirror rows, so it passes the emptiness test, and the `DELETE FROM buckets` that follows trips the `ON DELETE RESTRICT` foreign key on the retained generations. The SQLite error is rewrapped as `Backend_Unavailable` and the server answers 503 `SlowDown` instead of the 409 `BucketNotEmpty` that the defined `Bucket_Not_Empty` status exists to produce, so the client retries indefinitely instead of learning that it must remove the versions first. The memory and pure-files backends both return `Bucket_Not_Empty` for the same sequence.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The emptiness test consults three tables, none of which is `object_versions` (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:3353-3357`):

```ada
      DB.Prepare
        (Query, Item.Database,
         "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM buckets WHERE name=?1), " &
         "EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM objects WHERE bucket_name=?1), " &
         "EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM multipart_uploads WHERE bucket_name=?1)");
```

Only those two results produce the defined status (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:3368-3374`):

```ada
      elsif DB.Column (Query, 1) /= 0 or else DB.Column (Query, 2) /= 0 then
         DB.Rollback (Item.Database);
         In_Transaction := False;
         Result := Bucket_Not_Empty;
         Item.Gate.Release;
         Locked := False;
         return;
```

and the delete then runs (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:3376-3380`):

```ada
      DB.Prepare (Delete, Item.Database, "DELETE FROM buckets WHERE name=?1");
      DB.Bind (Delete, 1, Name);
      if DB.Step (Delete) /= DB.Done then
         raise Catalog_Error with "bucket delete returned a row";
      end if;
```

`object_versions` restricts that delete (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:387`):

```ada
     "FOREIGN KEY(bucket_name) REFERENCES buckets(name) ON DELETE RESTRICT" &
```

A key whose current generation is a delete marker leaves no `objects` row for the emptiness test to find, because publishing a marker removes the mirror row (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:6271-6279`):

```ada
      DB.Prepare
        (Delete_Object, Item.Database,
         "DELETE FROM objects WHERE bucket_name=?1 AND object_key=?2");
      DB.Bind (Delete_Object, 1, Bucket);
      DB.Bind_Bytes (Delete_Object, 2, Key);
      if DB.Step (Delete_Object) /= DB.Done then
         raise Catalog_Error with
           "current object mirror delete returned a row";
      end if;
```

and returns before it would be reinserted (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:6317-6319`):

```ada
      if Is_Marker then
         return;
      end if;
```

The backend wrapper turns the resulting exception into an ambiguous status (`sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:400-405`):

```ada
   exception
      when Flyology.Cancellation.Operation_Cancelled
         | Flyology.IO.Timeout_Error =>
         raise;
      when others =>
         Result := Backend_Unavailable;
```

The storage contract that `Backends` operations return defines a status for exactly this state, and a different one for what the backend actually reports (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:21` and `:38`):

```ada
   --  @enum Bucket_Not_Empty Bucket still contains objects
   --  @enum Backend_Unavailable Backend cannot currently serve the operation
```

The two answers are not interchangeable at the S3 boundary (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1947-1954`):

```ada
         when Bucket_Not_Empty =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 409, "BucketNotEmpty",
               "The bucket you tried to delete is not empty", Resource);
         when Capacity_Exceeded | Backend_Unavailable =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 503, "SlowDown",
               "Please reduce your request rate", Resource);
```

The other two backends count retained generations. The pure-files backend (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3846-3852`):

```ada
      elsif Has_Objects (Join (Path, "objects"))
        or else Has_Objects (Join (Path, "multipart"))
        or else
          (Ada.Directories.Exists (Join (Path, "versions"))
           and then Has_Objects (Join (Path, "versions")))
      then
         Result := Bucket_Not_Empty;
```

and the memory backend, whose object table retains markers (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:585-593`):

```ada
         for Index in 1 .. Highest_Object loop
            if Objects (Index).Used
              and then Ada.Strings.Unbounded.To_String
                (Objects (Index).Bucket) = Name
            then
               Result := Bucket_Not_Empty;
               return;
            end if;
         end loop;
```

The existing SQLite assertion does not cover the marker-only path; it deletes a bucket that still holds a current data object (`sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:7660-7663`):

```ada
      Store.Delete_Bucket
        ("sqlite-bucket", null, Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Result);
      Assert (Result = Bucket_Not_Empty,
              "SQLite backend deleted a nonempty bucket");
```

The versioning race test does reach `Delete_Bucket` on a versioned bucket, but it permanently removes the generation first (`sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:1502-1505`):

```ada
                     Store.Delete_Selected_Object
                       (Race_Bucket, "current", Null_Version_Selector,
                        No_Delete_Object_Conditions, False, null,
                        Ada.Real_Time.Time_Last, Publication, Result);
```

so its cleanup at `:1514-1518` expects `Success`. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour, and none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: one class-wide procedure over `Backends.Backend'Class` runs the identical sequence, `Create_Bucket`, `Put_Bucket_Versioning (Versioning_Enabled)`, `Put_Object`, `Delete_Object`, `Head_Object`, `Delete_Bucket`, and asserts `Bucket_Not_Empty` in both cases. It is driven first against a memory store as a control and then against a SQLite store on a fresh root.

```ada
   procedure Exercise
     (Store : in out OSB.Backend'Class; Label : String; Control : Boolean)
   is
   begin
      Store.Create_Bucket (Bucket, null, Never, Result);
      Store.Put_Bucket_Versioning (Bucket, Versioned, null, Never, Result);
         Store.Put_Object
           (Bucket, "k", Source, OSS.Default_Put_Options, null, Never,
            Info, Identity, Result);
      Store.Delete_Object (Bucket, "k", null, Never, Result);
         Store.Head_Object (Bucket, "k", null, Never, Info, Result);
      Store.Delete_Bucket (Bucket, null, Never, Result);
      Check (Result = OSS.Bucket_Not_Empty,
             Label & ": Delete_Bucket reports Bucket_Not_Empty" &
             (if Control then " (control)" else ""));
   end Exercise;
```

Output, exit status 1, with the scratch root path elided:

```
  ok: memory: Create_Bucket succeeds
  ok: memory: Put_Bucket_Versioning Enabled succeeds
  ok: memory: Put_Object succeeds
  -- memory: Delete_Object (publishes a delete marker) SUCCESS
  ok: memory: Delete_Object succeeds
  -- memory: Head_Object after the marker NOT_FOUND
  -- memory: Delete_Bucket BUCKET_NOT_EMPTY
  ok: memory: Delete_Bucket reports Bucket_Not_Empty (control)
  ok: sqlite: Create_Bucket succeeds
  ok: sqlite: Put_Bucket_Versioning Enabled succeeds
  ok: sqlite: Put_Object succeeds
  -- sqlite: Delete_Object (publishes a delete marker) SUCCESS
  ok: sqlite: Delete_Object succeeds
  -- sqlite: Head_Object after the marker NOT_FOUND
  -- sqlite: Delete_Bucket BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE
  FAILED: sqlite: Delete_Bucket reports Bucket_Not_Empty
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The memory control answers `BUCKET_NOT_EMPTY` for the identical call sequence, and SQLite answers `BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE`. `Head_Object` reports `NOT_FOUND` on both, which confirms the key is absent from the current view in both stores and that the difference is in the emptiness test, not in the delete marker.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

`Delete_Bucket` on a bucket that still holds retained generations returns `Bucket_Not_Empty`, the status documented as "Bucket still contains objects" (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:21`), so the S3 boundary answers 409 `BucketNotEmpty`. The memory backend (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:585-593`) and the pure-files backend (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3846-3852`) already do this, so the cross-backend expectation is unambiguous.

## Suggested treatment

Add the retained generations to the emptiness query at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:3353-3357`, for example a fourth `EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM object_versions WHERE bucket_name=?1)` column, and include it in the test at `:3368`. No interface, status, or documented policy changes, because `Bucket_Not_Empty` already exists and the other two backends already return it here.

The regression test belongs in the shared black-box suite, so every backend is held to the same answer. The class-wide exercisers that already run over `Backends.Backend'Class` live privately inside the body of `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb` and are driven only against the memory and pure-files stores, for example at `:19734` and `:19743`, so the SQLite suite cannot call them. `sqlite/tests/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.gpr:5` already puts `../../tests/src/` on the SQLite suite's source path, so a class-wide bucket-emptiness exerciser placed in the visible `Flyology.Object_Storage.Backends.Testing` package (`tests/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-testing.ads` and `.adb`) can be driven from both suites against all three backends. If that is not wanted, the narrower version belongs in `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb` next to the existing assertion at `:7660-7663`. The spike is the test, unchanged: one class-wide procedure asserting `Bucket_Not_Empty`, run against the memory store as a control and against a SQLite store.

## Severity

Every client that enables versioning, deletes its objects the ordinary way, and then tries to delete the bucket gets 503 `SlowDown`. Stock clients retry a 503 with back-off and then give up, so the caller never learns that it must remove the retained versions and delete markers first. Nothing is deleted and no data is lost, because the delete runs inside a transaction that rolls back, so the defect is the reported status rather than durability. A suspended bucket after DeleteObject reaches the same state. The failure is deterministic. SQLite backend only.

## Summary

The object-annotation qualification record names SQLite among the implementations and then states that the maintained backend and server corpora exercise annotation behaviour, including copy-directive behaviour, "across the implementations". No SQLite-backed test performs any annotation operation, and the only black-box corpus run against the SQLite server has no annotation coverage at all. A reader auditing the record concludes that SQLite annotation copy is qualified, when in fact the first CopyObject of any annotated SQLite object fails and can leave the store answering nothing.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`. This is contract drift between a qualification document and the maintained tests, so there is no runtime reproduction; the evidence is the document text and the absence of any annotation call in the SQLite-backed suites.

The record names the three implementations (`docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:26-27`):

```
Memory, pure-files, and SQLite implement the same interface. Pure-files keeps
annotation payloads in external files. SQLite schema version 23 records
```

and then states the coverage claim (`docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:42-46`):

```
CopyObject applies the parsed annotation directive to actual destination
state: COPY preserves the selected source generation's annotations, while
EXCLUDE publishes none. The maintained backend and server corpora exercise
payload, checksum, version, list, missing, condition, and copy-directive
behavior across the implementations.
```

The SQLite suite calls no annotation operation:

```
$ rg -c "Put_Object_Annotation|Get_Object_Annotation|Delete_Object_Annotation|List_Object_Annotations" sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb
exit 1
```

`rg` exits 1 with no output, meaning zero matching lines. The only `annotation` matches in that file are migration fixtures such as the repeated `"DROP TABLE object_annotations;"` at `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:551` through `:963`, the schema check at `:1861`, and empty annotation arguments passed to the catalog at `:3397-3398` and `:3439-3440`:

```ada
         Retired_Annotations, Result,
         Annotations => Catalogs.Annotation_Copy_Record_Vectors.Empty_Vector);
```

The black-box corpus has no annotation coverage either:

```
$ rg -ci annotation tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb
exit 1
```

and that corpus is the only one run against the SQLite server. The script selects the SQLite server binary (`tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh:39-42`):

```
  sqlite)
    (cd "$PROJECT_DIR/sqlite/tests" && alr -n build)
    SERVER="$PROJECT_DIR/sqlite/tests/bin/s3_sqlite_server"
    ;;
```

and drives it with that corpus alone (`tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh:106-116`):

```
  FLYOLOGY_S3_IMPLEMENTATION="flyology-$BACKEND" \
    "$SCRIPT_DIR/run-s3-implementation.sh" \
    "http://127.0.0.1:$PORT" \
    "http://host.docker.internal:$PORT" \
    "flyology-$BACKEND-corpus-$$" "$ACCESS_KEY" "$SECRET_KEY" yes
  if [ "$BACKEND" = files ] || [ "$BACKEND" = sqlite ]; then
    RESTART_BUCKET="flyology-$BACKEND-restart-$$"
    TIMESTAMP=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)
    FLYOLOGY_S3_IMPLEMENTATION="flyology-$BACKEND" \
      "$PROJECT_DIR/tests/bin/s3_implementation_corpus" \
      "http://127.0.0.1:$PORT" "$RESTART_BUCKET" "$TIMESTAMP" \
      restart-prepare
```

`run-s3-implementation.sh` invokes the same `./bin/s3_implementation_corpus` binary at `tests/scripts/run-s3-implementation.sh:51`, `:60` and `:94`.

Annotation behaviour is exercised, but against two backends only. The class-wide exerciser is declared over `Backends.Backend'Class` (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19555-19557`):

```ada
   procedure Exercise_Object_Annotations
     (Store  : in out Flyology.Object_Storage.Backends.Backend'Class;
      Bucket : String)
```

its copy-directive assertions are at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19660-19675`, and it is driven twice (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19734` and `:19743`):

```ada
         Exercise_Object_Annotations (Store, "memory-annotation-bucket");
         Exercise_Object_Annotations (Store, "files-annotation-bucket");
```

against a memory store declared at `:19732` and a pure-files store opened at `:19740-19741`. It is never driven against a SQLite store, because it lives in the body of a root-crate suite that the separate SQLite suite does not call.

The backend's own architecture document does not fill the gap either:

```
$ rg -ci annotation docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md
exit 1
```

## Failure scenario

A maintainer or auditor reads `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md`. Line 26 tells them that memory, pure-files and SQLite implement the same interface, and lines 44 to 46 tell them that the maintained corpora exercise copy-directive behaviour across the implementations. They conclude that a CopyObject of an annotated object has been verified on the SQLite backend, and treat that path as qualified.

What the maintained tests actually run on SQLite is the SQLite suite, which never calls `Put_Object_Annotation` or any sibling, and the black-box corpus, which contains no annotation request. Nothing in either exercises an annotation operation against a SQLite store. The paths the record presents as covered are exactly the ones where the first CopyObject of an annotated source raises out of the backend, reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/35, and where the same failure inside the lookup's exception handler leaves the catalog gate held so the store stops answering every operation, reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/36.

## What should happen

The qualification record states the coverage that exists. Either the corpora exercise annotation behaviour against a SQLite store, so that "across the implementations" at `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:44-46` is accurate, or the sentence names the implementations it actually covers.

## Suggested treatment

Prefer closing the coverage gap over narrowing the claim, because the gap conceals two live defects. `sqlite/tests/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.gpr:5` already puts `../../tests/src/` on the SQLite suite's source path:

```
   for Source_Dirs use ("src/", "config/", "../../tests/src/");
```

so the smallest change is to promote `Exercise_Object_Annotations` from the private body of `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19555-19696` into the visible `Flyology.Object_Storage.Backends.Testing` package (`tests/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-testing.ads` and `.adb`), leave the two existing drivers in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb` at `:19734` and `:19743` calling it, and add a third driver in `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb` against a SQLite store on a scratch root. That exerciser already asserts both copy directives at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19660-19675`, so it fails today on the first annotated copy.

The alternative, if annotation coverage is deliberately not wanted on this backend, is to rewrite the sentence at `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:44-46` to name the memory and pure-files implementations explicitly, and to say that the SQLite implementation is covered only by its own catalog tests. That leaves the defects unguarded but stops the record from overstating the evidence.

There is no program for this issue, and no existing test asserts the wrong behaviour.

## Severity

A qualification record overstates the evidence behind it: it presents annotation behaviour, including copy directives, as corpus-verified across three implementations when one of the three has no annotation coverage of any kind. Nothing misbehaves at run time because of this document, so the direct impact is on anyone auditing or relying on the record rather than on a running store. It matters because the uncovered path is where two reachable defects live, one of which stops the store answering. Not reproduced.

## Summary

`Head_Object_Response_Bound` requires a successful HeadObject response to echo the requested `versionId` byte for byte. An absent `x-amz-version-id` header decodes to the empty string, so `"" = "null"` is False and `Execute_Head_Object` raises `Invalid_Response` on an otherwise complete 200. A bucket that never had versioning enabled is exactly the state that answers `?versionId=null` without the header, and `versionId=null` is the documented way to name an object in such a bucket. This is the check that stops the repository's own conformance corpus against the pinned RustFS, MinIO and SeaweedFS images: the only server the corpus gets past is the in-repo one, which manufactures the header.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The predicate demands an exact echo (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5641-5658`):

```ada
   function Head_Object_Response_Bound
     (Value    : Head_Object_Result;
      Prepared : Prepared_Request) return Boolean
   is
      Requested_Version : constant String := US.To_String
        (Prepared.Requested_Head_Object_Version_ID);
      Requested_Payer : constant String := US.To_String
        (Prepared.Requested_Head_Object_Request_Payer);
      Returned_Payer : constant String := US.To_String
        (Value.Request_Charged);
   begin
      return
        (Requested_Version'Length = 0
         or else US.To_String (Value.Version_ID) = Requested_Version)
        and then
          (Returned_Payer /= "requester"
           or else Requested_Payer = "requester");
   end Head_Object_Response_Bound;
```

The decoded field is the header copied verbatim, which is the empty string when the header is absent (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5813`):

```ada
               Version_ID => H ("x-amz-version-id"),
```

`Prepare_Head_Object` retains the caller's selector unconditionally and validates nothing about it (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5153-5155`):

```ada
         Result.Requested_Head_Object_Version_ID := Parameters.Version_ID;
         Result.Requested_Head_Object_Request_Payer :=
           Parameters.Request_Payer;
```

and the executor turns a False predicate into an exception, so the decoded metadata never reaches the caller and no `Head_Object_Outcome` value is produced (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5880-5886`):

```ada
            if Result.Kind = Object_Found
              and then not Head_Object_Response_Bound
                (Result.Result, Prepared)
            then
               raise Invalid_Response with
                 "HeadObject response does not match prepared request";
            end if;
```

The exception is specified for a response that breaks its modeled contract (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:300-301`):

```ada
   --  Raised when a received S3 response violates its modeled contract.
   Invalid_Response : exception;
```

An omitted header breaks no modeled contract. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`:

```python
sh = model['shapes']['HeadObjectOutput']
print('HeadObjectOutput required:', sh.get('required'))
v = sh['members']['VersionId']
print(' ', {k: v[k] for k in ('shape', 'location', 'locationName')})
```

```
HeadObjectOutput required: None
  {'shape': 'ObjectVersionId', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-version-id'}
```

The crate's own specification calls the field optional too (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:1540`):

```ada
   --  @field Version_ID Optional selected object-version response value
```

Real servers omit it. RustFS 1.0.0-rc.3, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh:5`, answered a signed `HEAD /bucket/native-object?versionId=null` carrying `x-amz-request-payer: requester` and `x-amz-checksum-mode: ENABLED`, against a bucket that was never version-configured, with this response, identical on three consecutive runs apart from `x-request-id`:

```
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
accept-ranges: bytes
content-length: 64
content-type: application/octet-stream
etag: "014842d480b571495a4a0363793f7367"
last-modified: Mon, 07 Sep 2026 01:27:10 GMT
x-request-id: 8c68e1c0-a791-45ec-8778-d9b0729a1312
x-amz-request-id: 8c68e1c0-a791-45ec-8778-d9b0729a1312
date: Mon, 07 Sep 2026 01:27:10 GMT
```

No `x-amz-version-id` and no `x-amz-request-charged`. The same request without the two `x-amz-*` request headers, and the same request without `versionId`, return the same header set, so only the bucket's versioning state decides whether the header appears. The same container driven through the versioning states gives three shapes for `HEAD ?versionId=null`, none of them the literal `null`: never configured answers `200` with the header absent, enabled answers `404`, and suspended answers `200` with `x-amz-version-id: 00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000`.

The repository's own conformance corpus sends exactly that request and asserts success (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1144-1159`):

```ada
         declare
            Parameters : Low_Level.Head_Object_Parameters;
         begin
            Parameters.Version_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String ("null");
            Parameters.Request_Payer := US.To_Unbounded_String ("requester");
            Parameters.Checksum_Mode := True;
            declare
               Prepared : constant Low_Level.Prepared_Request :=
                 Low_Level.Prepare_Head_Object
                   (Origin, Low_Level.Path_Style, Bucket, Key, Parameters,
                    Identity, "us-east-1", Timestamp);
               Outcome : constant Low_Level.Head_Object_Outcome :=
                 Low_Level.Execute_Head_Object
                   (HTTP, Prepared, Timeout => 30.0);
            begin
               if Outcome.Kind /= Low_Level.Object_Found
```

and it does not get there. Running that corpus through `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh`, `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` and `tests/scripts/test-seaweedfs.sh` at this commit stops at this HeadObject case on all three pinned images, with the byte-identical line

```
S3 implementation corpus failed: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : HeadObject response does not match prepared request
```

so the omission is not RustFS specific. The RustFS run was repeated seven times, on fresh and reused containers, on four different host ports and through a logging proxy, with the same failure at the same exchange every time. The response that check refuses satisfies every assertion the corpus itself makes about it, including the composite checksum values at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1161-1166`.
