The one server that satisfies the demand does so with a fallback that exists for no other purpose (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:4189-4194`):

```ada
            if US.Length (Info.Version) > 0 then
               Apps.Set_Header
                 (X, "x-amz-version-id", US.To_String (Info.Version));
            elsif Object_Read_Request.Has_Version_ID then
               Apps.Set_Header (X, "x-amz-version-id", "null");
            end if;
```

and, in the HeadObject branch that starts at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16518`, unconditionally (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16619-16622`):

```ada
                     Apps.Set_Header
                       (X, "x-amz-version-id",
                        (if US.Length (Info.Version) > 0
                         then US.To_String (Info.Version) else "null"));
```

That server behaviour is reported separately as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/22. It is why the push and pull-request gate stays green: `s3-smoke` runs the corpus against the in-repo memory server (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:240-272`), while the reference servers run only on a schedule (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:283-292`).

The compatibility document describes the opposite direction as correct, so the client demand is not a documented limitation (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450`):

```
Successful PutObject returns its version identity from the same backend
publication snapshot as the body and metadata. The server omits the header for
unversioned publication, emits the exact opaque identity for enabled
memory/files/SQLite publication, and emits `x-amz-version-id: null` for
suspended memory/files/SQLite publication.
```

The only statement of the demand is a private-part comment (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:10533-10535`):

```ada
      --  Derived HeadObject response binding: an explicit version selector
      --  must be echoed by a successful response, and a charged response is
      --  valid only when the exact request admitted requester pays.
```

No test at this layer catches it, and none has to be inverted here. The check arrived with commit `2703ed6` (2026-08-30) and was qualified only against the in-process socket fake, whose HeadObject version fixtures all emit the header: `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:2761-2790` serves `scoped-head-version-mismatch`, `-control` and `-del`, all with a present value, and the one fixture that omits the header, `scoped-head-version-missing` at `:2753-2760`, is consumed by `Require_Invalid_Head` at `:9741`, which drives the composable `Objects.Head_Object` rather than `Execute_Head_Object`. That composable assertion belongs with the twin predicate reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/46, whose `Head_Object_Response_Bound` and `Get_Object_Response_Bound` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:121-155` have the identical shape. The corpus reaches this layer first, so both layers have to change together.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below scripts two exchanges on a loopback origin. The first replies to `HEAD /never-versioned/native-object?versionId=null` with the verbatim RustFS header set quoted above and no `x-amz-version-id`; the second is identical with `x-amz-version-id: null` added. Both legs drive `Prepare_Head_Object` with the corpus parameters and `Execute_Head_Object`, and assert that the outcome is `Object_Found` and that the ETag reaches the caller.

```ada
      Parameters.Version_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String ("null");
      Parameters.Request_Payer := US.To_Unbounded_String ("requester");
      Parameters.Checksum_Mode := True;
      declare
         Prepared : constant Low_Level.Prepared_Request :=
           Low_Level.Prepare_Head_Object
             (Origin, Low_Level.Path_Style, Bucket, Key, Parameters,
              Identity, "us-east-1", Timestamp);
         Outcome : constant Low_Level.Head_Object_Outcome :=
           Low_Level.Execute_Head_Object (HTTP, Prepared, Timeout => 10.0);
      begin
...
         Check (Outcome.Kind = Low_Level.Object_Found,
                Label & ": a 200 HeadObject without x-amz-version-id is a " &
                  "modeled success");
...
            Check (US.To_String (Outcome.Result.Entity_Tag) =
                     """014842d480b571495a4a0363793f7367""",
                   Label & ": the ETag reaches the caller");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- scripted loopback origin on 127.0.0.1:50449
  -- --- absent x-amz-version-id (RustFS/MinIO/SeaweedFS/AWS shape) ---
  -- no-header: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : HeadObject response does not match prepared request
  FAILED: no-header: a 200 HeadObject without x-amz-version-id is a modeled success
  -- --- control: the origin echoes x-amz-version-id: null ---
  -- control: Execute_Head_Object Kind = OBJECT_FOUND, status 200
  -- control: ETag = "014842d480b571495a4a0363793f7367", Content-Length = 64, Version_ID = "null"
  ok: control: a 200 HeadObject without x-amz-version-id is a modeled success
  ok: control: decoded Version_ID is "null"
  ok: control: the ETag reaches the caller
  -- request 1: HEAD /never-versioned/native-object?versionId=null HTTP/1.1
  -- request 2: HEAD /never-versioned/native-object?versionId=null HTTP/1.1
  ok: the origin served both scripted exchanges
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The two legs differ by one header and nothing else, so status, ETag and `Content-Length` are not what fires, and with no `x-amz-request-charged` in either response the payer clause of the predicate is True in both. The version clause is the sole cause.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Response headers replayed from the pinned RustFS 1.0.0-rc.3 image; shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A successful HeadObject that omits `x-amz-version-id` must be accepted, with the decoded `Version_ID` empty, because the pinned botocore model marks `VersionId` optional on `HeadObjectOutput`, `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:1540` calls the field optional, and RustFS, MinIO and SeaweedFS all omit it for an object in a bucket that never had versioning enabled. A response that carries the header with a different value is still a mismatch and must still be refused.

## Suggested treatment

Relax the predicate from "the response must echo the requested version" to "the response must not contradict it", so that only a present and different value is refused:

```ada
        (Requested_Version'Length = 0
         or else US.Length (Value.Version_ID) = 0
         or else US.To_String (Value.Version_ID) = Requested_Version)
```

Apply the same change at every site that carries this predicate, so the layers cannot disagree: `Head_Object_Response_Bound` here at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5652-5654`, the GetObjectAttributes check at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5383-5397`, the tagging gate at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12473-12475`, and the two predicates one layer up, `Get_Object_Response_Bound` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:131-133` and `Head_Object_Response_Bound` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:149-151`. The last two are reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/46 and must change in the same commit, because the conformance corpus reaches this layer first and the composable layer immediately after.

This needs a maintainer decision on what the contract becomes, because the relaxation does not settle the residual mismatch:

- Relaxed predicate only. It accepts the never-versioned shape from all three reference servers. It still refuses the versioning-suspended sentinel `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` that RustFS returns for `?versionId=null`, which is a present and different value, so a strict-echo contract remains unsatisfiable across real servers even after the change and such a caller still gets an exception.
- Relaxed predicate plus a typed outcome for the residual mismatch. Today the mismatch escapes as `Invalid_Response` before any `Head_Object_Outcome` exists, so no caller can classify a per-server deviation and no oracle mode in the conformance corpus can express one. Turning it into a distinct outcome variant widens the public outcome set, which is an interface decision under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`.
- Keep the strict echo and document that a version-selected HeadObject does not work against servers that omit the header. That leaves the crate unable to head an object in a never-versioned bucket by `versionId=null` on any stock server.

The in-repo server's `x-amz-version-id: null` fallback, https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/22, should be dispositioned once the client stops requiring the header, since it is what hides the defect from the in-tree gate.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing low-level HeadObject cases: serve a 200 with ETag, `Content-Length`, `Last-Modified` and no `x-amz-version-id` to a `versionId=null` request, and require `Object_Found` with `Version_ID = ""` from `Execute_Head_Object`, leaving `scoped-head-version-mismatch`, `-control` and `-del` asserting rejection. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1144-1172` already fails against all three reference servers and passes once the predicate is fixed, so a fix must make `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh` get past this exchange rather than stop at it. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A version-selected HeadObject cannot complete against any conforming S3 server on the default bucket state. The caller gets `Invalid_Response` instead of a complete 200 whose ETag, size, checksum and metadata are all present and correct, and because the failure escapes as an exception rather than as an outcome variant, the caller cannot classify it or degrade gracefully. `versionId=null` is the documented way to address an object in a bucket where versioning was never enabled, so this is what an ordinary version-aware caller writes. The repository's own conformance corpus fails here against the pinned RustFS, MinIO and SeaweedFS images, seven of seven RustFS runs with the same message at the same exchange, and the only server it passes against is the in-repo one, whose fallback supplies the header the client demands. The predicate is a pure function of two strings, so no timing, load or container state is involved. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Complete_Response` requires a successful GetObjectAttributes response to echo the requested `versionId`. An absent `x-amz-version-id` header decodes to the empty string, so a request that selects `versionId=null` against an object in a bucket that never had versioning enabled turns a valid 200, carrying the requested ETag and object size, into `Invalid_Response`. The pinned botocore model marks the member optional, and no server satisfies the demand: the repository's own in-process S3 application omits the header on this route, so the operation fails against the crate's own server as well as against third-party ones.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The decoder binds the header and then requires it to equal the retained selector (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5371-5372` and `:5383-5397`):

```ada
         Version_ID : constant String :=
           Singleton_Header ("x-amz-version-id");
```

```ada
         if Outcome.Kind = Object_Attributes_Found
           and then
             ((Request_Charged'Length > 0
               and then US.To_String
                 (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Request_Payer) /=
                   "requester")
              or else
                (US.Length
                   (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Version_ID) > 0
                 and then Version_ID /= US.To_String
                   (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Version_ID)))
         then
            raise Invalid_Response with
              "GetObjectAttributes response does not match request";
         end if;
```

An absent header is the empty string, because `Singleton_Header` returns `""` for a header count of zero (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5349-5350`):

```ada
         elsif Count = 0 then
            return "";
```

so `"" /= "null"` trips the check. The exception escapes `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes`, which has no other outcome for it (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5508-5510`):

```ada
         return Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Complete_Response
           (Response, Flyology.Bytes.To_Byte_String (Payload), Prepared,
            Limits);
```

The same decoder is reached from the composable `Client.Objects` GetObjectAttributes operation, so both entry points are affected.

The model does not require the header. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`:

```python
sh = model['shapes']['GetObjectAttributesOutput']
print('GetObjectAttributesOutput required:', sh.get('required'))
v = sh['members']['VersionId']
print(' ', {k: v[k] for k in ('shape', 'location', 'locationName')})
```

```
GetObjectAttributesOutput required: None
  {'shape': 'ObjectVersionId', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-version-id'}
```

The crate's own specification calls the field optional (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:2677`):

```ada
   --  @field Version_ID Optional selected-version response header
```

The repository's own server cannot satisfy the demand. Its GetObjectAttributes handler emits the header only when the stored version is nonempty, with no fallback (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16347-16352`):

```ada
                        HTTP_Last_Modified (Snapshot.Info.Modified));
                     if US.Length (Snapshot.Info.Version) > 0 then
                        Apps.Set_Header
                          (X, "x-amz-version-id",
                           US.To_String (Snapshot.Info.Version));
                     end if;
```

That is the difference from the read routes, where a `null` fallback exists (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:4189-4194` for GetObject and `:16619-16622` for HeadObject) and hides the same client demand, reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/39. On this route nothing hides it.

A maintained qualification document states the requirement that no server satisfies, so it is part of the defect rather than a defence of it (`docs/qualification/get-object-attributes.md:41-43`):

```
Unavailable optional attributes are omitted rather than invented. An explicit
nonempty `VersionId` must be echoed exactly; omission observes the generation
current at read time.
```

No test catches this, and no assertion has to be inverted, because no maintained test reaches the branch. `Require_Get_Object_Attributes` in the conformance corpus (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1675-1861`) never sets `Parameters.Version_ID`, so the real-server run never sends a selector here. The socket corpus does send one, but its fixture echoes it (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15918-15919`):

```ada
            Parameters.Version_ID :=
              US.To_Unbounded_String ("socket version");
```

so the absent-header case is unexercised rather than pinned.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below publishes one object into a bucket that never had versioning enabled through the in-repo S3 application, shows with a raw signed request that the server answers `GET /bucket/key?attributes&versionId=null` with a 200 carrying zero `x-amz-version-id` headers, then serves the same application on a loopback listener and drives `Prepare_Get_Object_Attributes` and `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes` against it, once with `Version_ID = "null"` and once with no selector.

```ada
      Parameters.Version_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String (Selector);
      Parameters.Attributes.Entity_Tag := True;
      Parameters.Attributes.Object_Size := True;
      declare
         Prepared : constant Low_Level.Prepared_Request :=
           Low_Level.Prepare_Get_Object_Attributes
             (Origin, Low_Level.Path_Style, Bucket, Key, Parameters,
              Identity, Server.Region, Server.Timestamp);
         Outcome : constant Low_Level.Get_Object_Attributes_Outcome :=
           Low_Level.Execute_Get_Object_Attributes
             (HTTP, Prepared, Timeout => 10.0);
      begin
...
         Check (Outcome.Kind = Low_Level.Object_Attributes_Found,
                Label & ": a 200 GetObjectAttributes without " &
                  "x-amz-version-id is a modeled success");
...
            Check (Outcome.Result.Attributes.Object_Size.Is_Set
                     and then Outcome.Result.Attributes.Object_Size.Value =
                       Body_Text'Length,
                   Label & ": ObjectSize is" & Body_Text'Length'Image);
```

The raw leg that establishes what the server sends:

```ada
      Raw : constant String := Server.Run
        (Server.Signed_Request
           ("GET", "/" & Bucket & "/" & Key,
            Extra_Headers => "x-amz-object-attributes: ETag,ObjectSize" & CRLF,
            Query => [Server.SigV4.Pair ("attributes", ""),
                      Server.SigV4.Pair ("versionId", "null")]));
...
      Check (Header_Count (Raw, "x-amz-version-id") = 0,
             "setup: that 200 carries no x-amz-version-id");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- in-process CreateBucket status 200
  ok: setup: CreateBucket is 200
  -- in-process PutObject status 200, x-amz-version-id = ""
  ok: setup: PutObject is 200
  ok: setup: the never-versioned publication returns no version id
  -- raw GET ?attributes&versionId=null -> status 200, x-amz-version-id count 0
  ok: setup: the in-repo server answers the versioned attributes request with 200
  ok: setup: that 200 carries no x-amz-version-id
  -- loopback S3 application on 127.0.0.1:50470
  -- --- explicit versionId=null against a never-versioned bucket ---
  -- versioned: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : GetObjectAttributes response does not match request
  FAILED: versioned: a 200 GetObjectAttributes without x-amz-version-id is a modeled success
  -- --- control: no version selector, same object ---
  -- control: Execute_Get_Object_Attributes Kind = OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND, status 200
  -- control: ETag = 6b344779a83cd6a352fbfcec796f62f2, ObjectSize set = TRUE, Version_ID = ""
  ok: control: a 200 GetObjectAttributes without x-amz-version-id is a modeled success
  ok: control: the requested ETag attribute reaches the caller
  ok: control: ObjectSize is 16
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The control leg is the same object, the same server and the same decoder with the selector removed, and it returns the ETag and an object size of 16. Adding `versionId=null` to the request is the only difference, and it destroys a response the server produced correctly. No third-party server and no container are involved.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A successful GetObjectAttributes that omits `x-amz-version-id` must be accepted, with the decoded `Version_ID` empty, because the pinned botocore model marks `VersionId` optional on `GetObjectAttributesOutput` and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:2677` calls the field optional. A response that carries the header with a different value is still a mismatch and must still be refused.

## Suggested treatment

Relax the version clause of the check at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5390-5393` so that only a present and different value is refused:

```ada
                (US.Length
                   (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Version_ID) > 0
                 and then Version_ID'Length > 0
                 and then Version_ID /= US.To_String
                   (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Version_ID))
```

The same relaxation belongs at the other three sites that carry this predicate, reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/39, so the four do not diverge. This needs the same maintainer decision recorded there: whether the contract becomes "the response must not contradict the selector" or whether the residual mismatch becomes a typed outcome instead of an exception, which widens the public outcome set and falls under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`.

`docs/qualification/get-object-attributes.md:42` must be reworded either way, because "An explicit nonempty `VersionId` must be echoed exactly" is the requirement being removed. The in-repo server's own asymmetry is worth settling in the same pass: its GetObjectAttributes handler omits the header where its GetObject and HeadObject handlers manufacture `null`.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, which already drives the in-process S3 application over signed requests, as a client-against-server round trip: publish an object into a never-versioned bucket, then require `Object_Attributes_Found` with `Version_ID = ""` and the requested attributes present from `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes` with `Version_ID = "null"`. A socket-corpus case with a present and different value should stay a rejection. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A GetObjectAttributes request that names a version cannot complete against an object in a bucket that never had versioning enabled, which is the default bucket state, and `versionId=null` is the documented way to address such an object. The caller gets `Invalid_Response` instead of a 200 whose ETag, size, checksums and parts page are all correct, and the exception escapes before any outcome value exists, so the caller cannot classify it. Unlike the sibling defects on the read routes, this one is not masked by the repository's own server: the failure above is against the in-repo S3 application, with no container and no third-party server, so a user of both halves of this crate hits it. It is reachable from `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes` and from the composable `Client.Objects` operation, which calls the same decoder. Deterministic.

## Summary

The nested `Response_Version_ID` inside `Execute_Object_Tagging` raises `Invalid_Response` whenever a successful PutObjectTagging, GetObjectTagging or DeleteObjectTagging response carries anything other than exactly one `x-amz-version-id` header. The demand never consults the request, so it fires on ordinary tagging calls that name no version at all, and a bucket that never had versioning enabled has no version to report. The pinned botocore model marks the member optional on all three output shapes, and the crate's own specification comment says an unrequested version is an observation rather than proof. For PutObjectTagging and DeleteObjectTagging the mutation has already been applied when the exception is raised, and the message says "multiplicity" although the count is zero.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The gate conflates header multiplicity with header presence (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12465-12492`):

```ada
         function Response_Version_ID
           (Response : Flyology.HTTP.Client.Response;
            Success  : Boolean) return String
         is
            Count : constant Natural :=
              Flyology.HTTP.Client.Header_Count
                (Response, "x-amz-version-id");
         begin
            if Count > 1 or else (Success and then Count /= 1) then
               raise Invalid_Response with
                 "invalid object tagging version header multiplicity";
            elsif Count = 0 then
               return "";
            end if;
            declare
               Value : constant String := Flyology.HTTP.Client.Header
                 (Response, "x-amz-version-id");
            begin
               if Value'Length = 0
                 or else not Valid_List_Response_Header_Text (Value)
                 or else not S3.Deletions.Valid_Version_ID (Value)
               then
                  raise Invalid_Response with
                    "invalid object tagging version header value";
               end if;
               return Value;
            end;
         end Response_Version_ID;
```

`Count > 1` is a genuine protocol violation. `Success and then Count /= 1` is not, because `Success` is only "the status matched the modeled response code" (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12528-12529`):

```ada
         Version_ID : constant String := Response_Version_ID
           (Response, Status = Model.Response_Code (Expected));
```

so the demand fires on every success of all three operations. The check that actually binds an explicit selector is separate, and it is correct (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12533-12539`):

```ada
         if Status = Model.Response_Code (Expected)
           and then Requested_Version_ID'Length > 0
           and then Version_ID /= Requested_Version_ID
         then
            raise Invalid_Response with
              "object tagging response does not match prepared request";
         end if;
```

The model makes the header optional in all three output shapes and fixes the success codes. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`:

```python
for op in ['PutObjectTagging', 'GetObjectTagging', 'DeleteObjectTagging']:
    o = model['operations'][op]
    out = model['shapes'][o['output']['shape']]
    v = out['members']['VersionId']
    print(op, 'responseCode:', o['http'].get('responseCode', 200))
    print(' ', o['output']['shape'], 'required:', out.get('required'))
    print(' ', {k: v[k] for k in ('shape', 'location', 'locationName')})
```

```
PutObjectTagging responseCode: 200
  PutObjectTaggingOutput required: None
  {'shape': 'ObjectVersionId', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-version-id'}
GetObjectTagging responseCode: 200
  GetObjectTaggingOutput required: ['TagSet']
  {'shape': 'ObjectVersionId', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-version-id'}
DeleteObjectTagging responseCode: 204
  DeleteObjectTaggingOutput required: None
  {'shape': 'ObjectVersionId', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-version-id'}
```

The crate's own specification says the opposite of the code (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:10540-10542`):

```ada
      --  A successful explicit-version tagging response must echo this exact
      --  selector. Empty means the request selected the current version, so
      --  any returned version remains an observation rather than proof.
```

Empty is exactly the case the gate refuses. The compatibility document does not require the header either; it says only that the tagging clients "validate bounded responses and version IDs" (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:366-372`).

A real server omits it. RustFS 1.0.0-rc.3, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh:5`, answered the repository's own `PUT /bucket/native-object?tagging` with

```
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
x-request-id: 2e90fdc8-9408-47de-b681-bb06d8dc699e
x-amz-request-id: 2e90fdc8-9408-47de-b681-bb06d8dc699e
content-length: 0
```

and nothing else. The identical predicate one layer up, `Tagging_Version_ID` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:7177-7211`, is what raised on that response, since the conformance corpus reaches tagging through `Client.Objects.Put_Tags`; it is reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/47 and the two are one defect at two layers.

The in-repo server is why CI never sees it. Its tagging branch always sets the header, defaulting to the literal `null` (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15846-15854`):

```ada
                  procedure Set_Version_Header is
                  begin
                     Apps.Set_Header
                       (X, "x-amz-version-id",
                        (if not Identity.Has_Version_ID
                           or else Identity.Is_Null_Version
                         then "null"
                         else US.To_String (Identity.Version_ID)));
                  end Set_Version_Header;
```

An existing test asserts the defective behaviour and must be inverted with the fix. `Require_Invalid_Version` (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15091-15253`) leaves the selector empty for every kind except `Mismatch` (`:15095-15099`):

```ada
                  Key : constant String :=
                    "tag-" & Name (Kind) & "-" & Suffix (Invalid);
                  Selected : constant String :=
                    (if Invalid = Mismatch
                     then "expected-" & Name (Kind) else "");
```

so the `Missing` case pins exactly the no-selector case. It records a pass only when the synchronous call raises (`:15166-15167`):

```ada
                        when Low_Level.Invalid_Response =>
                           Rejected := True;
```

and fails the corpus otherwise (`:15249-15253`):

```ada
                  if not Rejected then
                     raise Program_Error with
                       Name (Kind) & " object tagging accepted " &
                       Suffix (Invalid) & " version response";
                  end if;
```

It is driven for every operation and every kind, `Missing` included (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15256-15260`). The fixtures it consumes are the never-versioned shapes: `tag-put-missing` answers 200 with no version header (`:4331-4334`), `tag-get-missing` answers 200 with a `<Tagging>` body (`:4360-4362`), and `tag-delete-missing` answers 204 (`:4385-4388`). That assertion contradicts `Require_Object_Tagging` in the conformance corpus (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1617-1673`), which expects the same shapes from real servers to succeed.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below scripts six exchanges on a loopback origin. Three answer `PUT`, `GET` and `DELETE` on `/never-versioned/native-object?tagging` with the RustFS shapes quoted above and no `x-amz-version-id`; three are identical with `x-amz-version-id: opaque-version-1` added. None of the six prepared requests carries a `versionId` selector.

```ada
      declare
         Prepared : constant Low_Level.Prepared_Request :=
           Low_Level.Prepare_Put_Object_Tagging
             (Origin, Low_Level.Path_Style, Bucket, Key, Tags, Parameters,
              Identity, "us-east-1", Timestamp);
         Outcome : constant Low_Level.Object_Tagging_Outcome :=
           Low_Level.Execute_Put_Object_Tagging (HTTP, Prepared, 10.0);
      begin
         Report (Label, Outcome, Low_Level.Tags_Put, Expect_Version);
...
      Check (Outcome.Kind = Wanted,
             Label & ": a successful tagging response without " &
               "x-amz-version-id is " &
               Low_Level.Object_Tagging_Outcome_Kind'Image (Wanted));
...
            Check (Outcome.Result.Tags.Length = 1
                     and then US.To_String (Outcome.Result.Tags.Items (1).Key)
                       = "project",
                   Label & ": the decoded tag set reaches the caller");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- request 2: GET /never-versioned/native-object?tagging HTTP/1.1
  -- request 3: DELETE /never-versioned/native-object?tagging HTTP/1.1
  -- request 4: PUT /never-versioned/native-object?tagging HTTP/1.1
```

All six requests are byte-identical between the failing and the control legs; the responses differ only by the added header. The control GET shows that the tag set decodes correctly once the header is present, so the body handling is not what fires.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Response shapes replayed from the pinned RustFS 1.0.0-rc.3 image; shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A successful tagging response that carries no `x-amz-version-id` must be accepted, with the decoded `Version_ID` empty, because the pinned botocore model marks `VersionId` optional on `PutObjectTaggingOutput`, `GetObjectTaggingOutput` and `DeleteObjectTaggingOutput`, and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:10540-10542` says an unrequested version is an observation rather than proof. Two or more such headers remain an invalid response, and a value different from an explicitly requested selector remains a mismatch, which the separate check at `:12533-12539` already catches.

## Suggested treatment

Split the two rules at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12473`: keep `Count > 1` as `Invalid_Response`, and drop the `Success and then Count /= 1` clause so that a zero count returns the empty string on the existing `elsif Count = 0` path. Nothing else in the subprogram has to change, and the message stops claiming a multiplicity that did not occur. The identical gate one layer up at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:7189`, reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/47, must change in the same commit, and the same relaxation is due at the two version-echo predicates reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/39.

This needs a maintainer decision on how an absent version is represented, because the two states are otherwise indistinguishable to a caller:

- Leave `Version_ID` a plain `Unbounded_String` that is empty when the server omitted the header. This is the shape `Head_Object_Result` and `Get_Object_Attributes_Result` already use, and it adds no public entity. A caller cannot then tell "no version reported" from "version reported as the empty string", which no server sends.
- Give the tagging result an explicit optional, so the two states are distinct. That widens the public result types and falls under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`: invert the `Missing` variant driven by `Require_Invalid_Version` at `:15256-15260` into a success assertion, requiring `Tags_Put`, `Tags_Gotten` and `Tags_Deleted` with `Version_ID = ""` and the served tag set on the get, while `Duplicate`, `Mismatch`, `Control` and `Delete_Control` keep asserting rejection. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1617-1673` already expects those shapes to succeed against real servers, so the two files stop contradicting each other. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Every PutObjectTagging, GetObjectTagging and DeleteObjectTagging against an object in a bucket that never had versioning enabled fails, and no caller input avoids it, because the demand does not consult the request. That makes it the broadest of this family: the plain `Put_Tags`, `Get_Tags` and `Delete_Tags` paths are affected on the default bucket state of every stock server. For the two mutations the server has already applied the change when the exception is raised, so the caller is left unable to distinguish an applied mutation from a corrupt response, and the diagnostic it does get names a header multiplicity that is zero. The in-repo server hides it by always sending the header, so the in-tree gate is green while real servers are not. Deterministic.

## Summary

`S3.Errors.Parse` raises `Malformed_Error` for any non-2xx body that is absent, whitespace, HTML or otherwise not an S3 `<Error>` document, and the response decoders in the low-level client convert that into `Invalid_Response`, discarding the HTTP status. A reverse proxy answering 503 with an HTML page, or a peer answering 403 with no body, therefore reaches a synchronous caller as a protocol failure with no status at all, and a composable caller as `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` where `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` is the correct classification, so a transient outage is not retried. The repository has already made the opposite decision twice, unconditionally for HeadObject and with an explanatory comment for GetObject, so this is an internal inconsistency and every affected outcome type already carries the variant the fix needs.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

Error parsing raises on an empty or non-`<Error>` document (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-errors.adb:116-127`):

```ada
      XML.Parse (Document, Handler, Limits);
      if not Handler.Seen_Code
        or else not Handler.Seen_Message
        or else US.Length (Handler.Value.Code) = 0
        or else US.Length (Handler.Value.Message) = 0
      then
         raise Malformed_Error with "S3 error lacks Code or Message";
      end if;
      return Handler.Value;
   exception
      when XML.XML_Error =>
         raise Malformed_Error with "malformed S3 error XML";
```

The decoders convert that into a client protocol failure. ListObjectsV2 is representative (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1957-1959`):

```ada
   exception
      when S3.Listings.Malformed_Listing | S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed ListObjectsV2 response";
```

The same handler shape is repeated at more than sixty places in that one file. The operation decoders carrying it include ListObjects `:1694`, ListObjectsV2 `:1958`, ListObjectVersions `:2228`, CreateMultipartUpload `:3340`, CompleteMultipartUpload `:3884`, CreateBucket `:4369`, GetObjectAttributes `:5331`, PutObject `:7733`, bucket-configuration deletions `:8240`, bucket-control mutations `:10849`, DeleteObject `:11299`, object tagging `:12412`, DeleteObjects `:12732`, AbortMultipartUpload `:12925`, ListParts `:13193`, ListMultipartUploads `:13427`, UploadPart `:14044`, UploadPartCopy `:14426` and CopyObject `:14779`, plus every bucket-control GET between `:8875` and `:9784`.

The status the caller loses is available at the point of the conversion. The same decoder returns it on the path where the body does parse (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1954`):

```ada
            return (Kind => Rejected, Status => Status, Error => Value);
```

so every affected outcome type already has a `Rejected` variant carrying the wire status and no new public type is needed.

GetObject deliberately does the opposite, and says why (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:7148-7165`):

```ada
            begin
               return
                 (Kind   => Get_Object_Rejected,
                  Status => Status,
                  Error  => Error_Response
                    (Text, Request_ID, Host_ID, Limits));
            exception
               when S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
                  --  S3 peers and intermediaries may return bodyless or
                  --  non-XML HTTP errors.  Preserve the typed rejection and
                  --  wire status instead of turning a valid rejection into
                  --  a client protocol failure.
                  return
                    (Kind   => Get_Object_Rejected,
                     Status => Status,
                     Error  => Head_Error
                       ("GetObject", Status, Request_ID, Host_ID));
            end;
```

`Head_Error` is the shape those tolerant paths return (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4865-4871`):

```ada
      return
         (Code       => US.To_Unbounded_String ("HTTP" & Status_Text),
         Message    => US.To_Unbounded_String
           (Operation & " returned HTTP status " & Status_Text),
         Resource   => US.Null_Unbounded_String,
         Request_ID => US.To_Unbounded_String (Request_ID),
         Host_ID    => US.To_Unbounded_String (Host_ID));
```

and HeadObject uses it for every non-2xx without consulting the body at all (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5673-5678`):

```ada
      else
         return
           (Kind   => Head_Object_Rejected,
            Status => Status,
            Error  => Head_Error
              ("HeadObject", Status, Request_ID, Host_ID));
```

In the composable families the exception becomes a corruption verdict (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-bounded_rest_xml_reads.adb:105-111`):

```ada
         exception
            when Low.Invalid_Response =>
               Item.Final_Result :=
                 Normalize_Failure
                   (HTTP_Client.Response_Invalid,
                    HTTP_Client.Certainty (HTTP_Result),
                    HTTP_Client.Phase (HTTP_Result), "");
```

which surfaces as `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`, although the failure set has a separate value for exactly this case (`src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:35` and `:50`):

```ada
   --  @enum Unavailable_Or_Retryable Modeled transient service response
```

No existing test asserts either behaviour, so nothing has to be inverted. Every 4xx and 5xx fixture in the socket corpus carries a well-formed `<Error>` body, for example a 503 at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:3258-3264` and a 500 at `:5591-5597`:

```ada
         Serve
           (HTTP_Response
              ("503 Service Unavailable",
               "<Error><Code>SlowDown</Code>" &
                 "<Message>retry later</Message></Error>"),
```

and the only bodyless or non-XML error fixtures are the GetObject ones served to the already tolerant path.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below scripts four exchanges on a loopback origin. A signed `ListObjectsV2` is answered `503 Service Unavailable` with `content-type: text/html` and an HTML body, and a signed `DeleteObject` is answered `403 Forbidden` with `content-length: 0`. Both assert the typed rejection with the wire status and the `HTTP<status>` code the tolerant paths already synthesise. Two control legs follow: a bodyless 503 on `HeadObject`, and a 503 carrying a well-formed `<Error>` document on `ListObjectsV2`.

```ada
   Proxy_503 : constant String :=
     "HTTP/1.1 503 Service Unavailable" & CRLF &
     "content-type: text/html" & CRLF &
     "content-length:" & HTML'Length'Image & CRLF &
     "Connection: close" & CRLF & CRLF & HTML;

   Empty_403 : constant String :=
     "HTTP/1.1 403 Forbidden" & CRLF &
     "content-length: 0" & CRLF &
     "Connection: close" & CRLF & CRLF;
...
         Outcome : constant Low_Level.List_Objects_V2_Outcome :=
           Low_Level.Execute_List_Objects_V2 (HTTP, Prepared, 10.0);
      begin
...
         Check (Outcome.Kind = Low_Level.Rejected,
                Label & ": a non-XML 5xx is a typed rejection carrying the " &
                  "wire status");
...
            Check (Outcome.Status = 503, Label & ": status is 503");
            Check (US.To_String (Outcome.Error.Code) = Expect_Code,
                   Label & ": Error.Code is """ & Expect_Code & """");
...
         Outcome : constant Low_Level.Delete_Object_Outcome :=
           Low_Level.Execute_Delete_Object (HTTP, Prepared, 10.0);
      begin
...
         Check (Outcome.Kind = Low_Level.Delete_Object_Rejected,
                Label & ": a bodyless 4xx is a typed rejection carrying the " &
                  "wire status");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- scripted loopback origin on 127.0.0.1:50510
  -- every *_Outcome type already carries a Rejected variant with the wire Status; Head_Error (low_level.adb:4856-4872) synthesises Code = "HTTP<status>" for exactly this case
  -- --- 503 Service Unavailable with a text/html body ---
  -- html 503 ListObjectsV2: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : malformed ListObjectsV2 response
  FAILED: html 503 ListObjectsV2: a non-XML 5xx is a typed rejection carrying the wire status
  -- --- 403 Forbidden with Content-Length: 0 ---
  -- empty 403 DeleteObject: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : malformed DeleteObject response
  FAILED: empty 403 DeleteObject: a bodyless 4xx is a typed rejection carrying the wire status
  -- --- control: HeadObject, the route that already tolerates it ---
  -- control HEAD 503: Kind = HEAD_OBJECT_REJECTED, status 503
  ok: control HEAD 503: HEAD already maps a bodyless 5xx to the typed rejection
  -- control HEAD 503: Error.Code = HTTP503, Message = HeadObject returned HTTP status 503
  ok: control HEAD 503: Error.Code is "HTTP503"
  -- --- control: 503 carrying a well-formed <Error> document ---
  -- modeled 503 ListObjectsV2: Kind = REJECTED, status 503
  ok: modeled 503 ListObjectsV2: a non-XML 5xx is a typed rejection carrying the wire status
  -- modeled 503 ListObjectsV2: Error.Code = SlowDown, Message = Please reduce your request rate
  ok: modeled 503 ListObjectsV2: status is 503
  ok: modeled 503 ListObjectsV2: Error.Code is "SlowDown"
  -- request 1: GET /example-bucket?list-type=2&max-keys=1000 HTTP/1.1
  -- request 2: DELETE /example-bucket/native-object HTTP/1.1
  -- request 3: HEAD /example-bucket/native-object HTTP/1.1
  -- request 4: GET /example-bucket?list-type=2&max-keys=1000 HTTP/1.1
  ok: the origin served all four scripted exchanges
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The two control legs show both existing tolerant behaviours working: the same bodyless 503 that destroys the listing yields `Head_Object_Rejected` with `Error.Code = HTTP503` on the HEAD route, and a 503 that does carry an `<Error>` document yields `Rejected` with status 503 and `Error.Code = SlowDown` on the listing route. Only the non-XML and bodyless cases on the listing and delete routes fail.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A non-2xx response whose body is not an S3 `<Error>` document is a valid rejection whose HTTP status the caller needs, not a violation of a modeled contract. It should be reported through the `Rejected` variant of the operation's outcome, carrying the wire status and the synthesised `HTTP<status>` code, which is what `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:7148-7165` states for GetObject and what HeadObject does unconditionally at `:5673-5678`. `Invalid_Response` is specified for a response that "violates its modeled contract" (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:300-301`), which a proxy-generated 503 does not.

## Suggested treatment

Mirror the GetObject handler at each decoder that maps `S3.Errors.Malformed_Error` to `Invalid_Response`: on a non-2xx status, catch `Malformed_Error` and return the operation's `Rejected` variant with the wire status and `Head_Error (<operation>, Status, Request_ID, Host_ID)`, leaving the success-path parse failures raising as they do now. The change is mechanical and needs no new public entity, since every affected outcome already has that variant.

This needs a maintainer decision on one point, because it fixes a documented classification rather than an internal ordering: whether the synthesised `Code = "HTTP<status>"` convention that HeadObject and GetObject already use becomes the documented contract for the rest of the operations, so that callers may match on it. The alternative is a distinct error code or an empty code for a peer-generated rejection, which changes what those callers see.

The composable side follows from the same change: once the decoders return a rejection rather than raising, `src/flyology-object_storage-client-bounded_rest_xml_reads.adb:105-111` is no longer reached for these responses, and a transient 5xx can be classified `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` instead of `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing error fixtures, which today all carry a well-formed `<Error>` body: serve a 503 with an HTML body to `Execute_List_Objects_V2` and a bodyless 403 to `Execute_Delete_Object`, and require `Rejected` and `Delete_Object_Rejected` with the wire status and `Error.Code = "HTTP503"` and `"HTTP403"`. No existing assertion has to change. The spike is the test.

## Severity

The caller loses the HTTP status of a rejection it needs to act on, and gets a diagnostic that blames the response format. In the composable families the misclassification has a behavioural consequence: a transient 503 from a load balancer is reported as a corrupt response rather than as retryable, so a caller that retries on `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` will not retry. The trigger is a response produced by something other than a conforming S3 origin, such as a reverse proxy, load balancer or CDN answering on its behalf, or a peer answering 4xx with no body; a conforming origin always emits an `<Error>` document, so this is not on the ordinary path. The client fails closed rather than accepting something wrong, and no data is lost. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Create_Session_Parameters` is documented as preserving absent versus explicit `false` for `Bucket_Key_Enabled`, and `Prepare_Create_Session` raises `Invalid_Request` on exactly the explicit `false` that promise describes, with a message that does not name the member. The serialiser that would have emitted the value hard-codes the string `"true"` and never reads the caller's boolean, so if the policy check were relaxed the request would carry `true` for a caller who asked for `false`. The specification and the code cannot both be right, and the `"true"` literal is wrong under either answer.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The published contract promises presence preservation (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:6402-6409`):

```ada
   --  Every non-resource member in the pinned CreateSession input. Empty
   --  strings preserve omission; Bucket_Key_Enabled preserves absent versus
   --  explicit false.
   --  @field Session_Mode Optional modeled session mode
   --  @field Server_Side_Encryption Optional encryption algorithm
   --  @field SSE_KMS_Key_ID Optional KMS key identifier
   --  @field SSE_KMS_Encryption_Context Optional canonical Base64 KMS context
   --  @field Bucket_Key_Enabled Presence-preserving bucket-key request flag
```

The policy validator refuses that value (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2430-2434`):

```ada
        or else (Bucket_Key_Enabled.Is_Set
                 and then not Bucket_Key_Enabled.Value)
      then
         raise Invalid_Response with Failure;
      end if;
```

and `Prepare_Create_Session` remaps that raise to an `Invalid_Request` whose message names no member (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2474-2484`):

```ada
      begin
         Validate_Create_Session_Policy
           (Parameters.Server_Side_Encryption,
            Parameters.SSE_KMS_Key_ID,
            Parameters.SSE_KMS_Encryption_Context,
            Parameters.Bucket_Key_Enabled,
            "invalid CreateSession request policy");
      exception
         when Invalid_Response =>
            raise Invalid_Request with "invalid CreateSession parameters";
      end;
```

The serialiser that runs when the flag is set ignores the caller's boolean entirely (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2496-2502`):

```ada
      if Parameters.Bucket_Key_Enabled.Is_Set then
         Last := Last + 1;
         Values (Last) :=
           (Member_Name => US.To_Unbounded_String ("BucketKeyEnabled"),
            Map_Key => US.Null_Unbounded_String,
            Value => US.To_Unbounded_String ("true"));
      end if;
```

so the only reason a `false` never reaches the wire as `true` today is that the check at `:2430` fires first. Nothing in the file ties the two together.

The pinned model admits `false`, and its own documentation explains why a server might not. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`, with HTML stripped from the documentation string:

```python
sh = model['shapes']['CreateSessionRequest']
print('CreateSessionRequest required:', sh.get('required'))
m = sh['members']['BucketKeyEnabled']
print(' BucketKeyEnabled:', {k: m[k] for k in ('shape', 'location', 'locationName')})
print(' shape kind:', model['shapes'][m['shape']]['type'])
```

```
CreateSessionRequest required: ['Bucket']
 BucketKeyEnabled: {'shape': 'BucketKeyEnabled', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-server-side-encryption-bucket-key-enabled'}
 shape kind: boolean
```

with the member's documentation beginning

```
Specifies whether Amazon S3 should use an S3 Bucket Key for object encryption with server-side encryption using KMS keys (SSE-KMS). S3 Bucket Keys are always enabled for GET and PUT operations in a directory bucket and can’t be disabled.
```

So refusing `false` may well be the right wire behaviour for a directory bucket. That does not remove the defect, it relocates it: either the specification at `:6402-6404` is wrong to promise that explicit `false` is preserved, or the check at `:2430-2431` is wrong to refuse it. The two cannot both stand, and the `"true"` literal at `:2501` is a latent value inversion under either answer.

One existing test pins the current rejection, so it is part of the decision rather than a safety net. `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:14311-14316` builds an SSE-KMS parameter set and requires preparation to raise:

```ada
         Parameters.SSE_KMS_Encryption_Context := US.Null_Unbounded_String;
         Parameters.Bucket_Key_Enabled :=
           (Is_Set => True, Value => False);
         Reject_Prepare
           (Parameters,
            Message => "CreateSession accepted disabled bucket key");
```

where `Reject_Prepare` (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:14173-14198`) asserts that `Prepare_Create_Session` raises `Invalid_Request`. Nothing tests what the specification promises, and nothing tests the `"true"` literal against a `False` value, because that path is unreachable while the check stands.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below prepares a CreateSession request against an HTTPS virtual-hosted S3 Express origin with `SessionMode = ReadWrite`, `ServerSideEncryption = aws:kms` and a KMS key ARN, three times: with `Bucket_Key_Enabled = (Is_Set => True, Value => False)`, with the flag absent, and with an explicit `True`. It asserts that preparation succeeds and that the canonical request carries the header with the requested value. No server is involved.

```ada
      Parameters.Session_Mode := US.To_Unbounded_String ("ReadWrite");
      Parameters.Server_Side_Encryption := US.To_Unbounded_String ("aws:kms");
      Parameters.SSE_KMS_Key_ID := US.To_Unbounded_String
        ("arn:aws:kms:us-east-1:111122223333:key/" &
         "1234abcd-12ab-34cd-56ef-1234567890ab");
      Parameters.Bucket_Key_Enabled := Flag;
      declare
         Prepared : constant Low_Level.Prepared_Request :=
           Low_Level.Prepare_Create_Session
             (Origin, Low_Level.Virtual_Hosted_Style, Bucket, Parameters,
              Identity, "us-east-1", "20260907T012831Z");
         Canonical : constant String := Low_Level.Canonical_Request (Prepared);
         Wanted    : constant String := Header & ":" & Expect_Wire;
      begin
...
         Check (True, Label & ": Prepare_Create_Session accepts the " &
                  "presence-preserving flag");
...
            Check (Ada.Strings.Fixed.Index (Canonical, Wanted) /= 0,
                   Label & ": the canonical request carries """ & Wanted &
                     """");
...
   Run ("explicit false", (Is_Set => True, Value => False), "false");
   Run ("absent", (Is_Set => False, Value => False), "");
   Run ("explicit true", (Is_Set => True, Value => True), "true");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- spec: low_level.ads:6402-6404 -- "Bucket_Key_Enabled preserves absent versus explicit false"
  -- --- explicit false, exactly what the spec says is preserved ---
  -- explicit false: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_REQUEST : invalid CreateSession parameters
  FAILED: explicit false: Prepare_Create_Session accepts the presence-preserving flag
  FAILED: explicit false: the canonical request carries "x-amz-server-side-encryption-bucket-key-enabled:false"
  -- --- control: flag absent ---

  -- absent: signed headers = host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-create-session-mode;x-amz-date;x-amz-server-side-encryption;x-amz-server-side-encryption-aws-kms-key-id
```
