
Each control leg differs from its failing leg by that one header and nothing else, so status, body and tag decoding are not what fires. Note the convenience leg: the `Tagging_Outcome` overload does not soften the result, it re-raises `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` at the caller.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Response headers replayed from the pinned RustFS 1.0.0-rc.3 image; shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A successful tagging response that carries no `x-amz-version-id` must be accepted, with the decoded version empty. The pinned botocore model marks `VersionId` optional on `PutObjectTaggingOutput`, `GetObjectTaggingOutput` and `DeleteObjectTaggingOutput`, `docs/architecture/client-objects.md:100-101` calls it "the optional response version ID", and rows `:3`, `:5` and `:7` of `tests/corpora/composable-client/object-tagging-certainty.tsv` require a `200` or `204` with no S3 code and no requested version to be `Object_Tag_Mutation_Completed` with `No_Failure`. A duplicated header remains an invalid response, and a present value different from an explicitly requested one remains a mismatch.

## Suggested treatment

The smallest fix drops the presence half of the multiplicity gate at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:7189`, so that only a duplicate is rejected:

```ada
      if Count > 1 then
```

The `Count = 0` branch immediately below already returns `""`, and the low-level decoder already accepts an empty version at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12379-12383`, so nothing further downstream needs to change. Apply the same change to the low-level twin reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/41.

How an absent header is then reported needs a maintainer decision:

- Report it as `Version_ID = ""`, matching the low-level decoder and the existing "optional" wording in `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:6191`. No public type changes. A caller cannot distinguish "the server reported no version" from "the field was not populated", which is the situation the crate is already in for reads.
- Add an explicit "no version reported" discriminant or flag to the three tagging result records. This distinguishes the two states, and it is a public API change that widens the result types, so it falls under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`.

Either way `docs/qualification/object-tagging.md:72-74` ("successful responses preserve the selected `x-amz-version-id` from that same exchange") should be reconciled with `docs/architecture/client-objects.md:100-101`, because the tests currently read the qualification sentence as "the header must be present".

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`: invert the `Missing` variant driven by `Require_Invalid_Version` from `:15256-15260` into a success assertion, requiring `Put_Object_Tagging_Response_Available` with `Object_Tag_Mutation_Completed` and `No_Failure`, the served tag set for the get, and `Version_ID = ""` on all three, while `Duplicate`, `Mismatch`, `Control` and `Delete_Control` keep asserting rejection. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` already fails against real servers on this path, so a fix must let `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` get past its HeadObject case and then through `Require_Object_Tagging`. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Object tagging is unusable through this API against any server that omits the header for an object in a never-versioned bucket. That was observed directly on RustFS. MinIO and SeaweedFS omit the same header for the same bucket state on the read path, so they are expected to hit this too; that was not confirmed, because their conformance run stops earlier. The demand is unconditional, so no caller input avoids it: an ordinary `Put_Tags` with no version selector is enough. The damage is not confined to a lost read, because the server has already applied the replacement or the deletion when the client reports `Object_Tag_Mutation_Outcome_Unknown`, which is the disposition the certainty corpus marks `reconcile = yes`. The reconciling read is `Get_Tags`, and it fails the same way, so the caller can neither complete nor reconcile through this API. The `Tagging_Outcome` convenience overloads raise `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` instead. The predicate is a header count, so nothing about timing or load is involved. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response` assigns `Failure => No_Failure` to every complete GetObjectTorrent response, with no reference to the decoded status or S3 error code. Every other normalizer in the same package classifies those pairs, so a `503 SlowDown` or `500 InternalError` answered to `?torrent` carries `No_Failure` while the identical response answered to GetObjectAcl, GetObjectTagging, GetObjectLegalHold or GetObjectRetention on the same client carries `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`. A caller that routes retry policy off the shared `Failure_Reason` domain therefore does not retry a transient service rejection on this one operation.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The torrent normalizer returns a constant (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:5200-5210`):

```ada
   function Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response
     (Value     : Low_Level.Get_Object_Torrent_Outcome;
      Admission : HTTP_Client.Admission_Certainty)
      return Get_Object_Torrent_Result is
   begin
      return
        (Kind      => Get_Object_Torrent_Response_Available,
         Failure   => No_Failure,
         Admission => Admission,
         Response  => Value);
   end Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response;
```

`Value.Kind`, `Value.Status` and the error `Code` are all available and none is consulted. The sibling normalizer for GetObjectAcl, in the same file, classifies them (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:4899-4934`):

```ada
   function Normalize_Get_Object_ACL_Response
     (Value     : Low_Level.Get_Object_ACL_Outcome;
      Admission : HTTP_Client.Admission_Certainty)
      return Get_Object_ACL_Result
   is
      Code : constant String :=
        (if Value.Kind = Low_Level.Get_Object_ACL_Rejected
         then US.To_String (Value.Error.Code)
         else "");
      Failure : constant Failure_Reason :=
        (if Admission /= HTTP_Client.Response_Observed
         then Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response
         elsif Value.Kind = Low_Level.Object_ACL_Found
         then No_Failure
...
         elsif Value.Status = 404
           and then Code in "NoSuchBucket" | "NoSuchKey" | "NoSuchVersion"
         then Not_Found
...
         elsif (Value.Status = 409 and then Code = "OperationAborted")
           or else (Value.Status = 429 and then Code = "SlowDown")
           or else (Value.Status = 500 and then Code = "InternalError")
           or else (Value.Status = 502 and then Code = "BadGateway")
           or else (Value.Status = 503 and then Code = "SlowDown")
           or else (Value.Status = 504 and then Code = "RequestTimeout")
         then Unavailable_Or_Retryable
         else Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response);
```

Twelve normalizers in this file carry that same retryable table, among them the object tagging helper (`Retryable_Object_Tag_Response`, `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:7228`) and the object-lock helper used by the legal-hold and retention results (`Object_Lock_Response_Failure`, `:4876`, applied at `:5622`, `:5922`, `:6202` and `:6463`). `tests/corpora/composable-client/put-certainty.tsv` records the same mapping as the maintained policy for the mutating operations.

`Failure_Reason` is a single shared domain, not a per-operation one (`src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:22-23`):

```ada
   --  Stable reason domain shared by composable provider results.
   --  @enum No_Failure Successful or conclusively failed condition
```

and the torrent result record uses the same field wording as the other twenty result records in the package (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.ads:1527`):

```ada
   --  @field Failure Bounded expected failure reason
```

A `503 SlowDown` is neither a success nor a conclusive failure, so `No_Failure` misstates it. This is not an admission artefact: the normalizer is reached only for a decoded complete response (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:5416-5423`):

```ada
                  if Outcome.Kind =
                    Low_Level.Get_Object_Torrent_Rejected
                  then
                     Clear_Buffer (Item.Destination.all);
                  end if;
                  Item.Final_Result :=
                    Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response
                      (Outcome, HTTP_Client.Certainty (HTTP_Result));
```

No test covers a well-formed retryable rejection on this path. The torrent corpus asserts `No_Failure` only for a `404` (`tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:701-708`):

```ada
            if Result.Kind /=
                Objects.Get_Object_Torrent_Response_Available
              or else Result.Failure /= Client_API.No_Failure
              or else Result.Response.Kind /=
                Low_Level.Get_Object_Torrent_Rejected
              or else Result.Response.Status /= 404
              or else Buffers.Length (Destination) /= 0
            then
```

and its only `500` scenarios serve bodies that take the `Response_Invalid` path instead of producing a decoded rejection (`tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:341-348`):

```ada
               elsif Scenario = "malformed-error" then
                  Response := US.To_Unbounded_String
                    (HTTP_Response
                       ("500 Internal Server Error",
                        "<Error><Unknown/></Error>"));
               elsif Scenario = "error-limit" then
                  Response := US.To_Unbounded_String
                    (HTTP_Response ("500 Internal Server Error", Error_XML));
```

So the retryable statuses can be corrected without contradicting an existing assertion. There is no torrent certainty corpus under `tests/corpora/composable-client/` and no torrent-specific policy in the specification, so nothing documents a different domain for this operation.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below scripts five exchanges on a loopback origin and demonstrates both sides in one run. A well-formed `503 Service Unavailable` with `<Code>SlowDown</Code>` and a `500 Internal Server Error` with `<Code>InternalError</Code>` are answered to `Get_Torrent` and asserted to carry `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`. A `404 NoSuchKey` is answered to `Get_Torrent` and only reported, because the existing corpus assertion fixes that label. The identical `503` is then answered to `Get_ACL` as a control, and a `200 OK` torrent body as a second control.

```ada
   function Error_XML (Code : String) return String is
     ("<Error><Code>" & Code & "</Code><Message>service is busy</Message>" &
      "<Resource>/example-bucket/native-object</Resource></Error>");
...
         Result : constant Objects.Get_Object_Torrent_Result :=
           Objects.Get_Torrent
             (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, Key, Parameters, Destination, Identity,
              Timeout => 10.0);
      begin
...
            Check (Result.Failure = Expected,
                   Label & ": Failure is " &
                     Client_API.Failure_Reason'Image (Expected) &
                     ", as every sibling normalizer assigns for this " &
                     "status and code");
...
      Result : constant Objects.Get_Object_ACL_Result := Objects.Get_ACL
        (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, "native-object", Parameters, Identity,
         Timeout => 10.0);
...
   Run_Torrent
     ("503 SlowDown torrent", "busy-object",
      Client_API.Unavailable_Or_Retryable, Assert => True);
   Run_Torrent
     ("500 InternalError torrent", "faulted-object",
      Client_API.Unavailable_Or_Retryable, Assert => True);
...
   Run_ACL ("503 SlowDown ACL", Client_API.Unavailable_Or_Retryable);
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- scripted loopback origin on 127.0.0.1:60700
  -- --- GetObjectTorrent: well-formed transient S3 rejections ---
  -- 503 SlowDown torrent: Get_Torrent Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_RESPONSE_AVAILABLE, Failure = NO_FAILURE
  -- 503 SlowDown torrent: response Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_REJECTED, status 503, code SlowDown, destination length 0
  ok: 503 SlowDown torrent: the rejection is a complete modeled response
  FAILED: 503 SlowDown torrent: Failure is UNAVAILABLE_OR_RETRYABLE, as every sibling normalizer assigns for this status and code
  -- 500 InternalError torrent: Get_Torrent Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_RESPONSE_AVAILABLE, Failure = NO_FAILURE
  -- 500 InternalError torrent: response Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_REJECTED, status 500, code InternalError, destination length 0
  ok: 500 InternalError torrent: the rejection is a complete modeled response
  FAILED: 500 InternalError torrent: Failure is UNAVAILABLE_OR_RETRYABLE, as every sibling normalizer assigns for this status and code
  -- --- reported only: 404 is asserted as No_Failure by tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:703 ---
  -- 404 NoSuchKey torrent: Get_Torrent Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_RESPONSE_AVAILABLE, Failure = NO_FAILURE
  -- 404 NoSuchKey torrent: response Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_REJECTED, status 404, code NoSuchKey, destination length 0
  -- --- control: the identical 503 answered to GetObjectAcl ---
  -- 503 SlowDown ACL: Get_ACL Kind = GET_OBJECT_ACL_RESPONSE_AVAILABLE, Failure = UNAVAILABLE_OR_RETRYABLE
  -- 503 SlowDown ACL: response Kind = GET_OBJECT_ACL_REJECTED, status 503, code SlowDown
  ok: 503 SlowDown ACL: the rejection is a complete modeled response
  ok: 503 SlowDown ACL: Failure is UNAVAILABLE_OR_RETRYABLE
  -- --- control: a 200 GetObjectTorrent success ---
  -- 200 torrent: Get_Torrent Kind = GET_OBJECT_TORRENT_RESPONSE_AVAILABLE, Failure = NO_FAILURE, destination length 18
  ok: 200 torrent: a 200 torrent read is a complete response
  ok: 200 torrent: a success carries No_Failure
  ok: 200 torrent: outcome is Torrent_Opened
  ok: 200 torrent: destination holds the torrent bytes
  -- request 1: GET /example-bucket/busy-object?torrent HTTP/1.1
  -- request 2: GET /example-bucket/faulted-object?torrent HTTP/1.1
  -- request 3: GET /example-bucket/absent-object?torrent HTTP/1.1
  -- request 4: GET /example-bucket/native-object?acl HTTP/1.1
  -- request 5: GET /example-bucket/native-object?torrent HTTP/1.1
  ok: the origin served all five scripted exchanges
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The response payload itself is correct in every leg: the rejection decodes, the status and code are present, and the destination is empty as it should be. Only the `Failure` label differs from what the same client reports for the same bytes on the ACL path.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A decoded GetObjectTorrent rejection should carry the same `Failure_Reason` that every other operation in the package assigns for the same status and S3 error code, because `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:22` presents `Failure_Reason` as one stable domain shared by all composable results and defines `No_Failure` as a successful or conclusively failed condition. A `503 SlowDown` and a `500 InternalError` are transient service rejections and belong to `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`.

## Suggested treatment

Give `Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response` a `Failure` expression instead of the constant, in the shape `Normalize_Get_Object_ACL_Response` already uses at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:4904-4934`: derive `Code` from `Value.Error.Code` when `Value.Kind = Low_Level.Get_Object_Torrent_Rejected`, keep `No_Failure` for `Torrent_Opened`, and keep `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` when `Admission /= HTTP_Client.Response_Observed`.

How much of the sibling table to adopt needs a maintainer decision, because one of its rows contradicts an existing assertion:

- Map only the six retryable pairs (`409 OperationAborted`, `429 SlowDown`, `500 InternalError`, `502 BadGateway`, `503 SlowDown`, `504 RequestTimeout`) to `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` and leave every other decoded rejection at `No_Failure`. This closes the retry divergence and changes no existing test, since `tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:703` covers only a `404`. It leaves the torrent path reporting `No_Failure` for an authentication or authorization rejection, which no sibling does.
- Reuse the ACL table whole, so `404 NoSuchKey` becomes `Not_Found`, `401 InvalidAccessKeyId` becomes `Authentication_Failed`, and an unmodeled rejection becomes `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`. This makes the operation consistent with the rest of the package and changes the value a caller sees for a `404`, so `tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:703` has to be updated from `No_Failure` to `Not_Found`.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb`, alongside the existing `provider-rejection` case: add scenarios serving a well-formed `503 SlowDown` and `500 InternalError` error document and assert `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`, which the corpus does not cover today because its only `500` cases are the malformed and over-limit bodies at `:341-348`. The spike is the test.

## Severity

The value is a label. The response payload is complete and correct, no bytes or state are lost, and a caller can still read `Response.Kind`, `Response.Status` and `Response.Error.Code` and decide for itself. The harm falls on a caller that uses the shared `Failure_Reason` domain for retry policy, which is what that domain is for: it will not retry a `503 SlowDown` or `500 InternalError` from GetObjectTorrent while retrying the identical response from every other operation on the same client. It needs no unusual input, only a server that answers `?torrent` with a transient error. Deterministic.

## Summary

Every classifier in the bucket convenience client enumerates exact `(status, code)` pairs, and the only authentication pair enumerated is `(401, "InvalidAccessKeyId")`. No server in evidence answers a credential problem with 401: the pinned MinIO release answers an unknown access key with `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` and a wrong secret with `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch`, and this crate's own S3 application answers both with `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch`. The pair never matches, so a well-formed conclusive refusal is reported as `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`, and because the same predicate also decides conclusiveness, every bucket mutation additionally reports `..._Outcome_Unknown`. The caller is told to reconcile a mutation the server refused before touching any state, on the most common operator error there is.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

`Normalize_Create_Bucket_Response` decides conclusiveness from an enumeration whose only authentication entry is at 401 (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4104-4115`):

```ada
      Conclusive_Rejection : constant Boolean :=
        (Value.Status = 400
         and then Code in "IllegalLocationConstraintException" |
           "InvalidArgument" | "InvalidBucketName" |
           "InvalidLocationConstraint" | "InvalidRequest" | "MalformedXML")
        or else (Value.Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId")
        or else (Value.Status = 403 and then Code = "AccessDenied")
        or else
          (Value.Status = 409
           and then Code in "BucketAlreadyExists" |
             "BucketAlreadyOwnedByYou" | "TooManyBuckets")
        or else (Value.Status = 501 and then Code = "NotImplemented");
```

The reason derivation repeats the same two pairs and falls through to `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4123-4132`):

```ada
      Failure : constant Failure_Reason :=
        (if Value.Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId"
         then Authentication_Failed
         elsif Value.Status = 403 and then Code = "AccessDenied"
         then Authorization_Failed
         elsif Conclusive_Rejection
         then Invalid_Request
         elsif Retryable_Response
         then Unavailable_Or_Retryable
         else Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response);
```

and the same `Conclusive_Rejection` decides the mutation disposition (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4136-4143`):

```ada
         Disposition =>
           (if Admission /= HTTP_Client.Response_Observed
            then Bucket_Creation_Outcome_Unknown
            elsif Value.Kind = Low_Level.Bucket_Created
            then Bucket_Creation_Completed
            elsif Conclusive_Rejection
            then Bucket_Definitely_Not_Created
            else Bucket_Creation_Outcome_Unknown),
```

The shape is not local to CreateBucket. The classifier shared by the generated bucket mutations has the identical enumeration (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:26443-26453`):

```ada
   function Conclusive_Generated_Mutation_Rejection
     (Status : Flyology.HTTP.Status_Code; Code : String) return Boolean is
     ((Status = 400
       and then Code in
         "AccessControlListNotSupported" | "InvalidArgument" |
         "InvalidBucketName" | "InvalidRequest" | "MalformedXML")
      or else (Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId")
      or else (Status = 403 and then Code = "AccessDenied")
      or else (Status = 404 and then Code in "NoSuchBucket" | "NoSuchKey")
      or else (Status = 405 and then Code = "MethodNotAllowed")
      or else (Status = 501 and then Code = "NotImplemented"));
```

with the matching reason derivation at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:26464-26477`. Bucket tagging repeats it at `:24417-24426` and `:24437-24450`, and the read path repeats it too (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:23474-23477`):

```ada
         elsif Value.Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId"
         then Authentication_Failed
         elsif Value.Status = 403 and then Code = "AccessDenied"
         then Authorization_Failed
```

`grep -c 'Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId"' src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb` returns 88, and the same three-line pattern appears 18 times in `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb` and 11 times in `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb`.

Two documented public meanings are contradicted by the fall-through. `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:36`:

```ada
   --  @enum Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response Response was not conclusive or valid
```

The MinIO and in-repo answers below are both conclusive and valid. `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:145-150`:

```ada
   --  What is known about one CreateBucket mutation after terminal drain.
   --  Unknown creation requires caller-selected HeadBucket reconciliation of
   --  ownership and location before any retry.
   --  @enum Bucket_Creation_Completed Complete validated 200 proves creation
   --  @enum Bucket_Definitely_Not_Created Non-admission or exact rejection
   --  @enum Bucket_Creation_Outcome_Unknown Creation must be reconciled
```

A caller who follows that instruction issues a HeadBucket reconciliation for a request the server refused before touching any state.

MinIO does not send 401. The image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5` is `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`, whose `--version` reports

```
minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)
Runtime: go1.24.6 linux/arm64
```

Signing `GET /flyology-review-buckets?location` with this crate's own SigV4 over a raw loopback socket, a credential naming an access key MinIO does not have answers

```
HTTP/1.1 403 Forbidden
Content-Length: 378
Content-Type: application/xml
Server: MinIO

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<Error><Code>InvalidAccessKeyId</Code><Message>The Access Key Id you provided does not exist in our records.</Message>...</Error>
```

and the same request signed with a different secret answers

```
HTTP/1.1 403 Forbidden
Content-Length: 433
Content-Type: application/xml
Server: MinIO

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<Error><Code>SignatureDoesNotMatch</Code><Message>The request signature we calculated does not match the signature you provided. Check your key and signing method.</Message>...</Error>
```

A bodyless `HEAD /flyology-review-buckets` with the unknown key answers `403 Forbidden` with `Content-Length: 0` and the non-AWS header `X-Minio-Error-Code: InvalidAccessKeyId`, which this client does not read. No probed path produced a 401.

This crate's own server does not send 401 either. `Verify_Request` maps an unknown key and a bad signature alike to one status (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:213-217`):

```ada
            if not Allowed
              or else not Valid_Principal (US.To_String (Principal))
            then
               return (Status => Credential_Rejected, others => <>);
            end if;
```

and that status is rendered as a signature failure (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1907-1910`):

```ada
         when Authentication.Credential_Rejected =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 403, "SignatureDoesNotMatch",
               "The request signature does not match", Resource);
```

so the client cannot classify its own server's credential rejection. `InvalidAccessKeyId` appears nowhere outside the three client bodies: `grep -rl InvalidAccessKeyId src/` matches only `client-buckets.adb`, `client-objects.adb` and `client-transfers.adb`.

The pinned model cannot settle the status either way. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3` for shapes whose name contains either code returns an empty list, so neither `InvalidAccessKeyId` nor `SignatureDoesNotMatch` is modelled with an `httpStatusCode`. The status the AWS service itself returns for these two codes was not checked against a live AWS endpoint.

No maintained document states that only 401 is recognised. `docs/compatibility/s3.md` and the qualification documents say nothing about which authentication status and code pairs are accepted; the nearest text describes the corpus rather than a limitation (`docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:21-22`):

```
The strict normalization corpus covers every accepted service status/code
pair, every non-complete HTTP result across all admission certainties, and an
```

One classifier does not fall through, and is wrong differently. `Normalize_Head_Bucket_Response` switches on status alone (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4806-4811`):

```ada
         elsif Value.Status in 301 | 307 | 400 | 501
         then Invalid_Request
         elsif Value.Status = 401
         then Authentication_Failed
         elsif Value.Status = 403
         then Authorization_Failed
```

so the bodyless MinIO credential rejection above is labelled `Authorization_Failed`, an authorisation failure, rather than an authentication one.

The unit corpus pins the 401-only behaviour. `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:129-130` is the ListBuckets row:

```ada
      Check_List_Buckets_Response
        (401, "InvalidAccessKeyId", Authentication_Failed);
```

`:270-272` is the CreateBucket row and `:436-439` the DeleteBucket row:

```ada
      Check_Delete_Bucket_Response
        (401,
         "InvalidAccessKeyId",
         Bucket_Definitely_Not_Deleted,
         Authentication_Failed);
```

The file carries 32 such `401, "InvalidAccessKeyId"` rows in total. Neither that file nor `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb` contains a single `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` or `SignatureDoesNotMatch` row: `grep -rn 'SignatureDoesNotMatch' tests/src/` matches only the server-side corpus. The socket corpus builds its bucket rejection assertions on `403 AccessDenied` (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:11360`, `:10722`, `:10445`), which is one of the two enumerated pairs, so it passes. `Check_Head_Bucket_Response (403, Authorization_Failed)` at `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:588` pins the HeadBucket mislabel as well.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below first sends two requests to this crate's own S3 application in process, one whose Authorization credential names an access key the server does not have and one with a single signature hex digit flipped, and prints the status and `<Code>` of each answer. It then serves the verbatim MinIO 403 documents from a scripted loopback origin and drives three public entry points through them, one read and two mutations, asserting `Authentication_Failed` and a definite disposition. Six control legs replay the two enumerated pairs, `401 InvalidAccessKeyId` and `403 AccessDenied`, through the same code.

The scripted bodies are MinIO's, byte for byte:

```ada
   Invalid_Key_Body : constant String :=
     "<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" & ASCII.LF &
     "<Error><Code>InvalidAccessKeyId</Code><Message>The Access Key Id you " &
     "provided does not exist in our records.</Message><BucketName>" & ...
   Bad_Signature_Body : constant String :=
     "<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" & ASCII.LF &
     "<Error><Code>SignatureDoesNotMatch</Code><Message>The request " &
     "signature we calculated does not match the signature you provided. " & ...
```

The calls and the assertions:

```ada
      Result : constant Buckets.Get_Bucket_Location_Result :=
        Buckets.Get_Location
          (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, Parameters, Identity, Timeout => 10.0);
...
      Check (Result.Failure = Expected,
             Label & ": GetBucketLocation Failure is " &
               Client_API.Failure_Reason'Image (Expected));
...
      Result : constant Buckets.Put_Bucket_Tagging_Result :=
        Buckets.Put_Tags
          (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, Sample_Tags, Parameters, Identity,
           Timeout => 10.0);
...
      Check (Result.Disposition = Disposition,
             Label & ": PutBucketTagging Disposition is " &
               Buckets.Bucket_Tag_Mutation_Disposition'Image (Disposition));
...
      Result : constant Buckets.Create_Bucket_Result :=
        Buckets.Create
          (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, Parameters, Identity, Timeout => 10.0);
...
      Check (Result.Disposition = Disposition,
             Label & ": CreateBucket Disposition is " &
               Buckets.Bucket_Creation_Disposition'Image (Disposition));
```

driven with the expectations the documented enums require:

```ada
   Run_Location
     ("403 InvalidAccessKeyId/location", Client_API.Authentication_Failed);
   Run_Location
     ("403 SignatureDoesNotMatch/location", Client_API.Authentication_Failed);
   Run_Put_Tags
     ("403 InvalidAccessKeyId/put-tags", Client_API.Authentication_Failed,
      Buckets.Bucket_Tag_Mutation_Definitely_Not_Applied);
   Run_Put_Tags
     ("403 SignatureDoesNotMatch/put-tags", Client_API.Authentication_Failed,
      Buckets.Bucket_Tag_Mutation_Definitely_Not_Applied);
   Run_Create
     ("403 InvalidAccessKeyId/create", Client_API.Authentication_Failed,
      Buckets.Bucket_Definitely_Not_Created);
```


The control legs differ from the failing legs only in the status line or the error code, so nothing about the transport, the signature or the body parse is involved. The status and code that reach the classifier are printed on each line and are exactly the ones MinIO sent.

The same classification is reached against the live container with no scripted bytes at all, through the same public calls:

```
  -- location/wrong-key: status 403, outcome GET_BUCKET_LOCATION_REJECTED
  -- location/wrong-key: Error.Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId"
  -- location/wrong-key: Client.Buckets Failure = CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE
  -- location/wrong-secret: Error.Code = "SignatureDoesNotMatch"
  -- location/wrong-secret: Client.Buckets Failure = CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE
  -- put-tags/wrong-key: Client.Buckets Failure = CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE, Disposition = BUCKET_TAG_MUTATION_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN
  -- put-tags/wrong-secret: Client.Buckets Failure = CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE, Disposition = BUCKET_TAG_MUTATION_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN
  -- put-tags/control: status 200, outcome BUCKET_TAGS_REPLACED
  -- put-tags/control: Client.Buckets Failure = NO_FAILURE, Disposition = BUCKET_TAG_MUTATION_COMPLETED
```

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image.

## What should happen

A `403` carrying `InvalidAccessKeyId` or `SignatureDoesNotMatch` is a conclusive, valid, modelled authentication rejection. It should be reported as `Authentication_Failed`, and a mutation refused that way should be `..._Definitely_Not_Applied` or `Bucket_Definitely_Not_Created`, because the server refused the request before touching any state. `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:36` reserves `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` for a response that "was not conclusive or valid", and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:146-147` and `:150` reserve the unknown disposition for a creation that "must be reconciled".

## Suggested treatment

The mechanical change is to add the observed pairs beside the existing 401 pairing in each classifier, for example in `Conclusive_Generated_Mutation_Rejection` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:26449-26450` and in the matching `Generated_Mutation_Response_Failure` at `:26467-26470`:

```ada
      or else (Status = 403
               and then Code in "AccessDenied" | "InvalidAccessKeyId" |
                 "SignatureDoesNotMatch")
```

with `InvalidAccessKeyId` and `SignatureDoesNotMatch` mapping to `Authentication_Failed` and `AccessDenied` staying `Authorization_Failed`. The same edit applies to `Normalize_Create_Bucket_Response` (`:4104-4132`), `Conclusive_Bucket_Tag_Rejection` and `Bucket_Tag_Response_Failure` (`:24417-24450`), `Normalize_Get_Bucket_Location_Response` (`:23474-23477`) and the remaining sites of the 88 the file carries.

This needs a maintainer decision, because the accepted pair set is externally visible policy under the "Decision authority" section of `AGENTS.md`:

- Which pairs count as conclusive authentication rejections. At minimum `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` and `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch`, both measured above. `403 RequestTimeTooSkewed` and `400 AuthorizationHeaderMalformed` are the same class of conclusive credential refusal and were not measured here.
- Whether the `(401, "InvalidAccessKeyId")` pairing is kept. Keeping it costs nothing and preserves the 32 existing unit rows; removing it makes the table describe only observed behaviour and forces those rows to change.
- Whether `Normalize_Head_Bucket_Response` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4796-4816`) keeps classifying HeadBucket on status alone. It cannot distinguish authentication from authorisation without a code, and a bodyless 403 is exactly what MinIO sends for an unknown key, so today it answers `Authorization_Failed` for a credential failure.

The identical pattern appears 18 times in `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb` and 11 times in `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb`, so the decision should be made once and applied uniformly. The fall-through also catches any other structured rejection whose exact pair is unlisted; the complementary gap for a bodyless or non-XML rejection, which reaches these convenience overloads through the `Raise_*_Exchange_Failure` mappers at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:137-141`, is reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/42.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing bucket rejection assertions that use `403 AccessDenied` (`:11360`, `:10722`, `:10445`): add scripted `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` and `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` responses for a read and for a mutation, and require `Authentication_Failed` with a definite disposition. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` should additionally drive one bucket mutation with a deliberately wrong secret, since that is observable against every server the compatibility matrix lists. The 401 rows in `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb` (`:129-130`, `:270-272`, `:436-439` and 29 more) may stay if the 401 pairing is retained, but a fix must add the 403 rows beside them. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Anyone who mistypes an access key, lets a secret rotate, or points the client at a server that does not know their credential gets `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` for a plain refusal, and for a bucket mutation is additionally told the outcome is unknown and must be reconciled by a HeadBucket read before any retry. That is the most common operator error there is, and the wrong disposition is the documented decision input for whether a mutation is safe to retry. It applies against the pinned MinIO release, against this crate's own S3 server, and against any server that follows the same convention; the status the AWS service returns for these two codes was not checked live. A caller who ignores the classification and reads `Result.Response.Error.Code` still sees the true code, which bounds the damage. The classification is a pure function of a status and a string, so no timing, load or container state is involved. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Decode_Head_Bucket_Complete_Response` parses `x-amz-bucket-region` out of every HeadBucket response and hands it to `Decode_Head_Bucket_Response`, which uses it only on a 200 and discards it on every other status. The rejected variants of both the low-level outcome and the convenience outcome have no field a region could occupy, so the loss is structural: no caller of the typed decoder, the composable operation or the convenience `Head` can recover the one datum a HeadBucket redirect exists to convey. `Head` is documented as the call that reports a bucket's availability and region, and it meets that promise only on 200.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The complete-response decoder captures the header for every status (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4956-4968`):

```ada
         Headers : constant Head_Bucket_Result :=
           (Bucket_ARN           => H ("x-amz-bucket-arn"),
            Bucket_Location_Type => H ("x-amz-bucket-location-type"),
            Bucket_Location_Name => H ("x-amz-bucket-location-name"),
            Bucket_Region        => H ("x-amz-bucket-region"),
            Access_Point_Alias   => Optional_Boolean_Header
              (Flyology.HTTP.Client.Header
                 (Response, "x-amz-access-point-alias")));
      begin
         return Decode_Head_Bucket_Response
           (Flyology.HTTP.Client.Status (Response), Payload, Headers,
            Flyology.HTTP.Client.Header (Response, "x-amz-request-id"),
            Flyology.HTTP.Client.Header (Response, "x-amz-id-2"));
```

`Decode_Head_Bucket_Response` consults `Headers` only inside the 200 branch and never mentions it again (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4906-4913`):

```ada
         return (Kind => Bucket_Found, Status => Status, Result => Headers);
      else
         return
           (Kind   => Head_Bucket_Rejected,
            Status => Status,
            Error  => Head_Error
              ("HeadBucket", Status, Request_ID, Host_ID));
      end if;
```

`Head_Error` synthesises the whole error from the status, so there is no field a region could hide in (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4865-4871`):

```ada
      return
         (Code       => US.To_Unbounded_String ("HTTP" & Status_Text),
         Message    => US.To_Unbounded_String
           (Operation & " returned HTTP status " & Status_Text),
         Resource   => US.Null_Unbounded_String,
         Request_ID => US.To_Unbounded_String (Request_ID),
         Host_ID    => US.To_Unbounded_String (Host_ID));
```

The rejected variant carries exactly `Status` and `Error` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:1391-1405`):

```ada
   --  Completed HeadBucket response.
   --  @field Kind Active response variant
   --  @field Status Carried HTTP response status
   --  @field Result Successful bucket metadata
   --  @field Error Synthesized bodyless rejection
   type Head_Bucket_Outcome
     (Kind : Head_Bucket_Outcome_Kind := Head_Bucket_Rejected) is record
      Status : Flyology.HTTP.Status_Code := 500;
      case Kind is
         when Bucket_Found =>
            Result : Head_Bucket_Result;
         when Head_Bucket_Rejected =>
            Error : S3.Errors.Error_Response;
      end case;
   end record;
```

The convenience outcome has the same shape, with `Region` only on the available variant (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:10129-10142`):

```ada
   type Head_Outcome
     (Kind : Head_Outcome_Kind := Head_Rejected) is record
      Status : Flyology.HTTP.Status_Code := 500;
      case Kind is
         when Bucket_Available =>
            Bucket_ARN           : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
            Bucket_Location_Type : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
            Bucket_Location_Name : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
            Region               : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
            Access_Point_Alias   : Low_Level.Optional_Boolean;
         when Head_Rejected =>
            Error : Flyology.Object_Storage.S3.Errors.Error_Response;
      end case;
   end record;
```

and the convenience body simply forwards the rejection (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:2835-2838`):

```ada
            if Outcome.Kind = Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Rejected then
               return
                 (Kind => Head_Rejected, Status => Outcome.Status,
                  Error => Outcome.Error);
            end if;
```

The contract this breaks is the documented purpose of the call (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:10172-10179`):

```ada
   --  Probe one bucket without downloading a response body. Successful
   --  results preserve every modeled HeadBucket response header and expose
   --  the bucket region under the convenience-level Region name. If a
   --  compatible server omits the optional response header, Region falls
   --  back to the region used to sign the request.
   --  @param Client Configured, caller-owned Flyology HTTP client
   --  @param Origin Exact origin used to configure Client and sign requests
   --  @param Bucket Bucket whose availability and region are requested
```

The region is requested, and on any status other than 200 it is parsed and then thrown away.

The redirect is additionally reported as a request rejection (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4806-4807`):

```ada
         elsif Value.Status in 301 | 307 | 400 | 501
         then Invalid_Request
```

`src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:26` defines that reason as `Invalid_Request Local or modeled service request rejection`. A redirect is not a rejection of the request.

This is not a stated exclusion. `docs/qualification/head-bucket-composable.md:26-28` excludes redirect following, not exposing the region a redirect carries:

```
Directory-bucket and S3 Express endpoint selection, access-point, Object
Lambda, and Outposts endpoint discovery or rewriting, and cross-region
redirect following remain outside this focused lane.
```

The pinned model does not settle it either way. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`:

```
HeadBucketOutput required: None
BucketRegion: {'shape': 'Region', 'location': 'header', 'locationName': 'x-amz-bucket-region'}
HeadBucket errors: [{'shape': 'NoSuchBucket'}]
```

so `BucketRegion` is modelled as that header on the output shape, and the only modelled error is `NoSuchBucket`. The model neither requires nor forbids the header on a non-200 status. That AWS attaches `x-amz-bucket-region` to the 301 it returns for a bucket in another region was not checked against a live AWS endpoint. MinIO was checked and does not redirect at all: a HeadBucket signed with an `eu-west-1` credential scope against a bucket the pinned MinIO release reports as `us-east-1` answers `200 OK` with no `x-amz-bucket-region` header, so no server in the maintained matrix can produce the shape and the spike supplies the header itself.

Two existing assertions pin the discard. `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:583-584`:

```ada
      Check_Head_Bucket_Response (301, Invalid_Request);
      Check_Head_Bucket_Response (307, Invalid_Request);
```

and the helper those rows use builds the outcome from the status alone, with no headers at all (`tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:503-515`):

```ada
   procedure Check_Head_Bucket_Response
     (Status  : Flyology.HTTP.Status_Code;
      Failure : Failure_Reason)
   is
      Value : constant Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Outcome :=
        (if Status = 200
         then (Kind => Low_Level.Bucket_Found,
               Status => Status,
               Result => (others => <>))
         else (Kind => Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Rejected,
               Status => Status,
               Error =>
                 (Code       => US.To_Unbounded_String ("HTTP"),
```

so no test ever supplies `x-amz-bucket-region` on a non-200. The socket corpus does serve that header, but only on 200 responses (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:2874`, `:2912`).

## What happens now

Spike: the program below works at two layers. It first calls the public `Low_Level.Decode_Head_Bucket_Response` with `Status => 301` and a `Head_Bucket_Result` whose `Bucket_Region` is `eu-west-1`, prints every readable field of the returned outcome and asserts the region is still somewhere in it. It then serves `HEAD /flyology-elsewhere` from a scripted loopback origin as `301 Moved Permanently` with `Content-Length: 0` and `x-amz-bucket-region: eu-west-1`, and drives both the convenience `Head` and the parameter-record `Head`. Four control legs repeat every leg with `200 OK` and the same header.

```ada
   Bucket : constant String := "flyology-elsewhere";
   Elsewhere : constant String := "eu-west-1";
   Signing_Region : constant String := "us-east-1";
...
      Headers : constant Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Result :=
        (Bucket_ARN           => US.Null_Unbounded_String,
         Bucket_Location_Type => US.Null_Unbounded_String,
         Bucket_Location_Name => US.Null_Unbounded_String,
         Bucket_Region        => US.To_Unbounded_String (Elsewhere),
         Access_Point_Alias   => (others => <>));
      Outcome : constant Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Outcome :=
        Low_Level.Decode_Head_Bucket_Response
          (Status, "", Headers, "AMZREQ1", "AMZHOST1");
...
            Note (Label & ": every readable field = " & Readable);
            Check (Contains_Region (Readable),
                   Label & ": the decoded rejection still reports " &
                     Elsewhere);
```

The scripted response and the two public calls:

```ada
   function Head_Response (Status_Line : String) return String is
     ("HTTP/1.1 " & Status_Line & CRLF &
      "Content-Length: 0" & CRLF &
      "x-amz-bucket-region: " & Elsewhere & CRLF &
      "x-amz-request-id: AMZREQ1" & CRLF &
      "x-amz-id-2: AMZHOST1" & CRLF &
      "Server: AmazonS3" & CRLF &
      "Connection: close" & CRLF & CRLF);
...
      Result : constant Buckets.Head_Outcome :=
        Buckets.Head
          (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, Identity, Region => Signing_Region,
           Timeout => 10.0);
...
            Check (Contains_Region (Readable),
                   Label & ": Head still reports " & Elsewhere &
                     " after the redirect");
...
      Result : constant Buckets.Head_Bucket_Result :=
        Buckets.Head
          (HTTP, Origin, Bucket, Parameters, Identity,
           Region => Signing_Region, Timeout => 10.0);
...
            Check (Contains_Region (Readable),
                   Label & ": the composable result still reports " &
                     Elsewhere);
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- --- typed decoder, 301 with x-amz-bucket-region: eu-west-1 ---
  -- decode 301: decoded kind HEAD_BUCKET_REJECTED, status 301
  -- decode 301: every readable field = HTTP301|HeadBucket returned HTTP status 301||AMZREQ1|AMZHOST1
  FAILED: decode 301: the decoded rejection still reports eu-west-1
  -- --- typed decoder control, 200 with the same header ---
  -- decode 200: decoded kind BUCKET_FOUND, status 200
  -- decode 200: Result.Bucket_Region = "eu-west-1"
  ok: decode 200: the decoder keeps x-amz-bucket-region
  -- scripted loopback origin on 127.0.0.1:58243
  -- --- Client.Buckets.Head over the origin, 301 redirect ---
  -- convenience 301: Head kind HEAD_REJECTED, status 301
  -- convenience 301: every readable field = HTTP301|HeadBucket returned HTTP status 301||AMZREQ1|AMZHOST1
  FAILED: convenience 301: Head still reports eu-west-1 after the redirect
  -- composable 301: response kind HEAD_BUCKET_REJECTED, status 301, Failure = INVALID_REQUEST
  -- composable 301: every readable field = HTTP301|HeadBucket returned HTTP status 301|
  FAILED: composable 301: the composable result still reports eu-west-1
  -- --- control: the same header on a 200 ---
  -- convenience 200: Head kind BUCKET_AVAILABLE, status 200
  -- convenience 200: Region = "eu-west-1"
  ok: convenience 200: Head reports eu-west-1
  -- composable 200: response kind BUCKET_FOUND, status 200, Failure = NO_FAILURE
  ok: composable 200: the composable result reports eu-west-1
  -- request 1: HEAD /flyology-elsewhere HTTP/1.1
  -- request 2: HEAD /flyology-elsewhere HTTP/1.1
  -- request 3: HEAD /flyology-elsewhere HTTP/1.1
  -- request 4: HEAD /flyology-elsewhere HTTP/1.1
  ok: the origin served all four exchanges
BUG REPRODUCED: 3 failed expectation(s)
```

The control legs differ from the failing legs only in the status line, and they report `eu-west-1` through both APIs, so the header is parsed correctly and is present in `Headers` at the moment the non-200 branch drops it. The three failing legs print every readable field of the rejection, and the region is in none of them.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A HeadBucket rejection that carries `x-amz-bucket-region` should let the caller read that region, because `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:10179` documents `Head` as the call by which a bucket's "availability and region are requested" and the pinned model defines `BucketRegion` as exactly that response header. A 301 should also not be reported as `Invalid_Request`, which `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:26` reserves for a request rejection.

## Suggested treatment

The fix adds a component to public variant records, so this needs a maintainer decision under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`:

- Add a region component to the rejected variants of `Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Outcome` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:1396-1405`) and `Buckets.Head_Outcome` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:10129-10142`), and pass `Headers.Bucket_Region` through the `else` branch at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4907-4913`. Smallest surface, but it publishes a component that is empty for every server in the maintained matrix.
- Carry the whole modelled header set on the rejected variants, by replacing the rejection's payload with `Status`, `Error` and `Result : Head_Bucket_Result`. Uniform with the 200 branch and future-proof for `x-amz-bucket-arn` and the location members, at the cost of a larger public record.
- Fold the value into the synthesised `Error` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4865-4871`, for example as `Resource`. No record changes, but it changes the documented meaning of an existing field and makes the region something callers have to parse out of a message.

Whichever is chosen, the `301 | 307` arm at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4806-4807` should stop reporting a redirect as `Invalid_Request`; picking a different reason is itself a change to a documented public enum, so it belongs in the same decision.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, in the HeadBucket socket block that already scripts `x-amz-bucket-region` on 200 responses (`:2874`, `:2912`): add a `301 Moved Permanently` response with `Content-Length: 0` and that header, and require the region to be readable from both the convenience and the parameter-record results. The rows at `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:583-584` assert `(301, Invalid_Request)` and `(307, Invalid_Request)` and must change if the redirect reason changes; the helper at `:503-522` has no header parameter and needs one before it can express the new case. This case is not observable against any server in the maintained matrix, so `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` cannot cover it. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A caller that signs for one region and probes a bucket that lives in another gets a rejection carrying `HTTP301` and nothing else, and has no way, short of driving the raw HTTP exchange itself, to learn the region the server just told it. That defeats the documented purpose of `Head` and makes automatic retargeting impossible on top of this client. Reaching it needs a cross-region bucket plus a signing region that does not match, which is an unusual configuration rather than an everyday call, and no server in the maintained six-server matrix produces the shape: the pinned MinIO release answers `200 OK` with no region header instead of redirecting. The AWS behaviour the case rests on was not checked against a live endpoint, so the spike supplies the header itself. The discard is a branch in a pure decoder, with no timing or state involved. Deterministic; not reproduced against a live server.

## Summary

The ListBuckets decoder requires a server that was sent `prefix=` to echo `<Prefix>` with the same presence and bytes, a rule written down in the ListBuckets qualification document. The pinned MinIO release filters correctly and does not echo the element, so `Client.Buckets.List_Page` with a nonempty `Prefix` raises `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` for a valid, correctly filtered page, and the parameter-record overload reports `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`. MinIO is one of the servers the compatibility matrix claims for ListBuckets, no maintained document says the `Prefix` parameter does not work against it, and the diagnostic the caller gets names neither the prefix nor the server.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The rule is enforced in the ListBuckets response check (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4139-4149`):

```ada
               if Natural (Page.Buckets.Length) >
                 Natural (Prepared.Requested_List_Buckets_Max)
                 or else
                   (if Prepared.Requested_List_Buckets_Has_Prefix
                    then not Page.Has_Prefix
                      or else US.To_String (Page.Prefix) /= Requested_Prefix
                    else Page.Has_Prefix and then US.Length (Page.Prefix) > 0)
               then
                  raise Invalid_Response with
                    "ListBuckets response does not match prepared request";
               end if;
```

`not Page.Has_Prefix` is the clause that fires: a server that filters without echoing is rejected exactly like one that echoes the wrong prefix. The per-bucket check that follows (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4150-4172`) independently verifies that every returned name begins with the requested prefix, so the echo rule is not what establishes that the page is filtered.

Any nonempty `Prefix` arms it. The convenience overload sets the presence flag from the string length (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:189-197`):

```ada
      Parameters : constant Low_Level.List_Buckets_Parameters :=
        (Max_Buckets            => Maximum,
         Has_Max_Buckets        => True,
         Continuation_Token     =>
           US.To_Unbounded_String (Continuation_Token),
         Has_Continuation_Token => Continuation_Token'Length > 0,
         Prefix                 => US.To_Unbounded_String (Prefix),
         Has_Prefix             => Prefix'Length > 0,
         Bucket_Region          => US.To_Unbounded_String (Bucket_Region));
```

and the convenience turns the resulting invalid response into an exception whose message names neither the prefix nor the server (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:137-141`):

```ada
         when Flyology.HTTP.Client.Response_Invalid |
              Flyology.HTTP.Client.Response_Body_Too_Large |
              Flyology.HTTP.Client.Response_Sink_Failed =>
            raise Low_Level.Invalid_Response with
              "ListBuckets response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit";
```

The rule is deliberate and documented (`docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:13-16`):

```
page cannot exceed the exact prepared maximum. An explicitly supplied prefix
must be echoed with the same presence and bytes, every returned bucket name
must remain in that prefix, and nonempty returned bucket-region metadata must
match an exact requested region.
```

The interop consequence is that a public parameter stops working against a server the matrix lists. `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:961` documents the parameter with no restriction:

```ada
   --  @param Prefix Optional bucket-name prefix filter
```

and `docs/compatibility/s3.md:12` claims the operation across the whole matrix:

```
| ListBuckets | memory, files, SQLite | complete typed, composable, and paginated convenience clients; authenticated server | atomic backend pagination, strict request-bound presence/query/XML and signed socket/server corpora, owner-driven restart/cancellation, plus native/lightweight clients and s5cmd across the six-server matrix |
```

The prose section for the operation describes the strict binding as a feature and does not exclude any server (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:493-495`):

```
clients exercise fragmented success and structured S3 error responses over
real sockets. The composable client additionally gates exact prefix/maximum
and returned-region binding, physical singleton headers, typed failure and
```

MinIO does not echo the element. The image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5` is `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`, whose `--version` reports

```
minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)
Runtime: go1.24.6 linux/arm64
```

and its answer to `GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology` is a `200 OK` carrying `Owner`, one correctly filtered `Bucket`, and no `<Prefix>` element at all. Driven live against that container, `List_Page (Prefix => "flyology")` raises, while the same call with no prefix returns a page:

```
  -- List_Page (Prefix => flyology) raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : ListBuckets response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit
  -- List_Page (no prefix, control): kind PAGE_AVAILABLE, status 200
  -- composable List_Page (Prefix => flyology): kind LIST_BUCKETS_EXCHANGE_FAILED, Failure = CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE
  -- composable List_Page: HTTP_Result = RESPONSE_INVALID
```

The pinned model documents the echo for AWS but does not require it. Reading the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`:

```
ListBucketsOutput required: None
Prefix member: {'shape': 'Prefix', 'documentation': '<p>If <code>Prefix</code> was sent with the request, it is included in the response.</p> <p>All bucket names in the response begin with the specified bucket name prefix.</p>'}
```

`ListBucketsOutput` has no `required` list, so `Prefix` is an ordinary optional member and the shape itself does not mandate the echo. The rule is repository policy.

No maintained gate would notice. The real-server corpus calls `List_Page` without a prefix (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3181-3184`):

```ada
         Outcome : constant Client_Buckets.List_Outcome :=
           Client_Buckets.List_Page
             (HTTP, Origin, Identity, Maximum => 1_000,
              Timeout => 30.0);
```

The socket corpus does exercise the echo rule, but only with a prefix that is present and wrong (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:2164-2171`):

```ada
      Wrong_List_Buckets_Prefix_XML : constant String :=
        "<ListAllMyBucketsResult xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/" &
        "2006-03-01/""><Owner><ID>socket-owner</ID></Owner>" &
        "<Buckets><Bucket><Name>socket-bucket</Name>" &
        "<CreationDate>2026-08-22T01:02:03.000Z</CreationDate>" &
        "<BucketRegion>us-east-1</BucketRegion>" &
        "</Bucket></Buckets><Prefix>other-</Prefix>" &
        "</ListAllMyBucketsResult>";
```

asserted at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:11387-11388`:

```ada
               Require_Invalid_List_Buckets
                 ("ListBuckets accepted a wrong echoed prefix");
```

None of the five documents that assertion drives omits `<Prefix>` while a prefix was requested, so the case MinIO produces is untested.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below replays MinIO's exact `ListAllMyBucketsResult` for `GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology` from a scripted loopback origin and drives both the convenience `List_Page` and the parameter-record `List_Page` with `Prefix => "flyology"`, asserting that a page is returned and holds the one matching bucket. The control legs replay the identical document with `<Prefix>flyology</Prefix>` added.

```ada
   Prefix : constant String := "flyology";

   function List_Document (Echo : Boolean) return String is
     ("<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" & ASCII.LF &
      "<ListAllMyBucketsResult " &
      "xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
      "<Owner><ID>02d6176db174dc93cb1b899f7c6078f08654445fe8cf1b6ce98d885" &
      "5f66bdbf4</ID><DisplayName>minio</DisplayName></Owner>" &
      "<Buckets><Bucket><Name>flyology-review-buckets</Name>" &
      "<CreationDate>2026-09-07T06:18:28.511Z</CreationDate></Bucket>" &
      "</Buckets>" &
      (if Echo then "<Prefix>" & Prefix & "</Prefix>" else "") &
      "</ListAllMyBucketsResult>");
```

The bucket name in that document does begin with the requested prefix, so the per-bucket check is satisfied. The two calls and their assertions:

```ada
         Result : constant Buckets.List_Outcome := Buckets.List_Page
           (HTTP, Origin, Identity, Maximum => 10, Prefix => Prefix,
            Timeout => 10.0);
      begin
...
         Check (Result.Kind = Buckets.Page_Available,
                Label & ": the prefix-filtered page is returned");
         if Result.Kind = Buckets.Page_Available then
...
            Check (Natural (Result.Page.Buckets.Length) = 1,
                   Label & ": the page holds the one matching bucket");
...
      Parameters.Prefix := Ada.Strings.Unbounded.To_Unbounded_String (Prefix);
      Parameters.Has_Prefix := True;
      declare
         Result : constant Buckets.List_Buckets_Result := Buckets.List_Page
           (HTTP, Origin, Parameters, Identity, Timeout => 10.0);
      begin
...
         Check (Result.Failure = Client_API.No_Failure,
                Label & ": the composable listing succeeds");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- scripted loopback origin on 127.0.0.1:58253
  -- --- MinIO shape: 200 ListAllMyBucketsResult with no <Prefix> ---
  -- minio/convenience: raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE : ListBuckets response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit
  FAILED: minio/convenience: the prefix-filtered page is returned
  -- minio/composable: kind LIST_BUCKETS_EXCHANGE_FAILED, Failure = CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE
  FAILED: minio/composable: the composable listing succeeds
  -- --- control: the same document echoing <Prefix>flyology</Prefix> ---
  -- echoed/convenience: kind PAGE_AVAILABLE, status 200
  ok: echoed/convenience: the prefix-filtered page is returned
  -- echoed/convenience: buckets in page = 1
  ok: echoed/convenience: the page holds the one matching bucket
  -- echoed/composable: kind LIST_BUCKETS_RESPONSE_AVAILABLE, Failure = NO_FAILURE
  ok: echoed/composable: the composable listing succeeds
  -- request 1: GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology HTTP/1.1
  -- request 2: GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology HTTP/1.1
  -- request 3: GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology HTTP/1.1
  -- request 4: GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology HTTP/1.1
  ok: the origin served all four exchanges
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The control document differs from the failing one by the eight-element `<Prefix>flyology</Prefix>` and nothing else, and it parses into a page holding the one matching bucket. The echo rule is the whole difference.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image; shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

Either a prefixed `List_Page` returns the page that MinIO's answer represents, because every returned bucket name is inside the requested prefix and the count is within the prepared maximum, or the documentation states that a nonempty `Prefix` is not supported against servers that filter without echoing. Today `docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:13-16` requires the echo, `docs/compatibility/s3.md:12` claims ListBuckets "across the six-server matrix", and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:961` offers the parameter with no restriction, so the three cannot all be true at once.

## Suggested treatment

This needs a maintainer decision, because either direction changes documented policy or a public contract under the "Decision authority" section of `AGENTS.md`:

- Keep the strict echo rule and document the limitation. State at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:961` and in the ListBuckets rows of `docs/compatibility/s3.md` that a nonempty `Prefix` requires a server that echoes `<Prefix>`, and name MinIO as one that does not. No code changes, and the client keeps refusing to present an unfiltered page as a filtered one, but a public parameter stays unusable against a matrix-listed server.
- Relax the presence half of the rule. Change `not Page.Has_Prefix or else US.To_String (Page.Prefix) /= Requested_Prefix` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4143-4144` to reject only a present and different prefix, keeping the per-bucket containment check at `:4150-4172` that already proves every returned name is inside the requested prefix. This is a new public policy about what a filtered page is allowed to mean: an absent echo would no longer prove the server applied the filter, and the containment check would become the sole evidence. `docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:13-16` would have to be reworded, since "must be echoed with the same presence and bytes" is the sentence being changed.

Either way the diagnostic should name the real cause. `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:137-141` reports every invalid ListBuckets response as "invalid or exceeds the XML limit", which sends the caller looking for an oversized document.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing prefix fixtures: add a `ListAllMyBucketsResult` that omits `<Prefix>` while the prepared request carries one, with a bucket name inside the prefix, and assert the chosen outcome. The existing assertion at `:11387-11388` uses `Wrong_List_Buckets_Prefix_XML` (`:2164-2171`), which echoes a different prefix, and must keep failing under either option. If the rule is relaxed, `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3181-3184` should also gain a prefixed `List_Page` call, since that is the gate that would have caught the interop break against a real server. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Anyone who passes the public `Prefix` parameter to `List_Page` against MinIO gets an exception from the convenience overload, or `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` from the parameter-record overload, for a response that is a valid and correctly filtered page. The same applies to any server that filters without echoing the element. The failure is total for that parameter against that server, but it needs a caller who uses the prefix filter at all, and the unprefixed listing that the compatibility corpus exercises keeps working, which is why no maintained gate reports it. The behaviour is intended and written down, so the defect is the combination of a documented strict rule, a compatibility claim that covers the server, and parameter documentation that carries no restriction. The check is a comparison of two strings against a parsed document, with no timing or state involved. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Download_File` uses one status test for two opposite protocol events and gives them the same message. When the caller sent no `Range` and the server answered 206, "GetObject returned an unsolicited representation interval" is accurate. When the caller did send a `Range` and the server answered 200 with the whole representation and no `Content-Range`, the same text is emitted, and it is false in every particular: no interval was returned and nothing was unsolicited. The refusal itself is the documented range-mode policy and the destination file is correctly left untouched, so the defect is the diagnostic an operator has to act on.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

One `elsif` covers both events and raises one message (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb:1706-1717`):

```ada
            if Head.Kind = Low_Level.Get_Object_Rejected then
               return
                 (Kind   => Download_Rejected,
                  Status => Head.Status,
                  Error  => Head.Error);
            elsif (Byte_Range_Header'Length = 0 and then Head.Status /= 200)
              or else
                (Byte_Range_Header'Length > 0 and then Head.Status /= 206)
            then
               raise Low_Level.Invalid_Response with
                 "GetObject returned an unsolicited representation interval";
            end if;
```

That literal appears at exactly one site in the repository, so no other message distinguishes the two cases.

The decode step cannot produce a better one. `Validate_Get_Object_Headers` rejects a 200 only when it carries a `Content-Range`, so a plain 200 with a `Content-Length` and no `Content-Range` is a valid head and passes through (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5981-5987`):

```ada
   procedure Validate_Get_Object_Headers
     (Value : Get_Object_Result; Status : Flyology.HTTP.Status_Code) is
   begin
      if (Status = 206
          and then not Valid_Content_Range
            (US.To_String (Value.Content_Range), Value.Content_Length))
        or else (Status = 200 and then US.Length (Value.Content_Range) > 0)
```

The decoder never sees the request, so it cannot know a `Range` was sent, and the check in `Download_File` is the only gate.

Refusing is correct and documented. The `Download_File` contract states range mode as a 206 rule (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ads:1688-1689`):

```ada
   --  @param Byte_Range_Header Optional single bytes range; a successful 206
   --  atomically publishes the selected interval as the destination file
```

and the architecture document says the same (`docs/architecture/client-transfers.md:153-154`):

```
one S3 `Range` value; that mode accepts only HTTP 206 with a strict, coherent
single `Content-Range` whose interval length equals `Content-Length` and the
```

The wording is what is wrong, not the refusal.

The path is public and reachable. `Byte_Range_Header` is a public parameter with an empty default (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ads:1710`):

```ada
      Byte_Range_Header : String := "";
```

No maintained lane sends a `Range` and answers 200, so the suite never observes the text. The `download-unexpected-range` lane exercises only the first disjunct, an unranged request, and asserts nothing about the message (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:16936-16950`):

```ada
               begin
                  declare
                     Ignored : constant Transfers.Download_Outcome :=
                       Transfers.Download_File
                         (HTTP, Origin, "example-bucket",
                          "download-unexpected-range", Partial_Path,
                          Identity, Timeout => 5.0);
                     pragma Unreferenced (Ignored);
                  begin
                     null;
                  end;
               exception
                  when Low_Level.Invalid_Response =>
                     Partial := True;
               end;
```

and the `download-range` lane sends a real `Range` against a server that answers 206 (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:16960-16965`):

```ada
               declare
                  Result : constant Transfers.Download_Outcome :=
                    Transfers.Download_File
                      (HTTP, Origin, "example-bucket", "download-range",
                       Range_Path, Identity, Timeout => 5.0,
                       Byte_Range_Header => "bytes=7-13");
```

No existing test asserts the wording, so nothing has to change to fix it.

`Transfer_Many` cannot reach the wrong text. `Execute_Transfer` calls `Download_File` with an argument list ending at `Token` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb:663-669`):

```ada
               Outcome : constant Download_Outcome := Download_File
                 (Input.Client.all, Input.Origin,
                  US.To_String (Input.Value.Bucket),
                  US.To_String (Input.Value.Key),
                  US.To_String (Input.Value.Local_Path), Input.Identity.all,
                  US.To_String (Input.Region), Input.Style,
                  Remaining (Deadline), Token);
```

and `Subject` has no range field (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ads:1731-1737`):

```ada
   type Subject is record
      Kind       : Transfer_Kind := Upload;
      Bucket     : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Key        : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Local_Path : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
      Content_Type : Ada.Strings.Unbounded.Unbounded_String;
   end record;
```

so only the accurate first disjunct is reachable from a multi-subject transfer.

## What happens now

Spike: a loopback HTTP/1.1 server answers the first two GETs with `200 OK`, `Content-Length: 27` and the whole 27-byte body regardless of any `Range` header, and a third with an unsolicited `206` and `Content-Range: bytes 7-13/27`. The program drives `Download_File` against it three times: unranged as a control that the harness works, then with `Byte_Range_Header => "bytes=7-13"` asserting the diagnostic names the actual event, then unranged against the 206 showing which event the existing text describes. It also asserts the destination is preserved on both failures and that no download temporary is left behind.

```ada
               Result : constant Transfers.Download_Outcome :=
                 Transfers.Download_File
                   (HTTP, Origin, "example-bucket", "range-ignored",
                    Range_Path, Identity, Timeout => 20.0,
                    Byte_Range_Header => "bytes=7-13");
...
         Check (not Contains (Lower (US.To_String (Message)), "unsolicited"),
                "ranged-200 diagnostic does not claim an UNSOLICITED "
                & "interval (no interval was returned at all)");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- fake HTTP/1.1 server on 127.0.0.1:57480 answers every GET with 200 and the whole 27-byte body
  -- control unranged request: GET /example-bucket/range-ignored HTTP/1.1
  -- control unranged outcome: status 200, bytes 27
  ok: control: unranged Download_File publishes the 200 body
  ok: control: published bytes round-trip
  -- ranged request line: GET /example-bucket/range-ignored HTTP/1.1
  -- ranged request Range header: bytes=7-13
  -- server answered: HTTP/1.1 200 OK, Content-Length 27, no Content-Range
  -- Invalid_Response raised: TRUE
  -- message: "GetObject returned an unsolicited representation interval"
  ok: control: a 200 answer to a ranged request is refused
  ok: control: the refused ranged download leaves the destination untouched
  FAILED: ranged-200 diagnostic does not claim an UNSOLICITED interval (no interval was returned at all)
  FAILED: ranged-200 diagnostic names the actual event: the server ignored the Range and returned the whole representation
  -- unsolicited-206 message: "GetObject returned an unsolicited representation interval"
  ok: control: an unrequested 206 is refused
  ok: control: the unrequested-206 case is the one the 'unsolicited representation interval' text describes
  ok: control: the refused 206 leaves the destination untouched
  -- files left in the scratch directory: 3 (the three destinations)
  ok: control: no download temporary is left behind
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The two opposite events produce byte-identical messages. The refusal, the untouched destination and the absent temporary are all correct.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A ranged request answered with 200 and the whole representation should be refused with a message that names that event, so an operator reads that the server or an intermediary ignored the `Range`. The existing text belongs to the opposite case, an unrequested 206, which the contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ads:1688-1689` also refuses.

## Suggested treatment

Split the `elsif` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb:1711-1716` into two branches with two messages, keeping the existing text for the unrequested-206 case and giving the ranged-200 case its own, for example "GetObject ignored the requested Range and returned the whole representation". This adds no public constant, parameter or outcome, and the raised exception stays `Low_Level.Invalid_Response`, so it is not an interface change and needs no maintainer decision.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing `download-unexpected-range` and `download-range` lanes at `:16935-16978`. It needs one new key whose loopback response is 200 with the whole body, requested with a `Byte_Range_Header`, asserting the raised message text, which is the property the spike asserts. The spike is the test. No existing assertion has to change, because no maintained lane asserts the wording.

## Severity

The refusal is right and no data is lost: the destination file is untouched and no temporary is left behind. The cost is a misleading diagnostic. An operator whose proxy or cache strips `Range` reads that the server returned an interval nobody asked for and looks for a server that is sending 206, when the server actually returned 200 and the whole object. It needs a direct `Download_File` call with a `Byte_Range_Header` answered by a server or intermediary that ignores `Range`; multi-subject transfers never pass a range and cannot reach it. Deterministic, since the branch is a pure function of the range-header length and the status.

## Summary
