The ListObjectVersions decoder validates every `<Version>/<StorageClass>` against the pinned one-value `ObjectVersionStorageClass` enumeration, so a page in which any single version reports a different class raises `Malformed_Version_Listing` and the client turns it into `Invalid_Response`. The whole page is lost, not just the offending entry, and there is no cursor with which to skip it. MinIO accepts `x-amz-storage-class: REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` on PutObject and reports that class back inside `<Version>`, so `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` returns nothing for any bucket holding such an object. The same crate accepts all fifteen storage classes for the same object through the ListObjects decoder.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

`Finish_Field` validates the element against the model enumeration and raises (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:798-805`):

```ada
         when Storage_Class_Field =>
            Require_Name ("StorageClass");
            if not Valid_Enumeration (Value, Version_Member_Shape (5)) then
               raise Malformed_Version_Listing with
                 "invalid ObjectVersion storage class";
            end if;
            Item.Current_Version.Storage_Class := Item.Text_Value;
            Item.Current_Version.Has_Storage_Class := True;
```

`Version_Member_Shape` resolves output member 6, `Versions`, then that list's member shape, then member N of `ObjectVersion` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:355-363`):

```ada
   function Version_Member_Shape (Member : Positive)
      return Model.Shape_Index
   is
      Version_List : constant Model.Shape_Index := Output_Member_Shape (6);
      Version_Shape : constant Model.Shape_Index := Model.Shape_Index
        (Model.List_Member_Shape (Version_List));
   begin
      return Model.Member_Shape (Version_Shape, Member);
   end Version_Member_Shape;
```

`Valid_Enumeration` has no permissive branch: it returns False for any value outside the list, and False again when the shape carries no enumeration (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:365-381`):

```ada
   function Valid_Enumeration
     (Value : String; Shape : Model.Shape_Index) return Boolean
   is
      Scalar : constant Model.Shape_Index :=
        (if Model.Kind (Shape) = Model.List_Shape
         then Model.Shape_Index (Model.List_Member_Shape (Shape))
         else Shape);
   begin
      if Model.Enumeration_Count (Scalar) = 0 then
         return False;
      end if;
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) does define that member with a one-value enumeration, so the decoder is faithful to the model. Printed with `python3`:

```
ObjectVersionStorageClass: {"type": "string", "enum": ["STANDARD"]}
ObjectStorageClass enum count: 15
ObjectStorageClass: ['STANDARD', 'REDUCED_REDUNDANCY', 'GLACIER', 'STANDARD_IA', 'ONEZONE_IA', 'INTELLIGENT_TIERING', 'DEEP_ARCHIVE', 'OUTPOSTS', 'GLACIER_IR', 'SNOW', 'EXPRESS_ONEZONE', 'FSX_OPENZFS', 'FSX_ONTAP', 'AWS_BACKUP_WARM', 'AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM']
ObjectVersion required: None
ObjectVersion StorageClass member: {"shape": "ObjectVersionStorageClass"}
```

The defect is that the enumeration is applied to a decoder, where a real server's value arrives. The `Object` shape used by ListObjects has fifteen values for the same physical object, and the sibling codec validates against those (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:646-653`):

```ada
         when Object_Storage_Class_Field =>
            if not Valid_Object_Enumeration (Value, 7) then
               raise Malformed_Listing with
                 "invalid S3 object storage class";
            end if;
            Item.Current_Object.Storage_Class := Item.Text_Value;
```

So the same object decodes through ListObjectsV2 with `StorageClass=REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` and makes ListObjectVersions raise.

The client turns the exception into an invalid response with no partial page (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2226-2229`):

```ada
   exception
      when S3.Versions.Malformed_Version_Listing |
           S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed ListObjectVersions response";
```

MinIO returns the offending value. The pinned image, `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e` from `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, whose `--version` prints `minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)`, answered a signed `PUT` carrying `x-amz-storage-class: REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` with 200. A signed `GET /bucket?versions` on that versioned bucket then answered 200 with a `ListVersionsResult` whose first version element is, verbatim:

```
<Version><Key>obj-reduced-redundancy</Key><LastModified>2026-09-07T06:56:08.281Z</LastModified><ETag>&#34;9dd4e461268c8034f5c8564e155c67a6&#34;</ETag><Size>1</Size><Owner><ID>02d6176db174dc93cb1b899f7c6078f08654445fe8cf1b6ce98d8855f66bdbf4</ID><DisplayName>minio</DisplayName></Owner><StorageClass>REDUCED_REDUNDANCY</StorageClass><IsLatest>true</IsLatest><VersionId>8d8cfaec-244b-48b2-810c-3c203c9f335e</VersionId></Version>
```

The same MinIO refuses `STANDARD_IA`, `GLACIER`, `ONEZONE_IA` and `INTELLIGENT_TIERING` on PutObject with `InvalidStorageClass`, so `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` is the reachable non-`STANDARD` class there.

There is a second, smaller drift beside the enumeration: `Finish_Version` makes the member mandatory although the model marks it optional and gives `ObjectVersion` no `required` list at all (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:893-902`):

```ada
   procedure Finish_Version (Item : in out Version_Listing_Handler) is
      Value : Object_Version renames Item.Current_Version;
   begin
      if not Value.Has_Key or else US.Length (Value.Key) = 0
        or else not Value.Has_Version_ID
        or else US.Length (Value.Version_ID) = 0
        or else not Value.Has_Is_Latest
        or else not Value.Has_Last_Modified
        or else not Value.Has_Size
        or else not Value.Has_Storage_Class
```

The in-house server can never expose the defect, because its own serializer applies the same one-value enumeration before emitting a page (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:1202-1216`):

```ada
      procedure Validate_Version (Item : Object_Version) is
      begin
         if not Item.Has_Key or else US.Length (Item.Key) = 0
```

ending with

```ada
           or else not Valid_Enumeration
             (US.To_String (Item.Storage_Class), Version_Member_Shape (5))
```

No third-party lane can expose it either. The only implementation-corpus caller of `List_Versions_Page` sits inside a routine that returns immediately unless the implementation under test is a Flyology backend (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3318-3333`):

```ada
      Implementation_Variable : constant String :=
        "FLYOLOGY_S3_IMPLEMENTATION";
      Files_Implementation  : constant String := "flyology-files";
      SQLite_Implementation : constant String := "flyology-sqlite";
   begin
      if not Ada.Environment_Variables.Exists (Implementation_Variable)
        or else
          Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Implementation_Variable) not in
            Files_Implementation | SQLite_Implementation
      then
         if Phase = Complete_Durable_Routing then
            return;
         end if;
```

so no MinIO, RustFS or SeaweedFS lane runs ListObjectVersions at all, despite a Docker matrix that runs MinIO on every change.

Two artefacts assert the current behaviour and must change with a fix. `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb:335-341`:

```ada
   Expect_Bad
     (Page
        ("<Version><Size>1</Size><StorageClass>GLACIER</StorageClass>" &
         "<Key>key</Key><VersionId>v</VersionId>" &
         "<IsLatest>true</IsLatest>" &
         "<LastModified>2026-08-23T01:02:03Z</LastModified></Version>"),
      "accepted unmodeled storage class");
```

and the vector table that pins it, `tests/corpora/list-object-versions/members.tsv:30`:

```
version	491	5	StorageClass	body	decoded	Require exactly one pinned ObjectVersion storage class value.	LOV-RS-001,LOV-RS-006,LOV-RS-013
```

The strictness is documented as intended at `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:90-91`:

```
Every ObjectVersion has a unique key/version identity, nonnegative signed
64-bit size, exact pinned storage class, canonical boolean and ISO-8601
```

but the only place it reaches a user is the end of the matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:67`, which says `no external interoperability claim` and does not name storage classes.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below decodes a minimal `ListVersionsResult` holding one version, once per storage class, through `S3.Versions.Parse_List_Object_Versions`; runs the same five classes through `S3.Listings.Parse_List_Objects` to show the crate accepts them there; then replays the exact `ListVersionsResult` body the pinned MinIO returned, 1157 bytes, and asserts both versions decode. The `STANDARD` case is the control.

```ada
   function Versions_Page (Storage_Class : String) return String is
     ("<ListVersionsResult xmlns=""" & NS & """>" &
      "<Name>b</Name><MaxKeys>1000</MaxKeys>" &
      "<IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>" &
      "<Version><Key>k</Key><VersionId>v1</VersionId>" &
      "<IsLatest>true</IsLatest>" &
      "<LastModified>2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z</LastModified>" &
      "<ETag>&quot;e&quot;</ETag><Size>1</Size>" &
      "<StorageClass>" & Storage_Class & "</StorageClass>" &
      "</Version></ListVersionsResult>");
...
         Page : constant Versions.List_Object_Versions_Result :=
           Versions.Parse_List_Object_Versions (Versions_Page (Storage_Class));
      begin
         Check (Natural (Page.Versions.Length) = 1,
                "ListObjectVersions keeps the " & Storage_Class & " version");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- ListObjectVersions <StorageClass>STANDARD</StorageClass>: decoded, versions=1
  ok: ListObjectVersions keeps the STANDARD version
  ok: ListObjectVersions preserves STANDARD
  -- ListObjectVersions <StorageClass>REDUCED_REDUNDANCY</StorageClass>: REJECTED, malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document
  FAILED: ListObjectVersions accepts a REDUCED_REDUNDANCY version
  -- ListObjectVersions <StorageClass>STANDARD_IA</StorageClass>: REJECTED, malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document
  FAILED: ListObjectVersions accepts a STANDARD_IA version
  -- ListObjectVersions <StorageClass>GLACIER</StorageClass>: REJECTED, malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document
  FAILED: ListObjectVersions accepts a GLACIER version
  -- ListObjectVersions <StorageClass>INTELLIGENT_TIERING</StorageClass>: REJECTED, malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document
  FAILED: ListObjectVersions accepts a INTELLIGENT_TIERING version
  -- --- same classes through the ListObjects v1 decoder ---
  --   ListObjects v1 <StorageClass>STANDARD</StorageClass>: decoded, objects=1
  --   ListObjects v1 <StorageClass>REDUCED_REDUNDANCY</StorageClass>: decoded, objects=1
  --   ListObjects v1 <StorageClass>STANDARD_IA</StorageClass>: decoded, objects=1
  --   ListObjects v1 <StorageClass>GLACIER</StorageClass>: decoded, objects=1
  --   ListObjects v1 <StorageClass>INTELLIGENT_TIERING</StorageClass>: decoded, objects=1
  -- --- verbatim MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z body ---
  -- body bytes 1157
  -- MinIO page REJECTED, malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document
  FAILED: the MinIO ListVersionsResult decodes with both versions
BUG REPRODUCED: 5 failed expectation(s)
```

The five classes differ by one element and nothing else, and all five decode through the ListObjects path, so the document shape and the harness are not what fires.

A second program drove the same case live against the pinned MinIO through the crate's own high-level client. It created a versioned bucket, put one object with `x-amz-storage-class: REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` (accepted, 200) and one with the default class, read `GET ?versions` directly to record the 200 body quoted above, and then called `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` on that bucket and on a second versioned bucket holding only a default-class object. Both calls raised, exit status 1:

```
raised Invalid_Response: malformed ListObjectVersions response
```

The second bucket holds only `STANDARD` versions and still fails, for the separate reason reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/59.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A valid 200 `ListVersionsResult` from a stock server must decode, whatever storage class its versions report. The model's one-value enumeration describes what AWS itself writes, not what an S3-compatible server may send, and the crate already accepts the fifteen-value domain for the same object through `S3.Listings` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:646-653`). Losing a whole page over one entry is the wrong failure mode in any case, since the decoder offers no way to skip past it.

## Suggested treatment

This needs a maintainer decision, because either choice widens a publicly observable acceptance domain:

- Accept any non-empty `StorageClass` text in `Storage_Class_Field` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:798-805`), keeping the element required but not the value domain. Widest, and immune to future AWS storage classes.
- Validate against the fifteen-value `ObjectStorageClass` used by the sibling codec, which makes the two listing decoders agree. This also changes the server-side `Validate_Version` (`:1202-1216`), because the serializer would then be free to emit those classes.

The smaller drift beside it is a separate decision: `Finish_Version` requires `Has_Storage_Class` at `:900` although the model marks the member optional and gives the shape no `required` list, so a server that omits the element loses the page too.

Whichever is chosen, `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:90-91` states "exact pinned storage class" as the contract being changed and must be reworded, and `tests/corpora/list-object-versions/members.tsv:30` must be updated with it.

The regression tests belong in `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb`: invert `Expect_Bad` at `:335-341` into an acceptance assertion that the decoded version reports `GLACIER`, and add the MinIO `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` page as a second case. The spike is the test. Because the failure is a real-server interop break, one MinIO `?versions` call added to `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` outside the Flyology-only gate at `:3318-3333` would catch a regression the in-process corpus cannot.

This finding and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/59 both break ListObjectVersions against MinIO and must land together. Fixing this one alone changes nothing at all against MinIO, because the next-marker rule rejects every untruncated page before any storage class is reached.

## Severity

Every ListObjectVersions call against a bucket holding a version in any class other than `STANDARD` fails outright: `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` raises `Invalid_Response` and returns no page, and there is no marker with which to page past the entry. On MinIO that means any bucket holding a `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` object, which MinIO accepts by default on PutObject; on AWS it means any bucket with lifecycle-transitioned versions. It is a pure function of the response text, so no timing or container state is involved. The matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:67` disclaims external interoperability for this operation, so no documented promise is broken, and a caller using only Flyology backends never sees it. Deterministic.

## Summary

`S3.Listings.Validate` treats "AWS sends `NextMarker` only when a delimiter was requested" as a wire rule and raises `Malformed_Listing` when a truncated page carries the element without a non-empty `<Delimiter>`. MinIO, RustFS and SeaweedFS all emit `NextMarker` unconditionally, so paging any bucket with more than `max-keys` objects and no delimiter against those servers loses the very first page and pagination never starts. The compatibility matrix claims a six-server oracle for this operation while the three Docker scripts switch the case off through an environment variable, so the lane that would have caught it is disabled rather than fixed.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

An echoed empty `<Delimiter/>` cannot rescue the page, because the flag requires non-empty text (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1093-1094`):

```ada
      Effective_Delimiter : constant Boolean :=
        Value.Has_Delimiter and then US.Length (Value.Delimiter) > 0;
```

and the raise ties the presence of `NextMarker` to that flag (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1108-1116`):

```ada
      elsif Value.Has_Next_Marker /=
        (Value.Is_Truncated and then Effective_Delimiter)
        or else
          (Value.Has_Next_Marker and then US.Length (Value.Next_Marker) = 0)
        or else
          (not Value.Has_Next_Marker
           and then US.Length (Value.Next_Marker) > 0)
      then
         raise Malformed_Listing with "inconsistent S3 next marker";
```

`Parse_List_Objects` always calls `Validate`, with no bypass (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1152-1158`):

```ada
      if not Handler.Seen_Name
        or else not Handler.Seen_Max_Keys
        or else not Handler.Seen_Is_Truncated
      then
         raise Malformed_Listing with "S3 listing lacks required fields";
      end if;
      Validate (Handler.V1_Value);
```

and the client converts the exception into an invalid response, so no page is returned (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1693-1695`):

```ada
   exception
      when S3.Listings.Malformed_Listing | S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed ListObjects response";
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) imposes no such constraint. `ListObjectsOutput` has no `required` list and `NextMarker` is a plain optional string. The AWS rule the crate has turned into a wire check is carried in the model as prose addressed to a client, printed with `python3` and stripped of markup:

```
ListObjectsOutput required: None
NextMarker member: {"shape": "NextMarker"}
NextMarker documentation: When the response is truncated (the IsTruncated element value in the response is true), you can use the key name in this field as the marker parameter in the subsequent request to get the next set of objects. Amazon S3 lists objects in alphabetical order. This element is returned only if you have the delimiter request parameter specified. If the response does not include the NextMarker element and it is truncated, you can use the value of the last Key element in the response as the marker parameter in the subsequent request to get the next set of object keys.
```

That paragraph tells a client what it may rely on. It does not forbid a server from sending the element, and the model records no error for it.

Real servers send it. The pinned MinIO image, `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e` from `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, whose `--version` prints `minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)`, answered a signed `GET /bucket?max-keys=1` on a three-key bucket with 200 and, verbatim apart from the bucket name and the object entry:

```
<ListBucketResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/">...<Marker></Marker><NextMarker>k1[minio_cache:v2,return:]</NextMarker><MaxKeys>1</MaxKeys><IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated>...
```

The same request with `delimiter=/` answered 200 with the same `NextMarker` and one extra element:

```
...<NextMarker>k1[minio_cache:v2,return:]</NextMarker><MaxKeys>1</MaxKeys><Delimiter>/</Delimiter><IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated>...
```

The two bodies differ by exactly that `<Delimiter>` element, and that one element decides whether the crate accepts the page.

Accepting the element costs the client nothing, because it already derives the marker from the last key when `NextMarker` is absent (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:403-415`):

```ada
               if Page.Is_Truncated then
                  if Page.Has_Next_Marker then
                     Next := US.To_Unbounded_String
                       (Logical_Marker (US.To_String (Page.Next_Marker)));
                  elsif not Page.Contents.Is_Empty then
                     Next := US.To_Unbounded_String
                       (Logical_Marker
                          (US.To_String (Page.Contents.Last_Element.Key)));
```

The repository already knows about the behaviour and has switched the check off rather than fixed it. `Check_List_Objects_V1_Pagination` returns `False` for the three server-specific oracle modes, skipping the subcase (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:258-274`):

```ada
   function Check_List_Objects_V1_Pagination return Boolean is
      Name : constant String := "FLYOLOGY_LIST_OBJECTS_V1_ORACLE_MODE";
   begin
      if not Ada.Environment_Variables.Exists (Name) then
         return True;
      elsif Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Name) =
        "rustfs-rc3-next-marker-without-delimiter"
        or else Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Name) =
          "seaweedfs-4.43-next-marker-without-delimiter"
        or else Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Name) =
          "minio-2025-next-marker-without-delimiter"
      then
         return False;
```

and the three Docker scripts set exactly those values. `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:66-67`:

```sh
FLYOLOGY_LIST_OBJECTS_V1_ORACLE_MODE=\
minio-2025-next-marker-without-delimiter \
```

`tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh:71-72`:

```sh
FLYOLOGY_LIST_OBJECTS_V1_ORACLE_MODE=\
rustfs-rc3-next-marker-without-delimiter \
```

`tests/scripts/test-seaweedfs.sh:78-79`:

```sh
FLYOLOGY_LIST_OBJECTS_V1_ORACLE_MODE=\
seaweedfs-4.43-next-marker-without-delimiter \
```

The exclusion is recorded in prose at `docs/qualification/list-objects-v1.md:54-58`:

```
All 18 lanes passed on 2026-08-22. Pinned
RustFS, SeaweedFS, and MinIO each emit a non-model NextMarker on a truncated
v1 page without a delimiter. Their strict no-delimiter continuation subcase is
excluded explicitly; the basic v1 path remains enabled, while every Flyology
backend passes the complete two-page AWS fallback rule.
```

while the matrix row still advertises the oracle, `docs/compatibility/s3.md:65`, whose evidence column ends:

```
strict presence/query/XML/header and socket corpora, native/lightweight six-server oracle, and five-server signed raw-XML benchmark
```

The one existing test in this area covers the other direction and stays correct: `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:9597-9599` rejects a marker on a final page.

```ada
      Must_Reject_Document
        (Empty_Listing ("<NextMarker>unexpected</NextMarker>"),
         "ListObjects marker on final page was accepted");
```

The truncated no-delimiter case has no test. The socket corpus exercises the shape the in-house server emits, a truncated no-delimiter page with no `NextMarker` at all (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:11895-11900`), which never reaches the failing branch.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below puts four documents through `S3.Listings.Parse_List_Objects`. Two are the verbatim MinIO bodies quoted above, differing by the single `<Delimiter>` element; one is the truncated no-delimiter page without `NextMarker` that the in-house server emits; one is a minimal synthetic equivalent, so the finding does not rest on any MinIO-specific text. The delimiter page and the marker-free page are the controls.

```ada
   Minimal_No_Delimiter : constant String :=
     "<ListBucketResult xmlns=""" & NS & """>" &
     "<Name>b</Name><MaxKeys>1</MaxKeys>" &
     "<IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated><NextMarker>a</NextMarker>" &
     "<Contents><Key>a</Key><Size>1</Size></Contents></ListBucketResult>";
...
         Page : constant Listings.List_Objects_Result :=
           Listings.Parse_List_Objects (Document, XML.Default_Limits);
      begin
         ...
            Check (Page.Has_Next_Marker,
                   Label & ": the page keeps NextMarker");
            Check (US.To_String (Page.Next_Marker) = Expected_Marker,
                   Label & ": NextMarker text is preserved");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- MinIO max-keys=1 delimiter=/  : decoded, objects=1 truncated=TRUE has_next_marker=TRUE next_marker="k1[minio_cache:v2,return:]"
  ok: MinIO max-keys=1 delimiter=/  : the page keeps its one object
  ok: MinIO max-keys=1 delimiter=/  : the page is truncated
  ok: MinIO max-keys=1 delimiter=/  : the page keeps NextMarker
  ok: MinIO max-keys=1 delimiter=/  : NextMarker text is preserved
  -- Flyology-shaped page, no NextMarker: decoded, objects=1 truncated=TRUE has_next_marker=FALSE next_marker=""
  -- MinIO max-keys=1 no delimiter   : REJECTED, inconsistent S3 next marker
  FAILED: MinIO max-keys=1 no delimiter   : the page is accepted
  -- minimal truncated no-delimiter  : REJECTED, inconsistent S3 next marker
  FAILED: minimal truncated no-delimiter  : the page is accepted
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

A second program drove the same case live against the pinned MinIO through `Client.Objects.List_V1_Page`, on a three-key bucket with `Maximum => 1`, once with `delimiter=/` and once without. The delimiter call returned the first page; the undelimited call raised. Output, exit status 1, the two legs:

```
  -- List_V1_Page max-keys=1 delimiter=/: kind PAGE_AVAILABLE status 200
  ok: List_V1_Page returns the first page (delimiter=/)
  --   objects 1 truncated TRUE next_marker "k1[minio_cache:v2,return:]"
  -- List_V1_Page max-keys=1 no delimiter: raised Invalid_Response: ListObjects response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit
  FAILED: List_V1_Page returns the first page (no delimiter)
```

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A truncated v1 page carrying `NextMarker` must be accepted whether or not a delimiter was requested. The pinned model puts no cross-member constraint on `NextMarker`, its own documentation states the delimiter rule as advice to a client rather than a constraint on a server, and MinIO, RustFS and SeaweedFS all emit the element unconditionally. A `NextMarker` on a page that is not truncated remains an inconsistency and must still be refused.

## Suggested treatment

Relax the first disjunct at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1108-1109` to key on truncation alone:

```ada
      elsif Value.Has_Next_Marker /= Value.Is_Truncated
```

leaving the two emptiness clauses at `:1110-1114` untouched, so an empty `NextMarker` and stray marker text are still rejected and a final-page marker is still rejected. No new public constant, type or parameter is needed, and the client's existing fallback at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:403-415` already handles both page shapes.

The switch-off must be retired with the fix, in both places: delete the three `FLYOLOGY_LIST_OBJECTS_V1_ORACLE_MODE` assignments (`tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:66-67`, `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh:71-72`, `tests/scripts/test-seaweedfs.sh:78-79`) and the reader that skips the subcase for them (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:258-274`), so the three third-party lanes run the no-delimiter continuation the matrix already claims. The exclusion paragraph at `docs/qualification/list-objects-v1.md:54-58` must be removed with it.

The client-side regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing no-delimiter case at `:11885-11904`: a scripted origin serving the MinIO page with `NextMarker` and no `<Delimiter>`, asserting `Page_Available`, one object, `Is_Truncated`, and the marker text preserved. `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:9597-9599` keeps asserting the final-page rejection and needs no change. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Every ListObjects v1 listing of a bucket with more than `max-keys` objects and no delimiter fails on its first page against MinIO, RustFS and SeaweedFS: `Parse_List_Objects` raises, the low-level decoder converts it to `Invalid_Response`, and `List_V1_Page` never returns, so pagination cannot start. That is the ordinary case for a v1 listing, not an edge case, and the caller has no workaround short of passing a delimiter it does not want. The matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:65` claims a six-server oracle for the operation while the three Docker scripts disable exactly this subcase, so the claim is not met today. Flyology backends are unaffected, because the in-house server omits `NextMarker` in that case. Deterministic.

## Summary

Three otherwise identical UTF-8 validators guard the lifecycle, notification and replication serializers. Two of them stop the single-byte range one code point early, at `16#7E#` instead of `16#7F#`, so a lifecycle or notification configuration containing U+007F in any text member can be read from a server and never written back: `Serialize` raises before the request is built. U+007F is not a C0 control, XML 1.0 admits it, the matching parsers accept it, and the replication serializer accepts the same byte in the same position. The lifecycle serializer also accepts U+0080, an actual C1 control, so the stated control-character rationale does not describe what the range does.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The lifecycle validator's single-byte arm stops at `16#7E#` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:789-792`):

```ada
               if First in 16#20# .. 16#7E#
                 or else First in 16#09# | 16#0A# | 16#0D#
               then
                  Width := 1;
```

The notification validator is identical (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-notifications.adb:503-506`):

```ada
               if First in 16#20# .. 16#7E#
                 or else First in 16#09# | 16#0A# | 16#0D#
               then
                  Width := 1;
```

The replication validator, otherwise the same code, is one byte wider (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-replication.adb:828-831`):

```ada
               if First in 16#20# .. 16#7F#
                 or else First in 16#09# | 16#0A# | 16#0D#
               then
                  Width := 1;
```

The failure happens in `Add_Text`, before escaping and before any request is built (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:852-856`):

```ada
      procedure Add_Text (Text : String) is
      begin
         if not Valid_XML_UTF8 (Text) then
            raise Malformed_Lifecycle with
              "invalid lifecycle XML text";
```

and identically for notifications (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-notifications.adb:566-570`):

```ada
      procedure Add_Text (Text : String) is
      begin
         if not Valid_XML_UTF8 (Text) then
            raise Malformed_Notification with
              "invalid bucket-notification XML text";
```

The comment that justifies the range names two properties, and neither holds for U+007F (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:771-773`):

```ada
      --  XML 1.0 text is UTF-8 and excludes raw C0 controls other than tab,
      --  line feed, and carriage return. This structural wire authority keeps
      --  the serializer from emitting bytes the matching parser must reject.
```

U+007F is not a C0 control, and the matching parser does not reject it. The shared escape boundary every other codec writes through rejects only bytes below 32 other than tab, line feed and carriage return (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.adb:229-234`):

```ada
            if Character'Pos (Item) < 32
              and then Item /= Character'Val (9)
              and then Item /= Character'Val (10)
              and then Item /= Character'Val (13)
            then
               raise XML_Error with "invalid XML character";
```

so `Escape_Text` does not front-run the validators either, and the same byte survives `Replication.Serialize` and every writer-based codec.

The value is reachable from the crate's own key domain, which excludes only NUL (`src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-490`):

```ada
   function Valid_Object_Key (Value : String) return Boolean is
   begin
      if Value'Length not in 1 .. 1_024 then
         return False;
      end if;
      for Character_Value of Value loop
         if Character_Value = Character'Val (0) then
            return False;
         end if;
      end loop;
      return True;
   end Valid_Object_Key;
```

so a lifecycle `Prefix` or a tag value derived from an object key holding U+007F is a legal, if unusual, configuration.

No existing test covers U+007F. `tests/src/s3_put_bucket_lifecycle_configuration_corpus.adb` and `tests/src/s3_bucket_notification_configuration_corpus.adb` exercise C0 controls and invalid UTF-8 only, so the one-byte gap between the three validators is untested in both directions.

## What happens now

Spike: for each of Lifecycle, Notifications and Replication the program below parses a minimal valid configuration and hands the parsed value straight back to `Serialize`, once with a plain ASCII identifier and once with U+007F in it. Replication with U+007F is the key control: identical shape, identical byte, accepted. A final case uses U+0080 to show the boundary is not about control characters.

```ada
   DEL : constant String := (1 => Character'Val (16#7F#));

   function Lifecycle_Document (Suffix : String) return String is
     ("<LifecycleConfiguration><Rule><ID>rule" & Suffix & "</ID>" &
      "<Status>Enabled</Status><Expiration><Days>1</Days></Expiration>" &
      "</Rule></LifecycleConfiguration>");
...
         Value : constant Lifecycle.Lifecycle_Configuration :=
           Lifecycle.Parse (Document, XML.Default_Limits);
      begin
         ...
            Written : constant String :=
              Lifecycle.Serialize (Value, XML.Default_Limits);
...
   Round_Trip_Replication ("ID with U+007F", Replication_Document (DEL));
   Round_Trip_Lifecycle ("ID with U+007F", Lifecycle_Document (DEL));
   Round_Trip_Notifications ("Id with U+007F", Notification_Document (DEL));
   Round_Trip_Lifecycle
     ("ID with U+0080",
      Lifecycle_Document (Character'Val (16#C2#) & Character'Val (16#80#)));
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- DEL byte value 127
  -- lifecycle plain ASCII ID: Parse accepted (140 bytes)
  -- lifecycle plain ASCII ID: Serialize produced 188 bytes
  ok: lifecycle plain ASCII ID: Serialize round-trips Parse
  -- notifications plain ASCII Id: Parse accepted (196 bytes)
  -- notifications plain ASCII Id: Serialize produced 244 bytes
  ok: notifications plain ASCII Id: Serialize round-trips Parse
  -- replication plain ASCII ID: Parse accepted (211 bytes)
  -- replication plain ASCII ID: Serialize produced 259 bytes
  ok: replication plain ASCII ID: Serialize round-trips Parse
  -- replication ID with U+007F: Parse accepted (210 bytes)
  -- replication ID with U+007F: Serialize produced 258 bytes
  ok: replication ID with U+007F: Serialize round-trips Parse
  -- lifecycle ID with U+007F: Parse accepted (139 bytes)
  -- lifecycle ID with U+007F: Serialize REJECTED, invalid lifecycle XML text
  FAILED: lifecycle ID with U+007F: Serialize round-trips Parse
  -- notifications Id with U+007F: Parse accepted (195 bytes)
  -- notifications Id with U+007F: Serialize REJECTED, invalid bucket-notification XML text
  FAILED: notifications Id with U+007F: Serialize round-trips Parse
  -- lifecycle ID with U+0080: Parse accepted (140 bytes)
  -- lifecycle ID with U+0080: Serialize produced 188 bytes
  ok: lifecycle ID with U+0080: Serialize round-trips Parse
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

All three parsers accept the byte, the replication serializer writes it back, and the lifecycle serializer writes back U+0080, which is a real C1 control. The rejected cases differ from their controls by that one byte.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A configuration that `Lifecycle.Parse` or `Notifications.Parse` accepts must survive the matching `Serialize`. U+007F is XML 1.0 text, the shared escape boundary at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.adb:229-234` admits it, and the replication serializer already emits it, so the two narrow validators are the only place in the crate that refuses it.

## Suggested treatment

Change `16#7E#` to `16#7F#` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:789` and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-notifications.adb:503`, matching `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-replication.adb:828`. No public declaration changes and no new value is introduced; the accepted byte range widens by one code point, to the range the sibling serializer already uses. The comment at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:771-773` should be corrected with it, since it describes a C0-control rule that the range does not implement.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb`, in the lifecycle and notification round-trip regions: parse a configuration whose `ID` and `Id` carry U+007F and require `Serialize` to return the same document. The spike is the test. The two configuration corpora, `tests/src/s3_put_bucket_lifecycle_configuration_corpus.adb` and `tests/src/s3_bucket_notification_configuration_corpus.adb`, assert nothing about U+007F today and need no change.

## Severity

A lifecycle or notification configuration containing U+007F in `ID`, `Prefix`, a tag key or value, `Id`, or a filter value can be read from a server but never written back: `Serialize` raises `Malformed_Lifecycle` or `Malformed_Notification` before any request is built, so nothing is sent, nothing is lost, and no server sees a bad document. It needs an unusual byte to arrive in a configuration value, most plausibly a prefix or tag derived from an object key that the crate's own `Valid_Object_Key` permits, and no reachable caller produces it by accident. The failure is a clean exception with a correct diagnostic. Deterministic.

## Summary

The ListBucketInventoryConfigurations, ListBucketMetricsConfigurations, ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurations and ListDirectoryBuckets decoders compare the response root element against the botocore output shape name and raise when it differs. The AWS S3 API Reference documents a different root for each of those four operations, and AWS is the only provider that implements them, so every 200 response fails to decode. The pinned model carries no root name for any list output, so the comment that says the names come from the model is not supported by the model. The in-house server emits the same four names on the other side, which is why every in-repository round-trip passes.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

Each decoder pins the shape name as the root, under a comment naming the model as the authority (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-inventory.adb:608-614`):

```ada
   --  Both element names are fixed by the pinned Botocore operation/output
   --  model. Changing either changes accepted S3 wire documents.
   package Page_Decoder is new Paginated_REST_XML_Reads
     (Root_Name                   =>
        "ListBucketInventoryConfigurationsOutput",
      Item_Container_Name         => "",
      Item_Name                   => "InventoryConfiguration",
```

`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-metrics.adb:470-472`:

```ada
   package Page_Decoder is new Paginated_REST_XML_Reads
     (Root_Name                  =>
        "ListBucketMetricsConfigurationsOutput",
```

`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-analytics.adb:574-576`:

```ada
   package Page_Decoder is new Paginated_REST_XML_Reads
     (Root_Name                   =>
        "ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationsOutput",
```

and the generated ListDirectoryBuckets decoder (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:9-13`):

```ada

   begin
      case Element is
         when Root =>
            return "ListDirectoryBucketsOutput";
```

The comparison is exact, on open and on close (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-paginated_rest_xml_reads.adb:133-136`):

```ada
      elsif Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Item.Root_Seen or else Local_Name /= Root_Name then
            raise Malformed_Page with "invalid configuration-list root";
```

and `:283-286`:

```ada
      elsif Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Local_Name /= Root_Name then
            raise Malformed_Page with
```

The generated decoder's runtime does the same (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-strict_xml_codecs.adb:312-315`):

```ada
      if Item.Frames.Is_Empty then
         Element := Root_Element;
         if Local_Name /= Element_Name (Root_Element) then
            raise Malformed_Document with "invalid XML root";
```

The names come from one generator rule, not from the model (`tools/s3_codegen.py:815-816`):

```python
    root_shape = payload_shape(model, operation, entry)
    root_name = shapes[root_shape].get("locationName", root_shape)
```

The same rule is applied to request payloads at `tools/s3_codegen.py:1932`, where it is right, because every request payload member carries a `locationName`. Output shapes carry nothing, so the rule silently falls back to the shape name. Printed from the pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) with `python3`:

```
ListBucketInventoryConfigurationsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListBucketMetricsConfigurationsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListDirectoryBucketsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListBucketsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListObjectVersionsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
ListObjectsOutput locationName= None resultWrapper= None xmlNamespace= None payload= None
```

The model is silent for every one of them, including the three whose wire roots the crate already hard-codes correctly, so the model cannot be the authority the four comments claim. Those three are `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-buckets.adb:913-916`:

```ada
      if Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Local_Name /= "ListAllMyBucketsResult" then
            raise Malformed_Bucket_Listing with
              "unexpected ListBuckets root";
```

`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:689-692`:

```ada
      if Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Local_Name /= "ListBucketResult" then
            raise Malformed_Listing with
              "S3 listing root is not ListBucketResult";
```

and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:527-530`:

```ada
      elsif Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Item.Root_Seen or else Local_Name /= "ListVersionsResult" then
            raise Malformed_Version_Listing with
              "invalid ListObjectVersions response root";
```

The two metadata reads do the same at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-metadata_configurations.adb:209-213` and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-metadata_tables.adb:293-297`.

The AWS S3 API Reference, read from each operation's Response Syntax block and from the root-level-tag entry in its Response Elements section, shows a different root for each of the four:

- `API_ListBucketInventoryConfigurations.html` shows `<ListInventoryConfigurationsResult>`.
- `API_ListBucketMetricsConfigurations.html` shows `<ListMetricsConfigurationsResult>`.
- `API_ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurations.html` shows `<ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationResult>`, with `Configuration` singular.
- `API_ListDirectoryBuckets.html` shows `<ListAllMyDirectoryBucketsResult>`.

`API_ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurations.html` shows `<ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput>`, that is, the shape name really is the AWS root for that one operation, which is presumably why the pattern looked safe. That fifth decoder (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-intelligent_tiering.adb:530-532`) is correct and is used below as the control.

Four public entry points reach the affected decoders: `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:9277` (`Metrics.Parse_List`), `:9319` (`Analytics.Parse_List`), `:9577` (`Inventory.Parse_List`) and `:16793` (`Generated_List_Directory_Buckets_XML.Parse`).

No pinned third-party server can settle it. The pinned MinIO image, `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e` from `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, whose `--version` prints `minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)`, answered signed `GET /bucket?inventory`, `?metrics`, `?analytics` and `?intelligent-tiering` with 501 and an `<Error>` root in all four cases. AWS is the only provider that implements them.

No in-repository test can catch it either, because the server emits the same four names. `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:9599-9608`:

```ada
                  function Root_Name return String is
                    (case Family is
                        when Analytics_Family =>
                          "ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationsOutput",
                        when Metrics_Family =>
                          "ListBucketMetricsConfigurationsOutput",
                        when Intelligent_Tiering_Family =>
                          "ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput",
```

and `:7235-7237`:

One of the four is documented as the server's contract, `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:12-14`:

```

deadline, and backend-availability behavior, discards the general-purpose
```

so client, generator, server and that sentence disagree with AWS together and agree with each other, and every round-trip in the repository passes.

Separately, the generated decoder re-raises with no message, so its caller sees a source location instead of a reason (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:272-274`):

```ada
         raise Malformed_Document;
```
