## What happens now

Spike: the program below feeds each of `Inventory.Parse_List`, `Metrics.Parse_List`, `Analytics.Parse_List`, `Intelligent_Tiering.Parse_List` and `Generated_List_Directory_Buckets_XML.Parse` two documents that differ only in the root element name: the shape name the crate pins today, recorded but not asserted, and the root the AWS API Reference documents, asserted. Intelligent-Tiering, where the two coincide, is the passing control.

```ada
   function Empty_List (Root : String) return String is
     ("<" & Root & " xmlns=""" & NS & """>" &
      "<IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>" &
      "</" & Root & ">");
...
   Try_Inventory ("ListInventoryConfigurationsResult", True);
   Try_Metrics ("ListMetricsConfigurationsResult", True);
   Try_Analytics ("ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationResult", True);
   Try_Directory_Buckets ("ListAllMyDirectoryBucketsResult", True);
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- --- controls: the shape-name roots the crate pins today ---
  -- Inventory.Parse_List <ListBucketInventoryConfigurationsOutput>: accepted, has_is_truncated=TRUE
  -- Metrics.Parse_List <ListBucketMetricsConfigurationsOutput>: accepted, has_is_truncated=TRUE
  -- Analytics.Parse_List <ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationsOutput>: accepted, has_is_truncated=TRUE
  -- ListDirectoryBuckets <ListDirectoryBucketsOutput>: accepted, buckets=1
  -- --- control: Intelligent-Tiering, where AWS really does document the shape name as the root ---
  -- Intelligent_Tiering.Parse_List <ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput>: accepted, has_is_truncated=TRUE
  ok: Intelligent_Tiering.Parse_List accepts <ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput>
  -- --- the finding: the roots the AWS API Reference documents ---
  -- Inventory.Parse_List <ListInventoryConfigurationsResult>: REJECTED, malformed inventory-list XML
  FAILED: Inventory.Parse_List accepts <ListInventoryConfigurationsResult>
  -- Metrics.Parse_List <ListMetricsConfigurationsResult>: REJECTED, malformed metrics-list XML
  FAILED: Metrics.Parse_List accepts <ListMetricsConfigurationsResult>
  -- Analytics.Parse_List <ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationResult>: REJECTED, malformed analytics-list XML
  FAILED: Analytics.Parse_List accepts <ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationResult>
  -- ListDirectoryBuckets <ListAllMyDirectoryBucketsResult>: REJECTED, flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:274
  FAILED: ListDirectoryBuckets decoder accepts <ListAllMyDirectoryBucketsResult>
BUG REPRODUCED: 4 failed expectation(s)
```

Each pair of documents differs by the root name and nothing else, and the Intelligent-Tiering leg passes with the same document shape, so the body content and the harness are not what fires. The last line prints a source location rather than a diagnostic because of the message-less re-raise quoted above.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image, which answers 501 for all four operations. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

Each of the four decoders must accept the root element that the AWS S3 API Reference documents for its operation: `<ListInventoryConfigurationsResult>`, `<ListMetricsConfigurationsResult>`, `<ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationResult>` and `<ListAllMyDirectoryBucketsResult>`. The pinned model records no root name for any list output, so it cannot be the source for these values, and the crate already hard-codes the true wire root wherever it differs from the shape name for the older listings.

## Suggested treatment

One change across the generator, the three hand-written decoders and the server emitters, since the two sides must agree:

- Give `tools/s3_codegen.py` an explicit response-root table keyed by operation, consulted before the `locationName`-or-shape-name fallback at `:815-816`, and regenerate `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb`.
- Set the three `Root_Name` actuals to the documented roots at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-inventory.adb:611-612`, `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-metrics.adb:471-472` and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-analytics.adb:575-576`, and correct the comment above each, which currently names the model as the authority.
- Change the server emitters to match, at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:9599-9608` and `:7235-7237`, and reword `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:12-14`, which documents the wrong root as the server's contract.
- Leave `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-intelligent_tiering.adb:530-532` alone.

This needs a maintainer decision on where the four names live and how their authority is recorded. They are externally mandated protocol values with no source in the pinned model, which the "Decision authority" section of `AGENTS.md` requires be recorded at the declaration:

- An operation-keyed response-root table in `tools/s3_codegen.py` with the AWS API Reference cited beside it, so the generated and hand-written decoders draw on one recorded source.
- Four hand-written string literals in the four bodies, each with its own citation, matching how `ListAllMyBucketsResult`, `ListBucketResult` and `ListVersionsResult` are handled today.

While the generated decoder is being regenerated, its message-less re-raise at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:272-274` should carry the inner diagnostic, so a root mismatch reports a reason rather than a source location.

The regression tests belong in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing socket cases for the four operations: serve each documented root and require the page to decode, and keep one case per operation asserting that a foreign root is still refused. The spike is the test. No existing test asserts the current roots as client behaviour, but the four qualification suites drive the in-house server and will start failing the moment the client changes without the server, which is why both sides belong in one change.

## Severity

Every 200 response from AWS for ListBucketInventoryConfigurations, ListBucketMetricsConfigurations, ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurations and ListDirectoryBuckets fails to decode, so the four operations do not work against the only provider that implements them. The caller sees `Malformed_Inventory`, `Malformed_Metrics`, `Malformed_Analytics` or a bare `Malformed_Document`, and there is no workaround at the call site. It is a comparison of two strings, so it is total and involves no timing or server state. The matrix rows for the three configuration listings, `docs/compatibility/s3.md:37`, `:38` and `:40`, each end with "external interoperability are not claimed", so no documented promise is broken on the client side, and a caller that only drives the in-house server never sees it. ListDirectoryBuckets has no matrix row at all, and `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:12-14` positively documents the non-AWS root as the server's contract. Deterministic; not reproduced against a live AWS endpoint, since no pinned third-party server implements the operations.

## Summary

`Versioning.Parse` is the only request-body codec in the crate that treats the S3 namespace as a wire precondition, and the server calls exactly that one. A `PUT /bucket?versioning` whose body omits `xmlns` is answered 400 `MalformedXML`, while the same client's lifecycle, notification and CreateBucket bodies are accepted unqualified by their own parsers. MinIO's `mc`, the default way to turn versioning on against any S3 endpoint, sends the body without a namespace and with a correct `Content-MD5`, so `mc version enable` cannot enable versioning on a Flyology server at all. The leniency already exists in the same package and is simply not wired to the server path.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The handler rejects any root that is neither the S3 namespace nor, when permitted, the empty one (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versioning.adb:47-54`):

```ada
      if (Namespace_URI /= "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"
          and then
            (not Item.Allow_Empty_Namespace or else Namespace_URI'Length > 0))
        or else Attribute_Count /= 0
      then
         raise Malformed_Configuration with
           "bucket versioning namespace or attributes are invalid";
      end if;
```

The two entry points differ only in that flag (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versioning.adb:171-183`):

```ada
   function Parse
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := Default_Limits)
      return Bucket_Versioning_Configuration is
     (Parse_Document
        (Document, Limits, Allow_Empty_Namespace => False));

   function Parse_Response
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := Default_Limits)
      return Bucket_Versioning_Configuration is
     (Parse_Document
        (Document, Limits, Allow_Empty_Namespace => True));
```

and the server calls the strict one, after the Content-MD5 and checksum gates have already passed (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:10421-10425`):

```ada
                           else
                              declare
                                 Configuration : constant
                                   Bucket_Versioning_Configuration :=
                                     Versioning.Parse (Document);
```

Every sibling request-body parser the server calls accepts an unqualified root. Lifecycle treats it as one of two acceptable styles (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:70-74`):

```ada
      Style : constant Namespace_Style :=
        (if Namespace_URI'Length = 0 then Unqualified
         elsif Namespace_URI = "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"
         then S3_Qualified
         else Namespace_Not_Selected);
```

Notifications is identical (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-notifications.adb:166-170`), and `Buckets.Parse_Create_Configuration` has no namespace check at all, matching only the root element name (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-buckets.adb:539-544`):

```ada
      if Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Item.Root_Seen or else Local_Name /= "CreateBucketConfiguration"
         then
            raise Malformed_Bucket_Configuration with
              "invalid CreateBucket root";
         end if;
```

The crate's own specification says why the leniency exists (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versioning.ads:30-32`):

```ada
   --  Parse an implementation response. AWS responses use the S3 namespace,
   --  while a few otherwise compatible implementations omit it; foreign
   --  namespaces and all element attributes still fail closed.
```

That sentence is attached to `Parse_Response`, and the same implementations omit the namespace on the request.

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) does not make the namespace a parsing precondition. It records the namespace on the payload member, which is what a serializer writes. Printed with `python3`:

```
PutBucketVersioningRequest payload: VersioningConfiguration required: ['Bucket', 'VersioningConfiguration']
VersioningConfiguration member: {"shape": "VersioningConfiguration", "locationName": "VersioningConfiguration", "xmlNamespace": {"uri": "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"}}
```

A stock client sends the body without it. MinIO `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z (minio-go/v7.0.90), from the image `minio/mc@sha256:a7fe349ef4bd8521fb8497f55c6042871b2ae640607cf99d9bede5e9bdf11727`, was pointed at this crate's own in-process S3 application through a plain logging TCP relay, because `mc` runs inside Docker and the application listens on loopback. The bytes below are the relay's capture of the real socket for `mc version enable`:

```
PUT /stock-versioning-3/?versioning= HTTP/1.1
Host: host.docker.internal:58080
User-Agent: MinIO (linux; arm64) minio-go/v7.0.90 mc/RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z
Content-Length: 75
Accept-Encoding: zstd,gzip
Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=AKIDEXAMPLE/20260907/us-east-1/s3/aws4_request, SignedHeaders=content-md5;host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date, Signature=73a0504faf9bf169dcdd61d8d4da80c930e45f96c1e44da905946d7314467113
Content-Md5: 8qj8HSeDu3APPMQZVG06WQ==
X-Amz-Content-Sha256: dd6f6f21cc8680cc5c32bba98d4297e37552279d7e326a35df847ed2713f2d6a
X-Amz-Date: 20260907T065759Z

<VersioningConfiguration><Status>Enabled</Status></VersioningConfiguration>
===== SERVER -> CLIENT =====
HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request
Date: Mon, 07 Sep 2026 06:57:59 GMT
Content-Length: 235
Content-Type: application/xml

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/stock-versioning-3/?versioning=</Resource></Error>
```

`mc` reported:

```
mc: <ERROR> Unable to enable versioning. The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema.
```

The 75-byte body carries no `xmlns`, and the correct `Content-MD5` cleared every checksum gate, so the absent namespace is the only reason for the rejection. There is no client-side workaround: `mc` offers no option to add a namespace.

Two other stock clients are unaffected by this defect, because both serialize the model's `xmlNamespace`. Against the same listener, aws-cli 2.36.34 (Python 3.14.7) sent, captured on the same relay:

```
PUT /stock-versioning-2?versioning HTTP/1.1
Host: 127.0.0.1:58080
User-Agent: aws-cli/2.36.34 md/awscrt#0.36.2 ua/2.1 os/macos#25.6.0 md/arch#arm64 lang/python#3.14.7 md/pyimpl#CPython ... md/command#s3api.put-bucket-versioning
...
<VersioningConfiguration xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Status>Enabled</Status></VersioningConfiguration>
```

and boto3 with botocore 1.43.89 sent the same body. Both were refused for an unrelated checksum reason:

```
An error occurred (InvalidRequest) when calling the PutBucketVersioning operation: PutBucketVersioning requires one valid Content-MD5 header
```

so neither is evidence for or against the namespace rule.

The strictness is not disclosed where a client would look. The matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:57` claims an "authenticated path-style server" and a "configuration-only six-server oracle" for Put and Get bucket versioning, and neither it nor `docs/qualification/put-bucket-versioning.md` names the namespace as a client requirement.

One existing test asserts the current rejection and must change with the fix (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:21868-21871`):

```ada
      Expect_Malformed
        ("<VersioningConfiguration><Status>Enabled</Status>" &
         "</VersioningConfiguration>",
         "namespace-free versioning request accepted");
```

The neighbouring assertions stay correct: `:21872-21880` rejects a foreign namespace and an element attribute, and `:21881-21890` requires `Parse_Response` to accept the very same namespace-free bytes.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below has two parts. Part one runs four namespace-free request bodies through their own parsers side by side. Part two sends signed requests through the in-process S3 application: a namespaced `?versioning` body as the control, a second `CreateBucket` as a second control, then the exact 75-byte body `mc` puts on the wire, then `GET ?versioning` on that second bucket to show whether it became versioned.

```ada
   Unqualified_Body : constant String :=
     "<VersioningConfiguration><Status>Enabled</Status>" &
     "</VersioningConfiguration>";
...
         Ignored : constant
           Flyology.Object_Storage.Bucket_Versioning_Configuration :=
             Versioning.Parse (Unqualified_Body);
...
      Response : constant String := Run
        (Signed_Request
           ("PUT", Other_Bucket, Unqualified_Body,
            MD5_Header (Unqualified_Body), Versioning_Query));
   begin
      Check (Status_Code (Response) = 200,
             "namespace-free PutBucketVersioning is accepted");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- --- unqualified request bodies through their own parsers ---
  --   codec Lifecycle.Parse: accepted
  --   codec Notifications.Parse: accepted
  --   codec Buckets.Parse_Create_Configuration: accepted
  --   codec Versioning.Parse_Response: accepted
  --   codec Versioning.Parse: REJECTED
  --     malformed bucket versioning XML
  FAILED: Versioning.Parse accepts an unqualified root
  -- --- signed requests through the S3 application ---
  -- CreateBucket /domain-a-5 status 200
  -- PUT /domain-a-5?versioning  (xmlns present) -> 200
  ok: namespaced PutBucketVersioning is accepted
  -- CreateBucket /domain-a-5-other status 200
  ok: the second bucket is created
  -- PUT /domain-a-5-other?versioning (no xmlns) -> 400
  --   body: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/domain-a-5-other?versioning</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: namespace-free PutBucketVersioning is accepted
  -- GET /domain-a-5-other?versioning -> 200 body <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><VersioningConfiguration xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"></VersioningConfiguration>
  FAILED: the bucket ends up with versioning enabled
BUG REPRODUCED: 3 failed expectation(s)
```

The control PUT differs from the failing PUT only by the `xmlns` attribute, and both carry a correct `Content-MD5`, so signing, routing and the integrity gates are not what fires. The final `GET` shows the bucket never became versioned.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Stock-client bodies captured on the wire from MinIO `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z, aws-cli 2.36.34 and botocore 1.43.89. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

`PUT /bucket?versioning` with a valid body and no `xmlns` must be accepted, as `Lifecycle.Parse`, `Notifications.Parse` and `Buckets.Parse_Create_Configuration` already accept their unqualified bodies, and as the crate's own `Parse_Response` already accepts the identical document. A foreign namespace and any element attribute must still be refused.

## Suggested treatment

The mechanism already exists: `Parse_Document` takes `Allow_Empty_Namespace`, and `Parse_Response` already passes `True`. The smallest fix is to have the server-facing entry point pass `True` as well, at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versioning.adb:175-176`, leaving the foreign-namespace and attribute checks at `:47-54` intact. No new public constant, type, parameter or default is introduced.

This needs a maintainer decision only in that it widens the server's accepted request domain:

- Make `Parse` lenient, so one function serves both directions and the package no longer distinguishes them. Simplest, and matches every sibling request codec.
- Keep `Parse` strict and add a separate lenient request entry point that the server calls. Preserves the current strict function for callers that want it, at the cost of a new public subprogram, which the "Ada interface values" section of `AGENTS.md` puts under explicit approval.

`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:21868-21871` asserts the current rejection and must be inverted into an acceptance assertion that the parsed `Status` is `Versioning_Enabled`; `:21872-21880` and `:21881-21890` stay correct. The server-side regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, in the PutBucketVersioning block beside the foreign-namespace and attribute cases at `:10524-10546`: send the namespace-free body through `Signed_Versioning_Request` with its `Content_MD5` and require 200, then require the follow-up `GET ?versioning` to report `Enabled`. The spike is the test.

This is one of three request codecs with the same defect. https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/60 reports a namespace-free `<Delete>` refused by DeleteObjects and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/61 a namespace-free `<Tagging>` refused by PutBucketTagging. The fix is the same class of change in all three, letting the request parser accept an unqualified root the way the lenient sibling codecs already do, so the three should be decided together even if they are three commits.

## Severity

`mc version enable alias/bucket` cannot enable versioning on a Flyology server, and neither can anything else built on minio-go, which is the largest non-AWS S3 client family. The request is well formed, correctly signed and carries a correct `Content-MD5`; the server answers 400 `MalformedXML` and the bucket is left unversioned, with no client-side workaround, since `mc` does not offer to add a namespace. The matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:57` claims an authenticated path-style server and a configuration-only six-server oracle for this operation. Current AWS SDK clients are not affected, because botocore serializes the model's `xmlNamespace`. Deterministic.

## Summary

The ListObjects, ListObjectsV2 and ListObjectVersions query parsers reuse the response page bound `S3.Core.Page_Size'Last` as a request validity bound, so a `max-keys` above 1000 raises `Malformed_List_Request` and the server answers 400 `InvalidArgument`. The pinned model describes the parameter as a ceiling on the response, not a constraint on the request, and MinIO answers the same request with 200 and echoes the value back. A stock `aws s3api list-objects-v2 --max-keys 2000` therefore fails against a Flyology server and succeeds against MinIO, and any SDK paginator that passes a caller-supplied `MaxKeys` through does the same.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

All three query parsers compare against the page bound and raise. ListObjects v1 (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:111-118`):

```ada
                  if Name = "max-keys" then
                     Number := Wire_Core.Parse_Natural (Value);
                     if Seen_Max_Keys or else not Number.Valid
                       or else Number.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last
                     then
                        raise Malformed_List_Request with
                          "invalid ListObjects max-keys";
                     end if;
```

ListObjectsV2 (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:223-230`):

```ada
                  elsif Name = "max-keys" then
                     Number := Wire_Core.Parse_Natural (Value);
                     if Seen_Max_Keys or else not Number.Valid
                       or else Number.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last
                     then
                        raise Malformed_List_Request with
                          "invalid ListObjectsV2 max-keys";
                     end if;
```

ListObjectVersions (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:134-141`):

```ada
                  elsif Name = "max-keys" then
                     Number := Wire_Core.Parse_Natural (Value);
                     if Seen_Maximum or else not Number.Valid
                       or else Number.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last
                     then
                        raise Malformed_Version_Request with
                          "invalid ListObjectVersions max-keys";
                     end if;
```

The bound is a visible subtype in the public specification (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:27-28`):

```ada
   --  Requested or returned item count for one S3 page.
   subtype Page_Size is Natural range 0 .. 1_000;
```

and the server turns the exception into a 400 (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:10763-10767`):

```ada
               exception
                  when Listings.Malformed_List_Request =>
                     Send_Error
                       (X, 400, "InvalidArgument",
                        "The ListObjects request is invalid", Target_Text);
```

with the same shape in the two neighbouring listing handlers.

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) puts no bound on the value and states in its own documentation that the parameter caps the response. Printed with `python3`, markup stripped:

```
ListObjects ListObjectsRequest MaxKeys: {"shape": "MaxKeys", "location": "querystring", "locationName": "max-keys"} shape def: {"type": "integer"}
ListObjectsV2 ListObjectsV2Request MaxKeys: {"shape": "MaxKeys", "location": "querystring", "locationName": "max-keys"} shape def: {"type": "integer"}
ListObjectVersions ListObjectVersionsRequest MaxKeys: {"shape": "MaxKeys", "location": "querystring", "locationName": "max-keys"} shape def: {"type": "integer"}
```

```
ListObjectsV2 -> Sets the maximum number of keys returned in the response. By default, the action returns up to 1,000 key names. The response might contain fewer keys but will never contain more.
```

The same sentence appears for ListObjects, and ListObjectVersions repeats it with the truncation rule appended. The `MaxKeys` shape is a plain integer with no `min` or `max`.

MinIO answers the request. The pinned image, `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e` from `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, whose `--version` prints `minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)`, answered a signed `GET /bucket?max-keys=2000` with 200 and echoed the value:

```
<ListBucketResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/">...<Marker></Marker><MaxKeys>2000</MaxKeys><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>...
```

and `GET /bucket?max-keys=2000&versions` likewise with `<MaxKeys>2000</MaxKeys>`. Driven through aws-cli 2.36.34, `list-objects --max-keys 2000`, `list-objects-v2 --max-keys 2000` and `list-object-versions --max-keys 2000` all returned 200 against that MinIO.

The same three commands against this crate's own application, on its loopback listener with aws-cli 2.36.34 and no checksum or retry configuration:

```
$ aws s3api list-objects-v2 --bucket stock-versioning --max-keys 2000
An error occurred (InvalidArgument) when calling the ListObjectsV2 operation: The ListObjectsV2 request is invalid
$ aws s3api list-objects --bucket stock-versioning --max-keys 5000
An error occurred (InvalidArgument) when calling the ListObjects operation: The ListObjects request is invalid
$ aws s3api list-object-versions --bucket stock-versioning --max-keys 2000
An error occurred (InvalidArgument) when calling the ListObjectVersions operation: The ListObjectVersions request is invalid
$ aws s3api list-objects-v2 --bucket stock-versioning --max-keys 1000
{
    "IsTruncated": false,
    "Name": "stock-versioning",
    "Prefix": "",
    "MaxKeys": 1000,
    "EncodingType": "url",
    "KeyCount": 0
}
```

Nothing documents the rejection as a rule. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:861-864` describes the backend page contract, not the accepted request domain:

```
ListObjects v1 and v2 use the same bounded backend page contract across
memory, files, and SQLite. Both provide bytewise lexical ordering, prefix
filtering, delimiter/CommonPrefixes grouping, max-keys 0..1000, URL-encoded
response fields, 64-bit sizes, quoted ETags, and fixed-length XML responses.
```

and the two listing qualification documents mention 1000 only as the modeled default.

The typed client cannot express the value, since `Low_Level.List_Objects_Parameters.Max_Keys` is `S3.Core.Page_Size`, so only the server side refuses. No existing test in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb` or `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb` asserts a rejection for values above 1000; the listing query blocks exercise 0, 1 and 2 only.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates a bucket and one object in the in-process S3 application, then sends six signed GETs: `?max-keys=1000`, `?list-type=2&max-keys=1000` and `?versions&max-keys=1000` as controls, then the same three at 2000.

```ada
      Response : constant String :=
        Run (Signed_Request ("GET", Bucket, "", "", Query));
      Status   : constant Natural := Status_Code (Response);
...
   Probe ("GET ?max-keys=2000                 ",
          [SigV4.Pair ("max-keys", "2000")], True);
   Probe ("GET ?list-type=2&max-keys=2000     ",
          [SigV4.Pair ("list-type", "2"), SigV4.Pair ("max-keys", "2000")],
          True);
   Probe ("GET ?versions&max-keys=2000        ",
          [SigV4.Pair ("max-keys", "2000"), SigV4.Pair ("versions", "")],
          True);
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- CreateBucket /domain-a-6 status 200
  -- PutObject /domain-a-6/k1 status 200
  -- GET ?max-keys=1000                  -> 200
  ok: GET ?max-keys=1000                  is accepted
  -- GET ?list-type=2&max-keys=1000      -> 200
  ok: GET ?list-type=2&max-keys=1000      is accepted
  -- GET ?versions&max-keys=1000         -> 200
  ok: GET ?versions&max-keys=1000         is accepted
  -- GET ?max-keys=2000                  -> 400
  --   body: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidArgument</Code><Message>The ListObjects request is invalid</Message><Resource>/domain-a-6?max-keys=2000</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: GET ?max-keys=2000                  is accepted
  -- GET ?list-type=2&max-keys=2000      -> 400
  --   body: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidArgument</Code><Message>The ListObjectsV2 request is invalid</Message><Resource>/domain-a-6?list-type=2&amp;max-keys=2000</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: GET ?list-type=2&max-keys=2000      is accepted
  -- GET ?versions&max-keys=2000         -> 400
  --   body: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InvalidArgument</Code><Message>The ListObjectVersions request is invalid</Message><Resource>/domain-a-6?max-keys=2000&amp;versions</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: GET ?versions&max-keys=2000         is accepted
BUG REPRODUCED: 3 failed expectation(s)
```

The three failing requests differ from their controls only in the digits of `max-keys`, so routing, signing and the rest of the query parsing are not what fires.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image, and against aws-cli 2.36.34 driven at both servers. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

`max-keys` above 1000 must be honoured as a request for at most that many keys, and the server must return a page bounded by its own maximum. That is what the pinned model's own documentation for the parameter describes, and what MinIO does. A negative or non-numeric value, and a repeated `max-keys`, must still be refused.

## Suggested treatment

This needs a maintainer decision, because the bound is the visible subtype `S3.Core.Page_Size` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:27-28`), which the "Ada interface values" section of `AGENTS.md` puts under explicit approval:

- Clamp in the three query parsers. Replace the `Number.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last` disjunct at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:114` and `:226` and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:137` with an assignment of `Core.Page_Size'Last` when the parsed value exceeds it, keeping the "repeated" and "not valid" disjuncts. No public declaration changes, every response bound and every backend page contract stays exactly as it is, and the behaviour is what the model's own wording describes.
- Widen the request-side bound into a separate, wider subtype. This adds a public declaration whose value, name, type and visibility are a separate decision, and it changes what a caller may put in `List_Objects_Parameters.Max_Keys`.

The first option is the smaller change and leaves the interface untouched.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the existing ListObjects query block at `:16527-16533`: send `max-keys=2000` on each of the three listing operations and require 200 with `<MaxKeys>` reporting the server's own bound, keeping a case for a malformed and a repeated `max-keys` that still expects 400 `InvalidArgument`. The spike is the test. No existing test asserts the current rejection, so nothing has to be inverted. If the clamp is chosen, `docs/compatibility/s3.md:861-864` stays correct as written, because it describes the backend page contract rather than the request domain.

## Severity

A stock tool pointed at a Flyology endpoint with an explicit page hint above 1000 gets a hard 400 instead of a page: `aws s3api list-objects-v2 --max-keys 2000` is the plainest case, and an SDK paginator that forwards a caller-supplied `MaxKeys` behaves the same. No default invocation reaches it, because the user has to ask for more than 1000, and the caller can work around it by asking for 1000 or fewer. Nothing is lost or corrupted; the refusal is an honest 400 with a correct error code, and the response side is unaffected. The typed Flyology client cannot express the value at all, so this is a server-side defect only. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Versions.Validate` requires `NextKeyMarker` and `NextVersionIdMarker` to be present together and to match `IsTruncated`. MinIO emits an empty `<NextVersionIdMarker></NextVersionIdMarker>` on every `?versions` response, including untruncated ones, and omits `<NextKeyMarker>` there, so the first disjunct fires and `Malformed_Version_Listing` is raised for every page. The client turns it into `Invalid_Response`, so `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` returns nothing and ListObjectVersions is unusable against MinIO through the high-level client. The pinned model puts no cross-member constraint on either element.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The pairing rule is the first disjunct of the check, before emptiness or truncation are considered (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:1031-1041`):

```ada
      elsif Value.Has_Next_Version_ID_Marker /=
        Value.Has_Next_Key_Marker
        or else Value.Is_Truncated /= Value.Has_Next_Key_Marker
        or else
          (Value.Has_Next_Key_Marker
           and then
             (US.Length (Value.Next_Key_Marker) = 0
              or else US.Length (Value.Next_Version_ID_Marker) = 0))
      then
         raise Malformed_Version_Listing with
           "inconsistent ListObjectVersions next markers";
```

A page carrying one marker without the other is rejected whatever its emptiness or truncation, and the client turns the exception into an invalid response with no partial page (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2226-2229`):

```ada
   exception
      when S3.Versions.Malformed_Version_Listing |
           S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed ListObjectVersions response";
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) expresses no such pairing. `ListObjectVersionsOutput` has no `required` list, both markers are plain optional strings, and their documentation describes what a server sends when the page overflows without forbidding anything else. Printed with `python3`, markup stripped:

```
ListObjectVersionsOutput required: None
NextKeyMarker {"shape": "NextKeyMarker"}
   doc: When the number of responses exceeds the value of MaxKeys, NextKeyMarker specifies the first key not returned that satisfies the search criteria. Use this value for the key-marker request parameter in a subsequent request.
NextVersionIdMarker {"shape": "NextVersionIdMarker"}
   doc: When the number of responses exceeds the value of MaxKeys, NextVersionIdMarker specifies the first object version not returned that satisfies the search criteria. Use this value for the version-id-marker request parameter in a subsequent request.
```

MinIO sends exactly the shape the rule refuses. The pinned image, `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e` from `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, whose `--version` prints `minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)`, answered a signed `GET /bucket?versions` on a versioned bucket with 200 and this envelope, verbatim apart from the bucket name and the version entries:

```
<ListVersionsResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/">...<Prefix></Prefix><KeyMarker></KeyMarker><NextVersionIdMarker></NextVersionIdMarker><VersionIdMarker></VersionIdMarker><MaxKeys>1000</MaxKeys><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>...
```

`Has_Next_Version_ID_Marker` is therefore True while `Has_Next_Key_Marker` is False, and the first disjunct holds. The same server's truncated page carries both markers non-empty and is accepted:

```
...<KeyMarker></KeyMarker><NextKeyMarker>obj-reduced-redundancy[minio_cache:v2,return:]</NextKeyMarker><NextVersionIdMarker>8d8cfaec-244b-48b2-810c-3c203c9f335e</NextVersionIdMarker><VersionIdMarker></VersionIdMarker><MaxKeys>1</MaxKeys><IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated>...
```

so every untruncated page fails and every truncated one succeeds.

The rule is documented as an invariant at `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:86-88`:

```
singletons. Versions, delete markers, and common prefixes count together
against `MaxKeys`; truncated pages require both nonempty next markers, while
final pages reject either one.
```

which is what has to change with the fix. Two corpus cases enforce it. `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb:284-292`:

```ada
   Expect_Bad
     (Page ("", Is_Truncated => "true"),
      "accepted truncated page without markers");
   Expect_Bad
     (Page
        ("", Markers =>
           "<NextKeyMarker>key</NextKeyMarker>" &
           "<NextVersionIdMarker>version</NextVersionIdMarker>"),
      "accepted final page with next markers");
```

The first stays correct under any fix. The second asserts that a final page carrying both markers is refused, which is the assertion a truncation-driven rule relaxes.

Nothing in the repository catches it. The in-house server never emits a lone marker, and no third-party lane runs the operation at all: the only implementation-corpus caller of `List_Versions_Page` sits inside a routine that returns immediately unless the implementation under test is a Flyology backend (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3318-3333`):

```ada
      Implementation_Variable : constant String :=
        "FLYOLOGY_S3_IMPLEMENTATION";
      Files_Implementation  : constant String := "flyology-files";
      SQLite_Implementation : constant String := "flyology-sqlite";
   begin
      if not Ada.Environment_Variables.Exists (Implementation_Variable)
        or else
          Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Implementation_Variable) not in
            Files_Implementation | SQLite_Implementation
      then
         if Phase = Complete_Durable_Routing then
            return;
         end if;
```

so the Docker matrix that runs MinIO on every change has never sent a `?versions` request through the client.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below puts three documents through `S3.Versions.Parse_List_Object_Versions`. Every `StorageClass` in all three is `STANDARD`, so the storage-class rule reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/53 cannot interfere. The controls are MinIO's untruncated envelope with the empty `NextVersionIdMarker` removed, and MinIO's truncated envelope with both markers non-empty. The third document is MinIO's actual untruncated envelope.

```ada
   MinIO_Untruncated : constant String :=
     "<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" &
     "<ListVersionsResult xmlns=""" & NS & """>" &
     ... &
     "<KeyMarker></KeyMarker>" &
     "<NextVersionIdMarker></NextVersionIdMarker>" &
     "<VersionIdMarker></VersionIdMarker><MaxKeys>1000</MaxKeys>" &
     "<IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>" & Entry_Text & "</ListVersionsResult>";
...
         Page : constant Versions.List_Object_Versions_Result :=
           Versions.Parse_List_Object_Versions (Document, XML.Default_Limits);
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- untruncated, no NextVersionIdMarker: decoded, versions=1 truncated=FALSE
  ok: untruncated, no NextVersionIdMarker: the page keeps its version
  -- truncated, both next markers set   : decoded, versions=1 truncated=TRUE
  ok: truncated, both next markers set   : the page keeps its version
  -- MinIO untruncated, empty NextVersionIdMarker: REJECTED, inconsistent ListObjectVersions next markers
  FAILED: MinIO untruncated, empty NextVersionIdMarker: the page is accepted
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The first control differs from the failing document by that one empty element and nothing else, so the presence flag alone is what fires.

A second program drove the same case live against the pinned MinIO through `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page`, on a versioned bucket holding only a default-class object, so no storage class other than `STANDARD` is involved. The call raised, exit status 1:

```
raised Invalid_Response: malformed ListObjectVersions response
```

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

An untruncated page carrying a present-but-empty `NextVersionIdMarker` and no `NextKeyMarker` must decode, since the pinned model imposes no cross-member constraint on either element and both are optional. A truncated page must still carry usable continuation state, so requiring both markers non-empty when `IsTruncated` is true remains correct.

## Suggested treatment

This needs a maintainer decision, because it relaxes an invariant the repository documents:

- Key the rule on truncation and non-emptiness. Require both markers non-empty when `Is_Truncated` is true, and ignore a present-but-empty marker otherwise, by replacing the first two disjuncts at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:1031-1033` with a truncation-driven test over the marker lengths rather than their presence flags. This admits MinIO and every server that echoes empty continuation elements, and keeps a truncated page without usable markers rejected.
- Keep the presence pairing and accept that ListObjectVersions does not work against servers that echo an empty marker. This leaves the operation unusable against MinIO through the high-level client.

If the first is chosen, `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:86-88` states the invariant being relaxed ("final pages reject either one") and must be reworded, and `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb:287-292` asserts the rejection of a final page carrying next markers and must change with it.

The regression tests belong in `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb`, beside those marker cases: accept MinIO's untruncated envelope with the empty `NextVersionIdMarker`, keep the truncated-page-without-markers rejection at `:284-286`, and keep rejecting a truncated page whose markers are present but empty. The spike is the test. Because no third-party lane runs the operation, one MinIO `?versions` call added to `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` outside the Flyology-only gate at `:3318-3333` would catch a regression the in-process corpus cannot.

This finding and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/53 both break ListObjectVersions against MinIO and must land together. Fixing this one alone still loses any page containing a version whose storage class is not `STANDARD`, and fixing that one alone changes nothing against MinIO, because every page fails here first.

## Severity

Every untruncated ListObjectVersions page from MinIO is refused, so listing versions of any bucket that fits in one page fails outright: `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` raises `Invalid_Response` and returns nothing, with no marker to retry from. In reach it is worse than the storage-class defect reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/53, since it does not depend on what the bucket holds. It is a pure function of the response text, so no timing or server state is involved. The matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:67` disclaims external interoperability for this operation, so no documented promise is broken, and a caller using only Flyology backends never sees it, because the in-house server never emits a lone marker. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Delete_Handler.Start_Element_Details` raises `Malformed_Delete` unless the element namespace is exactly `http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/`, and because it is an override on the handler it runs for every element of a DeleteObjects request body. In the pinned botocore S3 model the namespace is a serializer trait on the `DeleteObjectsRequest.Delete` member, not a constraint on the `Delete` shape, so a receiver must not require it. minio `mc` and `s3cmd` both marshal a namespace-free `<Delete>` with a correct `Content-MD5` and are answered 400 `MalformedXML`, while MinIO answers 200 for the identical bytes. `mc rm --recursive`, `mc rb --force` and `s3cmd rm --recursive` therefore delete nothing against a Flyology endpoint.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The namespace check is an override on the whole handler, so it fires on the root and on every nested element (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-deletions.adb:66-79`):

```ada
   overriding procedure Start_Element_Details
     (Item            : in out Delete_Handler;
      Namespace_URI   : String;
      Attribute_Count : Natural)
   is
      pragma Unreferenced (Item);
   begin
      if Namespace_URI /= "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"
        or else Attribute_Count /= 0
      then
         raise Malformed_Delete with
           "DeleteObjects namespace or attributes are invalid";
      end if;
   end Start_Element_Details;
```

`Parse_Request` turns the handler exception into `Malformed_Delete` through its `XML.XML_Error` arm (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-deletions.adb:380-393`):

```ada
   function Parse_Request
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Delete_Objects_Request
   is
      Handler : aliased Delete_Handler;
   begin
      XML.Parse (Document, Handler, Limits);
      Validate (Handler.Value);
      return Handler.Value;
   exception
      when XML.XML_Error =>
         raise Malformed_Delete with "malformed DeleteObjects XML";
   end Parse_Request;
```

The specification promises no such rule (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-deletions.ads:76-83`):

```ada
   --  Parse one bounded DeleteObjects request document.
   --  @param Document DeleteObjects request XML
   --  @param Limits XML parsing limits
   --  @return Decoded DeleteObjects request body
   function Parse_Request
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Delete_Objects_Request;
```

The server hands the request body to that codec verbatim, with no namespace normalization in between (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:17729-17734`):

```ada
                  if Owner_Accepted then
                     declare
                        Request : constant
                          Deletions.Delete_Objects_Request :=
                            Deletions.Parse_Request
                              (Document,
```

and maps the exception to the wire code (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:17959-17963`):

```ada
                  when Deletions.Malformed_Delete =>
                     Send_Error
                       (X, 400, "MalformedXML",
                        "The XML provided was not well-formed or did not " &
                        "validate against the published schema", Target_Text);
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) puts the namespace on the member, not on the shape. Printed with python3:

```
--- Delete shape ---
{"type": "structure", "required": ["Objects"],
 "members": {"Objects": {"shape": "ObjectIdentifierList", "locationName": "Object"},
             "Quiet": {"shape": "Quiet"}}}
--- DeleteObjectsRequest.Delete member ---
{"shape": "Delete", "locationName": "Delete",
 "xmlNamespace": {"uri": "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"}}
```

`xmlNamespace` on a member instructs an SDK serializer to emit the attribute. It is not a receiver-side validation rule, and the `Delete` shape itself carries none.

MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, accepts the namespace-free body. A signed `POST /oracle-b?delete` carrying `Content-MD5` and the body `<Delete><Quiet>false</Quiet><Object><Key>single-crc</Key></Object></Delete>`:

```
DeleteObjects, namespace-free body (mc/s3cmd form)         : 200 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?> <DeleteResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Deleted><Key>single-crc</Key></Deleted></DeleteResult>
```

The same route also carries the mandatory `Content-MD5` gate reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/20, and the order of rejection matters for both fixes. Current boto3 1.43.89 and aws-cli 2.36.34 send `x-amz-checksum-crc32` instead of `Content-MD5`, so that gate stops them one step earlier and they never reach the namespace check. `mc` and `s3cmd` do send `Content-MD5`, so for them the namespace check is the first and only rejection. Driving boto3 1.43.89 at the prebuilt `tests/bin/s3_memory_server` with default settings:

```
boto3 1.43.89 botocore 1.43.89
delete_objects ERROR: An error occurred (InvalidRequest) when calling the DeleteObjects operation: DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header
```

Today no client can batch-delete at all. Fixing the `Content-MD5` gate alone would still leave `mc` and `s3cmd` broken here, and fixing this alone would still leave boto3 and aws-cli broken there.

Two maintained documents state the strictness as intended behaviour, so they are part of the defect rather than a defence of it. `docs/qualification/delete-objects.md:13-15`:

```
the pinned model. XML namespace, attributes, member order, duplicates, UTF-8,
key limits, booleans, nonnegative 64-bit sizes, ETag syntax, and HTTP dates are
validated before the relevant entry can reach storage.
```

and `docs/compatibility/s3.md:511-512`:

```
`BadDigest`. The namespace-aware request parser rejects attributes, unknown or
duplicate members, malformed scalars, and out-of-order structures before any
```

Neither is an S3 protocol rule, and MinIO accepts what this server refuses.

The unit suite pins the rejection, so it asserts the wrong behaviour and must change (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11450-11452`):

```ada
      Must_Reject_Request
        ("<Delete><Object><Key>k</Key></Object></Delete>",
         "namespace-free DeleteObjects request was accepted");
```

The adjacent case at `:11453-11456` rejects a foreign namespace (`urn:foreign`) and is a correct rejection that must keep failing. The only DeleteObjects in the cross-server wire slice is s5cmd's `rm` (`tests/scripts/run-s3-server-slice.sh:104-107`, image pinned at `:9`), whose requests this server accepts today, so no lane sends a namespace-free body.

The same change class appears twice more. https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/61 is the bucket-tagging request parser and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/57 is the PutBucketVersioning body parser. All three enforce a namespace the pinned model places on the member as a serializer instruction, and one decision about receiver-side namespace tolerance settles all three.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates `/domain-b1` and the keys `k1`, `k2` and `k3` in the in-process memory-backed application. It first calls `Deletions.Parse_Request` on the document captured from `mc`, then sends the captured `mc` and `s3cmd` bodies as signed `POST /domain-b1?delete` requests with a correct `Content-MD5`, asserting 200 and a `<Deleted>` entry for each. The namespaced boto3 and aws-cli body is the control.

```ada
   --  Captured from mc RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z (minio-go v7.0.90).
   MC_Body : constant String :=
     "<Delete><Quiet>false</Quiet><Object><Key>k1</Key></Object></Delete>";
   --  Captured from s3cmd 2.4.0.
   S3cmd_Body : constant String :=
     "<Delete><Object><Key>k2</Key></Object></Delete>";
   --  Captured from boto3 1.43.89 / aws-cli 2.36.34 (control: accepted today).
   SDK_Body : constant String :=
     "<Delete xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
     "<Object><Key>k3</Key></Object><Quiet>false</Quiet></Delete>";

   function Delete_Request (Document : String) return String is
     (Signed_Request
        ("POST", Bucket, Document,
         "Content-MD5: " & MD5_Base64 (Document) & CRLF &
         "Content-Type: application/xml" & CRLF,
         Delete_Query));
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- === fixtures ===
  ok: CreateBucket /domain-b1 is 200
  ok: PutObject k1 is 200
  ok: PutObject k2 is 200
  ok: PutObject k3 is 200
  -- 
  -- === unit level: Deletions.Parse_Request ===
  -- Parse_Request raised Malformed_Delete: malformed DeleteObjects XML
  FAILED: Deletions.Parse_Request accepts a namespace-free <Delete>
  -- 
  -- === server: stock-client bodies (must be accepted) ===
  -- mc (minio-go) DeleteObjects    body=<Delete><Quiet>false</Quiet><Object><Key>k1</Key></Object></Delete>
  --     status=400 body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/domain-b1?delete</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: mc (minio-go) DeleteObjects    is answered 200
  FAILED: mc (minio-go) DeleteObjects    reports k1 as deleted
  -- s3cmd DeleteObjects            body=<Delete><Object><Key>k2</Key></Object></Delete>
  --     status=400 body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/domain-b1?delete</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: s3cmd DeleteObjects            is answered 200
  FAILED: s3cmd DeleteObjects            reports k2 as deleted
  -- 
  -- === control: the same request with xmlns (passes today) ===
  -- boto3/aws-cli DeleteObjects    body=<Delete xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Object><Key>k3</Key></Object><Quiet>false</Quiet></Delete>
  --     status=200 body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><DeleteResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Deleted><Key>k3</Key></Deleted></DeleteResult>
  ok: boto3/aws-cli DeleteObjects    is answered 200
  ok: boto3/aws-cli DeleteObjects    reports k3 as deleted
BUG REPRODUCED: 5 failed expectation(s)
```

The control differs from the two failing legs by the `xmlns` attribute and nothing else, so the signature, the `Content-MD5` and the route are right and only the namespace is refused.

The two client bodies are not hypothetical. `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z with minio-go v7.0.90, run from the `minio/mc@sha256:a7fe349ef4bd8521fb8497f55c6042871b2ae640607cf99d9bede5e9bdf11727` image with `--rm`, was pointed at a logging TCP proxy forwarding byte for byte to `tests/bin/s3_memory_server`. `mc rm --recursive --force` reported

```
mc: <ERROR> Failed to remove `fly` recursively. The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema
```

and the bytes it put on the wire were

```
POST /mcbucket/?delete= HTTP/1.1
Host: host.docker.internal:62457
User-Agent: MinIO (linux; arm64) minio-go/v7.0.90 mc/RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z
Content-Length: 127
Accept-Encoding: zstd,gzip
Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=AKIDEXAMPLE/20260907/us-east-1/s3/aws4_request, SignedHeaders=content-md5;host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date, Signature=79482a93c9d27d4187578fdbb667862132d85c363c911e0892958f8e00f27c5c
Content-Md5: 31pfUAHQbxJHn3iLLTGlGg==
X-Amz-Content-Sha256: 807b2595e206da7671988f6a9c33fd280a2887a86263ae41a289181e80fc0303
X-Amz-Date: 20260907T070150Z

<Delete><Quiet>false</Quiet><Object><Key>m1</Key></Object><Object><Key>m2</Key></Object><Object><Key>m3</Key></Object></Delete>
```

There is a correct `Content-MD5` and a real payload hash, and no `xmlns`. s3cmd 2.4.0, resolved from PyPI with `uv run --python 3.13 --with s3cmd` and nothing installed system-wide, reported `ERROR: S3 error: 400 (MalformedXML)` for `s3cmd rm --recursive --force` and sent

```
POST /mcbucket/?delete HTTP/1.1
Host: 127.0.0.1:62457
Accept-Encoding: identity
Content-Length: 145
content-md5: YEfodgAxxXRNXnyq7zzi3g==
content-type: application/xml
x-amz-date: 20260907T070206Z
Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=AKIDEXAMPLE/20260907/us-east-1/s3/aws4_request,SignedHeaders=content-md5;content-type;host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date,Signature=3a06115c3b34a928df5ba37341f385c1884fbd2c515a3236804d6365c30fc6ba
x-amz-content-sha256: 3cfa2fc27dad77fb4d3b1460c8f1868b6bb0f3696de7c315d9144f2cdb719779

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Delete><Object><Key>m1</Key></Object><Object><Key>m2</Key></Object><Object><Key>m3</Key></Object></Delete>
```

Replaying both captured documents over plain HTTP against the same server with a valid SigV4 header signature, a real payload hash and `Content-MD5`, so that neither the integrity gate nor the UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD rule applies, isolates the namespace as the cause:

```
DeleteObjects     mc body (no xmlns)   : 400 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code>...
s3cmd DeleteObjects body (no xmlns)    : 400 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code>...
DeleteObjects     same body + xmlns    : 200 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><DeleteResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Deleted><Key>m1</Key></Deleted><Deleted><Key>m2</Key></Deleted><Deleted><Key>m3</Key></Deleted></DeleteResult>
```

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A DeleteObjects request body with an absent namespace must be accepted, because the pinned botocore S3 model puts `xmlNamespace` on the `DeleteObjectsRequest.Delete` member as a serializer instruction and puts none on the `Delete` shape, and because MinIO answers 200 for the exact bytes this server refuses. A foreign namespace and any attribute may still be rejected.

## Suggested treatment

The smallest fix accepts an empty `Namespace_URI` in `Delete_Handler.Start_Element_Details` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-deletions.adb:66-79`, which is the same test the bucket tagging handler already writes for its tolerant mode at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:434-448`:

```ada
      if (Namespace_URI /= "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"
          and then Namespace_URI'Length > 0)
        or else Attribute_Count /= 0
      then
```

This needs a maintainer decision, because it changes a documented policy and because the alternative adds public surface:

- Accept an absent namespace unconditionally in `Parse_Request`. One expression changes, no public declaration moves, and `Deletions` keeps one entry point. Foreign namespaces and attributes stay invalid.
- Thread an `Allow_Empty_Namespace` flag the way `Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Document` does at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:550-586`, and expose it as a new parameter on `Parse_Request` or as a second entry point. This keeps a strict mode available but adds a public parameter or subprogram, which is an interface decision under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`.
- Keep the rule and document that `mc` and `s3cmd` cannot batch-delete against this server.

Whichever is chosen, `docs/qualification/delete-objects.md:13-15` and `docs/compatibility/s3.md:511-512` state the current rule as a qualification claim and must be restated with it. Because https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/61 and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/57 are the same question on two other request codecs, the three should be sequenced together so the answer is uniform.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the DeleteObjects block, as a signed `POST /bucket?delete` carrying the captured namespace-free body and a correct `Content-MD5`, asserting 200 and a `<Deleted>` entry. The unit assertion at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11450-11452` pins the rejection and must be inverted; the foreign-namespace case at `:11453-11456` must keep asserting rejection. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Batch delete is unavailable from two stock clients with default settings, and nothing is deleted. A user running `mc rm --recursive`, `mc rb --force` or `s3cmd rm --recursive` against a Flyology endpoint gets 400 `MalformedXML` on every batch and no partial progress, while the same commands work against MinIO and AWS. The rejection is a string comparison on the namespace of each start element, so it fires on every request of this shape regardless of size, load or backend. Combined with the `Content-MD5` gate reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/20, which stops current boto3 and aws-cli one step earlier, no client tested here can batch-delete at all. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Bucket_Tagging_Handler.Start_Element_Details` rejects an empty namespace unless `Allow_Empty_Namespace` is set, and `Parse_Bucket` sets it to `False` while `Parse_Bucket_Response` sets it to `True`. The object tagging handler overrides no `Start_Element_Details` at all, so `PUT /bucket/key?tagging` accepts a document that `PUT /bucket?tagging` refuses with 400 `MalformedXML`. minio-go marshals `<Tagging>` with no namespace for both resources, so `mc tag set` on a bucket fails against a Flyology endpoint while MinIO accepts the identical bytes with 200. The pinned botocore S3 model puts no `xmlNamespace` on the `Tagging` shape.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The bucket handler's namespace rule is conditional on a flag (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:434-448`):

```ada
   overriding procedure Start_Element_Details
     (Item            : in out Bucket_Tagging_Handler;
      Namespace_URI   : String;
      Attribute_Count : Natural)
   is
   begin
      if (Namespace_URI /= "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"
          and then
            (not Item.Allow_Empty_Namespace or else Namespace_URI'Length > 0))
        or else Attribute_Count /= 0
      then
         raise Malformed_Tagging with
           "bucket tagging namespace or attributes are invalid";
      end if;
   end Start_Element_Details;
```

Both entry points share one body and differ only in that flag (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:574-586`):

```ada
   function Parse_Bucket
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Tags.Tag_Set is
     (Parse_Bucket_Document
        (Document, Limits, Allow_Empty_Namespace => False));

   function Parse_Bucket_Response
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Tags.Tag_Set is
     (Parse_Bucket_Document
        (Document, Limits, Allow_Empty_Namespace => True));
```

The specification grants the tolerance to the response decoder only (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:48-54`):

```ada
   --  Parse a bucket-tagging response from S3-compatible implementations.
   --  The official S3 namespace and an absent namespace are accepted;
   --  foreign namespaces and attributes remain invalid.
   function Parse_Bucket_Response
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Tags.Tag_Set;
```

The server calls the strict entry point for PutBucketTagging (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:8259-8262`):

```ada
                              declare
                                 Value : constant Tags.Tag_Set :=
                                   Tagging.Parse_Bucket
                                     (Document,
```

and maps the two exceptions to different codes (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:8283-8294`):

```ada
                        exception
                           when Tagging.Malformed_Tagging =>
                              Send_Error
                                (X, 400, "MalformedXML",
                                 "The XML provided was not well-formed or " &
                                 "did not validate against the published " &
                                 "schema", Target_Text);
                           when Tagging.Invalid_Tag =>
                              Send_Error
                                (X, 400, "InvalidTag",
                                 "The tag set contains an invalid tag",
                                 Target_Text);
```

The object tagging handler in the same package overrides no `Start_Element_Details`. Its `Start_Element` checks only local names (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:43-54`):

```ada
   overriding procedure Start_Element
     (Item : in out Tagging_Handler; Local_Name : String) is
   begin
      if Item.Depth = Natural'Last then
         raise Malformed_Tagging with "object tagging XML depth overflow";
      end if;
      Item.Depth := Item.Depth + 1;
      case Item.Depth is
         when 1 =>
            if Local_Name /= "Tagging" then
               raise Malformed_Tagging with "wrong object tagging root";
            end if;
```

so `PutObjectTagging` accepts any namespace, including none. The crate is therefore inconsistent with itself in two directions: the object request path and the bucket response path both accept what the bucket request path refuses.

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) puts the namespace on the member, not the shape. Printed with python3:

```
--- Tagging shape ---
{"type": "structure", "required": ["TagSet"],
 "members": {"TagSet": {"shape": "TagSet"}}}
--- PutBucketTaggingRequest.Tagging member ---
{"shape": "Tagging", "locationName": "Tagging",
 "xmlNamespace": {"uri": "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"}}
```

MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, accepts the namespace-free body on the bucket resource. A signed `PUT /oracle-b?tagging` with `Content-MD5` and the body `<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>`:

```
PutBucketTagging, namespace-free body (mc form)            : 200
```

`docs/qualification/bucket-tagging.md:50` records the rule as an offline gate:

```
rejection; strict namespace-aware XML and tag limits; SigV4 auth-before-
```

The model imposes no such rule and MinIO accepts the body, so that document states the defect rather than a documented limitation.

Two unit assertions pin the inconsistency. The rejection (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11267-11270`):

```ada
      Must_Reject_Malformed
        ("<Tagging><TagSet>" & Tag_XML ("a", "b") &
         "</TagSet></Tagging>",
         "bucket tagging document without the S3 namespace was accepted");
```

and the acceptance of the same string by the response decoder (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11233-11243`):

```ada
         Namespace_Free : constant String :=
           "<Tagging><TagSet>" & Tag_XML ("project", "flyology") &
           "</TagSet></Tagging>";
         Parsed : constant Tags.Tag_Set :=
           Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response (Namespace_Free);
         Rejected_Foreign : Boolean := False;
      begin
         Assert
           (Parsed.Length = 1
            and then US.To_String (Parsed.First_Element.Key) = "project",
            "namespace-free compatible response was rejected");
```

The first must be inverted with the fix. The foreign-namespace assertions at `:11244-11260` and `:11274-11278` and the attribute assertion at `:11271-11273` are correct rejections and must keep failing, and the namespace-free duplicate-`TagSet` case at `:11279-11282` stays rejected for its duplicate member.

https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/60 is the same question on the DeleteObjects request codec and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/57 is the same question on the PutBucketVersioning body parser. One decision about receiver-side namespace tolerance settles all three sites.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates `/domain-b2` and the key `k` in the in-process memory-backed application. It first decodes the document captured from `mc tag set` through both bucket entry points, then sends the same document as a signed `PUT ...?tagging` with a correct `Content-MD5` to the object resource and to the bucket resource, asserting 200 for both. The namespaced boto3, aws-cli and s3cmd body on the bucket resource is the control.

```ada
   --  Captured from mc RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z (minio-go v7.0.90),
   --  `mc tag set repro/probe-mc team=storage`.
   MC_Body : constant String :=
     "<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value>" &
     "</Tag></TagSet></Tagging>";
   --  Captured from boto3 1.43.89 / aws-cli 2.36.34 / s3cmd 2.4.0.
   SDK_Body : constant String :=
     "<Tagging xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
     "<TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet>" &
     "</Tagging>";

   function Tagging_Request (Target, Document : String) return String is
     (Signed_Request
        ("PUT", Target, Document,
         "Content-MD5: " & MD5_Base64 (Document) & CRLF &
         "Content-Type: application/xml" & CRLF,
         Tagging_Query));
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- === fixtures ===
  ok: CreateBucket /domain-b2 is 200
  ok: PutObject k is 200
  -- 
  -- === unit level: the two bucket entry points on one document ===
  -- Parse_Bucket_Response accepted it, tags=1
  ok: control: Parse_Bucket_Response decodes the mc document
  -- Parse_Bucket raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.TAGGING.MALFORMED_TAGGING: malformed bucket tagging XML
  FAILED: Tagging.Parse_Bucket accepts a namespace-free <Tagging>
  -- 
  -- === server: the same document on both tagging paths ===
  -- PutObjectTagging, mc document (accepted today)
  --     PUT /domain-b2/k?tagging body=<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>
  --     status=200 body=
  ok: PutObjectTagging, mc document (accepted today) is answered 200
  -- PutBucketTagging, mc document                 
  --     PUT /domain-b2?tagging body=<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>
  --     status=400 body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/domain-b2?tagging</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: PutBucketTagging, mc document                  is answered 200
  -- 
  -- === control: PutBucketTagging with xmlns (passes today) ===
  -- PutBucketTagging, boto3/aws-cli document      
  --     PUT /domain-b2?tagging body=<Tagging xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>
  --     status=200 body=
  ok: PutBucketTagging, boto3/aws-cli document       is answered 200
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

One document, three requests: accepted on the object resource, refused on the bucket resource, accepted again on the bucket resource once `xmlns` is added.

The client body is not hypothetical. `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z with minio-go v7.0.90, run from the `minio/mc@sha256:a7fe349ef4bd8521fb8497f55c6042871b2ae640607cf99d9bede5e9bdf11727` image with `--rm`, was pointed at a logging TCP proxy forwarding byte for byte to the prebuilt `tests/bin/s3_memory_server`. `mc tag set fly/mcbucket "team=storage"` put this on the wire, and `mc tag set fly/mcbucket/m1 "team=storage"` put the byte-identical body on the object resource:

```
PUT /mcbucket/?tagging= HTTP/1.1
Host: host.docker.internal:62457
User-Agent: MinIO (linux; arm64) minio-go/v7.0.90 mc/RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z
Content-Length: 84
Accept-Encoding: zstd,gzip
Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 Credential=AKIDEXAMPLE/20260907/us-east-1/s3/aws4_request, SignedHeaders=content-md5;host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date, Signature=d3100d8dc1dbaef9f61019cd7ab70ec96210d23537a3a30b8066e424ef10ab40
Content-Md5: SLw5gP7IN3lXnRzCSb/wzw==
X-Amz-Content-Sha256: UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD
X-Amz-Date: 20260907T070150Z

<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>
```

There is a plain-HTTP caveat, stated plainly. minio-go signs this body as `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`, which the crate refuses without TLS (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1903-1906`):

```ada
         when Authentication.Insecure_Unsigned_Payload =>
            Send_Error
              (X, 400, "InvalidRequest",
               "UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD requires a secure transport", Resource);
```

so over the plain-HTTP test endpoint `mc tag set` reports `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD requires a secure transport` and the namespace check is never reached. On an HTTPS endpoint that gate passes and the namespace check is what remains. To show what happens there, `mc`'s exact bytes were replayed over plain HTTP with a valid SigV4 header signature, a real payload hash and `Content-MD5`, so that neither the integrity gate nor the UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD rule applies:

```
PutBucketTagging  mc body (no xmlns)   : 400 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/replay-b?tagging</Resource></Error>
PutBucketTagging  same body + xmlns    : 200
```

Adding only `xmlns` turns the request into a 200.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A PutBucketTagging request body with an absent namespace must be accepted, because the pinned botocore S3 model puts `xmlNamespace` on the `PutBucketTaggingRequest.Tagging` member as a serializer instruction and puts none on the `Tagging` shape, because PutObjectTagging on this same server already accepts it, and because MinIO answers 200 for the exact bytes. A foreign namespace and any attribute may still be rejected.

## Suggested treatment

The smallest fix is one Boolean: give `Parse_Bucket` the same `Allow_Empty_Namespace => True` that `Parse_Bucket_Response` already uses, at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:574-579`. That brings the bucket request path in line with the bucket response path and with the object path, changes no public declaration, and leaves foreign namespaces and attributes invalid, because only the empty namespace is at issue.

This needs a maintainer decision only on scope, because the two remaining choices differ in what the strict mode is for:

- Flip `Parse_Bucket` to the tolerant mode. `Parse_Bucket` and `Parse_Bucket_Response` then differ in nothing, so one of them can be removed later. `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:48-50` should be reworded, since the namespace tolerance is no longer specific to the response decoder.
- Keep both modes and expose the choice to the server as a parameter. This preserves a strict parser for callers that want it, but it adds public surface, which is an interface decision under the "Decision authority" and "Ada interface values" sections of `AGENTS.md`.

`docs/qualification/bucket-tagging.md:50` states the current rule as a qualification claim and must be restated with the fix. Because https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/60 and https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/57 are the same question on two other request codecs, the three should be sequenced together so the answer is uniform.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, beside the existing bucket tagging block, as a signed `PUT /bucket?tagging` carrying the captured namespace-free body and a correct `Content-MD5`, asserting 200 and the stored tag. The unit assertion at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11267-11270` pins the rejection and must be inverted; the foreign-namespace cases around it must keep asserting rejection. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A bucket tag set cannot be written by any client that does not emit `xmlns`, and minio-go is such a client, so `mc tag set` on a bucket fails against a Flyology endpoint over HTTPS while it works against MinIO and AWS. The user sees 400 `MalformedXML`, which points at the XML rather than at a namespace, and the same client's object tagging succeeds on the same server, which makes the failure look like a bucket-specific outage. No data is lost or corrupted; the write simply never happens. Over a plain-HTTP endpoint minio-go is stopped one step earlier by the UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD rule, so the namespace rejection is only observable over TLS or through a replay of the same bytes with a signed payload hash. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Annotations.Parse_Query` matches the query parameters `annotationPrefix`, `maxAnnotationResults` and `continuationToken`, while the pinned botocore S3 model, the generated model descriptor and `Client.Low_Level.Prepare_List_Object_Annotations` all use `annotation-prefix`, `max-annotation-results` and `continuation-token`. Anything the codec does not recognise falls through to `Malformed_Annotation_Request`, which the server answers with 400 `InvalidArgument`. A Flyology client that asks for a prefix, a result limit or the next page is therefore refused by a Flyology server, and only the degenerate "list everything, one page" call works.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The three list-only members are matched by hand-written camelCase literals, and anything else is rejected (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-annotations.adb:137-178`):

```ada
                  elsif Name = "annotationPrefix" then
                     if Result.Has_Annotation_Prefix then

...
```

The route records that exception and answers 400. The sniff (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:6507-6521`):

and the answer (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:7015-7019`):

```ada
            "The object annotation request query is invalid", Target_Text);
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) names the querystring members with hyphens. Printed with python3:

and the shared request builder emits each one under its descriptor location name (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1217-1222`):

```ada
                  Query_Last := Query_Last + 1;

                    (Model.Member_Location_Name (Input_Shape, Member),
```

so the client puts the hyphenated spelling on the wire and the server refuses it. The two singular annotation verbs are correct: `Parse_Query` matches `annotationName` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-annotations.adb:119-127`) and `versionId` (`:128-136`), which is what the model gives them, so only these three names are wrong.

The in-tree gate sends the non-model spellings and so pins the defect (`tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:13550-13553`):
