
and again for the second page (`tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:13585-13589`):

```ada

              (SigV4.Pair ("annotation", ""),
```

Both must be updated with the fix. The qualification lane sets the three optional members only when environment inputs are present (`tests/src/s3_list_object_annotations_qualification.adb:85-95`):

```ada

      Has_Continuation_Token     =>

          ("FLYOLOGY_S3_QUALIFICATION_INPUT_CONTINUATION_TOKEN"),
```

so the default lane never sends them to a server and never noticed.

## What happens now

The names printed at the top come out of the compiled model through `Model.Member_Location_Name`, which is the same call the client's request builder makes, so descriptor and pinned JSON are not in conflict: the hand-written codec is the only place the camelCase names exist. The token in the last probe is the one the server itself issued on the preceding page, so pagination is impossible in the model's spelling.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

`Annotations.Parse_Query` must accept `max-annotation-results`, `annotation-prefix` and `continuation-token`, because that is what the pinned botocore S3 model gives those querystring members, what the generated descriptor at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-model.adb:45596-45601` carries, and what `Client.Low_Level.Prepare_List_Object_Annotations` puts on the wire.

## Suggested treatment

The descriptor is generated from the pinned model and agrees with it, and the "Ada source style" section of `AGENTS.md` forbids hand-editing generated sources, so the hand-written codec is the side that changes. Two shapes, neither of which needs a new public declaration:

- Correct the three literals at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-annotations.adb:137`, `:145` and `:158` to `annotation-prefix`, `max-annotation-results` and `continuation-token`. Smallest possible change.
- Compare against `Model.Member_Location_Name` for the operation's input shape, the way `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:492-504` already reads enumerations out of the model. This costs a little more code and cannot drift again.

Accepting both spellings is a third option, but it makes the non-model names part of the server's contract, so it is a policy choice rather than a fix.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`: change `SigV4.Pair ("maxAnnotationResults", "1")` at `:13552` and `:13588`, and `SigV4.Pair ("continuationToken", Token)` at `:13587`, to the model spellings, so the existing two-page assertion drives the names the crate's own client sends. Those three lines currently assert the wrong behaviour. The spike is the test.

## Severity

This is a self-interop failure: the crate's own client cannot use a prefix, a result limit or pagination against the crate's own server, and every such request is answered 400 `InvalidArgument`. Only the degenerate call that lists everything in one page works, so an object with more annotations than one page holds cannot be enumerated at all through the typed client. `ListObjectAnnotations` is a Flyology extension operation, so no third-party SDK is involved and the blast radius is limited to Flyology clients and to any client generated from the pinned model. The mismatch is a string comparison, so it fires on every request that carries one of the three parameters. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Multipart_Uploads.Validate` treats `Next_Key_Marker < Key_Marker` as proof that a page's cursor went backwards, but when `encoding-type=url` was requested the server has already percent-encoded both markers, and percent-encoding is not order preserving. `%` sorts below every unreserved byte, so `a9 < a:` raw becomes `a%3A < a9` encoded and the check fires on a page the backend answered correctly. `Validate` runs inside `Serialize_List_Multipart_Uploads`, and the ListMultipartUploads handler catches only `Malformed_List_Request`, so the exception escapes to the generic handler and the caller gets 500 `InternalError`. The same predicate runs in the client decoder, so a conforming non-Flyology server that encodes `NextKeyMarker` makes `Parse_List_Multipart_Uploads` raise `Invalid_Response`.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The ordering predicate compares the marker strings as they stand (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:655-667`):

```ada
      elsif Value.Is_Truncated
        and then
          (US.To_String (Value.Next_Key_Marker) <
             US.To_String (Value.Key_Marker)
           or else
             (Value.Next_Key_Marker = Value.Key_Marker
              and then
                (US.Length (Value.Next_Upload_ID_Marker) = 0
                 or else Value.Next_Upload_ID_Marker =
                   Value.Upload_ID_Marker)))
      then
         raise Malformed_Upload_Listing with
           "ListMultipartUploads next cursor does not advance";
```

`Validate` runs on both sides of the wire. In the client decoder (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:788`):

```ada
      Validate (Handler.Value);
```

and in the server serializer (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:823-828`):

```ada
   function Serialize_List_Multipart_Uploads
     (Value : List_Multipart_Uploads_Result) return String
   is
      Result : US.Unbounded_String;
   begin
      Validate (Value);
```

The server fills both key markers with encoded text when the request asked for it (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11200-11204`):

```ada
                        function Encoded (Value : String) return String is
                          (if Request.URL_Encoding
                           then Encoding.URI_Encode
                             (Value, Encode_Slash => False)
                           else Value);
```

`Key_Marker` is built from it (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11218-11220`):

```ada
                                Key_Marker => US.To_Unbounded_String
                                  (Encoded
                                     (US.To_String (Request.Key_Marker))),
```

and so is `Next_Key_Marker` (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11240-11246`):

```ada
                           if Page.Is_Truncated then
                              Response.Next_Key_Marker :=
                                US.To_Unbounded_String
                                  (Encoded
                                     (US.To_String (Page.Next_After.Key)));
                              Response.Next_Upload_ID_Marker :=
                                Page.Next_After.Upload_ID;
```

The upload identifier markers stay raw, so only the key markers are affected.

Percent-encoding inverts the order of any pair whose first divergent byte is one the encoder escapes. The unreserved set is `A-Z a-z 0-9 - . _ ~`, plus `/` when `Encode_Slash` is `False` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4_encoding.adb:10-17`):

```ada
   function Unreserved (Value : Character) return Boolean is
     (Value in 'A' .. 'Z'
      or else Value in 'a' .. 'z'
      or else Value in '0' .. '9'
      or else Value = '-'
      or else Value = '.'
      or else Value = '_'
      or else Value = '~');
```

Every one of those bytes is above `%` (16#25#), and every escaped byte is written as `%` followed by two hex digits (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4_encoding.adb:36-41`):

```ada
            else
               Result (Used + 1) := '%';
               Result (Used + 2) := Hex (Character'Pos (Item) / 16);
               Result (Used + 3) := Hex (Character'Pos (Item) mod 16);
               Used := Used + 3;
            end if;
```

so no non-ASCII key is needed. The ASCII keys `a9` and `a:` are enough: `a9 < a:` raw, `a%3A < a9` encoded. The same inversion happens for `:` `[` `=` `&` `?` `#`, a space, and any non-ASCII byte.

The handler around the serializer catches only the request-side exception (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11280-11286`):

```ada
                  exception
                     when Multipart_Uploads.Malformed_List_Request =>
                        Send_Error
                          (X, 400, "InvalidArgument",
                           "The ListMultipartUploads request is invalid",
                           Target_Text);
                  end;
```

so `Malformed_Upload_Listing` reaches the generic tail and the caller sees 500 `InternalError`.

The client decoder maps the same exception to `Invalid_Response` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:13422-13426`):

```ada
   exception
      when Occurrence : S3.Multipart_Uploads.Malformed_Upload_Listing =>
         raise Invalid_Response with
           "malformed ListMultipartUploads response: " &
           Ada.Exceptions.Exception_Message (Occurrence);
```

and URL encoding is a supported client parameter, echoed back through the decoder's own expectation function (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:13477-13484`):

```ada
         Expected_Encoding : constant String :=
           (if Prepared.Requested_URL_Encoding then "url" else "");
         function Expected_Echo
           (Value : US.Unbounded_String) return String is
           (if Prepared.Requested_URL_Encoding
            then Encoding.URI_Encode
              (US.To_String (Value), Encode_Slash => False)
            else US.To_String (Value));
```

so the client is affected symmetrically against any conforming server, Flyology or not.

No existing test produces the combination, and nothing asserts the wrong behaviour. The socket corpus requests URL encoding only to check echo mismatches, with markers it never pages past (`tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:12666-12673`):

```ada
            Uploads_Parameters.Delimiter := US.To_Unbounded_String ("/");
            Uploads_Parameters.URL_Encoding := True;
            Uploads_Parameters.Key_Marker := US.To_Unbounded_String ("a+b");
            Uploads_Parameters.Max_Uploads := 7;
            Uploads_Parameters.Prefix :=
              US.To_Unbounded_String ("photos/Jan &");
            Uploads_Parameters.Upload_ID_Marker :=
              US.To_Unbounded_String ("upload+/=");
```

and the codec suite only rejects an unsupported encoding type (`tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:10974-10975`):

```ada
      Must_Reject_Query
        ("uploads&encoding-type=xml", "invalid upload encoding accepted");
```

`rg "does not advance" src` matches only `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb`, so no sibling listing codec shares the predicate.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates multipart uploads for the keys `a9`, `a:` and `az` in the in-process memory-backed application. It first decodes a conforming truncated URL-encoded page through the client decoder, then pages the real server with `encoding-type=url&max-uploads=1` and asserts 200 with the encoded next marker on page 2. The same paging without `encoding-type` is the control.

```ada
   --  Hand-built page that a conforming server (AWS with EncodingType=url)
   --  produces for the same fixtures; the client decoder must accept it.
   Encoded_Page : constant String :=
     "<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" &

     "<MaxUploads>1</MaxUploads>" &
     "<IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated>" &
     "<EncodingType>url</EncodingType>" &
     "<Upload><Key>a%3A</Key><UploadId>u2</UploadId>" &
     "<Initiated>2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z</Initiated></Upload>" &
     "</ListMultipartUploadsResult>";
```

Output, exit status 1, with upload identifiers and repeated `<Upload>` bodies trimmed at `...`:

```
  -- 
  -- === client decoder: a conforming truncated url-encoded page ===
  -- Parse_List_Multipart_Uploads raised Malformed_Upload_Listing: ListMultipartUploads next cursor does not advance
  FAILED: client decodes an encoded truncated page
  -- 
  -- === server: paging with encoding-type=url&max-uploads=1 ===
  -- page 1 status=200
  --     body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><ListMultipartUploadsResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Bucket>domain-b4</Bucket><NextKeyMarker>a9</NextKeyMarker><NextUploadIdMarker>4fb6a2da...</NextUploadIdMarker><MaxUploads>1</MaxUploads><IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated><Upload>...<Key>a9</Key>...</Upload><EncodingType>url</EncodingType></ListMultipartUploadsResult>
  ok: page 1 is 200
  ok: page 1 NextKeyMarker is a9
  -- page 2 (key-marker=a9) status=500
  --     body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>InternalError</Code><Message>We encountered an internal error</Message><Resource>/domain-b4?uploads&amp;encoding-type=url&amp;max-uploads=1&amp;key-marker=a9&amp;upload-id-marker=4fb6a2da...</Resource></Error>
  FAILED: page 2 of a url-encoded listing is 200
  FAILED: page 2 reports the encoded next key marker a%3A
  -- 
  -- === control: the same paging without encoding-type ===
  -- plain page 2 status=200
  --     body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><ListMultipartUploadsResult ...><KeyMarker>a9</KeyMarker><NextKeyMarker>a:</NextKeyMarker>...<IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated><Upload>...<Key>a:</Key>...</Upload></ListMultipartUploadsResult>
  ok: control: page 2 without encoding-type is 200
BUG REPRODUCED: 3 failed expectation(s)
```

The control is the same paging over the same three uploads with `encoding-type` dropped, and it returns 200 with `<NextKeyMarker>a:</NextKeyMarker>`, so the fixtures, the backend page and the cursor are all correct and only the encoding turns the answer into a 500.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire.

## What should happen

A truncated `encoding-type=url` page whose next key sorts after the previous marker must be serialized and decoded normally. The cursor-advance rule is a statement about key order, and key order is defined on the decoded keys, which is what both sides hold before the server applies `Encoding.URI_Encode`. Percent-encoding is a transport spelling, and it is not order preserving, so it must not be the input to an ordering comparison. AWS encodes `NextKeyMarker` the same way when `EncodingType=url` is requested, so a conforming server produces this page.

## Suggested treatment

`Validate` must compare decoded markers. The server still holds them before encoding, in `Request.Key_Marker` and `Page.Next_After.Key` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11218-11246`, so validating before encoding fixes the server on its own. That is not enough, because `Validate` is shared with the client decoder, which only ever sees the encoded page.

This needs a maintainer decision, because it changes what a shared codec rule means:

- Have `Validate` decode `Key_Marker` and `Next_Key_Marker` when `Encoding_Type` is `"url"` before comparing. This is the only choice that also fixes the client, and it keeps the ordering guarantee for every page. It makes `Validate` depend on a decoder for the encoded form.
- Drop the ordering predicate for pages that carry `Encoding_Type = "url"`. Smaller, but it removes the check exactly where a wrong cursor is hardest to see.
- Validate before encoding on the server and leave the client decoder as it is. This fixes the 500 but leaves the crate unable to read a conforming AWS page.

Independently of the choice, the ListMultipartUploads handler at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11280-11286` should also catch `Multipart_Uploads.Malformed_Upload_Listing`, so a serializer failure cannot present as `InternalError`.

The regression tests belong in two places. The server half goes in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, as multipart uploads for `a9` and `a:` paged with `encoding-type=url&max-uploads=1`, asserting 200 and `<NextKeyMarker>a%3A</NextKeyMarker>` on page 2. The client half goes in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the existing URL-encoding block at `:12666-12692`, as a scripted truncated page with `<KeyMarker>a9</KeyMarker>` and `<NextKeyMarker>a%3A</NextKeyMarker>` that the decoder must accept. No existing assertion has to be inverted. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A valid, backend-answered ListMultipartUploads request ends as 500 `InternalError`, which reads as a server fault rather than as a cursor rule, and the listing cannot be paged past the first truncation. It needs `encoding-type=url` plus two keys that first differ at a byte the encoder escapes, which includes `:` and a space, so timestamps, URNs and `a:b` namespacing in key names are enough and no non-ASCII key is required. On the client side the same predicate turns a conforming page from any server, AWS included, into `Invalid_Response`. Nothing is lost or corrupted and nothing hangs: the request simply cannot be completed. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Attributes.Valid_Storage_Class` duplicates the pinned botocore S3 model's `StorageClass` enumeration as thirteen string literals, and the model has fifteen values. `Validate` rejects any present-but-unlisted value with `Malformed_Attributes "invalid storage class"`, which `Client.Low_Level` turns into `Invalid_Response "malformed GetObjectAttributes response"`. A GetObjectAttributes response naming `AWS_BACKUP_WARM` or `AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM`, both of which are modelled and both of which the crate's own replication codec already decodes, is therefore reported to the caller as a malformed answer.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The list is written out by hand (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:756-760`):

```ada
   function Valid_Storage_Class (Value : String) return Boolean is
     (Value in "STANDARD" | "REDUCED_REDUNDANCY" | "STANDARD_IA" |
       "ONEZONE_IA" | "INTELLIGENT_TIERING" | "GLACIER" |
       "DEEP_ARCHIVE" | "OUTPOSTS" | "GLACIER_IR" | "SNOW" |
       "EXPRESS_ONEZONE" | "FSX_OPENZFS" | "FSX_ONTAP");
```

and `Validate` applies it whenever the member is present, not only when it was selected (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:775-779`):

```ada
      if Value.Has_Storage_Class
        and then not Valid_Storage_Class (US.To_String (Value.Storage_Class))
      then
         raise Malformed_Attributes with "invalid storage class";
      end if;
```

The client maps the exception to a response-level failure (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5330-5332`):

```ada
   exception
      when S3.Attributes.Malformed_Attributes | S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed GetObjectAttributes response";
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) carries fifteen values. Printed with python3:

```
--- StorageClass enum ---
15 ['STANDARD', 'REDUCED_REDUNDANCY', 'STANDARD_IA', 'ONEZONE_IA', 'INTELLIGENT_TIERING', 'GLACIER', 'DEEP_ARCHIVE', 'OUTPOSTS', 'GLACIER_IR', 'SNOW', 'EXPRESS_ONEZONE', 'FSX_OPENZFS', 'FSX_ONTAP', 'AWS_BACKUP_WARM', 'AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM']
```

The model is not the limitation. The crate's own replication codec already decodes both missing spellings (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-replication.adb:485-488`):

```ada
      elsif Value = "AWS_BACKUP_WARM" then
         return AWS_Backup_Warm;
      elsif Value = "AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM" then
         return AWS_Backup_Low_Cost_Warm;
```

and the sibling listing codec validates the same shape dynamically against the model instead of copying it (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:492-504`):

```ada
   function Valid_Enumeration
     (Value : String; Shape : Model.Shape_Index) return Boolean is
   begin
      if Model.Enumeration_Count (Shape) = 0 then
         return False;
      end if;
      for Index in 1 .. Model.Enumeration_Count (Shape) loop
         if Model.Enumeration_Value (Shape, Index) = Value then
            return True;
         end if;
      end loop;
      return False;
   end Valid_Enumeration;
```

used for the upload storage class at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:687-689`. So the mechanism that would have prevented the drift is already in the same area.

The compatibility matrix claims a complete typed client for this operation (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:60`):

```
| GetObjectAttributes | memory, files, SQLite including exact retained generations | complete typed composable, synchronous, and convenience clients; ...
```

so the drift also contradicts a maintained document.

No test pins the list: `rg AWS_BACKUP tests/src` matches only the replication corpus, and the crate's own server never emits anything but `STANDARD`, so no lane can produce the value. Nothing asserts the wrong behaviour, so no test has to be inverted.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below prints the `StorageClass` enumeration straight out of the compiled model, then builds one GetObjectAttributes response body per class and decodes it through `S3.Attributes.Parse_Result` and through `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Response`. `STANDARD` and `GLACIER_IR` are the controls.

```ada
   function Response_Body (Storage_Class : String) return String is
     ("<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" &
      "<GetObjectAttributesResponse " &
      "xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
      "<ETag>""9a0364b9e99bb480dd25e1f0284c8555""</ETag>" &
      "<StorageClass>" & Storage_Class & "</StorageClass>" &
      "<ObjectSize>7</ObjectSize>" &
      "</GetObjectAttributesResponse>");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- === the pinned model's StorageClass enumeration ===
  ok: control: the model has a GetObjectAttributes output shape
  ok: control: the output shape has a StorageClass member
  -- model StorageClass values (15):
  --     STANDARD
  --     REDUCED_REDUNDANCY
  --     STANDARD_IA
  --     ONEZONE_IA
  --     INTELLIGENT_TIERING
  --     GLACIER

  --     OUTPOSTS

  --     EXPRESS_ONEZONE
  --     FSX_OPENZFS

  --     AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM
  -- 
  -- === control: a class the codec knows ===
  -- STANDARD

  --   Client.Low_Level: accepted, kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND
  -- GLACIER_IR

  -- AWS_BACKUP_WARM

  --   Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: client decodes StorageClass AWS_BACKUP_WARM
  -- AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM
  --   S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid storage class
  --   Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: client decodes StorageClass AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM
BUG REPRODUCED: 2 failed expectation(s)
```

The fifteen values are read out of the compiled model in the same run that rejects two of them, and the four response bodies differ only in the `<StorageClass>` text, so nothing but the enumeration membership decides pass or fail.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A GetObjectAttributes response naming any value in the pinned model's `StorageClass` enumeration must decode, including `AWS_BACKUP_WARM` and `AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM`. The model is the authority the crate already uses for the same shape in `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:687-689` and for the same two values in `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-replication.adb:485-488`.

## Suggested treatment

Replace the literal list at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:756-760` with a model-driven check, so it cannot drift again: look the value up in the `StorageClass` enumeration the compiled model already exposes, as `Valid_Enumeration` does at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:492-504`. No new public declaration is needed, and `Validate` keeps its current shape and exception. Adding the two missing literals would also close the defect, but it leaves the same duplication in place and it will drift again on the next model bump.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the other scripted GetObjectAttributes response cases, as one decode per modelled storage class driven through `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Response` and asserted to succeed. Driving the assertion off the model's enumeration rather than off a second hand-written list keeps the test from drifting with the codec. No existing assertion has to be inverted. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A valid, modelled 200 is reported to the caller as a malformed response, and every typed and convenience GetObjectAttributes client sees it, because the check runs whenever the member is present and the member is present exactly when the caller selected `StorageClass`. It needs an object in one of two uncommon storage classes, so it is reachable only against AWS or an implementation that reports the AWS Backup classes, and the crate's own server never emits anything but `STANDARD`. Nothing is written or lost; the read is refused. Deterministic; not reproduced against a live server.

## Summary

`Tagging.Parse` raises `Malformed_Tagging` for a structural XML error, an out-of-repertoire tag key, a duplicate key and an eleventh tag alike, so the object tagging path has no way to say "the XML was fine, the tags were not". The server maps that one exception to 400 `MalformedXML` with a message about the schema. The same tag set sent as `x-amz-tagging` on PutObject, or as a bucket tag set, is answered 400 `InvalidTag` by the same server, and MinIO answers `InvalidTag` for the repertoire and duplicate cases. The package already declares `Invalid_Tag` and never raises it on this path.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

An eleventh `<Tag>` is a structural error to this handler (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:60-65`):

```ada
         when 3 =>
            if Local_Name /= "Tag" or else not Item.Seen_Tag_Set
              or else Item.Tags.Length = Maximum_Object_Tags
            then
               raise Malformed_Tagging with "invalid object tag entry";
            end if;
```

and the repertoire and duplicate-key failures are folded into the same exception at the end of the parse (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:163-167`):

```ada
      if not Handler.Seen_Tag_Set or else Handler.Depth /= 0
        or else not Valid_S3_Tags (Handler.Tags)
      then
         raise Malformed_Tagging with "invalid object tagging document";
      end if;
```

`Invalid_Tag` is declared for the package (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:8-10`):

```ada
   Malformed_Tagging : exception;
   Malformed_Tagging_Query : exception;
   Invalid_Tag : exception;
```

and is never raised on the object XML path.

The server has one arm for the object route (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15955-15961`):

```ada
                     exception

                           Send_Error

                              "The XML provided did not validate against " &
                              "the object tagging schema", Target_Text);
                           return;
```

and it stores the parsed set directly at `:15950-15953`, so there is no later re-validation that could emit a different code.

Two other routes answer `InvalidTag` for the same validator failure. `x-amz-tagging` on PutObject (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15074-15079`):

```ada
                     exception
                        when Tagging.Malformed_Tagging_Query =>
                           Send_Error
                             (X, 400, "InvalidTag",
                              "The PutObject tag set is invalid", Target_Text);
                           return;
```

and CreateMultipartUpload (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11993-11999`):

```ada
                           Send_Error

```

PutBucketTagging keeps the two apart, so a bad tag there is `InvalidTag` and only a bad document is `MalformedXML` (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:8290-8294`):

```ada
                           when Tagging.Invalid_Tag =>
                              Send_Error
                                (X, 400, "InvalidTag",
                                 "The tag set contains an invalid tag",
                                 Target_Text);
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) does not settle the code. Printed with python3:

```
--- errors lists ---

 PutBucketTagging   errors=None
 DeleteObjects      errors=None
 InvalidTag shape present: False
```

There is no error shape on any of the three operations and no `InvalidTag` exception shape anywhere in the model, so the authority is the crate's own other two paths plus a real server. MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, answers the same three signed `PUT /oracle-b/k?tagging` documents:

```
PutObjectTagging with key a#b                              : 400 <Error><Code>InvalidTag</Code><Message>The TagKey you have provided is invalid</Message>...
PutObjectTagging with a duplicate key                      : 400 <Error><Code>InvalidTag</Code><Message>Cannot provide multiple Tags with the same key</Message>...
PutObjectTagging with eleven tags                          : 400 <Error><Code>BadRequest</Code><Message>Tags cannot be more than 10</Message>...
```

so the repertoire and duplicate cases are settled twice over, and the count case is where the crate and MinIO disagree.

The existing tests do not catch it because none of them asserts a code for an invalid tag on the object XML route. The header path is covered (`tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6419-6422`):

```ada

        (Has (Ten_Tag_Result, "200 OK")
         and then Has (Eleven_Tag_Result, "<Code>InvalidTag</Code>")
         and then Has (Malformed_Tag, "<Code>InvalidTag</Code>")
```

and the PutObjectTagging block covers admission only, asserting the accepted case (`tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:14403-14406`):

```ada
         Require
           (Has (Response, "200 OK")
            and then Has (Response, "x-amz-version-id: null" & CRLF),
            "PutObjectTagging rejected an unversioned tagging document");
```

No assertion has to be inverted for the two cases MinIO confirms.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below creates `/domain-b6/k` in the in-process memory-backed application and sends three namespaced `PUT /domain-b6/k?tagging` documents with a correct `Content-MD5`, an out-of-repertoire key `a#b`, a duplicate key, and eleven tags, asserting that the `<Code>` is `InvalidTag`. The controls are the same bad key sent as `x-amz-tagging` on PutObject and as a bucket tag set on the same server.

```ada
   function Document (Tags : String) return String is
     ("<Tagging xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
      "<TagSet>" & Tags & "</TagSet></Tagging>");
...
   Object_Case ("outside the AWS repertoire (key a#b)",
                Document (Tag_Element ("a#b", "v")));
   Object_Case ("duplicate key                       ",
                Document (Tag_Element ("dup", "1") &
                          Tag_Element ("dup", "2")));
   Object_Case ("eleven tags                         ",
                Document (Eleven_Tags));
...
   Control ("PutObject x-amz-tagging with key a#b",
            Run (Signed_Request
                   ("PUT", Bucket & "/hdr", "x",
                    "x-amz-tagging: a%23b=v" & CRLF)));
   Control ("PutBucketTagging with key a#b       ",
            Run (Tagging_Put (Bucket, Document (Tag_Element ("a#b", "v")))));
```

Output, exit status 1:

```

  ok: CreateBucket /domain-b6 is 200
  ok: PutObject k is 200
  -- 
  -- === the same tag failures on the object XML path ===
  -- PutObjectTagging  outside the AWS repertoire (key a#b)
  --     status=400 code=MalformedXML
  FAILED: PutObjectTagging reports InvalidTag for outside the AWS repertoire (key a#b)
  -- PutObjectTagging  duplicate key

  FAILED: PutObjectTagging reports InvalidTag for duplicate key                       
  -- PutObjectTagging  eleven tags                         
  --     status=400 code=MalformedXML
  FAILED: PutObjectTagging reports InvalidTag for eleven tags                         
  -- 
  -- === controls: the other two paths on the same server ===
  -- control PutObject x-amz-tagging with key a#b
  --     status=400 code=InvalidTag
  ok: control: PutObject x-amz-tagging with key a#b already reports InvalidTag
  -- control PutBucketTagging with key a#b       
  --     status=400 code=InvalidTag
  ok: control: PutBucketTagging with key a#b        already reports InvalidTag
BUG REPRODUCED: 3 failed expectation(s)
```

Every document in the program carries `xmlns`, and the bucket control with the identical body reaches `InvalidTag`, so the namespace rule is not what fires here.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A well-formed `<Tagging>` document whose tags violate the AWS repertoire, or repeat a key, must be answered `InvalidTag`, because that is what the crate's own `x-amz-tagging` and PutBucketTagging routes answer for the same `Valid_S3_Tags` failure and what MinIO answers for the same bytes. `MalformedXML` should stay reserved for a document that really is malformed.

## Suggested treatment

Raise `Tagging.Invalid_Tag`, already declared at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:10`, from the tag-validity failures, and give the server one more arm. Concretely, split the condition at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:163-167` so that `not Valid_S3_Tags (Handler.Tags)` raises `Invalid_Tag` while the structural conditions keep raising `Malformed_Tagging`, and add `when Tagging.Invalid_Tag => Send_Error (X, 400, "InvalidTag", ...)` beside the existing arm at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15955-15961`, matching the bucket route at `:8290-8294`. No new public declaration is needed. The `when others` re-raise at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:172-173` must be adjusted so `Invalid_Tag` is not swallowed into `Malformed_Tagging`, the way the existing `when Malformed_Tagging => raise;` arm at `:170-171` already does.

The eleventh-tag case needs a maintainer decision, because the three authorities disagree and the answer is an externally visible error code:

- `InvalidTag`, matching the crate's own header path, which already answers `InvalidTag` for an eleventh tag (`tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6421`). Consistent inside the crate; differs from MinIO.
- `BadRequest`, matching MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`. Consistent with the observed server; differs from the crate's own header path, which would then also have to change or stay inconsistent.
- Leave the count case as `MalformedXML` and change only the repertoire and duplicate cases. Smallest change, but the object route keeps one diagnostic that neither other route nor MinIO gives.

The pinned model names no error shape for `PutObjectTagging`, so it cannot settle this.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, in the PutObjectTagging block near `:14396-14407`, as three signed `PUT /bucket/key?tagging` requests with a correct `Content-MD5` asserting the chosen `<Code>` for the bad key, the duplicate key and the eleventh tag. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` is where a matching MinIO assertion belongs if the count case is aligned with that server. No existing assertion has to be inverted for the repertoire and duplicate cases. The spike is the test.

## Severity

Wrong diagnostics, not wrong data: the request is correctly refused and nothing is stored, but the caller is told its XML is malformed when the XML is fine and only a tag key is not. A user who hits it will look at their serializer rather than at their tag, and the same tag set sent through `x-amz-tagging` on the same server names the real cause. Any client that sets a tag key containing a character outside the AWS repertoire, repeats a key, or sends an eleventh tag reaches it. The three cases are pure functions of the document, so they fire on every such request. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Attributes.Validate` passes `Is_Multipart => Value.Has_Object_Parts` into `Validate_Object_Checksum`, and `Has_Object_Parts` is set on the `<ObjectParts>` start tag alone. The narrow multipart policy then applies, which permits only CRC32, CRC32C and CRC64NVME, instead of the ordinary-object policy the same package exports and documents. A server that reports a one-part container for an object written with a single PUT, which MinIO does, and returns a `FULL_OBJECT` SHA-256, MD5 or xxHash digest gets `Invalid_Response` for a well-formed 200. The same branch fires when the server omits `ChecksumType` and the decoder infers `FULL_OBJECT`.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The flag is set on element presence (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:422-428`):

```ada
         elsif Local_Name = "ObjectParts" then
            if Item.Seen_Object_Parts then
               raise Malformed_Attributes with "duplicate ObjectParts";
            end if;
            Item.Seen_Object_Parts := True;
            Item.Value.Has_Object_Parts := True;
            Item.In_Parts := True;
```

and is used as the multipart verdict (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:771-774`):

```ada
      if Value.Has_Checksum then
         Validate_Object_Checksum
           (Value.Checksum, Is_Multipart => Value.Has_Object_Parts);
      end if;
```

The `FULL_OBJECT` branch then requires the multipart predicate (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:731-738`):

```ada
         elsif Kind = "FULL_OBJECT"
           and then
             ((Is_Multipart
               and then not Policy.Supported
                 (Algorithm, Policy.Full_Object))
              or else not Raw)
         then
            raise Malformed_Attributes with "invalid full-object checksum";
```

`Policy.Supported` is documented as the multipart rule and allows three algorithms (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-checksum_policy.ads:69-75`):

```ada
   --  Report whether AWS permits the algorithm/type pair for multipart use.
   function Supported
     (Value : Algorithm; Kind : Checksum_Type) return Boolean is
     (case Kind is
         when Full_Object =>
           Value in Core.CRC32 | Core.CRC32C | Core.CRC64NVME,
         when Composite   => Value /= Core.CRC64NVME);
```

The package already exports the predicate for the other case, and says so (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-checksum_policy.ads:77-83`):

```ada
   --  Report whether an ordinary, non-multipart object can retain this
   --  checksum. PutObject and CopyObject accept every concrete algorithm in
   --  the pinned model, always as one full-object digest. Keep this predicate
   --  separate from the narrower multipart policy above.
   function Ordinary_Object_Supported
     (Kind : Checksum_Type) return Boolean is
     (Kind = Full_Object);
```

The decoder never reaches it, because it decides which world it is in from the container's presence.

The same branch fires for servers that omit the type, because `Parse_Result` infers `FULL_OBJECT` for a plain Base64 digest (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:871-876`):

```ada
            elsif Wire_Core.Valid_Base64
              (Text, Policy.Digest_Length (Algorithm))
            then
               Handler.Value.Checksum.Kind :=
                 US.To_Unbounded_String ("FULL_OBJECT");
            end if;
```

The client turns the exception into a response-level failure (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5330-5332`):

```ada
   exception
      when S3.Attributes.Malformed_Attributes | S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed GetObjectAttributes response";
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) ties nothing about `ObjectParts` to a multipart upload. Printed with python3:

```
--- GetObjectAttributesOutput.ObjectParts ---
{"shape": "GetObjectAttributesParts"}
required: None
--- ObjectAttributes enum --- ['ETag', 'Checksum', 'ObjectParts', 'StorageClass', 'ObjectSize']
```

`ObjectParts` is an ordinary optional output member, and the request enum lets a caller select it for any object.

MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, really does return a full-object SHA-256 together with a one-part container for an object written with one PUT carrying `x-amz-checksum-sha256`. A signed `GET /oracle-b/single?attributes` with `x-amz-object-attributes: ETag,Checksum,ObjectParts,StorageClass,ObjectSize`:

```
GetObjectAttributes single : 200 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?> <getObjectAttributesResponse><ETag>5eb63bbbe01eeed093cb22bb8f5acdc3</ETag><Checksum><ChecksumSHA256>uU0nuZNNPgilLlLX2n2r+sSE7+N6U4DukIj3rOLvzek=</ChecksumSHA256><ChecksumType>FULL_OBJECT</ChecksumType></Checksum><ObjectParts><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated><MaxParts>10000</MaxParts><NextPartNumberMarker>1</NextPartNumberMarker><PartNumberMarker>0</PartNumberMarker><PartsCount>1</PartsCount><Part><PartNumber>1</PartNumber><Size>11</Size></Part></ObjectParts><StorageClass>STANDARD</StorageClass><ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize></getObjectAttributesResponse>
```

Two separate rules stop that exact answer before the checksum rule is even relevant. Its root element is the lowercase `getObjectAttributesResponse`, which the decoder refuses (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:405-409`):

```ada
      if Item.Depth = 1 then
         if Local_Name /= "GetObjectAttributesResponse" then
            raise Malformed_Attributes with
              "wrong object-attributes response root";
         end if;
```

That deviation is already a documented exclusion (`tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:64` and `:73-74`):

```
FLYOLOGY_GET_OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_ORACLE_MODE=minio-2025-lowercase-root \
...
echo "MinIO GetObjectAttributes oracle: skipped, pinned response uses" \
  "a non-AWS lowercase root element"
```

and its `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` is refused by a separate bound, reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/67. Between them, MinIO's answer never reaches this checksum rule in any lane, which is why the rule was never exercised in tree.

The crate's own server cannot produce the combination either, because it emits the container only for multipart objects (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16314-16316`):

```ada
                     if Selection.Object_Parts and then Snapshot.Is_Multipart
                     then
                        Response.Has_Object_Parts := True;
```

The conformance corpus pairs `ObjectParts` with CRC-family checksums on completed multipart objects only (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1691-1700`):

```ada
         Parameters.Has_Max_Parts := True;
         Parameters.Max_Parts := 1;
         Parameters.Has_Part_Number_Marker := True;
         Parameters.Part_Number_Marker := 0;
         Parameters.Attributes :=
           (Entity_Tag => True,
            Checksum => True,
            Object_Parts => Multipart_Checksum_Oracle_Mode /=
              RustFS_RC3_Multipart_Checksum_Divergence,
            Storage_Class => True, Object_Size => True);
```

so no case combines a non-CRC full-object checksum with a present parts container, and nothing asserts the wrong behaviour.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below decodes MinIO's captured checksum and parts combination, re-rooted under the AWS root element so the root rule does not fire first, plus MD5, xxHash64 and a variant with `ChecksumType` omitted, through `S3.Attributes.Parse_Result` and `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Response`. The controls are a `FULL_OBJECT` CRC32 with the container present and a `FULL_OBJECT` SHA-256 with no container. The last block puts a single-PUT object with `x-amz-checksum-sha256` into the in-process memory-backed application and reads its `GetObjectAttributes` answer back through the crate's own client. MinIO's `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` is lowered to `1000` in the program so that only the checksum rule is exercised; the 10000 echo is https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/67, and against a live MinIO the two rejections stack.

```ada
   Object_Parts : constant String :=
     "<ObjectParts><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>" &
     "<MaxParts>1000</MaxParts>" &
     "<NextPartNumberMarker>1</NextPartNumberMarker>" &
     "<PartNumberMarker>0</PartNumberMarker><PartsCount>1</PartsCount>" &
     "<Part><PartNumber>1</PartNumber><Size>11</Size></Part></ObjectParts>";

   function Response_Body
     (Checksum : String; With_Parts : Boolean := True) return String
   is
     ("<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" &
      "<GetObjectAttributesResponse " &
      "xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
      "<ETag>""5eb63bbbe01eeed093cb22bb8f5acdc3""</ETag>" &
      "<Checksum>" & Checksum & "</Checksum>" &
      (if With_Parts then Object_Parts else "") &
      "<StorageClass>STANDARD</StorageClass>" &
      "<ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize>" &
      "</GetObjectAttributesResponse>");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- === controls: shapes the decoder accepts today ===
  -- FULL_OBJECT CRC32, parts container present   
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: accepted, Has_Checksum=TRUE Has_Object_Parts=TRUE
  --     Client.Low_Level: accepted, kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND
  -- FULL_OBJECT SHA256, no parts container       
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: accepted, Has_Checksum=TRUE Has_Object_Parts=FALSE
  --     Client.Low_Level: accepted, kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND
  -- 
  -- === the modeled combinations the decoder rejects ===
  -- FULL_OBJECT SHA256 + parts (MinIO's answer)  
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid full-object checksum
  --     Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: FULL_OBJECT SHA256 + parts (MinIO's answer)  
  -- FULL_OBJECT MD5 + parts                      
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid full-object checksum
  --     Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: FULL_OBJECT MD5 + parts                      
  -- FULL_OBJECT XXHASH64 + parts                 
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid full-object checksum
  --     Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: FULL_OBJECT XXHASH64 + parts                 
  -- untyped raw SHA256 + parts (type omitted)    
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid full-object checksum
  --     Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: untyped raw SHA256 + parts (type omitted)    
  -- 
  -- === self-interop: what this crate's own server emits ===
  ok: CreateBucket /domain-b7 is 200
  ok: PutObject with x-amz-checksum-sha256 is 200
  -- GetObjectAttributes status=200
  --     body=<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><GetObjectAttributesResponse xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><ETag>5eb63bbbe01eeed093cb22bb8f5acdc3</ETag><Checksum><ChecksumSHA256>uU0nuZNNPgilLlLX2n2r+sSE7+N6U4DukIj3rOLvzek=</ChecksumSHA256><ChecksumType>FULL_OBJECT</ChecksumType></Checksum><ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize></GetObjectAttributesResponse>
  ok: control: the crate's own GetObjectAttributes is 200
  -- this crate's own answer, read by its own client
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: accepted, Has_Checksum=TRUE Has_Object_Parts=FALSE
  --     Client.Low_Level: accepted, kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND
  ok: this crate's own answer, read by its own client
BUG REPRODUCED: 4 failed expectation(s)
```

The two controls isolate the rule. The same SHA-256 digest is accepted when the parts container is absent, and the same parts container is accepted when the digest is CRC32, so neither the checksum nor the container is refused on its own. The last block shows the crate's own server answering the identical single-PUT question without a parts container, so this is a client-side failure against other servers and not a self-interop failure.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A `FULL_OBJECT` checksum of any modelled algorithm must decode even when the response carries an `ObjectParts` container, because the pinned model makes `ObjectParts` an ordinary optional output member with nothing tying its presence to a multipart upload, and because `Checksum_Policy.Ordinary_Object_Supported` is the predicate the same package documents for a single full-object digest. A genuinely composite checksum must still be held to the multipart rule.

## Suggested treatment

`Validate` needs a real multipart signal instead of the container's presence, at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:771-774`. This needs a maintainer decision, because the choice fixes what "multipart" means to this decoder and each option accepts a different set of responses:

- Apply `Ordinary_Object_Supported` unless the checksum is explicitly `COMPOSITE`. Smallest change, and it accepts every server that labels its digest honestly. A composite digest with a bare `-N` suffix and no `ChecksumType` is inferred as `COMPOSITE` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:866-870`, so that case is still caught.
- Use `Value.Object_Parts.Total_Parts_Count > 1` as the multipart signal. Closest to the container's meaning, but the member is optional in the model and MinIO's one-part answer reports `PartsCount 1`, so an absent count needs its own rule.
- Use the composite `-N` suffix on the ETag. Independent of the checksum, but it depends on an opaque field the crate treats as opaque elsewhere.

`Checksum_Policy` already exports both predicates, so no new public declaration is required for any of them.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the other scripted GetObjectAttributes responses, as a decode of a `FULL_OBJECT` SHA-256 together with a one-part `ObjectParts` container that must succeed, with a `COMPOSITE` SHA-256 plus container case kept as a rejection. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1691-1700` selects `Checksum` with `ObjectParts` only for completed multipart objects and should gain the single-PUT case once the MinIO oracle can run it. No existing assertion has to be inverted. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A valid, modelled 200 is reported to the caller as a malformed response, so a GetObjectAttributes that selects both `Checksum` and `ObjectParts` fails against any server that reports a parts container for a single-PUT object and uses a non-CRC digest. MinIO is such a server. The trigger needs the caller to select both attribute groups, which the convenience clients expose, and the crate's own server never produces the combination, so a deployment that talks only to Flyology will not see it. Against MinIO specifically the failure is currently masked, because the documented lowercase-root exclusion and the `MaxParts` bound reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/67 both stop the same response earlier. Nothing is written or lost; the read is refused. Deterministic.

## Summary

The GetObjectAttributes response decoder rejects any `<MaxParts>` above `Core.Page_Size'Last`, which is 1,000. That subtype exists for Flyology's own pagination policy, and the pinned botocore S3 model puts no bound on this member at all. MinIO answers `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` on every GetObjectAttributes request that selects `ObjectParts`, regardless of what was asked for, so a Flyology client talking to a MinIO-compatible server gets `Invalid_Response` for a well-formed 200. The diagnostic is wrong as well as the rule: because the check runs inside the SAX handler, the range failure is rewritten as "malformed GetObjectAttributes XML".

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The scalar handler applies the bound while parsing (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:558-576`):

```ada
               if not Parsed.Valid
                 or else
                   (Item.Field = Part_Number_Field
                    and then Parsed.Value not in Core.Part_Number'Range)
                 or else
                   (Item.Field /= Max_Parts_Field
                    and then Item.Field /= Total_Parts_Count_Field
                    and then Item.Field /= Part_Number_Field
                    and then Parsed.Value > Part_Marker_Value'Last)
                 or else
                   (Item.Field = Max_Parts_Field
                    and then Parsed.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last)
                 or else
                   (Item.Field = Total_Parts_Count_Field
                    and then Parsed.Value > Core.Part_Number'Last)
               then
                  raise Malformed_Attributes with
                    "invalid object part scalar";
               end if;
```

Because that runs inside the handler, `Parse_Result` rewrites the message through its `XML.XML_Error` arm (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:881-885`):

```ada
   exception
      when XML.XML_Error =>
         raise Malformed_Attributes with
           "malformed GetObjectAttributes XML";
   end Parse_Result;
```

so a maintainer reading the diagnostic is pointed at the parser rather than at a range. `Validate` then applies the same bound a second time (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:786-790`):

```ada
         elsif Value.Object_Parts.Max_Parts.Is_Set
           and then Value.Object_Parts.Max_Parts.Value >
             Core.Page_Size'Last
         then
            raise Malformed_Attributes with "invalid max parts";
         end if;
```

The bound is a visible subtype meant for one page of a Flyology listing (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:24-28`):

```ada
   --  Valid S3 multipart part number.
   subtype Part_Number is Positive range 1 .. 10_000;

   --  Requested or returned item count for one S3 page.
   subtype Page_Size is Natural range 0 .. 1_000;
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) places no bound on the member. Printed with python3:

```
--- GetObjectAttributesParts.MaxParts + MaxParts shape ---
{"shape": "MaxParts"}
{"type": "integer"}
```

The `MaxParts` shape has no `min` and no `max`, and the request-side member is the `x-amz-max-parts` header, which uses the same unbounded shape. So the crate is not reusing a limit the model imposes.

MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, exceeds it. A signed `GET /oracle-b/single?attributes` with `x-amz-object-attributes: ETag,Checksum,ObjectParts,StorageClass,ObjectSize`, against an object written with a single PUT:

```
GetObjectAttributes single : 200 <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?> <getObjectAttributesResponse>...<ObjectParts><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated><MaxParts>10000</MaxParts><NextPartNumberMarker>1</NextPartNumberMarker><PartNumberMarker>0</PartNumberMarker><PartsCount>1</PartsCount><Part><PartNumber>1</PartNumber><Size>11</Size></Part></ObjectParts><StorageClass>STANDARD</StorageClass><ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize></getObjectAttributesResponse>
```

Nothing in tree asserts the wrong behaviour, and nothing exercises it either. The conformance corpus disables the GetObjectAttributes assertions entirely under MinIO's oracle mode (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:323-335`):

```ada
   function Check_Get_Object_Attributes return Boolean is
      Name : constant String :=
        "FLYOLOGY_GET_OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_ORACLE_MODE";
   begin
      if not Ada.Environment_Variables.Exists (Name)
        or else Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Name) =
          "rustfs-rc3-missing-error-message"
      then
         return True;
      elsif Ada.Environment_Variables.Value (Name) =
        "minio-2025-lowercase-root"
      then
         return False;
```

and that mode is what `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:64` sets, so MinIO's `MaxParts` never reaches the decoder in CI. The crate's own server never emits a value above its own `Page_Size`, so the codec suites do not see it either.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below prints the two bounds, then decodes one GetObjectAttributes response body twice through `S3.Attributes.Parse_Result` and `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Response`, once with `<MaxParts>1000</MaxParts>` and once with MinIO's real `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>`. Everything else in the two bodies is identical.

```ada
   function Response_Body (Max_Parts : String) return String is
     ("<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""UTF-8""?>" &
      "<GetObjectAttributesResponse " &
      "xmlns=""http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"">" &
      "<Checksum><ChecksumCRC32>" & Digest (Core.CRC32) &
      "</ChecksumCRC32><ChecksumType>FULL_OBJECT</ChecksumType></Checksum>" &
      "<ObjectParts><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>" &
      "<MaxParts>" & Max_Parts & "</MaxParts>" &
      "<NextPartNumberMarker>1</NextPartNumberMarker>" &
      "<PartNumberMarker>0</PartNumberMarker><PartsCount>1</PartsCount>" &
      "<Part><PartNumber>1</PartNumber><Size>11</Size></Part>" &
      "</ObjectParts>" &
      "<ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize>" &
      "</GetObjectAttributesResponse>");
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- Core.Page_Size'Last  = 1000
  -- Core.Part_Number'Last= 10000
  -- 
  -- === control: a MaxParts echo inside the crate's bound ===
  -- control: MaxParts within Page_Size (MaxParts=1000)
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: accepted, MaxParts=1000
  --     Client.Low_Level: accepted, kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND
  -- 
  -- === MinIO's actual MaxParts echo ===
  -- MinIO's GetObjectAttributes answer (MaxParts=10000)
  --     S3.Attributes.Parse_Result: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: malformed GetObjectAttributes XML
  --     Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response
  FAILED: MinIO's GetObjectAttributes answer
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The body uses a `FULL_OBJECT` CRC32 digest, which the multipart policy accepts, so the parts-container rule reported as https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/66 does not explain the rejection: the only difference between the passing and failing legs is the `MaxParts` value. The message that comes out names the XML, not the bound.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A `MaxParts` echoed by another server must not be rejected against Flyology's own page-size policy, because the pinned botocore S3 model puts no `min` or `max` on the `MaxParts` shape and MinIO answers 10000 for every request that selects `ObjectParts`. If a receive-side bound is kept, a failing value must be reported as a range violation and not as malformed XML.

## Suggested treatment

Separate the receive-side rule from Flyology's own pagination policy. Concretely, change the `Max_Parts_Field` arm at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:567-569` and the matching `Validate` branch at `:786-790`, and let the range failure surface with its own message rather than through the `XML.XML_Error` arm at `:881-885`, so the diagnostic names the bound.

This needs a maintainer decision, because `Core.Page_Size` is a visible subtype whose scope is a specification-level choice under the "Ada interface values" section of `AGENTS.md`, not an implementation detail:

- Bound the echo by `Core.Part_Number'Last` (10,000). Accepts MinIO and keeps a sanity limit that has an S3 meaning, since a multipart upload has at most 10,000 parts.
- Place no bound on the echoed value beyond what the field's own type allows. Follows the model exactly, and accepts any future server that echoes a larger number.
- Keep `Core.Page_Size'Last` and document that GetObjectAttributes with `ObjectParts` does not work against servers that echo more than 1,000.

Either of the first two is a change to a validation rule and needs no new public declaration.

https://github.com/flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage/issues/58 is the same 1,000 ceiling, the same `Core.Page_Size` subtype at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:28`, applied on the request side where the model also places no bound, and MinIO exceeds it there too. The two can be fixed independently, but the answer to "where does Flyology's own pagination policy apply" should cover both, so they should be decided together.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the other scripted GetObjectAttributes responses, as a decode of MinIO's `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` body that must succeed, with a value above whatever bound is chosen kept as a rejection asserting a message that names the range. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:323-340` disables the whole GetObjectAttributes group under MinIO's oracle mode, so this will not be covered there until that exclusion is narrowed to the lowercase root element it was written for. No existing assertion has to be inverted. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A well-formed, modelled 200 is reported to the caller as a malformed response, so GetObjectAttributes with `ObjectParts` selected fails against a server that echoes a `MaxParts` above 1,000, and MinIO does that on every such request. The wrong diagnostic makes it worse to diagnose than it needs to be, because it names the XML rather than the bound. Against MinIO specifically the failure is currently masked by the already-documented lowercase-root exclusion, so a deployment only meets it through a MinIO-compatible server that uses the AWS root element. Nothing is written or lost; the read is refused. Deterministic.

## Summary

`Parse_Bucket_Document` runs `Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` on the way in and on the way out, and that predicate is documented as validating a nonempty bucket tag set. It is a publication rule, applied here to a decoded response. MinIO serves `<Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>` with 200 for a bucket whose tag set was stored empty, and the pinned botocore S3 model puts no minimum on `Tagging.TagSet`, so `Decode_Get_Bucket_Tagging_Response` reports `Invalid_Response "malformed GetBucketTagging response"` for a well-formed answer.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

One shared body serves both the request parser and the response decoder, and both run the same set-level predicate (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:562-567`):

```ada
      XML.Parse (Document, Handler, Limits);
      if not Handler.Seen_Tag_Set or else Handler.Depth /= 0 then
         raise Malformed_Tagging with "bucket tagging document lacks TagSet";
      elsif not Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set (Handler.Result) then
         raise Invalid_Tag with "invalid bucket tag set";
      end if;
```

`Parse_Bucket_Response` is one of the two instantiations of that body (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:581-586`):

```ada
   function Parse_Bucket_Response
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Tags.Tag_Set is
     (Parse_Bucket_Document
        (Document, Limits, Allow_Empty_Namespace => True));
```

The predicate is documented as a publication rule that requires at least one tag (`src/flyology-object_storage-tags.ads:34-40`):

```ada
   --  Validate a nonempty bucket tag set: at most 50 unique, case-sensitive
   --  keys; UTF-8 scalar values in the AWS L/Z/N or `_.:/=+-@` character
   --  classes; 1..128 key and 0..256 value Unicode characters; and no
   --  user-defined key with the reserved case-insensitive `aws:` prefix.
   --  @param Value Candidate bucket tag set
   --  @return Whether every tag and the complete set satisfy the constraints
   function Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set (Value : Tag_Set) return Boolean;
```

The contract for the response decoder promises namespace tolerance and says nothing about emptiness (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:48-54`):

```ada
   --  Parse a bucket-tagging response from S3-compatible implementations.
   --  The official S3 namespace and an absent namespace are accepted;
   --  foreign namespaces and attributes remain invalid.
   function Parse_Bucket_Response
     (Document : String;
      Limits   : XML.Parse_Limits := XML.Default_Limits)
      return Tags.Tag_Set;
```

The client catches `Invalid_Tag` and re-raises it as a response-level failure (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:387-405`):

```ada
      if Status = 200 then
         if Charged'Length > 0 then
            raise Invalid_Response with
              "GetBucketTagging returned invalid request charging metadata";
         end if;
         Result.Value := S3.Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response (Payload, Limits);
...
   exception
      when S3.Tagging.Malformed_Tagging | S3.Tagging.Invalid_Tag |
           S3.Errors.Malformed_Error =>
         raise Invalid_Response with "malformed GetBucketTagging response";
```

The pinned botocore S3 model (botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768`, pinned at `coverage/corpora.lock.toml:3-8`) models an empty set. Printed with python3:

```
--- TagSet shape ---
{"type": "list", "member": {"shape": "Tag", "locationName": "Tag"}}
```

The list has no `min`, so zero members is a modelled value.

MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, from the image pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, distinguishes an absent tag set from an empty one and serves the empty one with 200. Four signed requests against the same bucket, in order:

```
DeleteBucketTagging                                        : 204
GetBucketTagging after delete                              : 404 <Error><Code>NoSuchTagSet</Code><Message>The TagSet does not exist</Message>...
PutBucketTagging with an empty TagSet                      : 200
GetBucketTagging after PUT with an empty TagSet            : 200 <Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>
```

`NoSuchTagSet` is what MinIO answers only when no tag set was ever stored. Once a client stores an empty one, which MinIO accepts, the empty response is what a reader gets, so the state is reachable and distinct.

Nothing in tree covers it and nothing asserts the wrong behaviour, because Flyology's own writer cannot create the state. `Serialize_Bucket` refuses an empty set with the same predicate (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:591-596`):

```ada
   function Serialize_Bucket (Value : Tags.Tag_Set) return String is
      Result : US.Unbounded_String;
   begin
      if not Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set (Value) then
         raise Invalid_Tag with "invalid bucket tag set";
      end if;
```

so the decoder never sees an empty set in any lane; another client has to create it.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below decodes MinIO's exact bytes through `S3.Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response` and through `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Bucket_Tagging_Response`. A one-tag body is the control.

```ada
   --  Exactly the bytes MinIO returns (no XML declaration, no namespace).
   Empty_Body : constant String :=
     "<Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>";
   One_Tag_Body : constant String :=
     "<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>k</Key><Value>v</Value></Tag>" &
     "</TagSet></Tagging>";
```

Output, exit status 1:

```
  -- === control: a one-tag GetBucketTagging response ===
  -- control: one tag                     body=<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>k</Key><Value>v</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>
  --     S3.Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response: accepted, tags=1
  --     Client.Low_Level: accepted, kind=BUCKET_TAGS_FOUND
  -- 
  -- === MinIO's empty-TagSet GetBucketTagging response ===
  -- client decodes an empty bucket TagSet body=<Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>
  --     S3.Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.TAGGING.INVALID_TAG: invalid bucket tag set
  --     Client.Low_Level: FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetBucketTagging response
  FAILED: client decodes an empty bucket TagSet
BUG REPRODUCED: 1 failed expectation(s)
```

The two bodies differ only in whether `<TagSet>` holds a `<Tag>`, and both are namespace-free, so the namespace tolerance the contract promises is working and the emptiness is the sole cause. The exception raised is `Invalid_Tag`, which is the set-level predicate and not a structural error, so the failure is the publication rule rather than the parser.

Host: macOS 26.6.2, arm64, GNAT 16.1.0 via Alire. Compared against MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` from the pinned image. Shapes checked against the pinned botocore S3 model.

## What should happen

A GetBucketTagging response carrying an empty `<TagSet>` must decode to an empty tag set, because the pinned botocore S3 model puts no minimum on `Tagging.TagSet`, because `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:48-50` promises only namespace tolerance for this entry point, and because MinIO serves exactly those bytes with 200 for a bucket whose tag set was stored empty. Publishing an empty set may still be refused.

## Suggested treatment

Split the receive-side check from the publication check, so that `Parse_Bucket_Response` accepts an empty decoded set while `Serialize_Bucket` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:591-596` and `Parse_Bucket` keep the nonempty rule. Concretely, that means the `elsif not Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` branch at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:565-566` must not fire for an empty set when the document is a response.

This needs a maintainer decision, because it changes what `Tags.Tag_Set` means at the boundary and `Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` is a visible predicate whose documented meaning is nonemptiness (`src/flyology-object_storage-tags.ads:34-40`):

- Skip the emptiness part of the predicate on the response path only, keeping every per-tag and set-level rule. Smallest change, and it leaves the publication contract untouched. It needs a way to express "empty is allowed here", either a second flag threaded beside `Allow_Empty_Namespace` in `Parse_Bucket_Document` or an explicit `Handler.Result.Is_Empty` short-circuit before the call.
- Redefine `Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` to accept an empty set and move the nonemptiness requirement to `Serialize_Bucket` and `Parse_Bucket`, which are the two places that publish. This makes the visible predicate mean "every tag is valid and the set is consistent", which is what a decoder wants, and it changes the documented meaning of a public function.
- Leave it and document that GetBucketTagging does not work against a bucket whose tag set was stored empty by another client.

The first two need no new public declaration.

The regression test belongs in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, beside the other scripted GetBucketTagging responses, as a decode of `<Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>` that must yield an empty tag set with `Bucket_Tags_Found`, keeping the existing one-tag and invalid-tag cases as they are. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` is where the MinIO leg belongs, since reaching the state requires storing an empty tag set first. Nothing in the suite asserts the wrong behaviour, so no test has to be inverted. The spike is the test.

## Severity

A well-formed 200 is reported to the caller as a malformed response, so reading the tags of a bucket whose tag set was stored empty fails outright rather than returning nothing. It needs another client to have deliberately stored an empty set, which Flyology's own writer cannot do, so a deployment that only ever writes tags through this crate will not reach it. The result is a wrong error rather than wrong data, and it is a pure function of the decoded set, so it fires on every read of such a bucket. Deterministic.

## Summary

Both HTTP listeners instantiate `Flyology.IO.Structured_Servers` with `Handler_Model => Flyology.Lightweight_Task`, and the automatic execution-group pool defaults to one, so every handler of both listeners is multiplexed onto a single event-loop thread. The management `Login` handler then runs a 600,000-iteration PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 derivation inline, with no I/O, no delay and no `Cancellation.Requested` poll, which is exactly the CPU-only case the `Structured_Servers` contract requires a handler to break up. One administrator sign-in holds the loop for about a second: a signed S3 request issued during it takes 0.883 s where the same request with no other traffic takes 0.002 s to 0.007 s. Ten simultaneous logins keep an S3 request waiting 9.780 s, while a plain TCP connect to the same port still completes in 0.001 s, so a health check that only opens a socket does not see the outage.

## Problem

Commit `5fe6e3e`.

The S3 listener runs its handlers as cooperative lightweight tasks (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:187-190`):

```ada
      package Structured is new Flyology.IO.Structured_Servers
        (Handler_Context => Server_Context,
         Handle          => Handle,
         Handler_Model   => Flyology.Lightweight_Task);
```

and so does the management listener (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:853-858`):

```ada
      package Structured is new Flyology.IO.Structured_Servers
        (Handler_Context => Service_Context,
         Handle => Handle,
         Handler_Model => Flyology.Lightweight_Task);

      Server : aliased Structured.Server (Capacity => 32);
```

Neither instantiation sets a `Handler_CPU`, so both are placed by the automatic pool. The pinned flyology 0.1.1-dev release defines that model (`flyology.ads:21-26`, Darwin platform source):

```ada
   --  Run the task cooperatively on an event loop. A CPU aspect in 1 .. 127
   --  selects that shared execution group. Ada reserves CPU 0 as
   --  Not_A_Specific_CPU, so it selects the configured pool exactly as an
   --  absent aspect does.
   Lightweight_Task : constant Execution_Model :=
     System.Task_Info.Process_Scope;
```

and states the pool's default size (`flyology-execution_groups.ads:154-159`):

```ada
   --  Return the automatic pool size without starting any group. The value of
   --  FLYOLOGY_LOOP_POOL_SIZE is captured once at process startup, defaults to
   --  one when absent, and establishes the initial size before task
   --  activation.
   --  Runtime growth or reduction may change it later. A lightweight task
   --  without a specific CPU is assigned among groups 0 .. Result - 1.
```

Nothing in the executable grows that pool, `server/README.md` does not mention `FLYOLOGY_LOOP_POOL_SIZE`, and the maintained test launches the server with `env -i` (`server/tests/scripts/test.sh:462-471`), so the shipped default of one group applies.

`Login` calls the password check inline in its handler (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:438-440`):

```ada
           or else not Admin_Credentials.Verify
             (Admin_Credential, "admin",
              Form (Form'First + Login_Prefix'Length .. Form'Last))
```

The work factor is 600,000 (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_credentials.adb:16`):

```ada
   Work_Factor : constant Positive := 600_000;
```

and the derivation loop suspends on nothing (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_credentials.adb:134-147`):

```ada
      for Iteration in 2 .. Iterations loop
         declare
            Next : String (1 .. Hash_Length) := HMAC (Password, U);
         begin
            for Index in Result'Range loop
               Result (Index) := Character'Val
                 (Interfaces.Unsigned_8 (Character'Pos (Result (Index))) xor
                  Interfaces.Unsigned_8 (Character'Pos (Next (Index))));
            end loop;
            Wipe (U);
            U := Next;
            Wipe (Next);
         end;
      end loop;
```

That is the case the server library requires a handler to break up (pinned flyology 0.1.1-dev, `flyology-io-structured_servers.ads:98-102`):

```ada
   --  other values are seconds. One deadline covers the drain. Connection I/O
   --  wakes on cancellation, while
   --  CPU-only Handle code must poll Cancellation.Requested cooperatively.
   --  Lightweight handlers suspend on event-loop I/O; native handlers block
   --  their threads.
```

A wrong password costs the same as a right one, because `Verify` derives first and compares afterwards in constant time (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_credentials.adb:329-340`):

```ada
      if User'Length /= Username'Length
        or else not Constant_Time_Equal (User, Username)
        or else Password'Length not in 1 .. 64
      then
         return False;
      end if;
      declare
         Candidate : String (1 .. Hash_Length) :=
           Derive (Password, Value.Salt, Value.Iterations);
         Matches : constant Boolean :=
           Constant_Time_Equal (Candidate, Value.Hash);
```

The length gate at `:331` never short-circuits a real attempt, because `Login` reaches `Verify` only with a password of exactly `Generated_Password_Length` characters. The constant-time comparison itself is correct and is not what this report is about.

Nothing documents the coupling. `server/README.md:8-12` couples the two listeners only through supervision order:

```
The server provides bounded S3 and management HTTP/1.1 listeners under a
dependency-ordered Flyology static supervisor. SIGTERM requests an orderly
stop, interrupts accepts and active connection handlers, and allows a
ten-second S3 drain. The management child depends on the S3 child, so an S3
generation failure restarts the workbench generation as well.
```

No existing test can catch it. The maintained script issues its logins one after another with a full second between them (`server/tests/scripts/test.sh:102-107`):

```sh
  code=$(admin_request POST "$base/api/login" "$body" "$headers" \
    -H "Origin: $base" \
    --data-binary 'username=admin&password=000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000')
  test "$code" = 401
  sleep 1
```

and never issues S3 traffic while a login is in flight, so no assertion covers S3 availability during a sign-in and none has to change.

## What happens now

Spike: the program below starts the built server itself on a fresh temporary root with `env -i`, `FLYOLOGY_S3_PORT=0 FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_PORT=0` and the memory backend, reads the two `READY` ports and the bootstrap password from the server's own output, then times three signed SigV4 `ListBuckets` requests with no other traffic, three logins on their own, and three signed S3 requests each issued 0.15 s into a valid login, with a plain TCP connect alongside. It asserts the correct behaviour, that a concurrent S3 request stays within the larger of ten times the worst baseline and 0.050 s, and it always stops the server.

```sh
for run in 1 2 3; do
  python3 "$probe" login "$admin_port" "$password" >"$work/login-$run" 2>&1 &
```
